# Kalshi Arbitrage Bot — Claude Context

This is a **production prediction-market pair-trading bot** (the "Kalshi Arbitrage Bot" is its product name; the same-title strategy is a near-arbitrage, the time-series strategy is a directional bet) that trades real money via RSA-signed Kalshi REST API calls. Read this file in full before making any changes to trading, fee, or order-submission logic.

---

## What This Is

The bot finds two types of mispriced binary contract pairs on Kalshi, sizes positions using the Kelly criterion, and submits fill-or-kill orders leg-by-leg (there is no batch order endpoint), unwinding the first leg if the second doesn't fill. With a sell level saved (`LiveSettings.sell_at`), a production run first sells the held positions that have reached it, by the take-profit rule the backtest uses and before it buys anything (see the live-selling paragraph under Key Patterns). A separate backtesting pipeline replays the same strategy on the full history of settled markets.

**Time-series pairs:** Two contracts asking the same question at different deadlines (e.g. "Will BTC exceed $80k by March?" vs. "by June?"). `market_a` is always the EARLIER contract — earlier by `close_time`, except for a same-event ladder (DR-73), where it is earlier by STATED deadline and the pair carries that gap on `CandidatePair.stated_gap_days`. A later deadline can only add probability, so the later contract's YES ask normally sits above the earlier's, and the gap `pB − pA` measures the market-implied probability that the event first happens *between* the two deadlines — plus half the later book's width less half the earlier book's (a book's width, its YES ask plus its NO ask less 1, is a cost of trading rather than a view of the odds), which is why the forecast reads that probability at the midpoints instead (the mid spread, DR-78; see its gotcha below). The bot disputes that number when it is large: when `pB − pA` passes the run's live spread rule (`config.time_series_spread_refusal`: strictly positive, at least the entry floor — the band floor alone with the tier floors off, as the seed saves them and config.py ships them since the operator decision of 2026-09-27 (a floor of 0, so any strictly positive spread), or the deadline-gap tier `min_price_diff_for_gap(gap_days)` with the band floor layered on it with the tier floors on, as before that decision — and within the spread band's ceiling, 0.5 in the seed; a live run reads both from the saved live defaults, `live_defaults.json`; see the saved-live-defaults and directional-filter paragraphs and the live-defaults decision record of 2026-09-27 under Key Patterns) it buys YES on the earlier contract (at `pA`) and NO on the later one (at `nB`). Exactly three settlement cells exist: the event happens by the earlier deadline (A=YES ⇒ B=YES; the YES on A pays), it never happens by the later deadline (A=NO, B=NO; the NO on B pays), or it happens in between (A=NO, B=YES; both legs expire worthless — the full stake is lost). A=YES with B=NO is impossible for a cumulative-deadline pair. **That premise is now screened on WORDING at pair formation (a heuristic) rather than assumed (DR-67):** both finders refuse a pair unless both legs' WORDING states a cumulative "by <date>" deadline and the two deadlines DIFFER — `scanner.deadline_phrasing` / `scanner.cumulative_deadline_pair`, one definition shared by `find_time_series_pairs` and `backtester._extract_pairs` (see the cumulative-deadline gotcha below). The backtester still treats a pair that settled A=YES/B=NO as a premise violation and excludes it with a counted WARNING, never pays it — but that counter is no longer the ONLY signal, it is defence in depth behind a text heuristic, and a non-zero count now means one of the WARNING's named causes fired — most likely a wording false negative, though legs ordered on an early REALIZED close (cross-event pairs only — a same-event ladder is ordered on its stated deadlines), or strike-blind grouping on a cache without subtitles, can also produce it (DR-72 widened the WARNING beyond "the wording screen missed it" — see the strategy-change gotcha below for the full cause list). A wording heuristic has residuals: recurring rolling-window series ("before <date>" families that publish a new deadline every week) pass the screen without genuinely nesting, but checked against real prices they are co-quoted only in the earlier leg's final hours at a YES ask near $0.01, where the trade reduces to a NO bet on the later window regardless — measured harmless; see the Known residuals list in the cumulative-deadline gotcha below. This is a **directional bet, not an arbitrage**: at market prices its expected value is zero minus fees, and it only profits if the market systematically overstates the in-between mass. Kelly sizes it with `config.time_series_profit_prob(spread)` = `1 − k·spread`, where `spread` is the MID spread — the later market's midpoint minus the earlier one's, a midpoint being halfway between a market's YES ask and its YES bid (`config.time_series_mid_spread`; DR-78) — and `k = TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT` (0.80 since the operator decision of 2026-09-27; 0.75 before it) is the fraction of that market-implied in-between mass the operator believes ("prices converge by 20%"; by 25% at 0.75) — an operator-tunable estimate, not a measured quantity. With no per-trade cap in config.py since that decision, `k` also bounds every time-series trade: `f* < 1 − k` = 0.20 (the seed adds a 10% per-trade cap; a live run reads `k` and its caps from the saved live defaults). Two contracts are only "the same question at two deadlines" when they ask about the same OUTCOME, so the grouping key is `scanner.time_series_group_key(pair_key(m), m.subtitle)` — the date-stripped `event_title | market_title` PLUS the market's outcome label — not the title alone: a daily price family lists dozens of strikes under ONE title and differs only in the subtitle (DR-01, see the cross-strike gotcha below). Two contracts of ONE event ticker were refused outright until DR-73, on the rationale that a shared event is a multi-choice OPTION set; that is true of an MVE event's labels and false of a deadline LADDER, so `config.TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS` (ON by operator decision of 2026-09-26; it shipped OFF) admits two dated cumulative rungs of one event, ordered and tiered on their stated deadlines — see the same-event-ladder gotcha below. And two contracts whose RAW wording is identical — same `title`, same `subtitle`, same event title — across two event tickers of ONE series are not a cumulative-deadline pair at all: the deadline lives outside the wording, so they are two instances of one recurring fixture and the finder skips them (DR-02, DR-54 — see the one-series gotcha below). This conjunct lives in the time-series finder as well as the same-title one because gating only the latter would merely relabel the trade.

**Same-title pairs:** Two contracts with identical title + subtitle on different event tickers of DIFFERENT series whose two markets close within `config.SAME_TITLE_MAX_CLOSE_GAP_SECONDS` (1 hour) of each other. Both conditions are required: two events of one series are two instances of one recurring fixture and are never paired (DR-02, DR-54 — see the one-series gotcha below), and identical wording on two different series closing more than an hour apart is two different games or instants — a men's and a women's college basketball game between the same two schools, or two competitions' fixtures of one matchup — and is never paired either (DR-74 — see the close-gate gotcha below; `scanner.closes_apart` is the one definition, and it fails closed on a close time it cannot compare). Any divergence of 5% or more is anomalous (the code rejects `pA - pB < SAME_TITLE_MIN_PRICE_DIFF`, i.e. `>=` qualifies, not a strict `>`); the bot exploits it assuming 95% co-resolution probability. The subtitle is the intra-title outcome discriminator and is sourced from the API's `yes_sub_title` field (see the subtitle-drift gotcha below) — it is load-bearing, not cosmetic, and since DR-01 it discriminates in BOTH finders: the same-title key `(event_title, title, subtitle)` and the time-series key `scanner.time_series_group_key`.

**Multivariate (MVE) markets:** Both pair types include MVE markets (option markets within a multi-choice event, e.g. "Trump" inside "2024 Election Winner") when `INCLUDE_MVE_MARKETS=True` in `config.py`. Pairs are grouped by `event_title + market_title` (see `scanner.pair_key`) so option labels like "Trump" or "Above $80k" can't false-positive across unrelated events; the time-series finder keys on that PLUS the subtitle (`scanner.time_series_group_key`), which still leaves one option label at two deadlines in a single group but splits two different labels. Since DR-54 no same-title pair is ever formed between two MVE combo events: Kalshi lists combos under several series prefixes, all beginning `KXMVE` (`KXMVECROSSCATEGORY`, `KXMVESPORTSMULTIGAMEEXTENDED`, `KXMVECROSSCATEGORY0`, `KXMVENBASINGLEGAME`), and since DR-55 `scanner.event_series` collapses that whole family onto one series (`config.MVE_SERIES_FAMILY_PREFIX`) so the one-series rule treats any two combo tickets as two instances of one recurring fixture — a combo ticket's wording names its legs but never its date, so one wording recurs across fixture instances and two tickets with identical leg wording are two DIFFERENT tickets. That is a pairing rule, not an ingest exclusion — MVE markets are still fetched, grouped and traded as time-series pairs and against non-combo same-title counterparties. **DR-73 does not open MVE option labels to pairing either, and for the same reason the cumulative-deadline rule does not: it turns on WORDING.** Two markets of one event pair only when both are worded as cumulative deadlines reading to two DIFFERENT calendar dates, which an option set ("Trump" / "Harris" under one election event) never is — so the branch admits a deadline ladder and still refuses the multi-choice options this exclusion was written for. **The cumulative-deadline rule (DR-67) does not change that, because it turns on WORDING rather than on a market being MVE:** an MVE market whose sub-contract label carries a "by <date>" deadline ("$80,000 by June 30") is eligible like any other, and so is one whose parent EVENT title carries it ("Presidential Election Winner by March 2026") — those two routes are pinned by `tests/test_scanner.py::TestOutcomeDiscriminator::test_dated_outcome_label_is_one_key` and `::test_mve_option_label_at_two_deadlines_still_pairs`. The event-title route survives DR-69's deciding-field spans because there the event title IS the deciding field (the option label and title carry no marker), so it supplies the spans itself (`tests/test_scanner.py::TestSpansFromDecidingField::test_mve_event_title_route_still_pairs`). What that rule does refuse is the narrower case of a combo/parlay ticket whose wording names its legs but no date at all ("Parlay" / "All legs hit"), which classifies `unknown` and fails closed — correct, since such a pair never had a cumulative-deadline premise either. That refusal is theoretical on the LIVE path today: live combo ingest currently returns zero nested markets (see the SDK-response-model drift gotcha, which records that the prod MVE listing returns zero nested markets regardless of `with_nested_markets`), so no live combo ticket reaches this rule to be refused by it. The CLASSIFIER does run routinely in the BACKTEST — cached day slices do carry combo records, and `_extract_pairs` classifies every member of a >=2-member time-series group up front, combo or not — but no combo PAIR ever reaches this refusal there either: two combos landing in one time-series group are always identically-worded duplicates of one KXMVE ticket, so the one-series conjunct (checked first) drops them a line earlier, before `cumulative_deadline_pair` is ever asked (measured on the 2026-09-08/09 day slices: 0 of 856,513 combo records share a group key with a differently-worded combo or with any non-combo). Since DR-68 the classifier does read combos worded "by Decision" (fight-method legs), which used to read as a "by Dec" deadline — on those same slices that was every one of the 525 combo records classified cumulative, and none remains. On those same 2026-09-08/09 day slices, the time-series pairs DR-67 removes from `main`'s set were mostly snapshot families (97.3% of 943,450 non-MVE pairs, per the verifier), plus dateless non-MVE look-alikes (2.7%) — combos contributed ZERO time-series candidates before and after this change (DR-02/54/55 already excluded them). Set `INCLUDE_MVE_MARKETS=False` to fall back to binary-only behaviour. With the flag off, event titles are still resolved and attached in both the scanner and the backtester — the flag narrows which markets are fetched, never how the remaining ones are grouped.

---

## Module Map

| Module | Role | Key exports |
|--------|------|-------------|
| `__init__.py` | Package initializer; marks the directory as the `kalshi_betting` package | — |
| `config.py` | All constants (bar one named backtest-only exception, `backtester.SIZE_CAP_SWEEP` — see the constants rule) and fee/threshold/model helpers, plus the shared collision-free output-file creator — imported by everyone | `PROJECT_ROOT`, `BUDGET_FRACTION`, `TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT`, `SAME_TITLE_LEG_SIDES`, `TIME_SERIES_LEG_SIDES`, `OUTPUT_NAME_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `create_new_output()`, `fee_per_pair_approx()`, `fee_leg_exact()`, `min_price_diff_for_gap()` (its `spread_min` band floor and keyword-only `tier_floors` switch are passed by the backtest, and on the live path only through `live_time_series_floor()`), `time_series_profit_prob()` (the time-series forecast: `1 − k·max(0, spread)` on the mid spread, the one definition every sizer calls; the live sizer never asks it with a spread at or below zero), `time_series_mid_spread()` (a time-series pair's mid spread: the later market's midpoint — halfway between its YES ask and its YES bid, 1 − its NO ask — minus the earlier one's, computed as the YES-ask gap less half the difference in the two books' widths (a book's width is its YES ask plus its NO ask, minus 1, a cost of trading), so it is the ask gap to the last bit whenever the two books' YES ask plus NO ask come to the same float, as whole-cent pairs such as 0.30 and 0.70 do, and within about 1e-16 of it on any other book with no width; live enrichment passes a NO ask of 1.0 (a bid of 0) for a market with no YES bid, while backtest candle quotes never hold a NO ask above 0.99, since the candle fetch clamps every NO ask into 0.01–0.99, so where an earlier market's YES bid is missing or under a cent the backtest's mid spread can sit up to 0.005 below the live one; the one definition `scanner._enrich_pair` and `backtester._candidate_pair` write `CandidatePair.mid_spread` with, and the time-series forecast's input: the live sizer reads it off the pair, the backtest's Kelly gate works it out from the same four candle quotes and the dashboard's Kelly scatter and spread calibration from each trade's entry quotes (`dashboard._entry_mid_spread`); the backtest's empirical `k̂` divides the realised in-between rate by it, read at each pair's first qualifying Monday (`backtester._interval_calibration`), so `k̂` and the forecast's `k` are fractions of one quantity; the entry rules read the YES-ask gap — DR-78), `max_affordable_pairs()` (its keyword-only `cash_cents` keeps the budget at or under the cash on hand), `kelly_budget()` (the one rule for what one trade may spend: `min(portfolio value × capped Kelly fraction, cash)` — `strategy._evaluate_size` budgets with it, `max_affordable_pairs` turns it into a count for the sizer and enrichment's depth bound alike, and `backtester._simulate_at_discount` sizes each simulated Monday with it, through `strategy.compute_trade`, on the checkpoint's cash plus its open trades at market and the cash left), `leg_cash_cents()` (one order leg's dollar cost rounded up to the whole cent: the one rounding `strategy.select_portfolio` spends the cash with and `trader._required_cents_by_shard` funds each shard with), `CONTRACT_PAYOUT_DOLLARS` (1.0: what one contract pays if it wins, so the most an open position can be worth per contract; `main._checked_positions_value` refuses Kalshi's value of the open positions when it is above this times the contracts held), `SIZE_SOLVE_MAX_ITERATIONS`, `TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS`, the toggle constants `TIME_SERIES_TIER_FLOORS`/`TIME_SERIES_SPREAD_BAND`/`SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` (the extra per-trade cap on same-title pairs)/`TRADE_CATEGORIES`/`TRADE_TAGS` (the category/tag filter, `None` = any)/`ADD_TO_HELD_PAIRS` (whether a production run may add to an exact pair the account holds, `True`; DR-77)/`SELL_AT`/`SELL_MIN_DAYS` (the share of potential profit a held position is sold at, and the fewest days before its last market stops trading it may be sold at; both `None`, which never sells) — config.py's own values, the backtest's `k` and caps and the no-settings fallback, never a live run's defaults (those are the saved file below) — and the caps' grid `SIZE_CAP_STEP` and the sell level's grid `SELL_AT_STEP` (0.01; `_step_share` holds a sell level to it), `LiveSettings` (one run's frozen, validated toggles, plus an `origin` that says where the defaults came from and is never compared — `LIVE_DEFAULTS_FROM_CONFIG` for toggles built from config.py's constants; its `sell_at` is `None` or a level on that grid in (0, 1], its `sell_min_days` `None` or a whole number of days, 1 or more, refused without a `sell_at`) with `LIVE_TOGGLE_FIELDS` (the ten toggle names, every field but `origin`; `add_to_held_pairs`, `sell_at` and `sell_min_days` come last, defaulting to `False`, `None` and `None`), `_OPTIONAL_TOGGLES` (the toggles a saved file may leave out — those three, which then read as off, never selling and no minimum — and which `save_live_defaults` leaves out while at their default, so a file saved with all three off holds only the other seven, byte for byte the file every save wrote before the first of them, and code that does not know them can still read it), `live_settings()` (config.py's ten constants as `LiveSettings`: what a live entry point falls back to when handed no settings — tests and direct calls only — never a live run's defaults), the saved live defaults — `LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE` (`live_defaults.json` in the repo root, gitignored operator state) with `LIVE_DEFAULTS_FORMAT` (the file's format tag), `LIVE_DEFAULTS_MAX_BYTES` (the largest file read) and `LIVE_DEFAULTS_SOURCE_MAX_CHARS` (the longest source note), `read_saved_live_defaults()` (a strict read: `None` when no file exists, `LiveDefaultsError` naming the file when something exists there but cannot be used — a directory or FIFO included, refused without waiting on it), `live_defaults()` (the same read, but a missing file raises `LiveDefaultsMissing`, a `LiveDefaultsError`; both are `ValueError`s, and it never falls back to the toggle constants; `main._resolve_live_settings` is its one live caller, so a live run starts only from the saved file) and `save_live_defaults()` (the text parsed as the reader will before anything is written, so a file the reader refuses never replaces a good one; then an atomic write, flushed with its directory, then read back), `LIVE_DEFAULTS_SEED` (the starting values `defaults_server --seed` offers for a first save: tier floors off, band 0-0.5, `k` 0.80, a 10% per-trade cap, a 20% same-title cap, any category or tag, adding to held pairs on, selling off) with `LIVE_DEFAULTS_SEED_SOURCE`, `LIVE_DEFAULTS_SOURCE_PATTERN` (the only two note shapes `defaults_server`'s confirmation page accepts besides no note at all, applied with `re.fullmatch` and `re.ASCII`, the seed's note only on the seed values; the writer and reader themselves accept any note `live_defaults_source()` passes, an empty one included) and `live_defaults_source()` (the note's own rules), and `live_settings_changes()` (saved defaults against proposed ones, toggle by toggle, in the "Live settings:" line's words), plus the defaults server's `DEFAULTS_SERVER_HOST`/`DEFAULTS_SERVER_PORT` (`127.0.0.1:8765`), `DEFAULTS_SERVER_MAX_REQUEST_BYTES`, `DEFAULTS_SERVER_SOCKET_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` and `DEFAULTS_SERVER_CONFIRM_ARM_MS`, and `DASHBOARD_FILENAME` (the one dashboard file a backtest writes, which `dashboard.py` imports and the server opens), `DASHBOARD_FILES_DIRNAME` (`backtest_dashboard_files`, the folder beside that page holding one sub-folder per build of the sidecar chunk files its Sell select loads) and `DASHBOARD_BUILD_STALE_SECONDS` (two days: how old a build folder still marked as being written must be before another build deletes it) with `DASHBOARD_MARKER_SCAN_BYTES` (1 MiB: how much of that file the server reads to tell whether it has the filter bar's Save and Trade buttons), `DEFAULTS_SERVER_CHECKOUT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`/`DEFAULTS_SERVER_CHECKOUT_MAX_BYTES` (the wait and the read limit of the one `/checkout` question a start asks a taken port; the wait is `2 * DEFAULTS_SERVER_SOCKET_TIMEOUT_SECONDS + 2`, so it outlasts two idle browser connections the running server may be holding first), and the runs the server starts: `LIVE_RUNS_DIR` (`live_runs/` in the repo root, gitignored, one folder per run — `run.json`, `output.log`, `result.json`; read at call time), `DEFAULTS_SERVER_RUN_REFRESH_SECONDS` (how often a run's page reloads while it goes), `DEFAULTS_SERVER_RUN_LOG_TAIL_LINES`/`DEFAULTS_SERVER_RUN_LOG_TAIL_BYTES` (how much of its output the page shows, read from the end only) and `DEFAULTS_SERVER_INDEX_RUNS` (how many runs the index lists) — `live_time_series_floor()`, `time_series_spread_refusal()` with `SPREAD_NOT_POSITIVE`/`SPREAD_BELOW_FLOOR`/`SPREAD_ABOVE_CEILING` (the one live definition of the time-series spread rule), `pair_size_cap()` (the one definition of a pair's per-trade cap, shared by the live sizer, enrichment's bound and the backtester), `held_pair_fraction()` (the one definition of an add-on's size: the fraction of the portfolio value an add-on to a held pair may take, `min(fraction, fraction − held stake / portfolio value)`, 0.0 when the portfolio value is not a positive, finite number or the difference is not finite — Kelly sizes the whole position, the held stake being the pair's worth plus the fees paid for it (live, `HeldPair.stake_dollars`: its worth at today's prices plus both legs' fees; in the backtest, its open trades at market plus their fees), fees the portfolio value leaves out, and the add-on's budget is `kelly_budget(portfolio value, that fraction, cash)`, never more than a new pair's; read by `strategy._evaluate_size`, `backtester._simulate_at_discount` and `main._run_prod`'s at-cap check), `max_kelly_fraction()` (enrichment's affordability bound: the largest capped Kelly fraction the sizer can return under the same settings), `count_text()` (a contract count or signed position written exactly, to six decimal places with trailing zeros dropped — never `%g`, which keeps six significant digits — for every "adds to N held" marker and every alert that names what the account held before a pair), `describe_time_series_rule()`, `describe_live_settings()` (the one-line "Live settings:" echo, marking each field that departs from the reference: `(default: X)` against saved live defaults, `(config: X)` against toggles built from config.py's constants — the reference's `origin` decides), `describe_trade_filter()` (the category/tag filter in that echo's words) and `live_rule_warnings()` (every setting that empties a gap range or lets one pair stake more than `LIVE_EXPOSURE_WARN_FRACTION`), `order_api_version_error()` (the one startup check of the order path: `None` only for exactly the str `"v2"`, otherwise a one-line message that names the value and points back at `"v2"`; `main.main` and `v2_probe.main` hand it to `parser.error`, exit 2), `BACKTEST_DEFAULT_SPREAD_BAND`, `SPREAD_BAND_SWEEP_FLOORS`, `SPREAD_BAND_SWEEP_CEILINGS`, `TAKE_PROFIT_LEVELS` (the backtest dashboard's sell levels, every 1% from 80% to 100%: the dashboard's grid only, which no live module reads — a live run sells at its own saved `sell_at`, any 1% step from 1% to 100%), `TAKE_PROFIT_MIN_DAYS` (the minimum-days options offered beside each sell level, 1 to 7, 14 and 21: a position at its level is sold only while at least that many days remain before its last market stops trading — the dashboard's grid only, as the levels are: a live `sell_min_days` is any whole number of days from 1), `TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS` (3: how many days in a row a position must stay at a sell level before it is sold — checked once a day, 24 hours apart, the last check at the weekly checkpoint where the backtest sells it, or at the moment of a live run's sale; a whole number from 1 to `TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS_MAX`, 7. Shared by the backtest and live selling, each reading its own binding when called: `backtester._resolve_hold_days` and `seller._hold_days`), `DASHBOARD_SELL_MAX_WORKERS` (8: the most worker processes the dashboard builds its Sell select in, the bound of `backtest.py --sell-workers`' default), the sell rule's tests, shared by the backtest and live selling — `take_profit_reached()` (whether a position has realized `sell_at` of its potential profit at one check, with `PRICE_EPSILON` of float slack; never when its potential profit is not above 0; `backtester._sells_at` and `seller.plan_sales` decide with it), `reached_every_check()` (that at every check; never with none; `seller.plan_sales` calls it, and `tests/test_config.py::TestReachedEveryCheck::test_it_is_the_backtests_rule` pins it equal to `backtester._reached_every_day`) and `days_to_maturity()` (the whole days from a check date to a position's last close date, `None` for an unknown date; it refuses a datetime; `backtester._days_left` and `seller.plan_sales` read it) — and the live sale's own constants: `CANDLE_NO_ASK_CEILING` (0.99: the highest NO ask a candle holds, which is no quote), `SALE_PARTNER_MAX_LOOKUPS` (20) and `SALE_PARTNER_MAX_AGE_DAYS` (60, twice `MAX_DEADLINE_GAP_DAYS`: the settled-market lookups a run may make, and the days back a settlement may be, to find a lone held market's paid-out partner), `SALE_HEDGE_SLIPPAGE_TICKS` (1: the ticks below its walked bid a pair's second sale order may fill), `SALE_READ_BACK_RECHECK_SECONDS` (1.0: the wait before a second positions read when the first does not yet show a live run's sales), `SETTLEMENT_PAGE_SIZE` (200) and `RECENT_CANDLES_RATE_LIMIT_SLEEP_SECONDS` (0.05: the pause after each request `historical.recent_candles` makes), `time_series_spread_band()`, `time_series_spread_too_wide()`, `ScheduledRun`, `SCHEDULED_RUN`, `SERIES_CATEGORY_CACHE_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` (how long the cached /series listing is reused — by the dashboard and by the live category/tag filter), the live-run lock's `LIVE_RUN_LOCK_FILE` (`~/.kalshi_betting/live_run.lock`, in the home folder so every checkout and worktree shares it; read at call time), `LIVE_RUN_LOCK_WAIT_SECONDS` (2 s) and `LIVE_RUN_LOCK_POLL_SECONDS` (0.1 s), `EXIT_RUN_IN_PROGRESS` (50), `SCHEDULER_STATE_FILENAME` (`scheduler_state.json`, the scheduler's slot record under `PROJECT_ROOT`), the run result's `LIVE_RUN_RESULT_FORMAT`, `RUN_REPORT_MAX_WARNINGS` and `RUN_REPORT_LINE_MAX_CHARS` (see `reporter.py`), and `live_settings_argv()` (a `LiveSettings` spelled as `main.py`'s ten toggle flags — caps as whole percents, numbers as their `repr`, `--add-to-held-pairs`/`--no-add-to-held-pairs` either way, the sell level as `--sell-at=PCT` or `--no-sell` and its minimum as `--sell-min-days=N` or `--no-sell-min-days`, each category or tag as `--category=NAME`/`--tag=NAME` so a name beginning with "-" stays a name, no filter as `--any-category`/`--any-tag` — which `main._resolve_live_settings` lays over any saved defaults to give back the same settings), `CANDLESTICK_CACHE_FIELDS_VERSION` (the fields a cached candle file carries; a file written under another version is fetched again) and the backtest depth model's `DEPTH_*` settings (read only by `depth_model.py`) |
| `auth.py` | Builds authenticated `KalshiClient`; reads the account balance — each exchange shard's cash, and Kalshi's value of the open positions (the reply's `portfolio_value`: integer cents, all shards, never including cash; `None` when unreadable, never an error) | `build_client(mode)`, `AccountBalance` (frozen: `shard_cash_cents`, `positions_value_cents`), `read_account_balance(client) -> AccountBalance` (the one retried balance read and its "Auth OK" line), `verify_auth(client) -> dict[int, int]` (that read, returning only the cash), `read_shard_balances(client) -> dict[int, int]` (single-shot, cash only) |

| `seller.py` | Decides which held positions the take-profit rule sells this run and plans each sale; sends no order. `plan_sales(client, held_positions, held_labels, markets_by_ticker, *, settings, now)` judges every held position — an exact held pair (`scanner.held_pairs(..., log=False)`) or a lone held market with its one paid-out partner among `scanner.get_settlements` (a settled market held on the other side, same count, same question, paid out within `config.SALE_PARTNER_MAX_AGE_DAYS`) — by `config.take_profit_reached` at each of `config.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS` checks 24 hours apart and `config.reached_every_check` over all of them: now on the held market's real order book (`scanner.bid_ladder`, walked for the count by `scanner.walk_bids`), each earlier day on its last hourly candle bid in any size (`historical.bid_before` over `historical.recent_candles`), a market worth its count at the price less `config.fee_leg_exact`, a paid-out partner at its settlement revenue. `settings.sell_min_days` is a days-to-maturity rule (`config.days_to_maturity` of the held markets' scheduled close dates) checked before anything is valued. It logs one `Take-profit check` line per position that reaches its checks and a summary, a `Not selling` line with the reason for each held market or position it will not sell (three or more on a ladder, unequal counts, an unreadable count or cost, a missing or ambiguous partner, a market not in the run's list), and returns the `SalePlan`s (frozen: the legs, count, cost, the bid ladders and walks the orders are priced from, proceeds, the profit at each check, days left, level); a held market whose ladder is unknown means no position is judged. Imports the standard library, `config`, `scanner` and `historical` only — never `backtester`, `depth_model`, `trader`, `strategy`, `reporter` or `main` — and is imported by `main.py` alone (`tests/test_seller.py::TestIsolation`); `trader` and `reporter` read its plans by their fields. Its arithmetic is the backtest's: `tests/test_seller.py::TestParityWithTheBacktest` builds one position as a backtest trade with `LegQuotes` and as live positions, books, candles and a settlement, and compares the realized and potential profit at every check, the cost, the proceeds now and the verdict at several levels | `plan_sales()`, `SalePlan`, `SaleLeg` |
| `main.py` | Orchestrator for live trading pipeline — orchestration plus two pair-list filters, `_dedup_pairs` and `_filter_by_category` (keep only the pairs filed under the run's Kalshi categories and tags, by the dashboard's own rule — see the category/tag filter paragraph under Key Patterns); right after parsing its arguments `main()` refuses any `config.ORDER_API_VERSION` but `"v2"` (`config.order_api_version_error`, through `parser.error`: exit 2 in either mode, before anything below runs), then resolves the run's one `config.LiveSettings` through `_resolve_live_settings` — the saved live defaults (`config.live_defaults()`, its one read of `live_defaults.json` per run, with no config.py fallback: none saved or a refused file exits 2), each flag of the "live trading toggles" group given (`--tier-floors`/`--no-tier-floors`, `--spread-min`/`--spread-max`, `--interval-discount`, `--size-cap`/`--same-title-size-cap` in percent, `--add-to-held-pairs`/`--no-add-to-held-pairs`, `--sell-at`/`--no-sell`, `--sell-min-days`/`--no-sell-min-days`, `--category`/`--any-category`, `--tag`/`--any-tag`) replacing its own field for THIS run (`--no-sell` clears the minimum of days too; a minimum with no level exits 2), validated before logging is configured (a bad value exits 2) — with the saved defaults as the reference; each run mode logs both (`_log_live_settings`: a "Live defaults: <origin>" line naming the saved file, one "Live settings:" line marking every departure `(default: X)`, a WARNING on a production run that submits orders under one, and every `live_rule_warnings` sentence) and hands the one object to the time-series finder, the category/tag filter, enrichment, the sizer (`compute_trade`, through `_compute_trade_specs`), `pre_execution_check`, `_no_pairs_msg` and the trade log's separator note (marked against the reference, as the "Live settings:" line is, and ending `| defaults: <origin>`); `_run_prod` also reads its held positions with their sides and costs (`scanner.get_held_positions`, with `complete_out` saying whether the listing was read to its end) and the ladders they are on (`scanner.resolve_held_ladders`, over the full market list before held markets are dropped, its `labels_out` keeping each market's labels) and hands the ladders to the time-series finder and `select_portfolio` — a held market it cannot identify means no time-series pair that run, and the run exits `EXIT_TIME_SERIES_SKIPPED` (DR-76; `_run_dev` holds nothing and passes none); `_run_prod` reads the balance before trading (`auth.read_account_balance`, read again after trading for the trade log's cash), sizes on the portfolio value (`_bankroll_cents`: the cash on every shard plus Kalshi's value of the open positions, or the cash alone, with a WARNING, when that value cannot be read) and spends only the cash — enrichment and the sizer (`_compute_trade_specs`, whose keyword-only `cash_cents` is required) get both numbers, `select_portfolio` the cash — gates `MIN_BALANCE_CENTS` on the portfolio value (cash alone below it is a WARNING, and the run goes on), logs `Sizing on portfolio value $X = cash $Y + open positions $Z` on every run (`not read (counted as $0.00)` in place of `$Z` when the reply carried no value), and shows each selected spec in the pairs table, one `select_portfolio` shrank included (`_display_specs`, matched on the pair's two tickers); `_run_dev` spends its virtual balance as both numbers (see the portfolio-value paragraph under Key Patterns); once it has read its positions (only a run past that minimum reads them), `_run_prod` checks Kalshi's value of the open positions against them (`_checked_positions_value`: a value of 0 is always kept, since it adds nothing; one above 0 only when it is at most `config.CONTRACT_PAYOUT_DOLLARS` × the contracts held, over a listing read to its end whose every count is readable), and a refused value is one WARNING, after which the run sizes on the cash alone, records the cash as `portfolio_value_before` and applies the minimum to it again (exit 10 below it); the low-cash WARNING comes after this check; with `settings.sell_at` set it sells before it buys (the sale block, see the live-selling paragraph under Key Patterns): once every held market is identified and the positions listing is complete (else one `Not selling this run` WARNING) it plans the sales (`seller.plan_sales`), sends them (`trader.sell_positions`, which sends nothing in a dry run) and writes them to the trade log before anything is bought (`_record_sales`; a live run that sold writes them after the read-back below, or at once if that raises); a live run that sold reads its positions again (`_positions_after_sales`, once more after `SALE_READ_BACK_RECHECK_SECONDS` if they do not yet show the sales), the cash and Kalshi's value of what is left (`_value_after_sales`), and a dry run adds each sale's estimated proceeds to the cash and its shard (`_with_simulated_proceeds`); either way the markets sold stay out of every purchase (`sold_tickers`, joined to `blocked_tickers`), a sold position's ladders are free again, and a sale left unbalanced or of unknown outcome makes the run return `EXIT_TRADES_NEED_ATTENTION` (`_SALE_ATTENTION_STATUSES`); with `settings.add_to_held_pairs` on, a complete listing, every held market identified and, when anything is held, Kalshi's value of the open positions read and kept, it finds the exact held pairs it may add to (`scanner.held_pairs(held_positions, held_labels, markets_by_ticker)`, each pair valued at today's prices from the run's full market list), leaves out any with no room even at the per-trade cap (`config.held_pair_fraction(settings.size_cap, pair.stake_dollars, portfolio value) <= 0`, one INFO count), keeps their markets in (`blocked_tickers` is the held tickers less theirs) and hands them to both finders as `add_on_pairs` (anything else with the setting on: one WARNING naming why, and no add-on — DR-77); a production run that sends orders (`--mode prod` without `--dry-run`) takes the machine-wide live-run lock (`run_lock.acquire`) after logging is configured and before `build_client`, holds it until the run mode returns or raises (closed in a `finally`, so two in-process runs each take it), and exits `EXIT_RUN_IN_PROGRESS` (50) with a WARNING naming the holder, before building a client or making any request, when another run still holds it after `LIVE_RUN_LOCK_WAIT_SECONDS`; an error making or opening the lock file propagates (exit 1); dry runs and dev runs neither take the lock nor wait for it; `--result-file PATH` (prod only — in dev it exits 2 right after the order-path check, before logging is configured, a client is built or any request is made; otherwise any file already at PATH is removed at that point, so what is there when `main()` ends is this run's result or nothing) makes `main()` fill a `reporter.RunReport` as the run goes (a `RunReportHandler` on the root logger from just after logging is configured, and `_run_prod(..., report=)`, which records the cash before and after trading and the portfolio value it sized on (`portfolio_value_before`), that it began sending orders (`submission_started`, set just before the first sale order or `execute_trades` on a run that is not a dry run, so a run interrupted while sending says orders may have been placed although its `trades` are still empty), each sale's outcome through `reporter.report_sales` and each pair's through `reporter.report_trades` before the trade log is written, and the line it logged when it stopped or finished, word for word — every return expression unchanged) and write it with `reporter.write_run_report` in a `finally`, so however the run ends once logging is configured: the exit code, or `null` with a one-line `error` (`_http.api_error_summary`) when an exception stops the run and still propagates; the result is written first, then the handler removed, then the lock released. A usage error that exits 2 before logging is configured (a bad flag, no or refused saved live defaults, a non-v2 order path) and a kill signal leave no file, so a caller reads the exit code first. The parser is built by `_build_parser()`, which tests use to parse `config.live_settings_argv`'s flags | `main()`, `print_pairs_table()` (the CLI entry point and the pairs-table logger are the only public names; `_run_dev()`/`_run_prod()` are the module-internal dispatch targets) |
| `scheduler.py` | Weekly daemon — runs main.py as a subprocess at `config.SCHEDULED_RUN`'s weekday/time (Monday 09:00) on the host's clock (`--mode prod` and no live toggle flag, so a scheduled run trades exactly the saved live defaults and logs them with no departure mark; with none saved, or a refused file, the run exits 2 and spends its slot, and `_check_live_defaults` logs an ERROR saying so at daemon start, never raising), checked against its zone at startup (`_host_clock_realises_run`: CRITICAL, naming the first mismatch over the next 104 fires, when the host's clock does not place them at `SCHEDULED_RUN.instant(d)`, and a separate CRITICAL naming the schedule, not the host, for any run date whose wall time the zone skips at a clock change (`ScheduledRun.clock_change`); the daemon keeps firing either way; the job stays on the host's clock, never `schedule`'s `.at(…, tz)`, whose pytz table has no US daylight time after 2037), killed after `SCHEDULER_JOB_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`; claims its slot in `scheduler_state.json` (`config.SCHEDULER_STATE_FILENAME` under `PROJECT_ROOT`, read at call time so tests patch `scheduler.PROJECT_ROOT`) before spawning and finalizes it after, maps the subprocess's `EXIT_*` code to a distinct log level/message (`EXIT_TIME_SERIES_SKIPPED`, a production run that could not identify a held market and so made no time-series trade, is an ERROR that still satisfies the slot — DR-76; `EXIT_RUN_IN_PROGRESS`, a run stopped because another live trading run held the live-run lock, satisfies the slot and is never retried — a WARNING naming that run from `run_lock.holder()`, or an ERROR when it has held the lock for over `SCHEDULER_JOB_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` or its start is not recorded, since it may be hung and every scheduled run stops the same way until it ends), catches up a missed Monday slot on daemon startup (guarded so a corrupt optional state key degrades with a WARNING instead of killing the daemon before the weekly job is registered — DR-24), and retries a BLIND run (`EXIT_NO_TRADEABLE_SHARDS`) hourly up to `SCHEDULER_BLIND_MAX_RETRIES` times because that slot was never satisfied; registers EVERY job through `_guarded_job` so an escaping exception cannot freeze that job's `next_run` (DR-59 — see the registration-guard gotcha); logs to its own `kalshi_scheduler.log` (rotating), never the `kalshi_arb.log` its subprocess rotates | `main()`, `run_job(retries=0)` |
| `run_lock.py` | The machine-wide lock that lets one live trading run place orders at a time: an flock on `config.LIVE_RUN_LOCK_FILE` (`~/.kalshi_betting/live_run.lock`, read at call time), shared by every checkout and worktree since they all trade one account. `acquire()` makes the folder, takes an exclusive lock, retrying every `LIVE_RUN_LOCK_POLL_SECONDS` for `LIVE_RUN_LOCK_WAIT_SECONDS`, rewrites the file with the holder's pid, checkout (`PROJECT_ROOT`) and UTC start time, and returns the descriptor (closing it releases the lock), or `None` when the lock is still held; an error making or opening the file propagates, so it never reads as `EXIT_RUN_IN_PROGRESS`. `held()` takes a shared lock and drops it at once (True when held, or when the file cannot be opened; False with no file or a free lock), so a momentary check never makes a starting run give up. `holder()` reads the record (a `LockHolder` whose fields are `None` when missing or unreadable, with `describe()` and `age_seconds()`), for messages only — whether a run may trade is decided by the flock. The operating system drops the lock however the holder ends, so a crash cannot leave it stuck; only a run that is alive but hung can hold it for long. An flock belongs to one open file, so two opens in one process exclude each other (the tests rely on it). Imports `config` only; `main.py` takes the lock, `scheduler.py` reads the holder for its exit-50 message, and `defaults_server.py` checks it with `held()` before it offers a real-money run, naming the holder. Not covered: `v2_probe` and runs on another computer | `acquire()`, `held()`, `holder()`, `LockHolder` |
| `historical.py` | Fetches and disk-caches historical market data; streams the assembled settled-market corpus rather than holding it, and brings an assembled cache from an earlier UTC day up to today before serving it (`_up_to_date_corpus`, `_extend_assembled_cache` — see the cache-semantics gotcha) | `fetch_all_settled_markets()`, `SettledCorpus`, `CorpusProvenance`, `AssemblyCounts`, `SettledCorpusError`, `fetch_candlesticks()` (the archive's candles, or, for a market settled after the archive cutoff, Kalshi's live candlestick endpoint — `series=`, `live_first=`, a 404 from either asking the other — see the archive-cutoff gotcha; each candle carries `ts`, `yes_ask_close`, `no_ask_close` and `volume`, the contracts traded in that hour, `None` when the API gave no readable count, and a cached candle file is reused only while its `fields` tag equals `config.CANDLESTICK_CACHE_FIELDS_VERSION`; `backtester._fetch_candles_parallel` logs one INFO line, silent at zero, counting the tickers none of whose candles carry a volume, so a renamed API field shows), `build_historical_client()`, `build_prod_live_client()`, `infer_category()`, `load_series_categories()` (Kalshi's official category + tags per series, cached a week, for the dashboard's category/tag breakdowns and filter and for `main._filter_by_category`, the live category/tag filter; with no client it reads the cached copy only, as a dev run does), `series_ticker()` / `series_labels()` (the one filing rule — market A's LITERAL series, its Kalshi category and FIRST tag, the ticker-prefix label for a series the listing lacks — shared by the dashboard and the live filter), and the candle bid rule a sale reads: `usable_candle_ask()` (a candle close that can value or sell a leg: strictly between 0 and 1, a NO ask also below `config.CANDLE_NO_ASK_CEILING`), `candle_sale_bids()` ((YES bid, NO bid) of one candle, each 1 minus the other side's usable ask, to six decimals, `NaN` for a side with none) and `bid_before()` (one side's bid on the last candle that ended at or before a moment and less than a window before it, `NaN` otherwise; only live selling reads it) — the first two being what the backtest's `_usable_ask` and `_leg_quotes` and live selling both read — and `recent_candles()` (a market's hourly candles over a window for a live run: fetched through the shared `_fetch_candles_from_endpoints`, live endpoint first, and parsed as `fetch_candlesticks` parses them, but never read from or written to the candle cache; `None` when a request fails) |
| `treasury.py` | Downloads the 8-week Treasury bill's auction yields from the Treasury's Fiscal Data API — one open, no-key, read-only GET, retried like every other market-data read and cached under `backtest_cache/treasury_bill_rates.json` — for the backtest dashboard's Sharpe and Sortino ratios to subtract, on each day of a curve, the yield of the most recent auction on or before that day. Never raises: on any failure it falls back to the last saved download, and with none to `SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE` (the dashboard then subtracts 0% and its header says so). REPORTING ONLY — nothing sizes, prices or settles on it, and no live-trading module may import it. `dashboard.py` imports `RiskFreeRates` and every ratio on the page subtracts it (`generate_dashboard(risk_free=)`), a strategy curve's on its capital in open trades only, reading the curve's dates as `day_numbers` once for both the yield and the deployed capital; `backtest.py` calls it beside its series-category read and hands the result in | `RiskFreeRates` (`annual_on()`, `annual_on_days()`), `day_numbers()`, `load_risk_free_rates()`, `SOURCE_API`, `SOURCE_CACHE`, `SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE` |
| `depth_model.py` | The backtest's model of order-book depth. Backtest only: no live module imports it; `backtester.py` (`volume_24h`, `book`, `bid_ladder`, `can_start_at`, `DepthModel`) and `backtest.py` (`load_depth_model`) do. Kalshi keeps no historical order books, so it saves snapshots of live books, fits a table of how many contracts rest near the best bid, and builds a synthetic book from that table for any market and moment. `snapshot()` samples up to `config.DEPTH_SNAPSHOT_MARKETS` open non-combo markets, reads each one's book and its last 25 hours of candles, and saves them under `backtest_cache/depth_snapshots/<UTC stamp>.json.gz`; every request is a read-only GET and nothing here can place an order. `fit()` files each bid ladder, YES bids and NO bids alike, by the market's 24-hour volume and the ladder's best bid, and keeps the median contracts resting within each of `config.DEPTH_MODEL_DISTANCES` of the best bid; a cell with too few ladders reads its volume row, then the whole table. `volume_24h(candles, ts)` is the one definition of a market's trailing volume: `snapshot()` saves it with each book and the backtest reads it from each market's candles at every checkpoint and at each daily check before a sale. `book()` makes a synthetic book, in `scanner._fetch_orderbook`'s shape, whose top levels reproduce the quoted prices; the backtest sizes each trade over it. `bid_ladder()` builds one side of that book, and the ladder a backtest sale walks; `can_start_at()` says whether it can start at a bid (1c to 99c). `load_depth_model()` fits every saved snapshot (logging `Depth model loaded from saved snapshots: …`) and returns `None`, with a WARNING, when none is usable; `backtest.py` calls it before the fetch, and `run_backtest_sweep` logs the model the run used (`Depth model: …`). Run `python3 -m kalshi_betting.depth_model snapshot [--markets N]` to save one | `DepthModel`, `volume_24h()`, `fit()`, `bid_ladder()`, `can_start_at()`, `book()`, `load_depth_model()`, `snapshot()` |
| `backtester.py` | Replays strategy on settled market history, entering every trade at the live run's instant (`config.SCHEDULED_RUN` via `_checkpoint_datetime`; it refuses a schedule whose checkpoints are not one UTC instant on their own date — see the entry-checkpoint gotcha); split at two boundaries — the spread band (the backtest's own; live reads only the saved live defaults' band, through `config.LiveSettings`) and the interval discount `k` — so `run_backtest_sweep(band_sweep=True)` can sweep a band x `k` x population grid for the dashboard's scenario explorer over one fetch, and with `tier_off_sweep=True` as well re-run every band a deadline-gap tier floor binds at with the tier floors off (`BacktestSweep.tier_off_scenarios`, at the run's own size cap); the per-trade Kelly size cap is a simulation parameter too (`_simulate_at_discount(size_cap=)`, default `BUDGET_FRACTION`; a same-title candidate also stays under `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` at every cap, through `config.pair_size_cap`, as live sizing caps it), and `run_backtest_sweep(cap_sweep=True)` adds a lazy `CapSweep` over every other cap of the tier-on grid — and, with `tier_off_sweep` too, a second one (`BacktestSweep.tier_off_cap_sweep`, `CapSweep.tier_floors` False) over the tier-floors-off family, seeded from its own points — each simulated one (band, `k`) cell at a time when read; its Kelly gate forecasts a time-series Monday at the mid spread of that Monday's four candle quotes (`config.time_series_mid_spread`, as live sizing does — DR-78) and skips a Monday whose quote is crossed on either market (a YES ask below its own YES bid), counted on its own silent-at-zero line (`Time-series Mondays skipped because a market's YES ask sat below its own YES bid (a crossed book)`, DEBUG when quiet); it sizes each Monday's candidates on that checkpoint's opening portfolio value — its cash plus every open trade at market (each leg at the ask of the side it holds, `_open_value`; at cost for a trade with no quotes), the equity curve's valuation — and the cash left, both in whole cents (`_cents`), through the live sizing code (`_size_trade`: with a depth model and volume data for both markets that Monday, `scanner._enrich_pair` walks a synthetic order book from `LegQuotes.book_at` and `strategy.compute_trade` picks the count, under one `config.LiveSettings` per simulation; otherwise `compute_trade` sizes at the candle prices, the top of the book), never spending more than the cash left, so a trade the cash left cannot buy in full is shrunk to what it can buy and a time-series pair waits for a later Monday for want of money only when its budget buys no contract pair that still pays in a win after the exact fees (`BacktestTrade.balance_at_entry` is that value, and `fill_price_a`/`fill_price_b` and `book_walked` record what the trade paid and whether a book was walked; with `sell_at=` (off by default; only the dashboard's Sell family sets it) it sells a whole position — a pair with everything added to it — at the first weekly checkpoint where its realized profit (each market's contracts walked down its modeled bid ladder from the fresh bid, or sold at the fresh bid alone in any size with no depth model, no volume data, or a bid below 1c or above 99c, less the sale's taker fee, less its cost) reaches that share of its potential profit (its contract pairs at $1 less its cost) and did so at a daily check on each of the `config.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS` − 1 days before it (24 hours apart, each reading the last quote of the 24 hours before it and walking the ladder built from that day's volume), as `BacktestTrade.sold` trades exiting on the sale day — with `sell_min_days=N` as well, only while at least N days remain before the position's last market stops trading (`_far_enough` over `_days_left`), and every selling run records each sale as a `SaleCheck` (its days left and its profit at each check) on `SweepPoint.sales` — and `run_backtest_sweep(sell_sweep=True)` returns `BacktestSweep.sell_sweep`, a lazy `SellSweep` over every level of `config.TAKE_PROFIT_LEVELS`, every minimum of `config.TAKE_PROFIT_MIN_DAYS` and every scenario the dashboard's filter bar shows, nothing simulated during the run, whose `sold_grid` reads one cell at every level and minimum simulating only the runs that differ (`_sale_reach`, `_sale_cover`, `SameSale`; cap helpers `_resolve_size_cap()` and `_resolve_same_title_size_cap()` (both through `_validated_cap()`, then the 5% grid rule), `_cap_label()`, `_sim_options()`, `_sharing_floor()` (the cap above which a point's simulation repeats); the sizing helpers `_size_trade()` (the one sizer), `_candidate_pair()` (the live sizer's pair at that Monday's quotes; it sets a time-series pair's `mid_spread` from those four quotes through `config.time_series_mid_spread`, never from the market records' own last quotes, and a walked book's enrichment writes it again from the book's top, which reproduces them; a residual: the candle fetch clamps every NO ask into 0.01–0.99, so a YES bid never reads below 0.01 there, and where an earlier market's YES bid is missing (live enrichment reads 0) or under a cent, its mid spread can sit up to 0.005 below what live enrichment would read off the same book), `_books_for()` (the one builder of a synthetic book) and `_cents()`; a band the tier floors never bind at uses its tier-on block for both) that the script inflates when a level is first chosen there (the base block's `sell_blocks` names them), and the page and its build folder are published together under a lock (`_publish_page`), every earlier build's folder deleted (see the Sell select gotcha); the address's fixed part, the run's same-title cap, whether trades are filed by Kalshi's series listing and a source note matching `config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_SOURCE_PATTERN` are `_save_target`'s, shipped as the base block's `save`, and each band entry carries its bounds as `value`), and beside it a plain "Trade using defaults…" link (`_trade_link_html`, id `flt-trade`, `target="_blank" rel="noopener"`, its address `_trade_url()` from the same config constants, its words `_TRADE_LABEL`/`_TRADE_TITLE`, drawn with `_BUTTON_LINK_STYLE`) to the server's trade page, which needs no script and so stays — in `<p id="live-trade">`, `_trade_paragraph_html` — under `_FILTER_UNAVAILABLE_HTML` on a page whose bar could not be built;
