| `backtest.py` | CLI entry point for the backtest pipeline; turns the band sweep (with its tier-floors-off family), the lazy size-cap sweep, the lazy add-on sweep and the lazy sell family ON by default (`--no-band-sweep` / `--no-cap-sweep` / `--no-add-on-sweep` / `--no-sell-sweep` opt out), and hands `generate_dashboard` how many worker processes to build the Sell select in (`--sell-workers N`; by default one less than the CPU count, at most `config.DASHBOARD_SELL_MAX_WORKERS` — the workers are spawned and re-import this module, so `main()` runs only under its `__main__` guard); also loads the 8-week Treasury bill's auction yields (`treasury.load_risk_free_rates()`, beside its series-category read) and hands them to `generate_dashboard(risk_free=)`, so every Sharpe and Sortino on the page subtracts the per-day yield; it fits the depth model from the saved order-book snapshots before the fetch (`depth_model.load_depth_model()`, which never raises) and hands it to `run_backtest_sweep(depth_model=)`, its pre-fetch echo ending with a `fills=` clause (`walked book (S snapshots, L ladders)`, or `top of book (no usable depth snapshot)`); `--interval-discount` must be above 0 and at most 1 (exit 2 otherwise); its pre-fetch echo's `live rule=` clause names the saved live defaults and their origin from a read of its own (`none saved (live runs refuse to start)` with no file, `not recorded — ...` with a refused one); its starting balance (`StartingBalance`: the amount and where it came from) is `--balance` (a positive, finite number of dollars, else exit 2 before logging is configured), or else what the account is worth when the run starts (`_account_starting_balance` → `auth.read_account_balance` on the production live client, one retried read-only GET before the fetch: the cash on every shard plus Kalshi's value of the open positions — the portfolio value live runs size on — or the cash alone, with a WARNING, when that value is unreadable; the live run's check of that value against the contracts held is not made, since nothing is at stake and both parts are shown), logged on its `Starting balance: $X (<source>)` line before the config echo (a WARNING follows when it is below `config.MIN_BALANCE_CENTS`, the value below which a live run does not trade) and handed to `generate_dashboard(balance_source=)`, whose header prints it beside the amount; a read that fails, or comes to nothing (an account worth $0, or no cash beside a positions value that could not be read), raises `StartingBalanceError`, which stops the run before the fetch (an ERROR in the log, the reason and the `--balance` remedy on stderr, exit 1) rather than start from an amount nobody chose | `StartingBalance`, `StartingBalanceError` |
| `v2_probe.py` | User-run live verification CLI for the V2 order mapping and the transfer unit (~1c exposure) — its closing reduce-only bid is priced at the top of the market's own grid rather than at the rollback builder's $0.62 loss floor, so that floor can no longer cause a FAIL unrelated to the mapping (a book with nothing resting at or below the top of grid still can), and its verdict is judged on one re-read when the read after the close is not exactly 0; the exchange's HTTP 409 kill response to a fill-or-kill (`trader._is_fok_kill`, the live path's one definition) is read as a kill — PASS on the unfillable ask, NEUTRAL on the NO buy, when the account reads flat (a first read that is not exactly 0 is re-read once); a non-object 2xx order body, in EITHER submission-response reader (DR-58), and an object whose fill counts are unreadable in the NO-buy step (`_step_no_mapping`, DR-60), are both a FAIL that still re-reads the position and names what to flatten through the one shared `_recheck_and_report_position`, never a traceback out of the fill readers (`_step_unfillable_ask`'s own unreadable-fill-counts branch is a recorded residual that still FAILs with no post-submission read); `_step_no_mapping` classifies READABLE fill counts into THREE outcomes — complete fill, true kill, fill-or-kill invariant violation (partial / over-fill / stale remainder) — so a partial fill FAILs naming the counts and re-reading the account instead of reporting a clean kill with the account "still flat" (DR-20), which is how `_step_unfillable_ask` always read one, and both steps judge on BOTH counts, deliberately stricter than `trader._v2_fill_status`'s `fill_count`-only contract; `--dest-shard` equal to the source shard is refused with a NEUTRAL at the top of the transfer step, before any transfer I/O, so it can no longer POST a net-zero self-transfer and then report a false in-flight FAIL (DR-22); refuses to start on any `config.ORDER_API_VERSION` but `"v2"` (`config.order_api_version_error`, exit 2 before the banner, logging or any request), the FAIL lines of `--step no-mapping` and `--step yes-close` that doubt the V2 path after a submission on an account it verified flat (a buy that opened the wrong side, an impossible fill-or-kill reply, a close or sale that did not leave the ticker flat) print `v2_probe._REMEDY` (stop trading, and flatten any position on the probed ticker by hand in the Kalshi UI, since there is no other order path), while `main()`'s closing line depends on the outcome — after an order step's FAIL it says to stop trading and to act only on the position warnings printed above, never by itself to flatten (the ticker may carry the bot's own position); after a `--step transfer` FAIL, to stop trading and check each shard's balance; after a NEUTRAL, that nothing needs doing; its informational fee check (`_report_fee`, no verdict) prints the exchange's `average_fee_paid` — the volume-weighted average fee PER CONTRACT by Kalshi's API reference, never to be scaled by the probe's 0.01-contract count — beside the fee model per contract at the order's average fill price (else its limit price), three ways: before rounding (`TAKER_FEE_RATE·p·(1−p)`), `config.fee_leg_exact(1, p)` (one whole contract rounded up to the cent), and rounded as Kalshi rounds an order (`_order_rounded_fee_per_contract`: the order's total fee up to the account's balance precision, `v2_probe.FEE_BALANCE_PRECISIONS` — $0.0001, or $0.01 on some accounts — the excess rebated later). The exchange's figure includes that rounding, so on the probe's tiny order it matches the rounded model, not the unrounded one (the 2026-09-28 probe: $0.0200 per contract on 0.58 and 0.59 fills, $0.0171/$0.0169 before rounding); at a 0.01 count the $0.0001-rounded figure equals `fee_leg_exact(1, p)` at every cent price; `--step yes-close` (`_step_yes_close`) checks the one order shape live selling sends that no other step does, a `reduce_only` ask that sells a held YES: it buys 0.01 YES (`_yes_buy_body`: the real YES-leg builder with a bid at the top of the grid) and judges that buy as the NO buy is judged, then sells it with the body `trader._build_sale_order_v2` builds (`_yes_close_body`: an immediate-or-cancel ask at `scanner.v2_bottom_of_grid_price`), and PASSes only when the account then reads exactly flat (re-read once if the first read is not 0) AND the sale's reply reports 0.01 filled with nothing remaining (`_sale_reply_problem`: live selling takes a sale's count from the reply's `fill_count` when it can read one); the closing lines after a PASS, a NEUTRAL and an order step's FAIL (not a `--step transfer` FAIL's) now say `--step yes-close` has to PASS before live selling is turned on; never imported by the pipeline | `main()` |
| `defaults_server.py` | User-run local web server (`./start_dashboard.sh [--seed]` — the repo root's launcher, which clears `CDPATH`, runs from its own folder, checks that its Python (`KALSHI_PYTHON`, else `python3`) can import `kalshi_betting.main` from that folder (exit 1 otherwise: a link to the script, or `PYTHONSAFEPATH`, would import another checkout's copy), and execs `python -m kalshi_betting.defaults_server` with every argument — or `python3 -m kalshi_betting.defaults_server [--seed] [--no-browser]` directly, on `config.DEFAULTS_SERVER_HOST`:`DEFAULTS_SERVER_PORT`, `127.0.0.1:8765`) whose pages save the live defaults, `live_defaults.json`, and start live trading runs with them — how the first file is made (`--seed` proposes `config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_SEED`), how a backtest scenario becomes the live defaults, and how a person trades without a terminal (see the trading-from-the-defaults-server paragraph under Key Patterns). `GET /confirm?<fields>` shows the defaults in force beside the proposed ones (`config.live_settings_changes`; every changed row highlighted, the `live_rule_warnings` sentences in red, "N of 10 settings change") with three buttons in a fixed order, each rendered disabled: Dry run (the proposal, no orders, nothing saved), Confirm and save, and, after a gap and a red line saying it places real orders, Confirm and trade (saves, then runs); `GET /trade` shows the saved defaults with Dry run and Confirm and trade. A button that does not apply is a placeholder (`<button type="button" disabled>`, no id, name or value) with its reason beside it, so the real-money button is always last. A save answers 303 to `GET /saved` ("Saved and verified: …", each `live_rule_warnings` sentence as a red "Saved (valid), but note: …", and a link to `/trade`); a run answers 303 to `GET /runs/<id>`, which refreshes every `DEFAULTS_SERVER_RUN_REFRESH_SECONDS` while the run goes and then sums up its outcome from its `result.json` and exit code by the first matching rule of `_outcome`'s table (refused to start; trades or sales need attention; ended without a result, or stopped while sending orders; orders placed, then an error; trades completed; dry run finished; no trade completed; no trades to complete — named "Positions sold" when the run sold, and "Dry run finished" when a dry run would have; not traded (exit 10, 30 or 50); stopped with an error), with its sales (a table of each position's status, what sold on each market and its profit when it sold in full, and the cash after them, an estimate in a dry run) and its pairs grouped (needs attention first), its warnings, its balances, the portfolio value Kelly sizes on ("Portfolio value $X (cash $Y) — what Kelly sizes on", from the result's `portfolio_value_before`, worded to hold on a run the minimum stopped too, and left out when the result carries no finite number there) and the end of its `output.log`. `GET /` lists the newest `DEFAULTS_SERVER_INDEX_RUNS` runs and links the seed page and `/trade`; `GET /checkout` answers `{"project_root": …, "code": …}` as JSON — `code` is `_LOADED_CODE`, the SHA-256 over the package's `.py` files taken when the module was imported (`_code_fingerprint`). A POST's checks, in order: the Host header (every request; DNS rebinding); the Origin (a missing one or `null` is refused, which is why no response may send `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`); a form; exactly one fingerprint, nonce and token (64, 32 and 64 hex digits; else 403) and one action of the page's (`_CONFIRM_ACTIONS` / `_TRADE_ACTIONS`; else 400 — the action is unsigned, since one form carries several buttons, and every action's own checks run again), the trade page's form carrying nothing else; the token, an HMAC (key made per process) over the page's purpose (`confirm` or `trade`, so one page's token never starts the other page's action), the fingerprint of the defaults the page was built on, the nonce and the raw proposal fields, compared as bytes; the nonce unused (`_used_nonces`: a second POST of one page is sent, 303, to what the first produced, or refused with 409 when that failed — nothing is done twice); a usable saved file (409 — fix or delete it first); a valid proposal (`_proposal`: plain ASCII numbers, one printable name per category or tag, a tag only with its category, no source note or one matching `config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_SOURCE_PATTERN` with ASCII digits only, the seed's note only on the seed values, then `LiveSettings`' own rules — `same_title_size_cap`, `add_to_held_pairs` (`on`/`off`), `sell_at` (`off` or a share in (0, 1]) and `sell_min_days` (`off` or a whole number of days, at most six digits) are the four fields that fall back to the saved value — or the seed's with none saved (`_kept`) — so a link that names none of them leaves all four as saved, except that a link carrying the seed's note takes the seed's `add_to_held_pairs`, `sell_at` and `sell_min_days`, so it still proposes exactly the seed; a minimum of days left with no level is refused by `LiveSettings`, and the page says why; a missing category or tag means any); the same defaults still in force (else 409, the page shown again against them with a fresh nonce); then, before anything is saved, a run's blockers (`_run_blocker`: a server built without a process starter runs nothing; a run started from this checkout's server that is still going blocks both kinds of run — one it started, or one in `LIVE_RUNS_DIR` from before it restarted, with no result yet and its `output.log` still locked by its process (`_own_run`); `run_lock.held()` blocks a real-money run, naming the holder, and calls it possibly hung past `SCHEDULER_JOB_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`), a dry run's need for saved defaults, and, for Confirm and trade, the attention acknowledgement (`_attention`: of the finished real-money runs — each run folder under `LIVE_RUNS_DIR`, read as its run page reads it, and the scheduler's `scheduler_state.json` — the newest that says anything about the account decides; a run that stopped before it could send an order (exit 2, 10, 30 or 50, or an error before its first order) and a dry run neither raise nor clear it; one that exited `EXIT_TRADES_NEED_ATTENTION` (a pair or a sale left for a person) or ended without a clean result (no result, a signal, an error after it began sending orders, a scheduled run with no exit code — stopped at the time limit) makes both trade pages show a red banner and a box to tick, and a trade without it is 409; a clean exit (0 or 40) clears it; and anything unexpected while reading fails closed, with the banner). Only then is the nonce marked used and, for Confirm and save or a Confirm and trade that changes something, `config.save_live_defaults` called — its one call site, in `_App._post_confirm`. A run is started only by `_App._start_run(self, *, settings, dry_run, saved_note)`, the module's one process start, through the `start_process` the `_App` was built with — `subprocess.Popen` from `main()` alone; the default `None` refuses every run, so no test can start `main.py`. It makes `LIVE_RUNS_DIR/<UTC start>-<16 hex id>/`, writes `run.json` before anything starts, locks `output.log` with an flock the run's process inherits through its output (so `_run_alive` tells a run is still going, across a server restart too), and starts `[sys.executable, "-m", "kalshi_betting.main", "--mode", "prod", *config.live_settings_argv(settings), "--result-file", <folder>/result.json]` (plus `--dry-run`) from `config.PROJECT_ROOT` in a new session (`start_new_session=True`: Ctrl-C on the server, or closing its terminal, never reaches a run placing orders); a start that fails is 500, saying whether the defaults were saved; when the process itself could not be started, its `run.json` records why (a folder or first `run.json` that cannot be made leaves nothing behind). The server never stops a run, and at shutdown logs a WARNING for each one it started that is still going. On start (`main()`) it opens one page (`_open_start_page`, through `_start_page`): with `--seed` the seed values' confirmation page; otherwise the backtest dashboard when `_dashboard_state` finds the Trade link's id (`_DASHBOARD_MARKER`, `id="flt-trade"`) in its first `DASHBOARD_MARKER_SCAN_BYTES`, else its own index — with an INFO line when there is no dashboard, a WARNING naming the rebuild and `/trade` for a page built before the buttons, and a WARNING with the reason for one it cannot read (the index says the same). When its port is taken (`EADDRINUSE`) it binds nothing and starts nothing — no second server, no `_App`, no run: `_reuse_running_server` asks the listener `GET /checkout` once (`_running_checkout`: no proxy, no redirect followed, `DEFAULTS_SERVER_CHECKOUT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, at most `DEFAULTS_SERVER_CHECKOUT_MAX_BYTES` read, never raises), and when the answer names this checkout's resolved `PROJECT_ROOT` and this checkout's code now (`_code_fingerprint()`) it logs (to the terminal only; the running server keeps its log file) "This checkout's defaults server is already running at …/" and opens the page this start asked for, then returns (exit 0); an answer naming another checkout exits 2 naming it (its non-printable characters escaped), one naming this checkout with other code, or none (a server left running across a pull or an edit, whose pages would check and start runs with its older code), exits 2 telling the user to stop it and start it again, and anything else (no answer within the wait, an error or redirect status, not that JSON — one nested too deeply to parse included) exits 2 with the port-in-use error. Every response carries a Content-Security-Policy that allows only the one page script by its hash, forbids framing and lets a form post only to the server; that script arms the page's real buttons (the ids of `_ARMED_BUTTONS` present) only after the page has been visible for `DEFAULTS_SERVER_CONFIRM_ARM_MS` and the mouse moves or a key is pressed (the DoubleClickjacking defence), and disables them for good once the form is sent (`tests/test_defaults_server.py::TestConfirmScript` runs it under node or `jsc`). Every response — a refusal the HTTP layer makes before the application sees the request (501 for another method, 400/414/431/505 for a request it cannot read) included, through `_Handler.send_error`, and all sent as HTTP/1.0, so each has a status line and headers — carries the same six headers (`/checkout`'s type is JSON). It answers one request at a time (`HTTPServer`, so two saves or two starts never interleave), drops a silent connection after `DEFAULTS_SERVER_SOCKET_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, refuses a request over `DEFAULTS_SERVER_MAX_REQUEST_BYTES`, never serves the dashboard (any script on a page from its own address could press its buttons), and logs to `kalshi_defaults_server.log` (rotating). These checks tell its own pages from other web pages; they do not authenticate local programs. It reads the saved file only through `_current_defaults` and writes it only in `_App._post_confirm` (the AST pin's `_DEFAULTS_READERS`/`_DEFAULTS_WRITERS`, which name a method with its class), and names the file's path only to print it (`_SAVED_FILE_PRINT_ONLY`), reading `config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE`, `config.PROJECT_ROOT`, `config.LIVE_RUNS_DIR` and `config.LIVE_RUN_LOCK_FILE` through the module at call time; imports the standard library, `config` and `run_lock` only, imported by NOTHING (pinned by `tests/test_defaults_server.py::TestIsolation`, which also pins that its one process start is `_App._start_run`'s, that `subprocess.Popen` appears only as `main()`'s `start_process=`, and that it imports only the standard library modules it needs (no `pty`, `multiprocessing`, `asyncio`, `concurrent`, `importlib` or `ctypes`), imports `os` and `subprocess` whole and never renamed, and never names `Popen` bare, so every process start is spelled where the checks read it) | `main()` |

**Dependency order (no circular imports):**
```
config.py, _http.py
  └─ run_lock.py (config only) ─→ main.py, scheduler.py, defaults_server.py
  └─ auth.py, scanner.py
       └─ strategy.py
            └─ reporter.py
                 └─ trader.py (imports reporter.TradeResult)
                      └─ main.py, scheduler.py

historical.py → auth.py (build_client, wrapped by build_historical_client/build_prod_live_client)
main.py → historical.py (load_series_categories, series_labels, infer_category — the live category/tag filter files a pair by the backtest dashboard's own rule)
dashboard.py → historical.py (series_labels, the one filing rule the live filter shares)
historical.py → scanner.py (event_series, the ONE definition of an event's series identity, so the event-title fallback's combo test — DR-51 — can never disagree with the one-series rule about what a combo is)
historical.py → backtester.py → dashboard.py → backtest.py
auth.py → backtest.py (read_account_balance: the backtest's starting balance when --balance is not given); _http.py → backtest.py (api_error_summary: the one-line reason a failed balance read stops the run with)
historical.py → treasury.py (CACHE_DIR, _exception_summary, _load_json_cache, _save_json_cache — the same cache idioms load_series_categories uses); treasury.py also imports config.py and _http.py, like every other leaf-adjacent module. treasury.py → dashboard.py (RiskFreeRates and SOURCE_CACHE: generate_dashboard(risk_free=...) and its header line; day_numbers, the one day numbering the yield lookup and the deployed-capital lookup share); treasury.py → backtest.py (load_risk_free_rates(), called beside the series-category read and handed to generate_dashboard(risk_free=))
backtester.py → scanner.py (time_series_group_key, the ONE definition of the time-series grouping key — title + outcome label — shared with the live scanner; event_series, the ONE definition of an event's series identity, so the one-series rule can never disagree; leg_sides, so _settlement_receipt pays by the side each leg bought; deadline_profile/cumulative_deadline_pair, the ONE definition of a two-cumulative-deadline pair; deadline_pair_refusal and its REFUSED_SNAPSHOT/REFUSED_NO_STATED_DEADLINE/REFUSED_SAME_DEADLINE constants, so _extract_pairs's DR-72 refusal-reason skip counts mirror the live finder's; stated_deadline/same_event_ladder and its SAME_DAY sentinel, the ONE definition of which calendar day a rung's deadline names and of how two rungs of one event are ordered and gapped, so the DR-73 ladder rule can never disagree between the paths; closes_apart, the ONE definition of the same-title close gate, so the DR-74 rule can never disagree between the paths about which cross-series pair closes at one moment, and close_gap_bound_text, the bound its refusal line prints, read from the same scanner binding so the two paths' lines stay verbatim twins; ladder_keys, the ONE definition of which ladders a market is on, so the DR-76 one-open-trade-per-ladder rule uses the same labels on both paths — its inputs can still differ: a cached market without an event title has a wider question label than it would live, see the DR-76 backtest residuals)

backtester.py → strategy.py and scanner.py (compute_trade, and scanner._enrich_pair with CandidatePair, HeldPair, _market_from_dict and leg_prices: the live sizing code every trade is sized with, over a synthetic book; and scanner._cash_binds, enrichment's test of whether the cash sets a budget, which the backtest counts its cash-bound skips by; backtest-only, since no live module imports backtester); backtester.py → depth_model.py (DepthModel, volume_24h, book, bid_ladder, can_start_at); backtest.py → depth_model.py (load_depth_model, called before the fetch and handed to run_backtest_sweep(depth_model=))

depth_model.py — backtest only; imports config.py, _http.py (api_error_summary), scanner.py (fetch_open_events_with_markets, _fetch_orderbook, event_series, fetch_shard_statuses, inactive_shard_indexes) and historical.py (CACHE_DIR, fetch_candlesticks, series_ticker, build_prod_live_client), never trader, strategy, reporter or main; imported by backtester.py and backtest.py only, never by a live module (tests/test_depth_model.py::TestIsolation, tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_no_live_module_imports_the_depth_model); its snapshot command is human-run and sends read-only GETs
seller.py — imports config.py, scanner.py (the order book, the bid walk, settlements, held positions) and historical.py (bid_before, recent_candles) only: never backtester, depth_model, trader, strategy, reporter or main; imported by main.py alone (plan_sales, before any purchase). trader.py and reporter.py read its SalePlan by its fields without importing it (tests/test_seller.py::TestIsolation)
v2_probe.py — standalone, human-run; imports _http/auth/config/scanner/trader, imported by NOTHING
defaults_server.py — standalone, human-run; imports config and run_lock only (never main, trader or any pipeline module: it starts main.py as its own process), imported by NOTHING
```

---

## Run Commands

All commands must be run from the **repo root**:

```bash
# One-time setup (Python >= 3.11)
pip install -e ".[dev]"

# Sandbox simulation with virtual $1k balance
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode dev --sandbox-balance 1000

# Sandbox simulation, dry-run (no orders submitted)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode dev --dry-run

# Production live trading, from the saved live defaults (live_defaults.json in the
# repo root: tier floors, spread band, k, per-trade and same-title caps, category/tag
# filter, adding to held pairs, the sell level and its minimum of days — each overridable for one run by the flags below, which the run marks
# "(default: X)" on its "Live settings:" line and, when it submits orders under one,
# names in a WARNING). With no saved file, or a refused one, every live run (prod and
# dev, scheduled or by hand) exits 2 before anything is logged or requested; it never
# falls back to config.py's toggles — read the saved-live-defaults paragraph under Key
# Patterns first, and the 2026-09-27 decision record with its addendum (V0's
# live-contest replay of the rule config.py ships said STOP on drawdown; the operator
# shipped it anyway). Pairs and sizes same-event ladders since the 2026-09-26
# decision — read config.TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS too
# Only one production run that sends orders (no --dry-run) trades at a time on this
# machine: while another holds ~/.kalshi_betting/live_run.lock (run_lock.py), this one
# exits 50 before contacting Kalshi
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod

# Production discovery only, no orders
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod --dry-run

# Either production run can also write what it did to a JSON file when it ends
# (--mode prod only; exit 2 in dev): the exit code, the line it stopped or finished
# on, the balances, whether it began sending orders, each pair's outcome, its
# WARNING-or-worse lines and any error. Any file already at the path is removed
# first; the result is written however the run ends once logging is set up (for a
# run that sends orders, before the live-run lock is released). A usage error
# (exit 2, before logging) or a kill signal leaves no file, so read the exit code
# first. The file holds balances and trades, so this example writes it outside the
# checkout (run_result.json and its staging file are gitignored as well)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod --dry-run --result-file /tmp/run_result.json

# Production dry run replaying the live rule as it stood before the 2026-09-27
# decision, for this run only. It gives all ten toggles, because a toggle no flag
# gives keeps its saved value (--no-add-to-held-pairs and --no-sell too, so a saved "on"
# or a saved sell level does not leak into a replay of a rule that never added to held
# pairs or sold); each flag that
# departs from the saved live defaults is marked "(default: X)" on the "Live
# settings:" line
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod --dry-run --tier-floors --spread-min 0 --spread-max 1 --interval-discount 0.75 --size-cap 20 --same-title-size-cap 100 --no-add-to-held-pairs --no-sell --any-category --any-tag

# Production dry run that may add to the pairs the account already holds (exactly the
# same two markets, the same side on each), for this run only: the log names the
# portfolio value it sizes on (its "Sizing on portfolio value" line must show the
# open positions, with no WARNING refusing them, or the run adds to nothing), each
# held pair it may add to with its cost, the fees in it and its worth at today's prices,
# and marks each add-on "adds to N held". --no-add-to-held-pairs never trades a held
# market. A saved file without the setting reads it as off. Run this before adding goes
# on for every run: the first-run order (the deploy steps in the saved-live-defaults
# paragraph under Key Patterns) saves the seed with adding off, runs this, then turns
# adding on. The dashboard's save button sends the choice only from a page that shows
# it, and otherwise keeps what is saved
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod --dry-run --add-to-held-pairs

# Production dry run that sells (no orders, nothing saved): judge every held position by the
# take-profit rule at 80% of its potential profit, and only while at least 7 days remain
# before its last market stops trading (--sell-min-days is optional). The log has one
# `Take-profit check (sell at 80%): ...` line per position (`-> sell`, or `-> keep` with the
# share it reached at each check read, up to the first one below the level), a `[DRY RUN] Would sell ...` line per sale,
# `Positions to sell this run: N of M` and, when something would sell, `Dry run: sizing as
# if the sales filled — cash $X (+$Y)`: the buys are then sized on the cash those sales
# would free, and the markets sold stay out of them. Run `v2_probe --step yes-close` (below)
# and see it PASS before saving a sell level: with one saved, every live run, the Monday one
# included, sells before it buys. --no-sell sells nothing this run whatever is saved (and
# clears the saved minimum of days too). First-run order for selling: the live-selling
# paragraph under Key Patterns
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod --dry-run --sell-at 80 --sell-min-days 7
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod --dry-run --no-sell

# Trade only one Kalshi category this run (as the backtest dashboard's Category select
# names it; repeatable, and --tag NAME filters on the series' first tag under every category —
# the dashboard's Tag option "C · T" is --category C --tag T; --any-category / --any-tag
# clear a filter the saved live defaults set)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod --dry-run --category Economics

# Live trading (either mode), only consider markets closing within 14 days from now
python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode dev --max-horizon-days 14

# Backtest from 2024-01-01, starting from what the account is worth now: its cash on
# every shard plus Kalshi's value of its open positions (the cash alone, with a
# WARNING, when that value is unreadable), read once from the production account
# before the fetch. A read that fails or comes to nothing stops the run (exit 1).
# It also fits the depth model from the saved order-book snapshots (the snapshot command
# below): each trade is then sized over a modeled order book, and the config echo ends
# `fills=walked book (...)`; with none usable every trade fills at the top of the book,
# in any size (`fills=top of book (no usable depth snapshot)`, and a red line on the page)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --start-date 2024-01-01

# The same from a $10k starting balance of your choosing (no balance read)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --start-date 2024-01-01 --balance 10000

# Backtest forcing fresh API fetch (ignores disk cache)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --no-cache

# Backtest, only enter trades whose later-closing leg is within 14 days of the simulated entry date
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --max-horizon-days 14

# Backtest at a specific time-series interval discount k, above 0 and at most 1 (this backtest run only; live
# sizing reads the saved live defaults' k, or main.py's own --interval-discount for one live run)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --interval-discount 0.60

# Backtest without the k-grid re-simulation (dashboard's k selector offers one point; the empirical-k calibration report still runs;
# with the band sweep on — the default — each band is simulated at the primary k only, so the scenario explorer's heatmap has a single k column)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --no-sweep

# Backtest with same-event deadline ladders forced on/off for THIS run only
# (default: config.TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS — ON by the 2026-09-26 decision, so
#  --no-same-event-ladders is the one that departs from a default run; never
#  reaches the live finder, which binds that constant at import)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --same-event-ladders
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --no-same-event-ladders

# Backtest at a specific PRIMARY-scenario time-series spread band on pB - pA
# (backtest only; the live band is the saved live defaults', or main.py's own
#  --spread-min/--spread-max for one live run, read only through config.LiveSettings — no module outside config/backtester/backtest/dashboard calls
#  config.min_price_diff_for_gap at all)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --spread-min 0.30 --spread-max 0.60

# Backtest without the band x k x population scenario sweep AND without its
# tier-floors-off re-runs of every band a deadline-gap tier binds at (both are
# ON by default in the CLI; the re-runs have no flag of their own, so this skips
# both); the primary scenario still runs, but the dashboard's Scenario Explorer
# section has no grid to show, and its filter bar offers the primary band only,
# with the Tier floors select disabled
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --no-band-sweep

# Backtest without the per-trade size-cap sweep (the cap sweep is ON by default in
# the CLI but simulates nothing during the run: BacktestSweep.cap_sweep simulates each
# cap other than config.BUDGET_FRACTION lazily, one (band, k) cell at a time, as the
# dashboard's one grid walk reads it for the filter bar, the Interval Discount section
# and the scenario explorer — skipping it gives a far smaller, faster page whose
# Size cap selects offer the run's own cap only — with the tier floors off too, since
# the tier-floors-off runs then have no size-cap sweep of their own either; backtest
# only — live sizing reads its caps from the saved live defaults, or main.py's --size-cap /
# --same-title-size-cap for one live run, through the run's LiveSettings)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --no-cap-sweep

# Backtest without the dashboard's "Add to held pairs" family (ON by default in the CLI, and
# lazy like the cap sweep: it simulates nothing during the run — BacktestSweep.add_on_cap_sweep,
# and add_on_tier_off_cap_sweep over the tier-floors-off family, simulate every band x k x cap
# cell with add_to_held when the dashboard reads it, the "all" population only — so skipping it
# makes that dashboard step faster and leaves the filter bar's Add to held pairs select
# disabled; backtest only — a live run adds to held pairs when its saved live defaults say so,
# or per main.py's --add-to-held-pairs / --no-add-to-held-pairs for one run)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --no-add-on-sweep

# Backtest without the dashboard's "Sell" family (ON by default in the CLI, and lazy like the
# add-on family: the run simulates nothing — the dashboard step then reads every sell level and
# minimum of days to maturity of every scenario the filter bar shows, in worker processes,
# simulating only the settings that differ, and writes the new trade lists as sidecar chunk files
# in backtest_dashboard_files/<build>/ beside the page, which must be kept with it). Skipping it
# makes that step much faster and leaves the filter bar's Sell and Min. days to maturity selects
# disabled; backtest only — a live run sells only at its own saved sell_at level (--sell-at for one run), not at the dashboard's grid
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --no-sell-sweep

# Backtest building the dashboard's Sell select in 4 worker processes (default: one less than
# this machine's CPU count, at most config.DASHBOARD_SELL_MAX_WORKERS = 8; 1 runs it in the main
# process). Each worker holds one spread band's entries, so memory grows with the count
python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --sell-workers 4

# Save live order books for the backtest's depth model: read-only, no orders, about 2,000 open markets, saved under backtest_cache/depth_snapshots/ (more snapshots near Monday 09:00 PT make its table better; a backtest fits every saved snapshot before its fetch)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.depth_model snapshot [--markets N]

# Weekly scheduler daemon (runs prod every Monday 09:00 on the host's clock —
# config.SCHEDULED_RUN, from the saved live defaults; logs CRITICAL at startup unless the
# host keeps America/Los_Angeles, and an ERROR when no live defaults are saved or the file
# is refused, since every run would exit 2)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.scheduler

# Save the live defaults, and trade from a browser: local pages on 127.0.0.1:8765. The
# launcher runs from its own folder (so from any directory), checks that its Python
# (KALSHI_PYTHON, else python3) can import the live bot from that folder (it refuses a
# link to the script, or PYTHONSAFEPATH, which would import another checkout's copy),
# and execs the defaults server with every argument. --seed proposes the seed values; without it the server opens the
# backtest dashboard, whose filter bar's "Save as live defaults…" button proposes the
# scenario on screen and whose "Trade using defaults…" link opens /trade (the saved
# defaults), or its own index when there is no dashboard or it was built before those
# buttons; --no-browser only logs the address. Run it in the scheduler's checkout before
# the first live run, in the first-run order of the saved-live-defaults paragraph under
# Key Patterns (--seed proposes adding to held pairs on): with no saved file every live
# run exits 2. Confirm and trade (on /confirm it saves first; on /trade it trades the saved
# defaults) and Dry run (no orders, nothing saved) start `python -m kalshi_betting.main
# --mode prod` with all ten toggles as flags, in its own session; each run's folder is
# under live_runs/, and its page is /runs/<id>. Run it again while this checkout's server
# runs and it only opens the page again (exit 0, nothing started); a port held by that
# server running code from before a change to the checkout (stop it and start it again),
# by another checkout's server, or by anything else, exits 2. Ctrl-C stops the server
./start_dashboard.sh [--seed] [--no-browser]
python3 -m kalshi_betting.defaults_server [--seed] [--no-browser]   # the same, without the launcher's import check

# Live verification of the V2 order mapping + transfer unit (~1c of real money, prod account)
python3 -m kalshi_betting.v2_probe --ticker <TICKER> [--step no-mapping|unfillable-ask|yes-close|transfer] [--dest-shard N] [--yes]

# Live verification of the reduce-only ask that sells a held YES (about 1c of real money; you run
# it, on a liquid, cheap market you hold nothing on): buys 0.01 YES, sells it back with the order
# live selling sends, and PASSes only when the account reads flat and the sale's reply reports the
# 0.01 sold. Run it, and see it PASS, before saving a sell level
python3 -m kalshi_betting.v2_probe --ticker <TICKER> --step yes-close

# Run tests
python3 -m pytest tests/ -v

# Lint check
python3 -m ruff check kalshi_betting/
```

## CI and Packaging

- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs two jobs, **Lint (ruff)** and **Test (pytest)**, on every push to `main` and on every pull request regardless of base branch (the `pull_request:` trigger carries no branch filter). Both must pass before merging — run them locally first.
- `pip install -e ".[dev]"` installs the packages CI needs. Backtests additionally benefit from the optional `perf` extra (`pip install -e ".[dev,perf]"`), which adds `orjson` to speed up settled-market JSON parsing — never required, and the on-disk cache format is identical with or without it.
- **Package discovery is explicit:** `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.setuptools.packages.find]` includes only `kalshi_betting*`. Without it, setuptools' flat-layout auto-discovery treats every top-level directory named like a Python identifier as a package, and a checkout that has run a backtest also has `backtest_cache/` at the root. So `pip install -e ".[dev]"` refused to build ("Multiple top-level packages discovered in a flat-layout") on exactly the machines that run backtests, while CI's fresh checkout never saw it. Pinned by `tests/test_packaging.py`; don't remove the table or widen its pattern.
- `pyproject.toml` declares `requires-python = ">=3.11"` and pins `kalshi-python-sync==3.2.0`. Do NOT bump the SDK pin to 3.13.0 — that version's own metadata requires Python >= 3.13 and breaks `pip install -e ".[dev]"` on 3.11 (i.e. breaks CI).
- Tests live in `tests/` (`test_auth.py`, `test_backtest.py`, `test_backtester.py`, `test_config.py`, `test_dashboard.py`, `test_depth_model.py` (offline, against a fake exchange; its `TestIsolation` pins that the module imports no order-path or report module, that no live module imports it and that it sends no order), `test_historical.py`, `test_http.py`, `test_main.py`, `test_packaging.py`, `test_reporter.py`, `test_scanner.py`, `test_scheduler.py`, `test_seller.py` (offline; `TestParityWithTheBacktest` builds one position as a backtest trade and as live positions and compares the realized and potential profit at every check, the cost, the proceeds now and the verdict at several levels; `TestIsolation` pins that `seller.py` imports only the standard library, `config`, `scanner` and `historical` and that `main` alone imports it), `test_strategy.py`, `test_trader.py`, `test_treasury.py`, `test_v2_probe.py`, `test_run_lock.py` (whose one cross-process test starts a plain Python child that holds the lock on a path it is handed and is killed), `test_launcher.py` (runs `start_dashboard.sh` under bash — skipping without one — with `KALSHI_PYTHON`, or once the PATH, pointing at a stand-in `sh` script that records how it was called and starts nothing: the script parses, git records it `100755`, it execs the server from its own folder with every argument, a folder path with a space included and whatever `CDPATH` says, and a missing Python, one that cannot import the bot, or one that imports it from another folder (a link to the script, `PYTHONSAFEPATH`) exits 1 with the message; one test runs the script's own import check under the real Python from the repo root, which imports the live bot and starts nothing), `test_defaults_server.py` (whose socket tests bind a loopback port and skip where binding is refused, CI binding — one of them a dry-run round trip whose stand-in starts a tiny Python child through the real `subprocess.Popen` in place of `main.py`, every other run being started through a stand-in that starts nothing; and whose `TestConfirmScript` runs the page script under node or `jsc` with a stand-in page, skipping when neither is present), plus the non-test helper `dashboard_golden.py` and its `fixtures/` directory, and `js/filter_harness.js` — the browser stand-in `test_dashboard.py`'s script tests (`TestFilterScript`, `TestFilterKAndCap`, `TestKhatCards`, `TestIntervalDiscountFollowsTheBar`, `TestScenarioExplorerScript`, `TestExplorerCapAndMetrics`, `TestTierOffAtEveryCap`, `TestSaveLiveDefaultsButton`, `TestAddOnScript`, `TestSellScript`) run the page's filter script under — and, when a test asks for it (`_run_script(..., explorer=True)`) and the page carries it, the scenario explorer's script before it, in one program, as the page orders them — with node in CI and macOS's `jsc` locally, skipping when neither is present; its strict mode (`strict=True`, or #68's name for the same switch, `strict_ids=True`) answers `getElementById` with null for an id the page lacks, as a browser does, a deferred block's inflate waits for a `resolve`/`reject` step, so a test can choose the order in which chunks arrive, `Plotly.update`, `restyle` and `relayout` apply their changes to the chart's data and layout as Plotly does (honouring the traces argument) and are recorded apart from the redraws while `Plotly.Plots.resize` does nothing, a created element's `innerHTML` setter throws (both scripts write `textContent`, which it takes), a snapshot records each element's colour, each table cell's font weight and each chart's layout the script set, a `call` step calls a page script's window function as another script would (the filter's `window.dashScenarioSelect`, with any labels), a `click` step calls an element's click listeners unless it is disabled, as a browser ignores a click on a disabled button, each button the page renders is created with its rendered disabled state (`_page_elements` returns them, parsed from `<button id=…>`), `window.open` is a stub that records each call as `{url, target, features}` and opens nothing, each snapshot carries `buttons` (id → disabled) and `opened` (the `window.open` calls since the previous snapshot), a `<script>` element the page appends to `document.head` loads a sidecar chunk file as a browser would — a tick later, with `document.currentScript` set while the file's one call to `window.__dashChunk` runs, then its `onload`; a src the test does not supply fires `onerror`, one marked empty runs `onload` alone, and one marked deferred waits for a `resolve_file`/`reject_file` step (`_run_script(..., files_root=, files_missing=, files_empty=, files_deferred=)`, the snapshot's `files` listing every src loaded) — and `wait` also waits for the explorer's selects when its script runs, but not for the Add to held pairs select, which a page without that view keeps disabled — `TestAddOnScript` adds `flt-add` to the wait list itself, through `setup_js`) and run fully offline against `MagicMock` clients. `tests/conftest.py` holds seven autouse fixtures: one (`_isolate_event_title_accumulator`) points the event-title accumulator (`historical._EVENT_TITLES_CACHE` and its legacy `_LEGACY_EVENT_TITLES_CACHE`) at each test's `tmp_path`, so no test can read, rewrite, migrate or delete a real `backtest_cache/` accumulator (DR-51) — and, since the live category/tag filter, Kalshi's cached /series listing too (`historical._SERIES_CATEGORIES_CACHE`), so a test that sets a filter reads the listing it seeds itself, never the operator's `series_categories.json`, and can never overwrite it; another (`_isolate_treasury_rates`) points `treasury._RATES_CACHE` at the same `tmp_path` and stubs `treasury._get_json` to raise a plain (non-transient) `RuntimeError`, so no test can reach the real Treasury Fiscal Data API or `backtest_cache/treasury_bill_rates.json`, and a test that does reach `treasury.load_risk_free_rates()` falls straight through to "no saved copy" instead of sleeping through `_http.api_call_with_retry`'s ~62s of backoff; the third (`_fresh_order_write_pacer`) gives every test a fresh, full `trader._ORDER_WRITE_PACER` built from `config.ORDER_WRITES_PER_SECOND` / `ORDER_WRITE_BURST`, so no test waits on writes an earlier test made (every test that submits orders takes fewer places than a burst — a pair takes two, its NO leg's and the one held for its YES leg — so none waits on it; a test that needs pacing to bite installs a pacer of its own); the fourth (`_fresh_v2_mapping_disproof_state`) clears `trader._V2_NO_MAPPING_DISPROVEN`, `_V2_UNCHECKED_NO_LEGS` and `_V2_UNCHECKED_BASELINES` before each test, since a test anywhere in the suite that disproves the V2 NO-leg mapping would otherwise make every later test's trades come back `failed` with nothing sent (`tests/test_trader.py`'s own autouse fixture clears its twin, `_V2_NO_MAPPING_CONFIRMED`); and the fifth and sixth point `config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE` away from the repo's `live_defaults.json` — `_isolate_live_defaults_for_the_session` (session-scoped, on a `pytest.MonkeyPatch()` of its own) at an empty `tmp_path_factory.mktemp("live-defaults")` directory, because class-scoped fixtures set up before any function-scoped one and would otherwise see the real file (pinned at the top of `tests/test_backtester.py`'s `golden_band_sweep`, which asserts during class setup that the path is not the checkout's own and sits in a directory directly under the session's base temporary directory), and `_isolate_live_defaults` at each test's own `tmp_path`, so a test that saves defaults never leaks them into the next and a test that saves none reads "none saved"; and the seventh (`_isolate_live_runs`) points `config.LIVE_RUN_LOCK_FILE` at `tmp_path / "lock" / "live_run.lock"` and shortens `LIVE_RUN_LOCK_WAIT_SECONDS` to 0.2 s and `LIVE_RUN_LOCK_POLL_SECONDS` to 0.01 s, so every test that runs `main.main()` in production without `--dry-run` takes a lock of its own, never the real one in the home folder, and a test that holds the lock (a second `run_lock.acquire()`, which an flock refuses even within one process) sees the refusal quickly (`run_lock` reads the path and waits at call time; no module binds them by value), and points `config.LIVE_RUNS_DIR`, where the defaults server keeps each run it starts, at `tmp_path / "live_runs"`, so no test writes a run folder into the checkout (`defaults_server` reads it at call time too). Nothing binds `LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE` by value: both patch `config`'s global, which every reader reads at call time, and a class-scoped fixture that needs saved defaults points the file at a directory of its own, never the session one — a redirect in force during class setup only, since each test body sees its own `tmp_path` (the per-test redirect), so a test that reads the saved defaults itself saves them there (the `saved_live_defaults` fixture). `save_config_live_defaults()` saves `config.live_settings()` (config.py's toggles as they read at that moment, so after any patch) as the live defaults, with an empty source note, and the `saved_live_defaults` fixture runs it for one test, after the per-test redirect (it requests `_isolate_live_defaults` by name). Since a live run starts only from the saved file, every test that runs `main.main()` saves one first (`saved_live_defaults`, or `pinned_config_toggles`, below), and every backtest report test that checks the live-rule line saves `config.live_settings()` first; a test with nothing saved sees the refusal (exit 2) or the "none recorded" report. It also defines `apply_pre_toggle_defaults(mp)` and the `pre_toggle_defaults` fixture that applies it: every binding of the live toggles put back to its value before the 2026-09-27 flip (tier floors on, no band, `k` 0.75, a 20% cap for every pair, no extra same-title cap, no filter, no adding to held pairs and no selling (the writer leaves those keys out while they are off)) — `config`'s ten toggle constants, which `live_settings()`, `max_affordable_pairs` and `time_series_profit_prob` read at call time, AND the by-value copies `backtester` (`BUDGET_FRACTION`, `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP`, `TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT`) and `backtest` (`TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT`) bind at import. A test whose figures were derived under the old values requests the fixture (`@pytest.mark.usefixtures("pre_toggle_defaults")`) rather than re-deriving them; `tests/test_main.py`'s `pinned_config_toggles` is that helper under its own name, which then also saves the pinned toggles as the test's live defaults (`save_config_live_defaults()`, after the per-test redirect), so a run through `main.main()` starts from them and `reference == live_settings()` still holds (`origin` is not compared); and each class-scoped sweep fixture in `tests/test_backtester.py` (`golden_band_sweep`, `cap_sweep_run`, `st_cap_sweep_run` — which lays the shipped caps back over it — `golden_tier_off_sweep`, `tier_off_cap_run`) applies it on a MonkeyPatch of its own, created at fixture start and undone only after its yield, because those fixtures undo their other patches mid-fixture while the lazy cap cells their tests read resolve the cap and `k` at call time; their tests index cells by the literal 0.20 or the run's recorded cap or `k`, never by a read of `backtester.BUDGET_FRACTION`. A test module never binds a toggle by value: it reads `config.X` (or the backtester's own binding) at run time, or states the literal. `tests/test_config.py::TestShippedLiveToggles` pins the shipped values themselves, unpatched, end to end (the finder, the price-sum ceiling and the fee cut, both pair types' stakes, `live_rule_warnings`, `validate_pair_price`'s re-check of the edge after the fee, and a default backtest's departure from the live rule), and `::TestPreToggleDefaults` pins the helper itself: after `apply_pre_toggle_defaults`, every module in `kalshi_betting/` that binds a toggle by value (found by an AST walk, so a new binder is caught) reads `config`'s value, and no test module binds one.
- `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestGoldenSections` pins the seven dashboard sections that predate the scenario explorer to SHA-256 digests captured on `main` @ `fe0a758` (`tests/fixtures/dashboard_golden_main.json`, rendered and normalised by `tests/dashboard_golden.py`) — several since re-recorded for intended changes, each named with its previous digest in the fixture's `amended` notes (the most recent: the two interval-discount renders, for the page-wide filter bar's `k` and size cap reaching that section — its `updatemenus` dropdown and one-trace-per-`k` figure replaced by ONE trace under the fixed div id `kd-equity`, two cards relabelled and keyed (`kpi-kd_k`, `kpi-kd_delta`) and the per-`k` table's rows in `<tbody id="kd-rows">`, every value unchanged — hand-amended digest by digest, with `source_sha`, `source_tree` and the other seven digests left as they were, and checked by diffing the normalised renders). The two interval-discount digests moved again after that (the live-trading-toggles PR), amended by hand the same way: their "Recommendation only" captions now read "live sizing reads config.TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT unless main.py --interval-discount overrides it for one run" (was "live sizing always reads config.TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT"); every other value is unchanged, and the other seven digests, `source_sha` and `source_tree` are again untouched. The 2026-09-27 flip of the live toggles moved none of the nine digests (`TestGoldenSections` ran, not skipped, and passed at the shipped values), so none was re-captured for it. The two interval-discount digests moved once more for the saved live defaults, amended by hand the same way: their "Recommendation only" captions now read "the backtester never writes config.py or the saved live defaults, and live sizing reads the saved live defaults' k (main.py --interval-discount overrides it for one run)"; every other value, the other seven digests, `source_sha` and `source_tree` are unchanged. The calibration digest moved for the mid-spread forecast (DR-78), amended by hand the same way: its caption now reads "the entry mid spread (B's midpoint minus A's; a midpoint is halfway between a market's YES ask and its YES bid)" where it read "the entry spread pB − pA", and its x-axis title "Predicted probability: mid spread" where it read "Predicted probability: spread pB − pA"; the fixture's books have no width, so its mid spread is its YES-ask gap and every value is unchanged, and that amendment left the other eight digests, `source_sha` and `source_tree` untouched. The two interval-discount digests moved for it too, in a second hand amendment the same way: one grey caption under the calibration table now reads "k̂ = realised in-between rate ÷ mean implied gap, where a pair's implied gap is its mid spread (B's midpoint minus A's)."; the "Mean implied gap" header and every value are unchanged (each render with that paragraph removed is the previous render byte for byte), and that amendment left the other seven digests (calibration's included), `source_sha` and `source_tree` untouched. A deliberate change to any of those seven sections fails it until the golden is re-captured, by the recipe in `tests/dashboard_golden.py`'s module docstring — which also describes the by-hand amendment, since the whole-fixture recipe drops every `amended` note. When a digest diverges in a plotly/pandas/numpy environment other than the captured one (plotly 6.9.0, pandas 3.0.3, numpy 2.4.6), the test SKIPS rather than fails. `pyproject.toml` pins none of the three exactly, so a CI skip there is not a pass: read the skip reason.

---

## Credential Setup

Two files must exist in the **repo root** (not committed — in `.gitignore`):

**`secrets.json`** — API key IDs:
```json
{
  "Kalshi-api-key": "your-prod-key-id-here",
  "dev_api_key": "your-sandbox-key-id-here"
}
```
The `dev_api_key` field is optional; if absent, `auth.py` falls back to `Kalshi-api-key` for sandbox mode.

**`kalshi_private_key.pem`** — RSA private key for production signing.
**`kalshi_demo_private_key.pem`** (optional) — RSA private key for sandbox. Falls back to the prod key if absent.

`PROJECT_ROOT` in `config.py` is derived from `__file__` and always resolves to the repo root, so credentials must live there.

---

## Key Patterns — Always Follow These

**Saved live defaults (live_defaults.json) — no file, no live run.** Every live run, prod and dev, scheduled or by hand, starts from `config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE` (`live_defaults.json` in the repo root, gitignored operator state like `scheduler_state.json`), and from nothing else. **Deploy consequence: after this change merges, the next live run — the Monday 09:00 scheduled one included, and a hand run with toggle flags — exits 2 until a file exists in the checkout the scheduler runs from.** After pulling it there, follow this first-run order in that checkout. It works with nothing saved, and it does not start with `./start_dashboard.sh --seed`, because the seed turns adding to held pairs ON. (1) Save the seed values with adding to held pairs off: start the defaults server (`./start_dashboard.sh`), open `http://127.0.0.1:8765/confirm?tier_floors=off&spread_min=0.0&spread_max=0.5&k=0.8&size_cap=0.1&same_title_size_cap=0.2&add_to_held_pairs=off`, review the table, click Confirm and save, and check that `/saved` names that checkout's `live_defaults.json`. The link carries no `source` note because `defaults_server._proposal` refuses the seed's note on any values but the seed's own; the seed caps each trade at 10%; a `main.py` flag still overrides a toggle for one run. From then on every live run, the Monday one included, trades these values and adds to nothing. A checkout whose defaults were saved before the toggle existed starts at (2), since that file reads adding as off. (2) Run `python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod --dry-run --add-to-held-pairs` (no orders, nothing saved) and, in `kalshi_arb.log`, check that the `Sizing on portfolio value $X = cash $Y + open positions $Z` line shows the open positions as a dollar figure (not `not read`) and that no `Kalshi's value of the open positions (…) is not used` WARNING follows it — either means the run adds to no held pair, and says so (`Not adding to held pairs this run: …`) — then check each `Held pair to add to: …` line's (and each `Held market to add to (its partner has paid out): …` line's, for its one market) `cost $C (fees $F)` against that pair's fills in the Kalshi UI (count × price + fees for the cost, the fees alone for `$F`, both legs together) and its `worth $W at today's prices` against the asks the Kalshi UI shows (count × the ask of the side held, both legs together); if the value is missing or refused, or a cost or its fees are wrong, stop here and leave adding off. (3) Only then turn adding on for every run: `./start_dashboard.sh --seed` and Confirm and save (after (1) the page shows 1 of 10 settings change), or the (1) link with `add_to_held_pairs=on` — either also sets every other toggle to the seed's — and check that the next run's `Live settings:` line ends `add to held pairs on`. A shorter order, if adding may be on until the check: save the seed at (1), then run (2) without the flag before the next scheduled run or any Confirm and trade, and if a cost is wrong, save again through the (1) link. (4) Rebuild the dashboard with the `--start-date` it was built from (for the 365-day window, `python3 -m kalshi_betting.backtest --start-date 2025-09-24`, about 23 minutes and 2.1 GB when that window's corpus is already cached — measured before the add-on family, whose cells the dashboard step also simulates, and not re-measured — one heavy process at a time; without `--start-date` it runs from 2024-01-01, a far longer fetch) — until then the old page has no Save or Trade buttons, the server WARNs so and opens its index, `http://127.0.0.1:8765/trade` trades the saved defaults, and a dashboard scenario can be saved only after the rebuild (a page built before the Add to held pairs select has none, and its Save button sends no `add_to_held_pairs`, so the server keeps the saved value, or the seed's "on" on a first save). (5) If the scheduler daemon runs, restart it after the pull so it has the exit-50 message, and do (1) before starting it, since on start it can run a missed slot at once. Later saves go through the same server: the backtest dashboard's "Save as live defaults…" button. **Precedence:** the file, then `main.py`'s toggle flags for one run; there is no fallback to config.py's toggle constants. `main._resolve_live_settings` reads the file once per run through `config.live_defaults()` — its only live caller — and that read travels as the run's `reference`, with where it came from on `LiveSettings.origin` (`live_defaults.json, saved <UTC time>[ from <source note>]`), which is `compare=False`, so `settings != reference` still reads only the ten toggles and no run-mode signature changed (a counting spy on the reader and on config's parse of the file's bytes pins the one read: `tests/test_main.py::TestSavedLiveDefaults`). **A missing file raises `LiveDefaultsMissing`, a refused one `LiveDefaultsError`** (both `ValueError`s; refused means anything at the path that is not a usable file — see the config.py Module Map row), and `_resolve_live_settings` turns either into `parser.error`: exit 2 before logging is configured, any client is built or any request is made, the stderr naming the file and, for a missing file, the two ways to save one, or, for a refused file, the reason and the remedy: fix the file, or delete it before saving new defaults — `defaults_server` answers 409 and saves nothing while the file is refused (`tests/test_main.py::TestLiveDefaultsRequired`). A scheduled run that exits 2 is `Job failed (exit 2)` in the scheduler's log and spends its slot (only 30 reopens one), so `scheduler._check_live_defaults` logs an ERROR as soon as the daemon starts whenever no usable file is saved — naming, for a missing file, the checkout (`PROJECT_ROOT`) the defaults must be saved in, since `python3 -m` run from another checkout's root (a worktree) saves that checkout's file, and run from any directory that is no checkout's root saves the pip-installed checkout's (the defaults server's first log line names the file it saves to) — and never raising, since the weekly job must still be registered. **The mark rule:** `describe_live_settings` marks each field a flag moved `(default: X)` against saved defaults and `(config: X)` against a reference built from config.py's constants (origin `config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_FROM_CONFIG`, which only a test or direct call hands a run mode); every live run logs `Live defaults: <origin>` before its `Live settings:` line, a production run that submits orders under a flag WARNs that it overrides the saved live defaults, and the trade log's separator note ends `| defaults: <origin>`. **config.py's ten toggle constants are no live run's defaults.** They remain the backtest's `k` and caps (`backtester` and `backtest` bind them by value) and what `config.live_settings()` returns: the fallback a whitelisted live entry point reads when a caller hands it no settings — tests and direct library calls only, since the AST pin makes every live call path hand the run's settings. Do not "fix" that fallback into `live_defaults()`: hundreds of tests call the finders and the sizer without settings under monkeypatched constants. **So a backtest sizes at config.py's `k` and caps, not the saved defaults'**: its report names the saved defaults (`BacktestSweep`'s eleven `live_*` fields, from one fail-soft pre-fetch read, `backtester._live_settings_for_report`), labelled `(saved live defaults)`, and says so whenever the saved `k` or caps differ from the run's primary (`backtester._live_sizing_note`, and on the page where the filter bar shows that sizing, `dashboard._live_sizing_bar_clause`); with no usable file it reads "none recorded — no usable live defaults were saved when this run started, and live runs refuse to start without them" (an INFO line with none saved, one WARNING with a refused file) and the run goes ahead. `backtest.py`'s pre-fetch echo is a separate read of its own (`live rule=<rule> (<origin>)`, `none saved (live runs refuse to start)`, or `not recorded — the saved live defaults are refused (...)`). **The seed** (`config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_SEED`): tier floors off, spread band 0–0.5, `k` 0.80, a 10% per-trade cap, a 20% same-title cap (which never binds under the 10% cap), any category or tag, adding to held pairs on, selling off — one new pair stakes at most 10% and `live_rule_warnings` is empty (an add-on never stakes more than a new pair, and never takes its whole position — the held pair at today's prices plus the fees paid for it, and the add-on — above 10% of the portfolio value); nothing trades on it until it is saved. **A saved file that leaves `add_to_held_pairs` out reads it as off** (`config._OPTIONAL_TOGGLES`: a saved file may leave that key out, and it then takes its `LiveSettings` default, `False`), though config.py and the seed ship it on: nobody confirmed a value for that file, so a file saved before the toggle existed starts every live run exactly as it did. A missing older toggle, an extra key or a non-bool (`1`, `"true"`, `null`) is still refused. The writer leaves the key out while it is off, so such a file is byte for byte the seven-toggle file and stays readable by code from before the toggle (a revert, or a defaults server still running old code); a file saved with it on carries `"add_to_held_pairs": true` last and is refused by that older code (exit 2 until it is saved again there). So after this merges, save the defaults once more to turn it on, after the dry-run check ((2) and (3) of the first-run order above): the seed page proposes it on (with every other seed value), a confirmation page whose address says `add_to_held_pairs=on` proposes it with the rest of that page's values, and the dashboard's save button sends the choice only from a page that shows it, otherwise keeping what is saved (pinned by `tests/test_config.py::TestSavedLiveDefaults::test_a_seven_toggle_file_reads_with_add_on_off`, `::test_the_written_text_is_pinned`, `::test_an_on_save_writes_the_key_last` and `::TestLiveSettings::test_every_optional_toggle_is_a_field_with_a_default_at_the_end`). **The sell level and its minimum of days are optional keys too (2026-10-08).** A saved file that leaves `sell_at` or `sell_min_days` out, or holds `null` there, reads it as off (never selling, no minimum), and the writer leaves each out while it is off, so a file with adding and selling all off is still the seven-toggle file; one that sells carries `"sell_at": 0.85` (and `"sell_min_days": 7`) after `add_to_held_pairs`, and code from before them refuses it as a file with an extra key (exit 2 until it is saved again there). The reader refuses a JSON `true`/`false` for either key and names the field; `LiveSettings` refuses a level off the 1% grid (`SELL_AT_STEP`) or outside (0, 1], a minimum that is not a whole number of days from 1 (a bool, `1.0` or a numpy integer included) and a minimum with no level. Nothing sells until a level is saved: the seed's and config.py's are off, and a file saved before the setting existed sells nothing, exactly as it traded. The confirmation page's address carries `sell_at` (`off` or a share such as `0.85`) and `sell_min_days` (`off` or a number of days); a link that leaves either out keeps the saved value (the seed's, with none saved). The first-run order for turning selling on is in the live-selling paragraph below. **The AST pin** (`tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_live_path_reads_toggles_only_through_live_settings`) allows no direct `live_settings()` call anywhere on the live path (`_DIRECT_RESOLVERS` is empty), allows each saved-defaults reader only where `_DEFAULTS_READERS` names it (`live_defaults` in `main._resolve_live_settings`, `read_saved_live_defaults` in `scheduler._check_live_defaults` and `defaults_server._current_defaults`, each called exactly once, every allowed function found), allows `save_live_defaults` only in `defaults_server._App._post_confirm` (`_DEFAULTS_WRITERS`: the confirmation page's Confirm and save, and its Confirm and trade, which saves before it starts a run), and forbids in every walked module the saved file's path, config's private parse of it and the seed (`_SAVED_FILE_INTERNALS`: `LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE`, `_settings_from_bytes`, `_saved_settings`, `LIVE_DEFAULTS_SEED`), so no second read can slip past the readers table — but for `_SAVED_FILE_EXEMPT`, which lets `defaults_server` alone read `LIVE_DEFAULTS_SEED` (the values `--seed` proposes; `_SAVED_FILE_IMPORTABLE`, so it may be imported by value) and print `LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE` (the path its pages and log show; `_SAVED_FILE_PRINT_ONLY`: only as `config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE` in a logging message call (`logging.info` and its siblings, never a handler), or its `.name` or `.absolute()` passed straight to `str()`, `html.escape()` or an f-string field, with every call around it in its statement one that prints or builds text — `str()`, `html.escape()`, a logging message call or one of the server's own functions — so `config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE.read_bytes()`, `Path(str(config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE.absolute())).write_text(...)` and `logging.FileHandler(config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_FILE)` fail, each checked by appending it to the server's source (`::test_ast_the_print_only_path_is_never_used_to_reach_the_file`); the check stops at the statement, so text of the path bound to a name or handed to one of the server's own functions is not followed, and the rule that the server writes the file only through `save_live_defaults` rests on review beyond that), never assign, delete or take either as a parameter, never spell either as a string, and fails when an exempted name goes unused; its sibling `::test_ast_the_reports_never_write_the_saved_live_defaults` pins that `backtester`, `backtest` and `dashboard` name none of those nor the writer (`save_live_defaults` and its helpers), so a report can never change what live runs trade. **Tests:** conftest's two redirects (`_isolate_live_defaults_for_the_session`, an empty session directory in force during class setup, and `_isolate_live_defaults`, each test's own `tmp_path`) keep every test off the real file; a test that runs `main.main()` saves one first (`saved_live_defaults`, which runs `save_config_live_defaults()`, or `tests/test_main.py`'s `pinned_config_toggles`, which pins the toggles and saves them); a class fixture that needs saved defaults points the file at a directory of its own, never the session one.

**Trading from the defaults server.** The defaults server's pages run the live bot from a browser: on `/confirm`, Confirm and trade saves the page's settings (exactly as Confirm and save does) and then starts one production run with them, and Dry run starts the same run with `--dry-run` and saves nothing; `/trade` does the same with the saved defaults. Each run is its own `python -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod` process, started by `defaults_server._App._start_run` with all ten toggles as explicit flags (`config.live_settings_argv`), so it trades exactly what its page showed whatever the saved file says by then, with `--result-file` in its own folder under `config.LIVE_RUNS_DIR` (`live_runs/`, gitignored), from `config.PROJECT_ROOT`, in a new session — so it starts with fresh process-level latches (the V2 mapping latches) as a scheduled run does, takes the machine-wide live-run lock like any real-money run, and nothing done in the server's terminal reaches it. Because every press starts a process whose V2 mapping latches are clear, the stop-trading remedy (`trader`'s two V2 NO-leg mapping-disproof CRITICALs, and `v2_probe._STOP_TRADING`, which the probe prints after a FAIL) says, beside stopping the scheduler daemon and not running `main.py --mode prod`, to stop the server with Ctrl-C in the terminal running `./start_dashboard.sh` or `python3 -m kalshi_betting.defaults_server` and not to press Confirm and trade. The dashboard reaches these pages through its filter bar ("Save as live defaults…" → `/confirm`, "Trade using defaults…" → `/trade`, each in a new tab), with the server started by `./start_dashboard.sh`; a second start in the same checkout reuses the running server when it runs the checkout's current code (it opens the page and exits, starting nothing), and a start on a port held by that server running older code, or by another checkout's server, is refused (see the `defaults_server.py` Module Map row). Five rules are load-bearing. (1) **The server never places an order itself and never imports the pipeline:** it imports `config` and `run_lock` only (besides the standard library), and it starts a process only through the `start_process` it was built with — `subprocess.Popen`, which `main()` alone passes; the default `None` refuses every run (the pages say why), so no test and no other `_App` can start `main.py`. (2) **Every check before a save or a start runs first:** the used-nonce check right after the token check (a second POST of one page — a double click, a resubmit — is sent to what the first produced, and nothing is done twice), then the stale-page check, then the run's blockers (a run started from this checkout's server that is still going, found after a restart of the server too; `run_lock.held()`, a momentary shared lock that `acquire()`'s `LIVE_RUN_LOCK_WAIT_SECONDS` retry rides out, for a real-money run only) and, for a trade, the attention acknowledgement — all before `save_live_defaults`, so a refused trade saves nothing. (3) **A failed save means no trade:** Confirm and trade starts the run only after `save_live_defaults` has written the file, read it back and matched it. (4) **Every run's outcome is readable:** its page reads `result.json` strictly (a damaged file reads "result could not be read", never a crash) beside the exit code the server collected, checks exit 2 with no result (a usage error: "refused to start", nothing sent) before "ended without writing a result", tells a real-money run that ended without a result, or stopped while sending orders (`submission_started` true, no trades, an error), to check positions before trading again, and has a rule of its own for every `config.EXIT_*` code (`tests/test_defaults_server.py::TestRunPage::test_every_exit_code_has_a_rule_of_its_own`). (5) **The real-money button is always last and never moves:** a button that does not apply is a disabled placeholder with its reason, and the page script arms only the ids of `_ARMED_BUTTONS` present and stops re-arming once the form is sent. When the newest finished real-money run that says anything about the account — one the server started or the scheduler's; a run that stopped before it could send an order (exit 2, 10, 30 or 50) or a dry run is passed over — exited `EXIT_TRADES_NEED_ATTENTION` (a pair or a sale left for a person) or ended without a clean result (no result, a signal, an error after it began sending orders, a scheduled run stopped at the time limit), both trade pages show a red banner (it says to read that run's result — a scheduled run, which has no run page, is named with `kalshi_arb.log`, where its result is — and, if the result reports a V2 order-mapping disproof, to stop trading — stop the scheduler daemon, do not run `main.py --mode prod` and do not press Confirm and trade — and then undo by hand in the Kalshi UI what its CRITICAL names: close out a market the account did not hold before the run, and put one it did hold back to what it held (`defaults_server._ATTENTION_TAIL`)) and a real-money run needs a ticked box; only a newer clean run (exit 0 or 40) clears it, and a failure to read the records fails closed, with the banner. The server never stops a run (killing one mid-trade can orphan a leg), runs have no time limit, and a page calls a run possibly hung past `SCHEDULER_JOB_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`. **Residuals, recorded rather than fixed:** the server does not authenticate local programs (anything that can connect to 127.0.0.1:8765 can start a run with valid settings, as it could already save them for the next scheduled run — stop the server when done); a crafted link can open `/confirm` with attacker-chosen valid settings, and one armed click on Confirm and trade then saves and trades them (every change is highlighted, the source note is restricted, and the real-money button is last); nothing keeps the Mac awake during a run (the run page says to); a run stopped by a kill signal writes no result (its page says so, and for real money says to check positions); the attention banner reads only this checkout's `live_runs/` and scheduler state, so a hand-run terminal run or `v2_probe` leaves nothing it can read, and a scheduled run whose daemon stopped mid-run records no finish time, so the banner passes over it; category and tag names are checked for form only (a name that no series in Kalshi's /series listing carries and no pair of the run was filed under matches nothing, and the run's own typo WARNING, shown on its run page, says so); and pair choice is unchanged — a run started here is an ordinary production run, so same-title pairs can still stack across runs, and one open time-series pair per ladder still holds. Pinned by `tests/test_defaults_server.py` (`TestRenderStates`, `TestTokenAndNonce`, `TestActions`, `TestBlockers`, `TestAttention`, `TestRunPage`, `TestIsolation`, and `TestOverASocket`'s dry-run round trip, whose stand-in starts a tiny Python child through the real `subprocess.Popen` in place of `main.py`).

**Constants live in `config.py` exclusively.** Never hardcode `0.07` (fee rate), `0.20` (the extra per-trade Kelly cap on same-title pairs — `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP`, config.py's value (the backtest's same-title cap; a live run reads the saved live defaults' `LiveSettings.same_title_size_cap`), `0.20` since the operator decision of 2026-09-27 and `1.0` before it; also the value, not the same constant, of `LIVE_EXPOSURE_WARN_FRACTION`, the largest per-pair stake — `config.max_kelly_fraction` of either pair type — that `config.live_rule_warnings` accepts without a WARNING on a live run, which bounds no trade, and, until that decision, of `BUDGET_FRACTION`, the per-trade Kelly cap for every pair, config.py's value (the backtest's per-trade cap; a live run reads the saved live defaults' `LiveSettings.size_cap`), `1.0` — no cap — since), `0.15`/`0.30` (tiered time-series price thresholds — always go through `min_price_diff_for_gap()`, which the live path reaches only through `config.live_time_series_floor()`; 0.15 is also the value, not the same constant, of the event-title fallback's per-lookup pause `EVENT_TITLE_FALLBACK_RATE_LIMIT_SLEEP_SECONDS`, DR-51), `0.80` (the time-series in-between discount `k` — `TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT`, `0.75` until the operator decision of 2026-09-27, config.py's value (the backtest's `k`;
