a live run prices at the saved live defaults' `LiveSettings.interval_discount`, which `main.py --interval-discount` overrides for one run); always go through `time_series_profit_prob()`, which every live call hands that `k`), `(0.0, 1.0)` (the BACKTEST's default time-series spread band on `pB − pA`, i.e. no band — `BACKTEST_DEFAULT_SPREAD_BAND`; read only through `time_series_spread_band()`, whose floor `backtester._find_entry` passes to `min_price_diff_for_gap(gap_days, spread_min=…)` (so it also sets that pass's leg-price-sum ceiling, `1 −` the raised floor) and whose ceiling it tests per Monday through `time_series_spread_too_wide()` — time-series pairs only, a Monday above the ceiling is skipped rather than ending the scan, and the default band, with the tier floors on, reproduces the tier rule alone — the live rule until the 2026-09-27 decision, not since (the backtest's `Live time-series rule (saved live defaults)` log line and the dashboard's `Live rule (saved live defaults)` header line name the cell of its grid that holds the saved live defaults' rule); beside `spread_min` the helper takes a keyword-only `tier_floors` switch that `_find_entry` always hands on, and only an explicit `False` — passed in the package only by the band sweep's tier-floors-off family, by `backtester._tier_floors_bind`, which asks the helper both ways, and by `config.live_time_series_floor` for a run whose `LiveSettings.tier_floors` is `False` — drops the deadline-gap tier and returns the band floor alone (`0.0` with no floor), so that pass's sum ceiling is `1 −` the floor; the live path reads no BACKTEST band value: it reaches `min_price_diff_for_gap` only through `config.live_time_series_floor` (called by `config.time_series_spread_refusal`, `scanner._pair_max_sum` and enrichment's below-floor WARNING), with the run's own `LiveSettings`, and no module outside `config`, `backtester`, `backtest` and `dashboard` may reference a band or tier-floor helper or constant (`min_price_diff_for_gap` included), import one of those three modules, or read `live_settings` anywhere but a whitelisted entry point's one resolving statement — a deny-by-default walk of the package pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_live_path_reads_toggles_only_through_live_settings`), `False`/`(0.0, 0.5)` (the LIVE time-series toggles as shipped since the operator decision of 2026-09-27 — `True`/`(0.0, 1.0)` before it — `TIME_SERIES_TIER_FLOORS`, whether the deadline-gap tier floors apply, and `TIME_SERIES_SPREAD_BAND`, the band on `pB − pA`; read only by `config.live_settings()`, at call time — the no-settings fallback, never a live run's defaults, which are the saved live defaults' `LiveSettings` (one per run, over which `main.py`'s toggle flags may lay a value for one run) — see the saved-live-defaults and directional-filter paragraphs), `(0.0, 0.20, 0.25, 0.30, 0.35, 0.40)`/`(0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 1.00)` (the band grid for the backtest's band × k scenario sweep — `SPREAD_BAND_SWEEP_FLOORS`/`SPREAD_BAND_SWEEP_CEILINGS`; read only by `backtester._sweep_from_candidates`, reached through `run_backtest_sweep(band_sweep=True)` — the CLI's default — which crosses all 36 bands with `INTERVAL_DISCOUNT_SWEEP`'s 13 `k` values into 468 `population="all"` scenarios, plus a standalone time-series (ladders + cross-event, same-title excluded), ladder and cross-event simulation per non-empty cell, a split-half out-of-sample check per "all" and per time-series cell and an excluding-top-event concentration check per "all" and per time-series cell that traded at least one event, all over the ONE fetch `_prepare_candidates` already ran, and with ONE full `_find_entry` pass — at the no-band band, every other band rescanning only the pairs that entered there; measured 2026-09-23 on the DR-73 calibration corpus (10,733 time-series pairs, 10,530 with candles on both legs, start 2020-01-01, ladders on) at 26.4–26.6 s for the whole sweep (4,590 simulations, 3.2–3.3 s of it the entry passes; 52.2 s, 37.7 s of it the entry passes, before the pre-pass and the time-series population) and ~258 MB for the 1,872 equity frames it keeps; with `tier_off_sweep=True` — which the CLI turns on together with the band sweep — the 18 bands whose floor sits below a deadline-gap tier (floors 0, 0.20, 0.25) are entered again — a second full pass at the no-band band with the tiers off, then 17 rescans on the shipped grid — and simulated again with the tier floors off, measured 2026-09-26 on the same corpus at about 16 s more (42.8–44.4 s against 26.9–28.2 s without it, over four runs each; the entry passes 6.4–6.5 s against 3.2–3.3 s, 55 against 37), 6,882 simulations against 4,590 (+2,292: 936 points, 936 halves and 420 ex-top re-simulations) and 2,808 kept equity frames against 1,872 (+936, i.e. +129.2 MB of retained equity frames, 387.5 MB against 258.3 MB); peak RSS is not quoted, since it varied more between repeat runs of one setting than between the two; every tier-on cell and `k` point (trade and win counts, final balance, mean return per trade, both halves, the ex-top check) and every band's calibration (the pooled row's n, realised rate, mean implied spread and `k̂`; each bucket's label, tier, n and `k̂`) came out identical to both the run without it and the code before it — see the constant's own `config.py` comment for the per-band/per-simulation breakdown), `("no", "yes")`/`("yes", "no")` (the per-pair-type leg sides — `SAME_TITLE_LEG_SIDES`/`TIME_SERIES_LEG_SIDES`; read only through `scanner.leg_sides()`), `0.05` (same-title price threshold — `SAME_TITLE_MIN_PRICE_DIFF`; also the value, not the same constant, of `SIZE_CAP_STEP`, the 5% grid every size cap must sit on, which `LiveSettings` validates `BUDGET_FRACTION`, `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` and `main.py`'s `--size-cap`/`--same-title-size-cap` percentages against), `1e-6` (the float-noise tolerance on every price comparison — `PRICE_EPSILON`; see the TS-09 note), `0.0001`/`0.9999` (the extreme tradeable ORDER-BOOK LEVEL prices on the finest grid — `MIN_ACTIVE_PRICE_DOLLARS`/`MAX_ACTIVE_PRICE_DOLLARS`, read by `scanner._bids_to_ask_levels`, and the first also by `scanner.held_pairs` (a held exposure below it times the count is no cost an add-on can be sized on); NOT to be conflated with `scanner._MIN_ACTIVE_PRICE`/`_MAX_ACTIVE_PRICE` (0.01/0.99), which bound MARKET eligibility and deliberately did not move — see the sub-cent-levels gotcha), `5000` (min balance cents — `MIN_BALANCE_CENTS`; also the value, not the same constant, of the scanner cursor-loop page ceiling `SCANNER_MAX_PAGES`, of `EVENT_TITLE_FALLBACK_MAX_LOOKUPS`, and of `CANDLESTICK_MAX_CANDLES_PER_REQUEST` — the most candles one `/historical/markets/{ticker}/candlesticks` request serves, read only by `historical.fetch_candlesticks`, which pages any longer window), `12` (max accepted per-contract rollback loss in cents — `ROLLBACK_MAX_LOSS_CENTS_PER_CONTRACT`), `8` (trader thread-pool size — `TRADER_MAX_WORKERS`, used by both `pre_execution_check` and `execute_trades`; also the value, not the same constant, of `SETTLED_FETCH_MAX_WORKERS`, `CANDLESTICK_FETCH_MAX_WORKERS`, `EVENT_TITLE_FALLBACK_MAX_WORKERS`, the order-write pacer's `ORDER_WRITES_PER_SECOND` / `ORDER_WRITE_BURST`, and `DASHBOARD_SELL_MAX_WORKERS`, the most worker processes the backtest dashboard builds its Sell select in), `25` (scanner pagination progress cadence — `SCANNER_PROGRESS_LOG_EVERY_PAGES`), `50` (the sequential archive fallback's barren-page stop — `ARCHIVE_MAX_BARREN_PAGES`; also the value, not the same constant, of `EVENT_TITLE_LISTING_MAX_BARREN_PAGES`), `50_000` (day-slice worker flush size — `SETTLED_FETCH_CHUNK_RECORDS`), `date(2021, 6, 1)` (the first created-day the archive phase reads — `ARCHIVE_FIRST_CREATED_DATE`), `500_000`/`2_700` (the backtest RAM-budget warning's groupable-market threshold (SS-1) and its per-record byte estimate — `BACKTEST_MARKETS_RAM_WARN`/`BACKTEST_RECORD_BYTES_ESTIMATE`), `0`/`10`/`20`/`30`/`40`/`50` (process exit codes — `EXIT_OK`/`EXIT_SKIPPED_LOW_BALANCE`/`EXIT_TRADES_NEED_ATTENTION`/`EXIT_NO_TRADEABLE_SHARDS`/`EXIT_TIME_SERIES_SKIPPED`/`EXIT_RUN_IN_PROGRESS`), `3600` (scheduler job timeout — `SCHEDULER_JOB_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`; also the value, not the same constant, of `SCHEDULER_BLIND_RETRY_SECONDS` and of `SAME_TITLE_MAX_CLOSE_GAP_SECONDS`, spelled `60 * 60`), `60 * 60` (the largest gap between a same-title pair's two close times, in seconds — `SAME_TITLE_MAX_CLOSE_GAP_SECONDS`, DR-74; the gate reads it only inside `scanner.closes_apart`, so scanner's binding is the one that decides, and both paths' refusal lines print that same binding through `scanner.close_gap_bound_text()` — `backtester` holds no copy of the constant, so its verbatim line cannot state a bound its gate did not apply; `main._no_pairs_msg` renders its own binding through the same helper, like every other threshold it names; also the value, not the same constant, of `SCHEDULER_JOB_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` and `SCHEDULER_BLIND_RETRY_SECONDS` — patch `scanner.SAME_TITLE_MAX_CLOSE_GAP_SECONDS` in a test, never `config.*`, the `TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS` precedent), or `4` (blind-run retry cap — `SCHEDULER_BLIND_MAX_RETRIES`), `"09:00"`/`"America/Los_Angeles"` (the weekly live run's time and zone, and the backtest's entry checkpoint — `SCHEDULED_RUN`, one frozen `ScheduledRun` holding the weekday, hour, minute and zone together; read them from `SCHEDULED_RUN` — its fields or its accessors (`weekday_name()`, `at_time()`, `label()`, `instant(d)`), never a literal — and patch `scheduler.SCHEDULED_RUN` / `backtester.SCHEDULED_RUN` in a test, never `config.*`, since both modules bind it by value at import; `backtester._checkpoint_datetime`, `_monday_timestamps`, `_can_ever_enter` (through `_checkpoint_floor`, cached per schedule and date) and `_prefilter_cache_tag` all read the backtester binding when called, so one patch moves the checkpoint, the prefilter and the cache key together), `"checkpoint-v3"` (the version of `backtester._can_ever_enter`'s logic — `SETTLED_PREFILTER_CACHE_TAG`; read only through `backtester._prefilter_cache_tag()`, which appends `SCHEDULED_RUN.cache_slug()` when called; bump it when that logic changes AND when `CANDLESTICK_PERIOD_INTERVAL_MINUTES` does, since the predicate floors each checkpoint onto the candle period (`backtester._checkpoint_floor`) and the tag does not name the period — pinned beside it by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestPrefilterCacheTag::test_the_version_names_this_candle_period`), `252`/`365` (the dashboard's two annualization bases — `TRADING_DAYS_PER_YEAR` for a trading-day series such as the `^GSPC` benchmark, `CALENDAR_DAYS_PER_YEAR` for `_build_equity_curve` output and the default of `dashboard._sharpe`/`_sortino`; 365 is also the value, not the same constant, of `backtester._find_entry`'s one-year candlestick scan window, which is a calendar lookback and must NOT be re-pointed at an annualization base), `"KXMVE"` (the combo series-prefix family `scanner.event_series` collapses onto one series — `MVE_SERIES_FAMILY_PREFIX`), `86_400` (one day in seconds: historical's and backtester's private `_DAY_SECONDS`, and historical's private `_EMPTY_CANDLE_TTL_SECONDS`, the empty-candle-cache staleness rule — module-private, not config constants, since DR-13's empty-assembled-cache rule and its `EMPTY_ASSEMBLED_CACHE_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` were retired by the cache extension; `DASHBOARD_BUILD_STALE_SECONDS` is `2 * 86_400` and `SERIES_CATEGORY_CACHE_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` `7 * 86_400`), `True` (whether the time-series finders may pair two rungs of one event's cumulative deadline ladder — `TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS`, read at call time by both finders' same-event branch and by the backtest's resolution and reporting of its ladder flag; never re-spell it as a literal in a test or a harness, monkeypatch the name), or the Treasury Fiscal Data auctions endpoint's own shape — `TREASURY_AUCTIONS_URL`, `RISK_FREE_BILL_TERM` (`"8-Week"`), `RISK_FREE_RATE_FIELD` (`"high_investment_rate"`, the bond-equivalent yield, never the discount rate), and the `TREASURY_API_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`/`TREASURY_API_PAGE_SIZE`/`TREASURY_API_MAX_PAGES` bounds `treasury._fetch_rows` pages against — read only by `treasury.py`, a backtest-dashboard-only module the live bot never imports (see the `treasury.py` Module Map row) — `1e-12` (`FLAT_RETURN_TOLERANCE`, the smallest max-minus-min daily-return range `dashboard._varies` counts as a curve that moved), `(0.80, 0.81, …, 1.00)` (the backtest dashboard's sell levels, every 1% from 80% to 100% — `TAKE_PROFIT_LEVELS`, the dashboard's grid only: no live module reads it, and a live run sells at its own saved `sell_at`, any 1% step from 1% to 100%), `(1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 14, 21)` (the dashboard's minimum-days options: a position at its level is sold only while at least that many UTC calendar days remain before its last market stops trading — `TAKE_PROFIT_MIN_DAYS`, the dashboard's grid only, as the levels are — a live `sell_min_days` is any whole number of days from 1; read by `backtester` at call time through `_resolve_min_days_options`, so patch `backtester.TAKE_PROFIT_MIN_DAYS` in a test), `3` (the days in a row a position must stay at a sell level before it is sold — `TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS`, shared by the backtest and live selling, a whole number from 1 to `TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS_MAX`, 7; each reads its own by-value binding when called, `backtester` through `_resolve_hold_days` and `seller` through `_hold_days`, so patch `backtester.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS` or `seller.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS` in a test, never `config`'s), or `"backtest_dashboard_files"` (the folder of the dashboard's sidecar chunk files beside the page — `DASHBOARD_FILES_DIRNAME`) inline. Always import from `config.py`. Never re-spell `BUDGET_FRACTION` as a literal either — the per-trade Kelly cap for every pair, `1.0` (no cap) since the operator decision of 2026-09-27, a value too common to list above: a live run reads the saved live defaults' `LiveSettings.size_cap` and `LiveSettings.same_title_size_cap` instead, and the backtester reads both constants at call time (validated alike), through `config.pair_size_cap`, the one definition of a pair's cap. Nor `TRADE_CATEGORIES`/`TRADE_TAGS` — the live category/tag filter, `None` today (any), another value too common to list: a live run reads the saved live defaults' `LiveSettings.categories`/`.tags` instead (validated by `config._names`: `None` or a non-empty tuple of non-empty names, none of them `any` in any case), which only `main._filter_by_category` applies (the `Live settings:` line, the trade-log note and `main._no_pairs_msg` name them, through `config.describe_live_settings`/`describe_trade_filter`); `SERIES_CATEGORY_CACHE_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` (`7 * 86_400`) is how long the /series listing it files pairs by is reused. Nor `ADD_TO_HELD_PAIRS` — whether a live run may add to an exact pair the account holds, `True` (DR-77): a live run reads the saved live defaults' `LiveSettings.add_to_held_pairs` instead (`False` for a file saved before that toggle existed), which only `main._run_prod` applies, and the backtest never reads the constant. Nor `SELL_AT`/`SELL_MIN_DAYS` — `None` (never sell, no minimum of days): a live run reads the saved live defaults' `LiveSettings.sell_at`/`.sell_min_days` instead (off for a file that leaves them out), which only `main._run_prod` applies, through `seller.plan_sales`, and the backtest never reads the constants; `SELL_AT_STEP` (0.01) is the grid every sell level, the saved one and `main.py --sell-at` in whole percent, must sit on, as `SIZE_CAP_STEP` is for the caps. Nor the live sale's own settings (`SALE_PARTNER_MAX_LOOKUPS`, `SALE_PARTNER_MAX_AGE_DAYS`, `SALE_HEDGE_SLIPPAGE_TICKS`, `SALE_READ_BACK_RECHECK_SECONDS`, `SETTLEMENT_PAGE_SIZE`, `RECENT_CANDLES_RATE_LIMIT_SLEEP_SECONDS`) nor `CANDLE_NO_ASK_CEILING` (0.99: `historical.fetch_candlesticks` clamps a candle's NO ask to it and `historical.usable_candle_ask` refuses it as no quote). Nor `CONTRACT_PAYOUT_DOLLARS` — `1.0`, what one contract pays if it wins, so the most an open position can be worth per contract: `main._checked_positions_value` refuses Kalshi's value of the open positions when it is above this times the contracts held. Nor `CANDLESTICK_CACHE_FIELDS_VERSION` — `2`, the fields a cached candle carries (`ts`, the two asks and the hour's traded volume): `historical.fetch_candlesticks` reuses a cached file only when its `fields` tag equals it, so bump it when a candle gains a field. Nor the backtest depth model's settings, read only by `depth_model.py`: `DEPTH_MODEL_DISTANCES` (`0.0, 0.01, 0.02, 0.03, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20` dollars below the best bid), `DEPTH_MODEL_VOLUME_EDGES` (`1, 100, 1_000, 10_000` contracts traded in 24 hours), `DEPTH_MODEL_PRICE_EDGES` (`0.05, 0.20, 0.50, 0.80, 0.95`), `DEPTH_MODEL_QUANTILE` (`0.5`, the median), `DEPTH_MODEL_MIN_LADDERS` (`20`), `DEPTH_SNAPSHOT_MARKETS` (`2_000`), `DEPTH_SNAPSHOTS_DIRNAME` (`"depth_snapshots"`), `DEPTH_VOLUME_WINDOW_SECONDS` (`86_400`), `DEPTH_SNAPSHOT_MAX_WORKERS` (`4`) and `DEPTH_SNAPSHOT_PROGRESS_EVERY` (`250`). **One named exception:** `backtester.SIZE_CAP_SWEEP` — the backtest-only grid of per-trade Kelly size caps a size-cap sweep offers (`0.05`…`0.95` in 5% steps, then `1.0` = no cap, which is full Kelly since f* ≤ p ≤ 1) — lives in `backtester.py`, not beside `INTERVAL_DISCOUNT_SWEEP` in `config.py`, by the operator's instruction for that change (only the dashboard and backtest code could move). It is read only by `_sweep_from_candidates`; moving it to `config.py` is a one-line change. It is not the Kelly cap: live sizing reads the run's `LiveSettings.size_cap` (the saved live defaults', unless `main.py --size-cap` overrides it for one run), and every eager backtest point keeps reading `config.BUDGET_FRACTION` (`1.0` since the 2026-09-27 decision, `0.20` before), which the grid contains by value. Each grid cap is the cap for EVERY pair; a same-title candidate also stays under `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` at every one (`config.pair_size_cap`), so "no cap" on that axis means `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` for same-title pairs — no cap at all while it is `1.0`, as it was until that decision (it ships `0.20` since, so a default run's own cap is the grid's "no cap" option and its same-title rows size at 20% at every cap from 20% up).

**Use `@dataclass` for all data transfer objects.** Existing: `CandidatePair`, `ApiMarket`, `PriceRange`, `HeldPosition`, `HeldPair`, `Settlement` (scanner.py; all but `CandidatePair` and `ApiMarket` frozen), `TradeSpec`, `TradeResult`, `SaleResult` (a sale's outcome, built by `trader.sell_positions`; reporter.py, not frozen, like `TradeResult`), `SaleLeg`, `SalePlan` (one market of a position the take-profit rule judges, and a position it sells; seller.py, both frozen), `BacktestTrade`, `LiveSettings` (frozen; validated in `__post_init__`, so `dataclasses.replace` re-validates an override), `ScheduledRun`, `RunReport`, `TradeRecord`, `LegRecord`, `SaleRecord`, `SaleLegRecord` (the run result, reporter.py; all but `RunReport` frozen), `AccountBalance` (one balance read, auth.py; frozen at the top level), `StartingBalance` (a backtest's starting balance and where it came from, backtest.py; frozen). Never use a plain dict when a dataclass fits.

**Return `None` for validation failures; don't raise.** `compute_trade()` returns `None` when there's no edge. Both finders drop markets with a missing/unparseable `close_time` in the shared `scanner._filter_active_markets()` they each call first — reported as one summary WARNING carrying the count (silent at zero) — once per RUN, not once per finder: both run modes call `_filter_active_markets` on the SAME list twice, so `find_same_title_pairs`, always the second to run, passes the keyword-only `warn_missing_close=False`. The flag suppresses only the REPORT; the markets are dropped either way, and any standalone caller keeps the default `True` (TS-22). A `None` deadline would otherwise crash the close-time sort, `_pair_max_sum()`, and `compute_trade()` — and skip the rest of the bad markets silently. Only raise for truly unexpected errors.

**Two-stage fee calculation:**
1. `fee_per_pair_approx(price_a, price_b)` — continuous approximation over the two LEG prices (`scanner.leg_prices(pair)`: `(nA, pB)` for same-title, `(pA, nB)` for time-series); use during pair *filtering* before the integer contract count `n` is known.
2. `fee_leg_exact(n, p)` — ceiling-rounded exact fee; use for *final validation* once `n` is determined.
Never swap these — the approximation underestimates and will let bad trades through if used for final validation.

**Retry pattern:** Use `api_call_with_retry()` from `_http.py` for all new market-data API calls (this includes read-only portfolio calls like the balance read in `auth.read_account_balance()` and the positions listing in `scanner.get_held_positions`, which `get_held_tickers` reads; both now go through `fetch_json_page` on the SDK's raw `*_without_preload_content` variant rather than the modeled call — see the shard-balance gotcha). It handles HTTP 429 and 5xx with exponential backoff doubling 2s → 32s across 6 attempts (`_MAX_DELAY = 60s` is only a defensive ceiling). It ALSO retries status-less transport failures — `urllib3.ProtocolError`, `ReadTimeoutError`, `http.client.IncompleteRead`, and the builtin `ConnectionError`/`TimeoutError` — matched through a bounded `__cause__`/`__context__` walk (`_is_transient_network_error`), because urllib3 wraps the real error (a `ProtocolError: Connection broken: IncompleteRead` from `resp.read()` killed a multi-hour backtest fetch on 2026-08-03; it carries no `.status`, so the old status-only classifier treated it as fatal). Retrying these is safe *because* every caller of this wrapper is a read-only GET. Do NOT add retry logic to order submission in `trader.py` NOR to the collateral transfer POST — `_submit_order_v2` / `_submit_sale_v2` / `_execute_transfer` deliberately call `_http.signed_request_json` directly (which itself contains no retry logic, precisely so those call sites stay single-shot); a failed leg means the price moved, not a transient error, and a retried transfer moves the money twice. `trader._position_count()` **is** wrapped (it's a read-only GET like the rest): the no-retry rule covers submission only, and an unretried transient 429 there reads as "fill state unknown", escalating a recoverable ambiguity into a rollback or `manual_review`. Don't "unify" `_position_count` with `_submit_order_v2`/`_submit_sale_v2`/`_execute_transfer` in either direction — the asymmetry is the rule, and `trader.py` therefore DOES import `api_call_with_retry` (for the position lookups only); the no-retry rule is asserted per-function on the submission/transfer sources, and at `_submit_order_v2`'s two call sites (`_execute_one`, `_rollback_no_leg`), never as a module-wide import ban. The position reads that are deliberately unretried go through `trader._position_count_once`, and both sit inside the window where the NO leg MAY be filled (on the re-read path that is exactly what is being determined) and is certainly unhedged, because the YES leg has not been submitted: `_confirm_v2_no_mapping`'s mapping check (see the V2 backstop bullet) and `_execute_one`'s NO-leg ledger-lag RE-READ (DR-64 — see the delta bullet). It shares `_read_position`'s parse with `_position_count`, so only the retry policy differs; the YES-leg re-read is deliberately the RETRIED reader, because the first read beside it is already retried and a single-shot re-read that fails yields an unknown delta, i.e. `manual_review`, which leaves the NO leg unhedged INDEFINITELY — strictly worse than a bounded wait followed by a correct unwind. The accepted residual is that in the branch where the YES leg genuinely did not fill, that retried re-read can hold the loss-floored unwind for up to ~62s of backoff on top of the 1s pause, which can turn a `rolled_back` into a `rollback_failed` orphan; the pre-existing read beside it already carried that same exposure. One consequence to respect: the cause-chain walk means anything raised *inside* an `except` block that is handling a `TimeoutError` inherits it as `__context__` and is misclassified as transient, so `_execute_one` takes its post-failure position snapshots **outside** the `except` clause (on the NO leg and the YES leg alike) — otherwise a fatal lookup error stalls an urgent decision for the full ~62s backoff. `trader._sell_leg` follows the same rule for a sale: when its POST raised, or its reply says nothing, it reads the position after the `except` clause has ended (the retried read, and once more after a pause when the move is short of the whole count).

**Order and transfer POSTs are paced, never retried (2026-09-28).** Kalshi limits an account's writes with a token bucket that refills continuously rather than per window, and a request over the limit gets HTTP 429 and is rejected outright, not processed (Kalshi's rate-limit docs, docs.kalshi.com/getting_started/rate_limits), with no `Retry-After` header. `GET /account/limits` on this account (2026-09-28) returned `usage_tier` basic with a write `bucket_capacity` of 100 and a `refill_rate` of 100 a second, and `GET /account/endpoint_costs` (2026-09-28) lists no override for `POST /trade-api/v2/portfolio/events/orders`, `POST /trade-api/v2/portfolio/orders` or the `intra_exchange_instance_transfer` path, so each costs the default 10 tokens: 10 orders a second and 10 back to back. (The legacy `/portfolio/orders` endpoint takes no orders; see the order-path bullet under the V2 order semantics gotcha.) The first live production run (2026-09-28 02:26 PDT) ran 7 pairs on `execute_trades`' concurrent workers and POSTed all 7 NO legs and then the YES legs inside about one second; two YES legs came back 429, `_execute_one` read each as ambiguous (retried position read, delta 0, 1 s pause, re-read, delta 0) and rolled its NO leg back, and both unwinds filled — 5 pairs executed, 2 rolled back, about $2.40 lost to spread and fees. That fits the continuous model: 12 of the 14 accepted means the 14 went out within about 0.2 s (10 + 10 × 0.2 = 12). The same burst could have given a ROLLBACK a 429, i.e. `rollback_failed` with an open NO position, and a YES leg the exchange kills with its HTTP 409 is unwound at once, with no pause to spread the writes. So `_submit_order_v2`, `_submit_sale_v2` (in turn: `_ORDER_WRITE_PACER.acquire()`, before its `Submitting V2 sale order` line; sales run one position at a time before any buy, so none competes with a hedge write) and `_execute_transfer` each take exactly one place on `trader._ORDER_WRITE_PACER` immediately before their one POST (in `_submit_order_v2` before the `Submitting V2 order` line too, so the log time is the send time) — the order submitter through a keyword-only `pace` hook (`_execute_one` and `_rollback_no_leg` pass it on), `_ORDER_WRITE_PACER.acquire` when none is given: one `trader._WritePacer`, a thread-safe token bucket shared by every worker, at `config.ORDER_WRITES_PER_SECOND` (8) with bursts of at most `config.ORDER_WRITE_BURST` (8, and at least 2 — the constructor refuses less, since a pair's NO leg takes two places at once) — 20% under the Basic budget, for writes the bot does not see (another client on the account, a manual order, a probe). A caller joins one of two lines under a `threading.Condition`, each first come, first served in the order callers take its lock (a lock does not promise strict arrival order): the hedge lane (`acquire_hedge`), served first, and the in-turn line (`acquire`, `acquire_with_hold`). The balance never goes negative: the front of the line is served once the refill covers it — by whichever waiting thread wakes first, and none behind it goes first — waits happen in `Condition.wait` with the lock released, a caller that stops waiting (Ctrl-C) leaves the line, and one INFO line (`Paced an order or transfer write …`) is logged after a wait over 0.25 s. Under a continuous bucket the pacer's at most 8 + 8·T sends in any T seconds never exceed the exchange's 10 + 10·T. **Pacing is not a retry**: each submission, sale and transfer is still sent exactly once and the no-retry rule above is unchanged. A 429 that comes back anyway takes the path it always took, which differs by write: on a buy leg it raises into `_execute_one`'s ambiguous path (the live run's two YES-leg 429s were resolved that way and rolled back, the $2.40 above); on the unwind it is `rollback_failed`, an open NO position; on a sale order it raises into `_sell_leg`, which asks the account how many contracts sold; on a collateral transfer it is a failed POST, and the trades needing that shard are dropped. **Residuals:** pacing controls when a request is sent, not when it arrives, so large network jitter could still bunch arrivals over the limit (and the pacer counts a write as sent when its place is granted, a few milliseconds before the POST leaves — the 20% headroom covers up to 0.25 s of either); and the pacer is per process, so a second process writing to the account (`v2_probe`'s transfer step, or a run on another computer — two real-money runs on one machine never overlap, since `run_lock` stops the second with exit 50) has its own full-rate pacer. A 429 from either takes the paths just listed. **Hedge writes go ahead of opening NO legs (2026-09-28, the pacer's follow-up).** With one first-come-first-served line, each hedge write — the YES leg, and the unwind — waited for the pacer while its NO leg was filled and unhedged, behind other pairs' opening NO legs, up to `TRADER_MAX_WORKERS / ORDER_WRITES_PER_SECOND` (1 s) per write: in simulation at the shipped values (8 workers, 50–300 ms per POST and 100 ms per position read), NO fill to YES sent took up to 0.45–0.95 s and NO fill to unwind sent up to 1.3–1.95 s, with 7 and 14 pairs. Now each pair's places come from one `trader._PairWrites`. The NO leg's POST takes `acquire_with_hold`: it waits in turn until two tokens are free together, sends one and HOLDS the other (`_HeldWrite`). The YES leg sends the held place with no wait. An unwind takes `acquire_hedge`, the hedge lane, ahead of every waiting opening NO leg (it would send the held place instead only if the YES leg's submission raised before taking it). The held place goes back (`_PairWrites.close`) as soon as the NO leg's POST raises — working out whether that leg filled can take a minute of retried position reads (plus the 1 s ledger-lag pause and re-read), and a place held through them kept room from every other pair: with `burst − 1` pairs stuck there during a transport failure, every other pair's opening waited about 60 s (reproduced by the adversary critic; fixed before merge) — so the unwind of an unclear NO leg takes the hedge lane too; otherwise `_execute_one` gives an unsent held place back in a `finally` (its body is `_execute_legs`), whether the pair filled, was killed, stopped for review or raised. In the same simulation NO fill to YES sent is 0.000 s in every case. When every YES leg is killed, YES return to unwind sent is at most 0.40–0.45 s (0.575–0.95 s before), and NO fill to unwind sent — the measure quoted above for before — at most 0.45–0.70 s, which includes the YES leg's own round trip (four unwinds land in the hedge lane at once and the k-th waits for k tokens; one unwind alone waits at most 1/8 s). The cost is throughput, because each pair's NO leg now waits for two free tokens and holds the second through its whole round trip — and, until the V2 NO-leg mapping latches (the first V2 fills of a process), through `_confirm_v2_no_mapping`'s single-shot reads and their pauses (1, 2 and 4 s when the ledger reads unchanged). While the mapping is unsettled `execute_trades` runs pairs one at a time (see the runtime-backstop bullet under V2 order semantics), so that hold keeps room from no other pair; only once the phase has lasted `V2_MAPPING_CHECK_SERIAL_BUDGET_SECONDS` do pairs run together again, each still holding its place through its own check. The verifier's measurement of this cost (14 pairs with a lagging ledger: last return 3.2 s → 3.475 s) was taken when unverified pairs still ran together, before the one-at-a-time phase and the longer re-read schedule. Keeping the hold there is deliberate, since releasing it would put the YES leg in the hedge lane: a run's total time grew by up to 0.475 s at 50–300 ms per POST (7 pairs, every YES leg killed, 300 ms) and by 0.55–0.75 s at 1 s per POST, where the old pacer had put no wait in the window at all. **Why a held place is safe to send late, and why it must count against the bucket.** A write sent later than its admission can overdraw a plain token bucket: its token was taken from the pacer's balance at admission while the exchange's bucket, never having spent it, sat at its cap and gained nothing from the refill, so without a cap a held write could go out at the same instant as a full bucket of other writes and, in some stretch, the sends would exceed burst + rate × stretch by as many writes as were held. So the refill stops at `burst` minus the held writes. With free tokens L, held writes H and a bucket X at the pacer's rate and burst that is charged at each actual send: L + H ≤ burst always, and X ≥ L + H is kept by every event (a grant takes from L what X loses at the send, a hold moves a token from L to H, a held send takes one from H and from X, a release moves one from H back to L, and X refills no slower than L while L + H is under the cap), so X ≥ H ≥ 1 whenever a held write is sent, however late. `acquire_with_hold` needs two free tokens, so at most `burst − 1` writes are ever held: a hedge-lane write can always be served by the refill alone, a pair waiting for room is woken by any hold's send or release, and no caller waits on the pacer while it holds a write, so there is no deadlock whatever `TRADER_MAX_WORKERS` is. Raising the two constants is safe only up to the account tier's own write budget divided by the order cost (10 tokens). `v2_probe` is human-paced and does not call `_submit_order_v2` for its orders (its transfer step goes through `_execute_transfer`, one POST). Position and balance reads are GETs on the separate read bucket and are not paced. `tests/conftest.py` gives every test a fresh pacer; `tests/test_trader.py::TestWritePacer`, `::TestHeldWrites`, `::TestHedgeLane`, `::TestWritesArePaced`, `::TestPairWrites` and `::TestExecuteTradesArePaced` pin the bucket (its balance read under the lock, the wait handed the lock to release, a caller that stops waiting leaving the line, and by AST the module's one `_WritePacer(ORDER_WRITES_PER_SECOND, ORDER_WRITE_BURST)` construction), the held write (two places taken, counted against the bucket until it is sent or released, its room freed only after the balance is brought up to date when it is sent late, ended once, given back by the pacer when its caller stops just after being served, at most `burst − 1` held, a pair waiting for room woken by a send on real threads, and — on 12 seeded random mixes of late held sends, releases, single and hedge-lane writes, each caller's plan drawn before the threads start — sends never exceeding `burst + rate · T`), the hedge lane (served ahead of a waiting pair, with the in-turn control), a caller that stops waiting removing its own place and not an equal-looking one (`_PaceRequest` is `eq=False`), one place per POST before the POST (a 409 kill, a 429 and a place the caller hands in included), each `_execute_one` path's places, read as the number held at each POST and the hedge-lane places handed out (the held place sent by the YES leg; the hedge lane for every unwind; the held place given back before the first read after the NO leg's POST raises, on a killed NO leg, a disproven mapping and an escaping exception), and concurrent pairs at the shipped rate and burst whose YES legs go out the moment their NO legs return (7 and 14 pairs), whose opening NO legs are spread to the limit (7), and whose unwinds never let an opening NO leg go first (7 and 14). Apart from two real-thread tests, the multi-thread cases run in simulated time (`_SimTime`: time stands still while any thread runs and jumps to the earliest deadline once all are blocked, so the times are exact and do not depend on the machine); `tests/test_config.py::TestOrderWriteBudget` pins the shipped rate and burst inside the Basic-tier budget.

**Live selling (take-profit) — sales before purchases (operator decision of 2026-10-08).** Until then live trading held every position until it paid out, and only the backtest sold (see the selling gotcha below). A production run whose `LiveSettings.sell_at` is set now sells the held positions that have reached that share of their potential profit, by the backtest's rule, before it buys anything. `sell_at` is `None` in the seed, in config.py and in a saved file that leaves the key out, and a run with it `None` is what it was (two prod dry runs on 2026-10-08, back to back, one on the code from before this change and one on it with selling off, sized the same pairs at the same sizes and logged the same orders; the `Live settings:` line differed by its two new fields, and the market list moved by a few markets between the runs). The weekly scheduler passes no toggle flag, so it sells at the saved level; a dev run holds nothing and never sells. **The rule** is `seller.plan_sales`, which decides and sends no order. A position is an exact held pair (`scanner.held_pairs(..., log=False)`) or a lone held market with exactly one paid-out partner among the account's settlements (`scanner.get_settlements`): a settled market the account no longer holds, held on the other side with the same count (and none on the held side), that carries the held market's question label (`scanner.market_for_labels`, at most `config.SALE_PARTNER_MAX_LOOKUPS` lookups per run), paid out at most `config.SALE_PARTNER_MAX_AGE_DAYS` (60) days ago, settled yes or no, and is not recorded as settling after the run's moment. Anything else is never sold, and a `Not selling …` line gives the reason: a held market's ladder is unknown (then no position is judged at all), three or more held markets share its ladder, the counts differ, one side is held twice, a count or cost is unreadable or too small, the partner is missing or ambiguous, the market is not in this run's market list, or its count is not whole. With `sell_min_days`, `config.days_to_maturity` of the held markets' scheduled `close_time` dates (UTC, against today) must be at least the minimum, and an unknown date fails it; this is checked before anything is valued, so a position too near maturity reads no book. Then come `config.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS` (3) checks, 24 hours apart from the run's moment, the last one now. Now, each held market's real order book (`scanner._fetch_orderbook`) gives its bids for the side held (`scanner.bid_ladder`), walked for the position's count (`scanner.walk_bids`); a book that holds fewer contracts means no sale. Each earlier day reads the market's last hourly candle that ended in the 24 hours before that check (`historical.recent_candles`, never cached, then `historical.bid_before`), as one bid in any size; a partner that had paid out by the check counts at its settlement's revenue, otherwise at its candle bid. A market is worth its count at the price less `config.fee_leg_exact` on that sale (a paid-out partner's revenue carries no fee). The position's cost is each held market's listing exposure plus the fees paid, plus the partner's cost basis on the side held and its fees; its potential profit is its count at `CONTRACT_PAYOUT_DOLLARS` less that cost. The verdict is `config.take_profit_reached` at each check, stopping at the first below the level (so earlier days' candles are read only for positions still in line to sell), and `config.reached_every_check` over all of them: the functions the backtest decides with (see the selling gotcha for its one-line wrappers). A position that gets as far as its checks logs one `Take-profit check (sell at N%): …` line (`-> sell`, or `-> keep` with the share it reached at each check read, which stops at the first one below the level), and a summary reads `Positions to sell this run: N of M`.

**Where live selling differs from the backtest** (all recorded, none fixed): the earlier days' checks read one candle bid in any size (the backtest walks a modeled ladder when it has depth data), and the check now walks the real book; the days rule reads each held market's scheduled close (the backtest the realized close, which for an event decided early is earlier) and only the held markets' (the backtest reads both markets of every trade, a paid-out one's too); the sale's fee is charged once per market (the backtest per trade); a paid-out partner counts at the revenue its settlement reports (the backtest at $1 or $0 a contract by the result), and one that settled other than yes or no is never used; a ladder with three or more held markets, or two of unequal count, is never sold; a ladder whose rungs get different question labels (a question relisted in other words) leaves its lone legs unsold, because the partner must carry the held market's question; and the partner is found by side, count, question and age alone, since the settlements do not say which markets were bought together, so a held market that was never one half of a pair (an orphan left by a failed unwind, or one bought by hand) is judged with a settled market that fits as if it were its partner, and two that fit leave it unsold.

**The sale orders** (`trader.sell_positions`, called once, one position at a time, before any buy). Each held market is sold by one `reduce_only`, `immediate_or_cancel` order for exactly the contracts the plan holds there, never a count read from the account: a held YES by an ask on the YES book (`_V2_LEG_SIDE["close_yes"]`), a held NO by a YES bid (`"close_no"`, the unwind's side). `trader._sale_limit` prices it from the lowest bid the walk reached, on the market's own grid: the YES ask is rounded UP and the NO's YES bid (1 minus the NO bid) DOWN (`scanner.floor_to_tick`), so neither sells below the walk, and the price is kept between `scanner.v2_bottom_of_grid_price` (the one definition of a grid's lowest level, which `v2_limit_price` and the probe read too) and `_v2_top_of_grid_price`. A pair is two orders: first the market with less depth left past its walked count, for all its contracts at no slippage; then exactly as many contracts of the other as the first sold, at no worse than the bid its walk reaches for that count less `config.SALE_HEDGE_SLIPPAGE_TICKS` (1) tick of its grid (where the grid is coarser just past that bid, at a band boundary, the rounding takes the tick back and the order sits at the bid), so what is left is still an exact pair. Before any order of a position, every held market is read (the retried `_position_count`) and must still hold the plan's count on its side (`_holding_problem`), else nothing is sent (`not_sold`); nothing is sent either while `_V2_NO_MAPPING_DISPROVEN` is set. A part fill is a normal outcome of an immediate-or-cancel order, so a reply is read by `_sale_fill_count`, never by `_v2_fill_status`, which raises on one; a reply that says nothing, or a POST that raised, is judged as a buy leg's is, by the account: how far the position moved toward zero (`_contracts_sold`), read once more after `_V2_MAPPING_RECHECK_DELAY_SECONDS` when the move is short of the whole count (the ledger lags a fill by about a second), those reads made after the `except` clause has ended; a count that cannot be known stops the position at `manual_review` with no further order. `_submit_sale_v2` takes one place on the write pacer and makes one POST, never retried (`tests/test_trader.py::TestSaleOrdersAreSentOnce` pins by AST that it makes one POST after one pacer place with no loop or `try`, that no other sale function posts, and that none reads a reply as a buy leg's). Statuses (`reporter.SaleResult`, a vocabulary of its own): `sold`; `partly_sold` (a pair's rest is still an exact pair); `not_sold` (nothing sold or nothing sent, in a dry run too for a plan whose orders cannot be built; the error says which); `unbalanced` (a pair's second order sold fewer than its first: a CRITICAL naming what each market still holds and the extra to sell by hand); `manual_review` (a CRITICAL; an unexpected error in `sell_positions` is one too, logged with its traceback); `simulated` (a dry run). `unbalanced` and `manual_review` make the run exit `EXIT_TRADES_NEED_ATTENTION` (20) at every clean return, even when nothing is bought after the sales, and end its closing line with " Sales that need a person to check: N (see the CRITICAL lines above)."; the scheduler's exit-20 message still says "trades" only.

**Where selling sits in a run** (`main._run_prod`). After the balance, positions, ladders and market list are read, and before add-ons or either finder: with `sell_at` set, and only when every held market was identified and the positions listing was read to its end (else one `Not selling this run: …` WARNING, as adding to held pairs fails closed), `plan_sales`, then `sell_positions`, then `_record_sales` writes the sales to `trade_log.xlsx` before anything is bought (a dry run writes them right after sending; a live run that sold writes them once it has read its positions and cash back, or at once if that read raises): a banner row, then one row per position under the 18 trade columns (Status `sold`/`partly sold`/`not sold`/`unbalanced`/`check`/`simulated`; Notes `[sale: YES A / NO B, 84% of potential profit (level 80%), 9 days left, fees=$0.42]`; the cost and profit cells hold the plan's figures, the profit only when the whole position sold). If that write fails the sales are dumped as CRITICAL `RESCUE SALE` lines and the error stops the run before it buys. `--result-file` gets `sales` (one `SaleRecord` each) and `cash_after_sales`, and `submission_started` is set before the first sale order. A **live** run that sold anything (or left a sale for a person to check) reads its positions again first (`_positions_after_sales`, once more after `config.SALE_READ_BACK_RECHECK_SECONDS` when the listing does not yet show the sales), then the balance, and sizes its buys on the cash the sales freed and on Kalshi's value of what is left (`_value_after_sales`, which checks that value as at the start of the run, refuses it while the listing still does not show every sale — the buys are then sized on the cash alone — and does not apply the minimum-balance check again); it logs `After the sales: portfolio value $X = cash $Y + open positions $Z`. A **dry run** sends nothing: it adds each sale's estimated proceeds, its count at the average walked price less the fee, floored to the cent, to the cash and to the shard its market is on (`_with_simulated_proceeds`), leaves the portfolio value as read, and logs `Dry run: sizing as if the sales filled — cash $X (+$Y)`. In both, the markets sold stay out of every purchase (`blocked_tickers = (held_tickers - add_on_tickers) | sold_tickers`, which also holds both markets of a sale left for a person), a held pair sold this run is not added to, and a sold position's ladders are free again (`held_ladders` is rebuilt from the markets still held), as the backtest frees them. Pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_the_live_run_sells_before_it_buys` (the order of the calls, `dry_run=args.dry_run`, `settings=settings`, the read-backs, `sold_tickers` in `blocked_tickers`) and `tests/test_main.py::TestRunProdSellsInADryRun` and `::TestRunProdSellsLive`.

**Measured and first-run order for selling.** Prod dry runs on the live account on 2026-10-08 (read-only): with selling off, the same pairs, sizes and orders as the code before this change; `--sell-at 80`, 16 positions judged and none sold; the first `--sell-at 5` run, the GTA VI trailer pair (`KXGTATRAILER-26NOVE` YES, `KXGTATRAILER-26DEC` NO, 9 each) at 7%, 9% and 12% of its potential profit on its three checks, would have sold for an estimated $6.13 (checked by hand against the live books), and the buys were then sized on $7.67 of cash ($1.54 plus the proceeds); a run on the final code about an hour and a half later judged the same 16 positions and sold none, the pair then reading −2% now. **Before turning selling on:** (1) you run `python3 -m kalshi_betting.v2_probe --ticker <TICKER> --step yes-close` (about one cent, on a liquid, cheap market the account holds nothing on) and see it PASS, since no other step has ever sent the reduce-only ask that sells a held YES; (2) run a prod dry run with `--sell-at` at the level you intend (and `--sell-min-days` if you want one) and check its `Take-profit check` lines against the Kalshi UI; (3) save a Sell scenario from the dashboard's filter bar, whose Save button sends the Sell level and Min. days shown, or open `/confirm?tier_floors=…&spread_min=…&spread_max=…&k=…&size_cap=…&sell_at=0.85` (and `&sell_min_days=7`) with the five required fields set to the values you want kept (and `category`, with `tag`, when a filter is saved: a link without them proposes no filter), and check that the next run's `Live settings:` line reads `sell at 85% of potential profit`.

**Pair dedup prefers same-title.** When both scanners detect the same ticker pair, `main._dedup_pairs(same_title_pairs, time_series_pairs)` keeps the same-title entry — a same-title pair is a near-arbitrage (both legs pay under co-resolution, assumed at 95%), whereas the time-series copy of the same tickers is a directional bet on the in-between mass sized by an operator estimate (`k`). This matches `strategy.select_portfolio()`'s same_title > time_series tie-breaker. Don't flip the argument order. **Dedup is not a safety net, and DR-02 is why.** It only ever drops a time-series copy when a same-title copy EXISTS, so a rule that removed the same-title copy alone would hand the identical trade straight through as a time-series bet — which is exactly why the one-series rule is applied inside BOTH finders (see the one-series gotcha below) rather than here. Since DR-02/DR-54 dedup never sees the one shape it was load-bearing for: an identical-wording pair on two event tickers of ONE series is now dropped by BOTH finders, so there is no same-title copy for dedup to prefer and no time-series copy for it to drop. **Since DR-67 the finders cannot produce a cross-type collision on the same ticker pair at all**: a same-title pair requires identical wording, a time-series pair requires two DIFFERENT stated deadlines, and identical wording states identical spans, or none — never two different ones. Dedup stays as a guard for a collision that could arise another way, and its argument order still matters. **That exclusivity is also why the DR-74 close gate sits on the same-title finder ONLY.** The one-series rule had to gate both finders because, before DR-67, a same-series pair refused as same-title could come back as a time-series bet. A same-title candidate the close gate refuses has identical wording, so it can never be a time-series pair. The gate therefore removes the trade rather than relabelling it, and nothing reaches dedup — pinned end to end by `tests/test_scanner.py::TestDeadlineGuardFinders::test_identical_wording_closing_apart_forms_no_pair_of_either_type` (no pair from either finder, nothing out of `main._dedup_pairs`) and its backtester mirrors in `TestRunBacktestCrossTypeDedup`. The same preference runs through `select_portfolio`'s ladder rule (DR-76, see its gotcha): a same-title pick blocks a later time-series spec on its ladder, while a time-series pick never blocks a same-title one — the rule itself refuses only time-series specs. That does not make it free for same-title: a refused spec keeps its cash, so the greedy walk can then take a lower-ranked spec of either kind that it would otherwise have skipped, or take it at a larger size than it would otherwise have shrunk it to (see the `select_portfolio` paragraph below), and that spec can use up the cash (or a ticker) a later same-title spec needed. So one run's portfolio can lose a same-title trade, or gain a time-series one, beside the specs the rule refused — reproduced on synthetic specs in DR-76's review, when the walk still skipped rather than shrank a spec that did not fit (a $110 balance picks time-series STAR-1, time-series STAR-10 and same-title GOLD-A without the rule, and time-series STAR-1 and RAIN-1 with it); on the 2026-09-27 live books, with nothing held, the rule refused no spec, so nothing moved there.

**The live category/tag filter files a pair as the backtest dashboard files a trade.** `main._filter_by_category(pairs, settings, listing_client)`, the second pair-list filter beside `_dedup_pairs`, runs right after dedup and before enrichment (so a dropped pair costs no order-book request) in both run modes, and keeps a pair only when its category is one of `LiveSettings.categories` and its tag one of `LiveSettings.tags` — case-insensitive, `None` meaning any on that axis, the two axes combined by AND (the saved live defaults' `categories`/`tags`, `null` — any — in the seed; `main.py --category NAME`/`--tag NAME`, each repeatable, or `--any-category`/`--any-tag` to clear a saved filter for one run). **The rule is the dashboard's, by construction:** MARKET A's LITERAL series (`historical.series_ticker` — never `scanner.event_series`, which collapses the whole `KXMVE*` family onto one series for the one-series pairing rule, while Kalshi files each literal series under its own category) is looked up in Kalshi's /series listing (`historical.load_series_categories`, cached a week in `backtest_cache/series_categories.json`, `config.SERIES_CATEGORY_CACHE_MAX_AGE_SECONDS`) and filed under its category (`"Uncategorised"` when Kalshi gives none) and FIRST tag (`"General"` when it has none) by `historical.series_labels` — the function the dashboard's Returns Decomposition, filter bar and `k̂` breakdown file by (it moved there from `dashboard.py`, which imports it as `_series_labels`; pinned by `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestReturnsByCategory::test_the_page_files_by_the_one_rule_the_live_filter_shares` and, by value against `dashboard._trade_category`, `tests/test_main.py::TestCategoryFilter::test_it_files_every_pair_as_the_dashboard_files_the_trade`); a series the listing lacks is filed under its ticker-prefix label (`historical.infer_category`, the fallback `BacktestTrade.category` carries) and `"General"`. So the filter bar's Category option is `--category` and its Tag option "Sports · Basketball" is `--category Sports --tag Basketball` (a `--tag` naming "category · tag" gets that hint in its WARNING). **That filing rule is shared; the MATCHING is not the page's, in two ways a reader must not flatten into "the same slice".** A tag is matched under EVERY category while the page's Tag options are category-scoped, so `--tag Soccer` alone also keeps Economics' and Entertainment's Soccer series (62 of the 210 first tags on Kalshi's 2026-09-25 listing sit under more than one category); and matching ignores case, so a first tag Kalshi spells two ways under one category ("Anime Awards" / "Anime awards", both Entertainment; "COVID" / "Covid", both Health, on that listing) is one tag here and two options there. None of Kalshi's 20 categories differs from another only in case, so one `--category` is exactly its Category option; several names on one axis keep their union. Pinned by `tests/test_main.py::TestCategoryFilter::test_a_tag_is_matched_under_every_category_and_in_any_case`. A name reading `any` (in any case) is refused by `config._names` — it would match nothing yet render exactly like `None` on the `Live settings:` line and the trade-log note — so `--category any` exits 2 pointing at `--any-category`; a name merely holding those letters (Kalshi's "Companies" category) is a name. **A request only when a filter is set:** with both axes `None` — every scheduled run on the seed values — the list is returned untouched, nothing is logged and no listing is read; with one set and a listing to file by, the filter logs `Category/tag filter (<filter>): kept K of N candidate pairs — dropped <category · tag> <count>, …` on every run. A production run hands the filter its own client, which refreshes a stale listing through a retried read-only GET (the same `historical._historical_get` the backtest uses); a DEV run hands it `None`, and `load_series_categories(None)` makes no request at all and returns the cached copy however old (or `{}`) — the sandbox key must never sign a request to the production host. **It fails CLOSED:** with no listing to file by (the request failed and there is no cached copy, or a dev run with none) it keeps NOTHING and logs a WARNING saying why, rather than filing nearly every `KX…` ticker under `infer_category`'s `"Other"` (its prefix table names fourteen `KX` prefixes — `KXBTC`, `KXFED`, … — and files every other `KX` series there) — so a filter set on a machine that has never fetched the listing trades nothing until one is fetched (`load_series_categories`' own failure WARNING says so too). Such a run finds no pair and exits `EXIT_OK` like any run that finds none, so a scheduled one satisfies its Monday slot — exit codes deliberately unchanged by the live toggles — and the filter's WARNING is the one line that says why. A name no series in the listing carries, and no pair of the run was filed under, draws a typo WARNING (it matches nothing). The filter also appears on the `Live settings:` line and the trade-log note as `categories … | tags …` (`any` for `None`; `config.describe_live_settings`), and `main._no_pairs_msg` names it when set, since it can empty the list by itself. Tests seed the listing into their own `tmp_path` (`tests/conftest.py` redirects `historical._SERIES_CATEGORIES_CACHE`). A listing that was fetched is never thrown away over its cache: `load_series_categories` writes `series_categories.json` in a `try` of its own, so a write that fails — a full disk, or a live run and a backtest refreshing a stale listing at the same moment and colliding on `historical._save_json_cache`'s tmp file, whose name is derived from the destination — logs `could not cache Kalshi's /series listing` and still returns the listing in hand (pinned by `tests/test_historical.py::TestLoadSeriesCategories::test_a_fetched_listing_is_returned_when_its_cache_cannot_be_written`). Recorded residual: in the worst interleaving of that collision the file left on disk is unreadable, which the next run reads as no cached copy (a production run refetches it; a dev run with a filter set then has no listing and trades nothing until one is fetched).

**Directional, deadline-gap-tiered price filter — the run's live spread rule.** `find_time_series_pairs()` admits a candidate only when `config.time_series_spread_refusal(pB − pA, gap_days, settings)` returns `None` — the ONE live definition of the time-series spread rule, shared with enrichment and `validate_pair_price` and applied under the run's `config.LiveSettings`: `pB − pA` strictly positive (the LATER-deadline leg's YES ask priced HIGHER than the earlier's — never `abs(pB - pA)`), at or above the entry floor `config.live_time_series_floor(gap_days, settings)`, and at or under the spread band's ceiling, in that order and with `backtester._find_entry`'s `PRICE_EPSILON` placement. A pricier EARLIER contract is never a candidate: the strategy disputes an outsized in-between mass, and a pair with no in-between mass has nothing to dispute (reintroducing `abs()` would admit such pairs; the forecast reads the mid spread rather than this YES-ask gap, and the sizer refuses a time-series pair whose mid spread is not above zero, which the forecast's zero clamp, `time_series_profit_prob`, would read as riskless). The entry floor is `max(tier, band floor)` with `LiveSettings.tier_floors` on and the band floor alone with it off; the live toggles are the saved live defaults' `tier_floors` and `spread_band` (see the saved-live-defaults paragraph above; the seed saves `False` and `(0.0, 0.5)` — the band floor alone (0, so any strictly positive spread) under a 0.5 ceiling — which are also config.py's `TIME_SERIES_TIER_FLOORS` and `TIME_SERIES_SPREAD_BAND` since the operator decision of 2026-09-27, and `True` and `(0.0, 1.0)` before it: the tier alone and no ceiling, exactly the rule before the toggles existed); `main.py`'s `--tier-floors`/`--no-tier-floors` and `--spread-min`/`--spread-max` override either for one run (the run logs its rule, and a production run that submits orders under a departure from the saved defaults WARNS; see the live-toggle flags note under What NOT To Do). `gap_days` comes from `scanner.deadline_gap_days(market_a, market_b)`, which is order-independent (`abs(close_b − close_a).days`), so the tier never depends on which market was passed first — except for a same-event ladder (DR-73), whose gap is the one between its two STATED deadlines and is carried on `CandidatePair.stated_gap_days`. Downstream of pair formation there is exactly ONE reader of either, `scanner.pair_gap_days(pair)`, and every site that re-derives the floor goes through it: `_pair_max_sum` (the price-sum ceiling, the one definition enrichment and `validate_pair_price` both read), enrichment's spread rule (`time_series_spread_refusal`, and `live_time_series_floor` in its below-floor WARNING) and `validate_pair_price`'s band-ceiling test (`time_series_spread_refusal` again); calling `deadline_gap_days` at any of them would re-tier a ladder whose rungs share a `close_time` from 0.70 down to 0.85. The tier is set by the deadline gap between the two legs: 15% (`MIN_PRICE_DIFF_SHORT_GAP`) when the deadlines are ≤ 15 days apart (`SHORT_DEADLINE_GAP_DAYS`, inclusive), 30% (`MIN_PRICE_DIFF_LONG_GAP`) for 16–30 days; gaps over `MAX_DEADLINE_GAP_DAYS` (30) are never candidates — a wider gap leaves more room for the event to genuinely land between the deadlines, so more of the market-implied in-between mass is real rather than mispricing, and a bigger gap must be demanded before disputing it. **Where each toggle bites.** The floor (tier floors and band floor) is tested in the finder on the two scan-time YES asks, and sets the order-book price-sum ceiling `1 − floor` through `_pair_max_sum` in both `scanner._enrich_pair` (which `enrich_with_orderbook_prices()` calls for each tradeable pair whose two books it read) and `validate_pair_price()` (0.85 / 0.70 with the tiers on; 1.0 at a floor of 0 with them off) — the ceiling applies to the LEG prices (`pA + nB` for time-series, `nA + pB` for same-title — `scanner.leg_prices(pair)`), not to the YES-ask gap, and with the tiers on it already implies an executable gap of at least the tier. The band's CEILING is tested three times: in the finder on the scan-time YES asks — right after the `$1` guard, so a pair refused by both still counts there, and BEFORE the group's one-best contest, so an in-band runner-up represents a group whose widest candidate is refused, exactly as `_find_entry` refuses the Monday; in enrichment on the refreshed reference minus the TOP of the qualifying YES book (the widest spread any size prices at); and in `validate_pair_price` on a freshly fetched book's reference minus its best YES ask. The finder counts a floor refusal and a ceiling refusal each on its own silent-at-zero line (cross-event and ladder apart, per DR-73) and logs the rule it applied on EVERY run (`Time-series entry rule: …`, worded by `config.describe_time_series_rule`); a spread that is not strictly positive stays uncounted, like the direction filter it always was. **`min_price_diff_for_gap()` takes a band floor, `spread_min`** (`config.min_price_diff_for_gap(gap_days, spread_min=None)`), layered on the tier as `max(tier, spread_min)` — `backtester` passes one (`_find_entry` to filter, `_interval_calibration(spread_min=…)` only to label each gap band's tier with the floor its entries cleared, and `_tier_floors_bind` only to ask, both ways, whether a tier sits above a band's floor), and so does `config.live_time_series_floor`, with the run's band floor. **It also takes a keyword-only `tier_floors` switch** (`min_price_diff_for_gap(gap_days, spread_min=None, *, tier_floors=True)`): only an explicit `False` throws it, and the tier is then not applied at all — the result is the band floor alone (`spread_min`, or `0.0` with none), which drives both the gap test and the leg-price-sum ceiling (`1 −` the floor) exactly as `max(tier, floor)` does. Anything but `False` keeps the tier. The backtest's band sweep passes it only through its tier-floors-off family (`run_backtest_sweep(..., band_sweep=True, tier_off_sweep=True)`, which the CLI turns on with the band sweep), re-running the bands whose floor sits below a tier (on the shipped grid floors 0, 0.20 and 0.25 — `backtester._tier_floors_bind`, which asks this helper both ways at every admissible gap) with the tiers off; the live rule passes it through `config.live_time_series_floor`, from `LiveSettings.tier_floors`. At a floor of 0 what is left of the gap and sum tests is that `pB` must EXCEED `pA` — `_find_entry` refuses a time-series Monday whose spread is not strictly positive (`pB − pA ≤ PRICE_EPSILON`), and `time_series_spread_refusal` refuses it first, because a pair with no in-between mass has nothing to dispute; the refusal is inert with the tiers on, where every tier demands more — and a sum ceiling of 1, which the fee check beside them already implies. The backtest's primary band is resolved by `config.time_series_spread_band()`, from the tuple `backtest.py` builds out of `--spread-min`/`--spread-max`, or from `config.BACKTEST_DEFAULT_SPREAD_BAND` when none is given; each grid band of a band sweep goes through the same function, and so does `LiveSettings.spread_band` (which refuses `None` rather than read it as the backtest's default). No LIVE module calls `min_price_diff_for_gap`, a band helper or a toggle constant at all: pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_live_path_reads_toggles_only_through_live_settings`, a deny-by-default AST walk outside `config`/`backtester`/`backtest`/`dashboard` — which also forbids `TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT`, `BUDGET_FRACTION`, `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP`, `TRADE_CATEGORIES`, `TRADE_TAGS`, `ADD_TO_HELD_PAIRS`, `SELL_AT` and `SELL_MIN_DAYS` there, so `k`, both caps, the category/tag filter, adding to held pairs and the sell level with its minimum of days reach their sites only through the run's `LiveSettings` too — that also lets only whitelisted entry points (`find_time_series_pairs`, `enrich_with_orderbook_prices`, `validate_pair_price`, `strategy.compute_trade`, `pre_execution_check`, `main._run_dev`, `main._run_prod`, `main._no_pairs_msg`) resolve `live_settings()` — each exactly once, as `settings = live_settings() if settings is None else settings`, each defaulting `settings` to `None` — and no direct `live_settings()` call anywhere (`_DIRECT_RESOLVERS` is empty): `main._resolve_live_settings` reads the saved live defaults through `config.live_defaults()`, the one function `_DEFAULTS_READERS` allows to call it (the table's other reader, `read_saved_live_defaults`, is allowed only in `scheduler._check_live_defaults` and `defaults_server._current_defaults`, and `_DEFAULTS_WRITERS` allows `save_live_defaults` only in `defaults_server._App._post_confirm`), once, and lays `main.py`'s toggle flags over them; the pin also requires every other def that declares `settings` to declare it with NO default (`main._log_live_settings` included, which takes the run's settings AND the reference explicitly and never resolves either), requires every call to a function whose def declares `settings` to pass it, as the bare name `settings` — positionally or as `settings=settings`, never a literal `None` or any other expression — (`pool.submit(validate_pair_price, …, settings=settings)` included, and `main()`'s calls to its two run modes, with no exemption: `main()` hands both the settings it resolved), so the `reference` each run mode holds beside the run's settings (the saved live defaults, for the `Live settings:` marks) can never be handed where the run's settings belong, requires every `time_series_profit_prob` and `strategy._kelly_p_at` call to pass its `k` and every `max_affordable_pairs` call its fraction — never a literal `None`, which each would resolve to a `config.py` constant — and pins that the finder, enrichment (`_enrich_pair`) and `validate_pair_price` reach `time_series_spread_refusal`, enrichment `max_kelly_fraction`, `_pair_max_sum` `live_time_series_floor`, and `live_time_series_floor` `min_price_diff_for_gap` (its sibling `::test_ast_every_sizer_caps_through_pair_size_cap` pins that `strategy._evaluate_size`, `config.max_kelly_fraction` and `backtester._simulate_at_discount` all cap through `config.pair_size_cap`). The live rule and `_find_entry`'s per-Monday tests agree on every spread BY VALUE: `tests/test_backtester.py::TestLiveBacktestSpreadParity` calls the unmodified `_find_entry` across the 36 sweep bands and the no-band band, tiers on and off, gaps 0/15/16/30 and every boundary's float-noise neighbours. See the "Interval-discount calibration" gotcha below for the full backtest-only band × k × population sweep; its grid bands never reach the live path. The backtester's `_find_entry` mirrors all of it (at the default `tier_floors`), including the DR-73 same-event-ladder gap (DR-73c), which it reads through the same `scanner.same_event_ladder` behind the same switch. Never bypass `min_price_diff_for_gap()` with a flat threshold (the tier-floors-off rule is flat by design and still goes through the helper) — a flat 15% admits wide-gap pairs whose in-between mass is largely genuine. **After enrichment a time-series pair must survive the spread rule on the FRESH book, and three checks keep Kelly under `1 − k` (TS-34, and the live toggles).** These checks live in `scanner._enrich_pair`, which `enrich_with_orderbook_prices` calls for each tradeable pair whose two books it read. `enrich_with_orderbook_prices` used to rewrite only the two LEG prices and leave the non-leg YES ask — `pB` for time-series, `pA` for same-title — at its scan-time value, so `strategy._kelly_p` (whose forecast then read `pB − pA`) subtracted a scan-time `pB` from a depth-weighted `pA`; once the fresh `pA` reached the stale `pB`, `time_series_profit_prob`'s `max(0, pB − pA)` clamp returned `p = 1.0`, the pair modelled as RISKLESS and Kelly sized it at the cap — the same failure the `abs()` warning above describes, reached from the other end. `scanner._reference_yes_ask(pair, ob_a, ob_b)` re-derives that quote from the book already in hand and enrichment writes it back beside the leg fills when that side had resting bids. The refresh costs ZERO extra API calls: in each pair type `_leg_ask_levels` consumes only one side of each book, and on the market that does not carry the YES leg the unused side is exactly the one whose complement is the reference ask (`ob_b["no"]` for time-series, `ob_a["no"]` for same-title); for a time-series pair the other book's unused side, the earlier book's YES bids, is read too, also at no extra call, and its mid spread (`CandidatePair.mid_spread`, the forecast's input) reads the best level of all four sides, both unused ones included (see the DR-78 gotcha's residual (5)). For a TIME-SERIES pair enrichment then applies, in order: (1) it FAILS CLOSED when the later book has no YES ask (no resting NO bids) — the later market then has no midpoint, so there is no mid spread to forecast at and no fresh quote for the spread rule (the scan-time `pB` used to stand in, and under the ask-gap forecast a stale `pB` below the book's own YES bid let Kelly exceed `1 − k`: pA 0.30, bid 0.90, stale pB 0.50 gave f* 0.74 at k 0.75, 0.72 at k 0.80, which no size cap bounds once the per-trade cap is lifted, as it is since the operator decision of 2026-09-27); (2) it drops a CROSSED later book — `ref_yes + no_levels[0][0] < 1 − PRICE_EPSILON`, the market's YES ask below its own YES bid — and then a CROSSED earlier book — `yes_ask_a + no_ask_a < 1 − PRICE_EPSILON`, its top YES ask below its own YES bid (DR-78) — the two states in which Kelly can exceed `1 − k`, since the forecast reads both books' midpoints; (3) the spread rule — positivity and the floor on the reference minus the YES leg's prefix-average fill (the YES-ask gap at what the YES leg pays), the band's ceiling on the reference minus the TOP of the qualifying YES book. Each drop is one WARNING naming its reason. Once the guards pass, every qualifying level has `yes ≤ bid − floor + ε ≤ ref − floor + 2ε`, so the floor cannot fire beyond `2·PRICE_EPSILON`, and the fee truncation (see the marginal-fill paragraph) leaves a spread above the fee: those two checks are defence, and only the ceiling is live there. With neither book crossed, `f* < 1 − k` for every time-series pair — `f* = 1 − k·s/(1 − c)` with `s` the mid spread and `c = pA + nB + fee` at the fills; the later market's midpoint is at least its YES bid `≥ 1 − nB`, and the earlier market's at most its YES ask `≤ pA`, each to within `PRICE_EPSILON/2`, so `s ≥ 1 − c + fee − PRICE_EPSILON > 1 − c` — which is what makes `config.max_kelly_fraction` a true bound for a sizer pricing with the same `k` and cap (see the marginal-fill paragraph). A burst of crossed-book WARNINGs on a healthy exchange is a stop-and-report signal, not something to tune away. **same_title is deliberately NOT guarded**: its model is the fixed co-resolution prior, so `pA` is not a model input and there is no clamp to protect, and it keeps its scan-time reference when that book side is empty. **`validate_pair_price` adds two time-series checks of its own** on the freshly fetched books, beside the price-sum ceiling it already re-applies (which is the floor there): it fails closed when the later book has no YES ask now, and refuses a spread above the band's ceiling on the fresh reference minus the fresh best YES ask — each its own single WARNING. It adds no crossed-book guard and no floor test beyond that price-sum ceiling — but it does cut the fresh book at the first level with no edge left after the fee, `scanner._levels_with_edge_after_fee`, the one definition enrichment prices with, and counts reachable depth only over what remains (a book under the ceiling with no such level is dropped with its own WARNING, `no contract pair on the book now keeps an edge after the fee`). At a floor of 0 (tier floors off, as shipped since the 2026-09-27 decision) the 1.0 price-sum ceiling re-checks no edge at all, so without the cut a book that moved a tick against a thin-edge spec passed, and the FoK caps' one tick of slippage per leg filled it where every settlement cell lost (reproduced on the shipped settings: pA 0.01 / pB 0.03 / nB 0.97 sized at n=748 on $10,000, the book then one tick worse on both legs, win cells −$2.06; pinned by `tests/test_scanner.py::TestValidatePairPriceSpreadRule::test_a_fresh_book_with_no_edge_after_the_fee_is_dropped` and `tests/test_config.py::TestShippedLiveToggles::test_validate_re_checks_the_edge_after_the_fee`). The cut is inert while the ceiling leaves more edge than any fee (every same-title pair and every tier-on rule). **Known residual:** a book that moves AFTER `validate_pair_price`, before submission, can still fill within the caps' tick of slippage per leg, and a spec whose edge is thinner than that then loses in the win cells too — with the tiers on, the 0.85 / 0.70 ceiling's edge of at least 0.15 absorbed that.

**Known residual of the live spread rule: the backtest can enter on an older candle where live now fails closed.** Live enrichment and `validate_pair_price` refuse a time-series pair whose later book has no YES ask at that moment. `backtester._find_entry` has no equivalent — it prices each Monday checkpoint on the latest candle at or before it (`_candle_at_or_before`), which has no staleness bound — so a Monday whose later leg's latest candle is hours or days old is still entered on that older quote, where the live path would have found no current ask and dropped the pair. `_find_entry` does not apply the crossed-book guards either, but the Kelly gate does: a time-series Monday whose quote is crossed on either market (`pA + nA` or `pB + nB` under 1) is skipped there, walked book or not, and counted on its own silent-at-zero line (DR-78), so the entry still counts toward the entries and the `k̂` calibration but never trades on that Monday. Recorded, not fixed: bounding candle age would move every backtest result, and `TestLiveBacktestSpreadParity` pins only the spread tests the two paths share.

**The live defaults of 2026-09-27 — decision record.** Five live toggles were flipped on 2026-09-27, together: `TIME_SERIES_TIER_FLOORS` `True` → `False`; `TIME_SERIES_SPREAD_BAND` `(0.0, 1.0)` → `(0.0, 0.5)`; `TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT` `0.75` → `0.80`; `BUDGET_FRACTION` `0.20` → `1.0` (no per-trade cap); `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` `1.0` → `0.20`. `TRADE_CATEGORIES` and `TRADE_TAGS` stay `None` (any). One pair's largest stake is still 20% of the balance: a time-series trade sizes under `1 − k` = 0.20 (`config.max_kelly_fraction`), a same-title trade at most at `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` = 0.20, so `live_rule_warnings` is empty at these values (pinned by `tests/test_config.py::TestShippedLiveToggles`). Lifting the per-trade cap changed which bound binds, not how large a pair can get; the effective same-title cap is unchanged at 20% (every same-title trade V0 replayed sat at it). Turning a value back is an edit to its constant (re-pinning `TestShippedLiveToggles`) or, for one run, its `main.py` flag, and two of them go back only with `BUDGET_FRACTION` at 0.20: `k` 0.75 with no per-trade cap lets a time-series pair size up to `1 − k` = 0.25, above `LIVE_EXPOSURE_WARN_FRACTION`, so `live_rule_warnings` warns on every run; and `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` 1.0 with no per-trade cap sizes same-title at full Kelly (about 0.89 on a wide divergence), which `live_rule_warnings` also names. The whole earlier rule, for one run, is `main.py --tier-floors --spread-min 0 --spread-max 1 --interval-discount 0.75 --size-cap 20 --same-title-size-cap 100 --no-add-to-held-pairs --no-sell --any-category --any-tag` (see Run Commands). A default BACKTEST follows `k` and the two caps (backtester binds them) but keeps its own primary band (0, 1) with the tier floors on, so its primary is not the live rule; its `Live time-series rule (saved live defaults)` log line and the dashboard's `Live rule (saved live defaults)` header line name the filter-bar cell that holds the saved rule. Re-measured on the 2026-09-22 snapshot at these defaults, of the same-event ladder funnel's 350 candidates within the stated-gap cap, 110 are skipped at the $1 guard and 41 refused above the 0.5 ceiling (a spread that is not strictly positive is refused uncounted), and 48 pairs are emitted, in 48 events, 47 of them tradeable; 1 of the 48 has an intervening rung of its own event priced outside [pA, pB] — `KXHEAVENLYOPEN-27-22NOV26` / `-20DEC26`, whose `-13DEC26` rung quotes a YES ask of 1.00 against a pB of 0.97, most likely an empty ask rather than a stale quote, which was not verified (see `config.TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS`' comment for the funnel under the earlier rule). The evidence the decision was taken against, with its sources, verbatim:

1. The 365-day backtest dashboard. Source: backtest_dashboard.html, built 2026-09-26 21:05; corpus assembled 2026-09-25 11:06 UTC; 365 days from 2025-09-24, ladders on.
