At market prices its expected value is zero minus fees; it only profits if the market systematically overstates the in-between mass. "Risk-free"/"guaranteed" wording is gone for time-series everywhere; same-title keeps its co-resolution framing and its wire bytes unchanged. (2) **The Kelly model is `p = 1 − k·spread`** — the spread was the YES-ask gap `pB − pA` when this change shipped, and is the MID spread (the later market's midpoint minus the earlier one's) since DR-78; see the midpoint gotcha below — (`config.time_series_profit_prob(spread, k=None)`, the single definition that `strategy._kelly_p_at`, `backtester._simulate_at_discount` and `dashboard._kelly_fraction(pA, nA, pB, nB, pair_type, k=None, *, spread=None)` all call — pinned by value and by a two-link AST chain, see the calibration note below), with `k = TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT = 0.75` at the time (0.80 since the operator decision of 2026-09-27 — 80%, "prices converge by 20%"): the operator believed 75% of the market-implied in-between mass ("prices converge by 25%"). This is an operator-tunable ESTIMATE; the worked figures in this paragraph were measured at `k` 0.75. With `k = 1` (take the market at face value) or an independence model of the new loss cell, Kelly is ≤ 0 for every time-series pair and nothing ever trades (illustrative fixture 0.30 → 0.60, NO ask 0.40: `k=1` ⇒ p 0.70, f* −0.12; independence ⇒ p 0.58, f* −0.56; `k=0.75` ⇒ p 0.775, f* +0.16). Leaving the LITERAL old expression `1 − pA·(1−pB)` in place would NOT have disabled trading — it would have modelled the impossible A=YES/B=NO cell and sized real trades on it, which is why the formula is pinned rather than merely retuned. (3) **Kelly, not the 20% cap, sized typical pairs** — measured at `k` 0.75 and the `BUDGET_FRACTION` of 0.20 shipped until the 2026-09-27 decision; since then no per-trade cap ships and `1 − k` = 0.20 bounds every time-series trade (`config.max_kelly_fraction`; see the live-defaults decision record of 2026-09-27 under Key Patterns). With `k=0.75`, reading off an earlier contract at `pA` = 0.30 on a tight book (`nB = 1 − pB`), the uncapped fraction is ≈ 0.05 at the minimum short-tier gap and ≈ 0.16 at a 0.30 gap, so `BUDGET_FRACTION` (then 0.20) only bound for gaps of roughly 0.45 or more; **name the reference `pA` whenever quoting these** — the fraction is strongly `pA`-dependent (at the same 0.15 tier it is ≈ 0.14 at `pA` 0.10, ≈ 0.08 at 0.20, ≈ 0.03 at 0.40); a wide book (large `pA + nB` for the same YES-ask gap) drives Kelly negative and the pair is skipped (the same fixture with the later NO ask at 0.50 instead of 0.40 returns `None`). And because a pricier later contract is NORMAL term structure, far more time-series groups qualify than before (one best pair per normalized title+outcome key still applies — `scanner.time_series_group_key`, see the cross-strike gotcha — keyed on the largest `pB − pA`, which no longer bounds a whole daily family to one pair). **What to look for in outputs:** the prod Excel log keeps 18 columns but its x/y headers are now "x — A leg"/"y — B leg", its "Total Cost ($)" column is "Total Cost incl. fees ($)" and its "Min Profit ($)" column is "Profit if won ($)" (headers on new workbooks only — an existing shared `trade_log.xlsx` keeps its old header row, since `_append_locked` writes headers only when creating the file; the VALUES change in every workbook) and every row's Notes cell is prefixed `[<pair_type>: <SIDE_A> A / <SIDE_B> B[ nB=0.xxxx] fees=$x.xx[ adds to N held]] ` (the last part only on a trade that adds to a held pair) — the `fees=` suffix is what makes a row readable under an old header, since the cost beside it is now fee-inclusive (TS-12); for time-series rows the retained "nA (NO ask)" column is A's best NO ask as the finder read it at scan time (reporting only, not a traded price; enrichment does not refresh it, so it is not the book quote the DR-78 forecast reads, whose mid spread only the `Trade computed` log line prints) and the traded `nB` lives in Notes. The dev-simulation candidates sheet gains an "nB (NO ask)" column (13 headers) and its "Price Diff" is `pB − pA` for time-series, `pA − pB` for same-title; `main.print_pairs_table` gains an "nB (NO)" column and its "Min Profit" header is now "Profit (win)" (and, from DR-17, "Outcome A"/"Outcome B" columns carrying each leg's subtitle). `BacktestTrade` carries `entry_nB` (from the candle's `no_ask_close` — old caches need no refresh and there is no `SETTLED_PREFILTER_CACHE_TAG` bump) and `deadline_gap_days` (the calendar-day gap between the two legs' DEADLINES that `_find_entry` selected the price tier and applied the `MAX_DEADLINE_GAP_DAYS` cutoff on, carried out rather than recomputed — the legs' `close_time` gap for a cross-event pair, the two STATED deadlines' gap for a same-event ladder (DR-73), which for a ladder routinely differ (measured on a 1,506-market archive corpus: 101 of 408 ladder pairs have a ZERO-day close gap, 37 closing at the identical instant, while every stated gap is in [1, 30]); it is also what `_interval_calibration` bands `k̂` on, through `CalibrationObservation.gap_days`; `None` for same_title, which has no deadline-gap concept, and for any trade built without it, e.g. test fixtures — reporting only, nothing sizes, prices or settles on it), its `profit` is negative only in the in-between cell, and its `slippage` for time-series is zero in both win cells and negative in the loss cell (there is no positive-slippage cell). `run_backtest` SKIPS (never trades or pays) any time-series candidate that settled A=YES/B=NO and logs ONE summary WARNING with the count (silent at zero): most likely a wording false negative — a snapshot family, or a recurring window worded "before <date>", read as cumulative — though DR-72 widened the WARNING to also name legs ordered on an early REALIZED close (cross-event pairs only — a same-event ladder is ordered on its stated deadlines), and strike-blind grouping on a cache without subtitles, rather than pointing at the wording heuristic alone. **Since DR-67 that is no longer the only signal** — both finders screen the legs' wording first (`scanner.cumulative_deadline_pair`), so the counter is defence in depth behind a heuristic and a non-zero count means one of those causes fired, most likely the wording one. Expect it at or near zero on a post-DR-67 run. That signal is only trustworthy because `_find_entry` orders a time-series pair's legs on the close **DATETIMES**, like the live finder's `scanner.find_time_series_pairs` sorting its group members on `m.close_time`: two contracts closing on the same UTC date at different times of day are a valid zero-day-gap pair, and deciding the swap on the parsed DATES made them a tie, leaving "market A" as whichever the group list held first — so the pair was either dropped (the direction test goes negative) or replayed with its legs inverted, whose genuine in-between settlement then read as the impossible A=YES/B=NO cell and was booked as a *false* premise violation, corrupting the interval-discount calibration's denominator too (TS-06, fixed 2026-09). **That parity is on the DATETIME-vs-DATE fix only, not on what `close_time` means.** The two paths read the same field name on the close datetimes, but not the same MOMENT: live reads the SCHEDULED close of a still-open market, while the backtest reads the REALIZED close Kalshi recorded for a settled one, which for an event that resolved early (a sports market decided before its listed deadline) can sit before the original schedule. A later-deadline leg that resolved early is therefore ordered first, on the realized rather than the scheduled gap — a recorded, pre-existing residual (backtest-only; it needs a scheduled-close field via a refetch, or span-date parsing off the wording), not something this fix addresses. `_extract_pairs`' close-time sort deliberately stays a DATE sort (a datetime sort would raise on a naive/aware mix from a hand-edited cache); only the swap decision reads datetimes, keeping the date comparison as its `TypeError` fallback. **Not updated by this change:** `kalshi_bot_flowchart.pdf` predates it (it already showed `|pA−pB|` and the wrong dedup order) and is NOT regenerated; `BUG_SWEEP_FINDINGS.md` is a dated, frozen record and is left untouched; `scheduler.py`'s cosmetic "arbitrage" wording is left alone (it never parses trader or main log text). **Deferred follow-up:** fractional-contract sizing remains outstanding (`_format_count` still always emits `.00`). Follow-up (a) — calibrating `TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT` from the backtester — shipped 2026-09; see the next note for what it built.

**The time-series forecast reads the midpoints (DR-78, 2026-10).** **What.** A time-series trade's chance of profit is `p = 1 − k·spread` (`config.time_series_profit_prob(spread, k)`), and `spread` is the MID spread: the later market's midpoint minus the earlier one's, a market's midpoint being halfway between its best YES ask and its best YES bid (its YES bid is 1 minus its best NO ask). `config.time_series_mid_spread(yes_ask_a, no_ask_a, yes_ask_b, no_ask_b)` is the one definition, written as the YES-ask gap less half the difference in the two books' widths (a book's width is its YES ask plus its NO ask, less 1) — the same number as mid B − mid A, and the ask gap itself, bit for bit, whenever the two books' YES ask plus NO ask come to the same float (as every whole-cent book with no width written as decimals does, 0.30 and 0.70 say, so every fixture with such quotes keeps its pinned figures); on asks built as the book reader builds them (1 minus the other side's bid) it can differ from the ask gap in the last bit (on 648 of the 4,851 whole-cent books with no width, by at most 1.1e-16). Before DR-78 the forecast read the YES-ask gap `pB − pA`, re-priced at each size's YES fill. **Why.** The ask gap carries half the later book's width less half the earlier book's: a cost of trading, not the market's view of the odds, and on the backtest data the later book is usually the wider one, so the gap overstated the in-between chance. On the strictly tradeable backtest points of that analysis (154 ladders, each counted once) a constant `k` on the mid spread beat a constant `k` on the ask gap in 93% of whole-ladder resamples (97% with a smoothed variant), in every draw of a stratified 75/25 test in both halves of the year, with a Brier skill of about +5%. **Where it is read.** Live: `scanner._enrich_pair` writes it on each time-series pair it prices at its fills whose later book has a YES ask (`CandidatePair.mid_spread`, from the tops of the two books it already fetched, at no extra request: the best level of all four sides, two of them sides neither leg buys from — the earlier book's YES bids and the later book's NO bids, whose complement is the reference YES ask), and `strategy._kelly_p_at` reads it through `scanner.pair_mid_spread` (by type: a real, finite number above `PRICE_EPSILON`, else `None`). Backtest: `_candidate_pair` sets it from that Monday's four candle quotes (never the market record's end-of-life quotes; a walked book's enrichment writes it again from the book's top, which reproduces them), and Pass 1b's Kelly gate computes it from the same four quotes. Dashboard: the Kelly-vs-actual scatter forecasts each trade at its entry quotes' mid spread (`_entry_mid_spread`) while pricing its legs at what it paid (`_quotes_at_fills`), as the live sizer forecasts at the books' tops and prices at the fills, and the spread calibration scores the same entry mid spread (`_spread_observations`). Calibration: the backtest's empirical `k̂` (`_interval_calibration`) divides the realised in-between rate by the mean mid spread, each pair's read at its first qualifying Monday's four quotes (`CalibrationObservation.implied`), so `k̂` and the forecast's `k` are fractions of one quantity and the `k̂ − k` cards and heatmap compare them directly; its log header reads `Interval-discount calibration (k_hat = realised in-between rate / market-implied gap at the midpoints)`. **What follows from it.** `p` is the same at every order size; the prices an order pays set only the cost, the fees and Kelly's `b` (DR-62, unchanged). A time-series pair with no usable mid spread — one never priced off its books — has no forecast: `_kelly_p_at` returns `None` and `compute_trade` refuses it with one INFO line (DEBUG when quiet), `No forecast for '<title>': no usable mid spread — skipping`, or for an add-on its add-on line alone, `Not adding to held pair '<title>': no forecast (no usable mid spread)`, so the forecast's zero clamp, which would read such a pair as riskless, is never reached on the live path. Every `Trade computed` line of a time-series pair names its spread after `p` (`| p=0.66 (mid spread 0.490) kelly=…`). **A missing YES bid** on the earlier market counts as a bid of 0 (a NO ask of 1.0, so its midpoint is half its YES ask), which can only lower that midpoint and so raise the spread and lower `p` — the cautious direction, by operator decision. **The `1 − k` bound needs BOTH books uncrossed, and has two guards.** `f* = 1 − k·s/(1 − c)` with `c = pA + nB + fee` at the fills; on books neither of which is crossed by more than `PRICE_EPSILON`, the later midpoint is at least its YES bid (`≥ 1 − nB`) and the earlier at most its YES ask (`≤ pA`), so `s ≥ 1 − c + fee − PRICE_EPSILON > 1 − c` and `f* < 1 − k` (`config.max_kelly_fraction`'s docstring gives the argument; enrichment's depth bound relies on it; the backtest's `cap_free_from` does not, since it is never below the peak Kelly fraction, so no size depends on the cap above it either way). A crossed EARLIER book puts its midpoint above its YES ask and can break the bound, so enrichment now refuses it as well as a crossed later book (`ENRICH_CROSSED`, WARNING `Pair '<title>' dropped: the earlier contract's YES ask 0.3000 sits below its own YES bid 0.3500 — a crossed book`), and the backtest's Kelly gate skips a time-series Monday whose quote is crossed on either market (`pA + nA` or `pB + nB` under `1 − PRICE_EPSILON`), counted once per pair per Monday on its own silent-at-zero line, `Time-series Mondays skipped because a market's YES ask sat below its own YES bid (a crossed book) (<label>): N` (DEBUG when quiet). On uncrossed quotes that pass the fee check the mid spread is above the fee less `PRICE_EPSILON`, so above zero, and the clamp never decides a Monday there either. **What does not move.** The entry rules — positivity, the band, the tier floors, the price-sum ceiling, the fee check, `_find_entry`, and the live finder's one-best-pair contest by the largest `pB − pA` — all still read the YES-ask gap (operator decision: forecast only). Costs, fees, Kelly's `b`, the caps, budgets and the cash reserve; every `k` value (config.py's 0.80, the seed's 0.80, whatever is saved); same-title (its fixed 0.95 prior); the order path (`trader`, and `validate_pair_price`, which never re-checks `p`); the trade log's columns and the run result's fields; the cache tags (no `SETTLED_PREFILTER_CACHE_TAG` bump); the candidate pair list; and the `k̂` calibration's population (every time-series entry with a binary settlement, premise violations excluded — no crossed-quote skip there either). **What moves.** Every time-series `p`, so sizes and which pairs trade, live and in the backtest; the new crossed-book refusals (none fired on the two recorded books below); the `Trade computed` line; the empirical `k̂` (log and dashboard) and the dashboard's spread calibration, wherever a pair's two books differ in width (on books with no width the mid spread is the YES-ask gap, so the dashboard's golden fixture kept every value, and only words moved: the calibration section's caption and x-axis title, and a caption under the interval-discount section's calibration table saying its "Mean implied gap" is the mid spread); and the backtest's `SweepPoint.peak_kelly_fraction` wherever a crossed quote had set it (on the golden band sweep, 156 tier-on points, the time-series and cross-event populations whose peak was the TA/TB pair's crossed later quote — every trade, curve and check was unchanged). **Measured** (2026-10-06, offline replays of the live pipeline over the recorded books of 2026-09-27 and 2026-10-07 at the saved defaults' settings — tier floors off, band 0–0.8, `k` 0.70, a 15% per-trade cap, a 20% same-title cap — but with adding to held pairs off (held markets dropped; the saved defaults have it on), at $1,000 and $211, main and this change back to back): identical candidate pairs, enriched leg prices and depth in all four; main's code with only its forecast swapped for the mid spread reproduces this change's specs, portfolio and validations exactly; the portfolio moved from 12 to 14 trades ($954.91 → $953.74) and 10 to 11 ($200.85 → $200.50) on the September books, and from 16 to 14 ($764.96 → $693.91) and 12 to 11 ($200.91 → $200.74) on the October ones; of the tradeable time-series pairs, the mid spread sat below the top-of-book ask gap on 20 and above it on 21 of 54 (September) and below on 26 and above on 18 of 60 (October). The `k̂` calibration (2026-10-07, offline, the cumulative-wording subset of the corpus starting 2025-10-01, candles from the disk cache only, main and this change's code over the same entries): pooled `k̂` 0.852 → 0.873 at band 0–1 with the tier floors off (1,028 entries), 0.850 → 0.858 at band 0–0.8 with them off (975) and 0.891 → 0.904 at band 0–1 with them on (592), each equal to within 1e-15 to `k̂` recomputed outside the package on its own basis (in-between count over the summed YES-ask gaps, then over the summed mid spreads). **Residuals, recorded.** (1) The candle bid floor: the candle fetch clamps every NO ask into 0.01–0.99, so a backtest YES bid never reads below 0.01 where live reads a missing bid as 0; where an earlier market's YES bid is missing or under a cent, the backtest's mid spread can sit up to 0.005 below the live one, and its `p` up to `k × 0.005` above. (2) `k` is now relative to the mid spread and no `k` value changed (operator decision); on the 365-day data the empirical `k̂` reads about 0.04 higher on mids than on the ask gap (0.008 to 0.022 higher on the 2025-10-01 cumulative subset measured above), and at one `k` a pair whose later book is wider than its earlier one now forecasts a higher `p` than before at the top of the book, and so can size larger. Deeper it can be the other way round: the old forecast re-priced `p` at each size's YES fill, so once the YES leg's average fill sits more than `(wB − wA)/2` above its top (`wA` and `wB` the earlier and later books' widths), the new `p` is the lower one, and a size that deep can come out smaller than before. (3) The mid spread is read at the top of both books while the legs fill deeper: a size that walks past the top pays more, and `b` falls with it, but the forecast does not move. (4) The backtest's entry (`_find_entry`) still records a crossed Monday as a qualifying Monday; the gate never trades it, but the `k̂` calibration, which reads each pair's first qualifying Monday, still counts it, at that Monday's mid spread — and a crossed earlier quote is the one way an entry's mid spread can be zero or negative, since on any other entry the fee check keeps the later book's width under the YES-ask gap, which keeps the mid spread above half that gap. On the 2025-10-01 cumulative subset no entry's first Monday is crossed on either market and no mid spread is at or below zero (the smallest is 0.015 with the tier floors off and 0.16 with them on), so the `k̂` measured there does not depend on this. (5) The forecast reads two quotes neither leg buys from, each at the top of its book at whatever quantity rests there: the earlier market's best YES bid and the later market's best NO bid (whose complement is the reference YES ask). `scanner._bids_to_ask_levels` keeps any level above 0 contracts, fractional included, so a quote worth under a cent can set the forecast; and `validate_pair_price`, which re-reads both books just before submission, re-checks the price-sum ceiling, the edge after the fee, the later market's YES ask (that it is there, and the band's ceiling on the YES-ask gap between it and the earlier market's best YES ask) and the reachable depth, but never the earlier market's YES bid, the mid spread or `p`. A higher earlier YES bid or a lower later YES ask narrows the mid spread and so raises `p`. Measured as a change in `p` the effect looks small (under 0.09 on the recorded books below), but it can move the stake from nothing to near the cap. The final review's probes (`.git/mid-forecast/final-review/`), at the saved defaults' settings (tier floors off, band 0–0.8, `k` 0.70, a 15% cap) on $10,000, with 5,000 contracts on each leg's side, an earlier YES ask of 0.45 and a later YES ask of 0.75 beside a later YES bid of 0.72: earlier YES bids of 5,000 at 0.21 alone give a mid spread of 0.405 and no trade; with 4 contracts at 0.42 above them, 0.300, a Kelly fraction of 11.97% and a $1,196.99 stake; with 0.01 contract at 0.44 above them instead, 0.290, 14.91% and $1,490.14. Before DR-78 the forecast read one such quote, the later market's YES ask, at full weight: on the same books but with a later YES ask of 0.85 (NO bids of 5,000 at 0.15), a 0.01-contract later NO bid at 0.28 above them moved main from no trade to the full 15% cap ($1,499.28). DR-78 reads that quote at half weight and the earlier market's YES bid at half weight: the same total weight on quotes neither leg buys from, now split over two, but how far a stray quote can move the forecast depends on both books' widths. A quote can sit no further than its own book's other side, since each crossed-book check stops it there, so main's spread could move by up to the later book's width and DR-78's by up to half of each book's width: further than main's wherever the earlier book is the wider. On the probe's books with the later YES ask of 0.75 (widths 0.24 earlier, 0.03 later), one 0.01-contract quote moves main from $1,196.99 to $1,499.28 (a later NO bid at 0.28) and DR-78 from no trade to $1,490.14 (the earlier YES bid at 0.44). With the later YES ask of 0.85 (width 0.13) one later NO bid moves main to the cap, while DR-78 needs a quote on each side to reach it (an earlier YES bid alone gives $22.85, a later NO bid alone no trade). On the recorded books the earlier book is the wider at the top on 21 of the 54 tradeable time-series pairs of 2026-09-27 and 18 of the 60 of 2026-10-07, so there quotes on both sides could move DR-78's mid spread further than any quote could move main's spread, and on 6 and 7 of them a single quote could. The earlier market's top YES bid rests under 10 contracts on 23 of the 54 and 25 of the 60, and the later market's top NO bid on 9 and 12; read at its first level of 10 or more contracts instead, the earlier bid would move the mid spread by 0.02 or more on 5 and 1 of them (more than 0.02 on 3 and 1), by up to 0.105 and 0.125 (`p` by up to 0.0735 and 0.0875 at `k` 0.70; `KXTRUMPAICZARWHEN-26-26OCT09`, a top bid of 0.42 for 4 contracts, and `KXGEMINI-GEM4-26NOV01`, 0.91 for 9), and the later bid by more than 0.02 on 1 and 0 of them, by up to 0.105 (`p` by 0.0735; `KXBIGSKYOPEN-27-29NOV26`, a top NO bid of 0.29 for 3, on a pair the $1,000 replay traded); a top worth under a cent set the forecast of 2 of the 54 (one of them a pair the $1,000 replay traded) and 1 of the 60, moving the mid spread by at most 0.010 against the next level down. Hardening this — reading each of those two tops at its first level of at least one whole contract, or re-checking the mid spread on `validate_pair_price`'s fresh books — is an operator decision not made here. **Every backtest trade figure recorded before DR-78 — sizes, which trades enter, returns, drawdowns, peak Kelly fractions — forecast at the YES-ask gap and is not comparable, and neither is any `k̂` recorded before it, which divided by the mean YES-ask gap** (see the interval-discount calibration gotcha). Pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestForecastReadsTheMidSpread` (the same `p` at every size on a multi-level book; the ask gap and the mid spread disagreeing both ways; no usable mid spread refused and said, INFO and quiet DEBUG, an add-on on its add-on line alone; a spec without one never shrunk; the trade line; 10,000 random two-sided uncrossed books through `_enrich_pair` and `compute_trade` with every share under `1 − k` and the cap — 8,025 tradeable and 3,767 sized, and of the tradeable pairs' Kelly fractions at the top of the book 159 within 3% of `1 − k`, the largest at 0.9988 of it; and books crossed on either market never tradeable — of 2,000 each, 1,851 crossed earlier books and 1,830 crossed later ones refused as crossed, 825 and 601 of which would otherwise reach `1 − k`), `::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_the_forecast_reads_the_midpoint_spread` (`_kelly_p_at` and `compute_trade` read `pair_mid_spread`; `_enrich_pair`, `_candidate_pair`, `_simulate_at_discount`, `_interval_calibration`, `_kelly_fraction` and `_entry_mid_spread` call `time_series_mid_spread`, each with the earlier market's YES ask and NO ask, then the later market's, in the helper's order — a swap of `pB` with `nA` keeps the value, so no value test can see it; the scatter hands `_kelly_fraction` `spread=_entry_mid_spread(t)`, and `_spread_observations` calls `_entry_mid_spread`; every `time_series_profit_prob` call in the package takes a bare name its own function binds from one of the two mid-spread helpers — exactly three, in `strategy._kelly_p_at`, `backtester._simulate_at_discount` and `dashboard._kelly_fraction`), `tests/test_scanner.py::TestEnrichmentSpreadRule::test_a_crossed_earlier_book_is_dropped`, `::test_the_earlier_guard_reads_its_best_yes_bid` and `::test_an_earlier_book_one_float_step_short_is_not_crossed` (the eight whole cents whose book with no width the book reader builds one float step under 1.0, kept by the guard's `PRICE_EPSILON`), `tests/test_backtester.py::TestKellyGateReadsTheMidSpread` (a quote one float step under 1.0 on both markets is not skipped), `::TestCandidatePairCarriesTheMidSpread`, `::TestIntervalCalibration::test_implied_is_the_mid_spread_not_the_ask_gap`, `::test_a_crossed_earlier_quote_can_put_the_mid_spread_below_zero` (an entry `_find_entry` records with the tier floors off; the observation keeps its sign, and a bucket whose mean mid spread is not above zero reports no `k̂`) and `::test_a_crossed_first_monday_is_measured_at_its_mid_spread` (crossed on either market, skipped by the gate and still counted), `::TestRunBacktestTimeSeriesFlow::test_calibration_reads_the_entry_mid_spread` (through the real entry pass), `::TestLogIntervalCalibration::test_report_lines` (the header), `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestFillsOnRowsAndScatter::test_the_scatter_forecasts_at_the_entry_mid_spread_not_the_fills`, `::TestSpreadCalibration::test_the_prediction_is_the_entry_mid_spread_not_the_ask_gap`, `::test_the_caption_names_the_mid_spread` and `::test_the_section_names_its_axes`, `::TestKhatCards::test_the_caption_names_the_midpoint_basis`, `::TestKhatBreakdown::test_the_intro_names_the_midpoint_basis`, `::TestSectionIntervalDiscount::test_a_caption_says_the_implied_gap_is_the_mid_spread` (the caption under the interval-discount section's calibration table, in its place) and `tests/test_config.py::TestTimeSeriesProfitProb`; putting the ask gap back in the live forecast (at the pair's quotes or at each size's YES fill), in the backtest's gate, in `k̂` or in the spread calibration, forecasting the dashboard's scatter or scoring its spread calibration at the fills, reading `k̂` at a later qualifying Monday, clamping its spread at zero or leaving out a first Monday crossed on either market, dropping the earlier book's guard or the backtest's skip, dropping either new guard's float tolerance, swapping two quotes in a mid-spread call, dropping the INFO line, putting back any of the old calibration words (the log header, the caption, the x-axis title, the cards' caption, the breakdown's intro), or dropping, rewording or moving the interval-discount section's caption each fails them (checked one mutant at a time).

**Interval-discount calibration (2026-09 follow-up) — `k` is measurable now, but only inside the backtest.** `config.time_series_profit_prob(spread, k=None)` takes an optional per-call override, resolved at call time (`discount = TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT if k is None else k`, so a test that monkeypatches the constant still takes effect whenever `k` is omitted — mandatory, since a `k: float = TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT` default would bind at def time instead), and `backtest.py` exposes it as `--interval-discount K` (`parser.error`s at or below 0 or above 1; omitted/`None`, the default, reads the config constant) plus `--no-sweep` (skips only the extra per-`k` re-simulation grid — the calibration measurement below still runs and logs either way). The live sizer is untouched by construction, not by discipline: it prices at the run's `LiveSettings.interval_discount` (`strategy._kelly_p_at` hands it to `time_series_profit_prob` as `k`, which the AST pin requires of every live call), and a live run's `LiveSettings` comes from the saved live defaults (only `main.py`'s own `--interval-discount` replaces their `k`, for one live run); no live module imports a backtest module (the same pin), so live sizing reads the saved `k` (or that flag) no matter what any backtest run passes — calibration writes a recommendation into the backtest log and the dashboard, and neither the backtester nor the dashboard writes `config.py` or the saved live defaults. `backtester.run_backtest()` keeps its exact signature and `tuple[list[BacktestTrade], pd.DataFrame]` return by becoming a thin wrapper split at the `k` boundary, so every pre-existing caller of `run_backtest()` (all of `tests/test_backtester.py`, unchanged) still gets byte-identical behavior: `_prepare_entries(hist_client, live_client, start_date, use_cache, max_horizon_days, same_event_ladders=None, *, depth_model=None) -> tuple[list[dict] | None, OutcomeLabelCoverage | None]` is the `k`-INDEPENDENT prologue (the Monday-feasibility pre-check, `fetch_all_settled_markets`, the eligibility prefilter, both groupings, `_extract_pairs`, the parallel candlestick fetch, and the `_find_entry` sweep — none of it touches a probability model, so it runs exactly once no matter how many discounts get simulated), and `_simulate_at_discount(raw_entries, start_date, initial_balance, k=None, spread_band=None, population="all", *, tier_floors=True, size_cap=None, quiet=False, end_date=None, add_to_held=False, sell_at=None) -> SweepPoint` is the `k`-DEPENDENT tail (the Kelly gate, the outcome-validity and premise-violation checks, the one-pair-per-group dedup, `_drop_cross_type_duplicates`, chronological Pass 2, and `_build_equity_curve`; `population` only labels its completion line and stamps the point; `spread_band` and `tier_floors` must be the band and tier setting the entries were found under, because they go into the simulation's `LiveSettings`, and a walked book applies the live spread rule with them — they also label the line and stamp the point, an explicit `tier_floors=False` appending " with the tier floors off" to the band on that line, so the tier-off family never shares a prefix with the tier-on one), re-run once per swept discount over the SAME prepared entries. `_prepare_entries` is itself the composition of `_prepare_candidates` (everything through the candlestick fetch — band- and `k`-independent, returned as a `_Candidates` that carries the candles, the census and the start date / horizon / unresolved ladder flag every entry pass must reuse) and one `_entries_for_band` `_find_entry` pass at the default band; `run_backtest_sweep` does NOT call `_prepare_entries` — it runs `_prepare_candidates` once and then `_sweep_from_candidates`, which makes one `_entries_for_band` pass per spread band before any simulation. **The prologue's return is a PAIR, and the infeasibility sentinel lives on element 0** (DR-66b): a caller that forgets to unpack holds a 2-tuple, which is never `None`, so its `if raw_entries is None` guard silently goes false and an infeasible window walks into `_simulate_at_discount` holding a tuple. Element 1 is the run's `OutcomeLabelCoverage` census, carried out to the dashboard, and is `None` on exactly the feasibility-short-circuit path, where the fetch never ran — distinct from a censused corpus of zero records, which carries `total=0`. `run_backtest` unpacks and discards it (`raw_entries, _ = ...`), keeping its exact signature and two-tuple return. Each swept point of the primary band ANNOUNCES itself, and every simulation names its resolved `k` (and the band and population it simulated) on its `Backtest complete at k=..., band ..., <population>:` line, with `k` and each band bound printed exactly whenever the short form (`%.3f`, `%g`) would print two different values alike, so every prefix within one sweep is unique: that line is emitted once per swept discount — thirteen times on a run without the band sweep (`--no-band-sweep`, or `run_backtest_sweep`'s own `band_sweep=False` default), and once per band x `k` x population plus each split-half and ex-top run on a band sweep, which the CLI runs by default (with the tier-floors-off family, also on by default there, once more per binding band's cell and each of its checks, carrying " with the tier floors off" after the band) — and used to carry no `k` at all, while the primary's slot went unnumbered so the `Sweeping i/13` counter appeared to start at 2 (TS-21). A simulation at any per-trade size cap other than `BUDGET_FRACTION` names it BEFORE the colon, after the tier suffix and the population (`..., <population>, cap 35%:` / `..., no cap:`, through the injective `backtester._cap_label` — `k=..., band 0-1 with the tier floors off, all, cap 35%:` on a tier-off point at another cap), so the default-cap line is byte-identical to the pre-cap one; the lazy size-cap runs are `quiet=True`, which logs that line — and the premise-violation WARNING, text unchanged — at DEBUG, so a run's INFO log carries exactly the eager lines it did before (see the size-cap gotcha below). The premise-violation WARNING's PREFIX (up to "(earlier YES, later NO)") and its pinned substring "snapshot markets" are held across DR-72's rewrite; its full wording is no longer pinned byte-for-byte — DR-72 rewrote the CAUSES it names — and it is labelled by the lines around it. **`_build_equity_curve` opens every curve ONE DAY BEFORE `start_date` at the untouched initial balance**, so `run_backtest`'s `equity_df` runs from `start_date − 1 day` through today (UTC) and a future `start_date` yields exactly two rows, not one. That leading row is what makes a day-0 entry visible: the default window starts on a Monday (`--start-date 2024-01-01`), `_find_entry` can open a trade on `start_date` itself, and applying that day's charges to the FIRST row hid them from `pct_change` and `cummax` — the dashboard reported max drawdown 0.0% and Sortino 0.00 on a fixture that ended day one holding $1.84 of CASH out of its $10,000, while `_section_interval_discount`'s per-`k` row divided by the depleted opening (`iloc[0]`) and the performance card divided by `initial_balance`, reporting one run two ways on one page (DR-03). DR-61 later changed WHAT day 0 costs (see the mark-to-cost note below) without touching that guarantee — on the same fixture the day-0 step is now its $476.56 of taker fees rather than the whole $9,998.16 stake, and the leading row is still what keeps the `cummax` peak at $10,000 instead of at the already-charged $9,523.44 (measurably: dropping it shallows the reported drawdown from −75.3616% to −74.1287%). Don't re-anchor the curve on `start_date`: `dashboard.py` reads `equity_df` whole-column, by date, or by `iloc[0]`/`iloc[-1]` — never by positional index against a separately-built array — so the leading row needs no positional realignment anywhere (the order-dependent whole-column reads, `cummax` and `pct_change`, are exactly the ones it is meant to change), and `_section_benchmark` opens its `^GSPC` download on `start_date − 1 day` so the benchmark window starts on the same date as the strategy trace (yfinance serves trading days only, so its first bar can land later). Two things change in the return series, not one: it gains a leading `0.0`, and `start_date`'s own return stops being forced to `0.0`. **Every Sharpe/Sortino figure DR-03 and DR-56 recorded on this fixture is now HISTORICAL and does not reproduce** — DR-61 (next paragraph) replaced the cash-only series they were measured on, so the whole before/after log that stood here (`_sharpe` 1.53 → 1.52 and `_sortino` 0.00 → 2033.35 at a hardcoded `sqrt(252)`; their DR-56 rebases to ~1.83 and ~2447 at 365; the per-row-count tables at 40/60/79/100/109 rows) describes a curve whose settlement-day rebound no longer exists. Do not diff against those numbers; three mechanisms from them are still live and are the reason the log is summarized rather than deleted. (1) **The annualization base:** `_sharpe`/`_sortino` take a caller-named `periods_per_year` defaulting to `config.CALENDAR_DAYS_PER_YEAR` (365), because `_build_equity_curve` emits one row per CALENDAR day while the `^GSPC` benchmark row is a TRADING-day series and passes `config.TRADING_DAYS_PER_YEAR` (252) explicitly; at `rf = 0` the 252 → 365 change is an exact `sqrt(365/252)` = 1.2035 rescale of MAGNITUDE that leaves the sign alone (at `rf != 0` it is not a constant factor at all, since the per-period hurdle moves too). (2) **Both ratios drift with the CURRENT DATE** — the curve runs through `datetime.now(UTC)`, so the fixture gains a flat row every day — which is why **every re-measurement must be labelled with WHICH ratio it is and how many rows it covers**; an unlabelled `x/y` pair beside a sentence quoting a Sharpe AND a Sortino reads as sharpe/sortino and manufactures a false regression signal. (3) **A Sortino of exactly `0.00` is base-INDEPENDENT** (the pre-DR-03 series had no negative day at all, so `_sortino` takes its `dd > 0 else 0.0` early return at every base and every length — verified 2026-09-16 at bases 1/252/365/1000 and at 40/79/109/200 rows); a `0.00` is therefore a structural reading, never a rescale. **Re-measured on the current (DR-61) curve, 2026-09-16, 110 rows** (the curve runs to today UTC, which was already 2026-09-17 when this was measured — the row count, not the date, is the label that matters)**:** the DR-03 fixture is monotone declining — its only two non-flat days are the entry-day fees and the settlement write-off — so BOTH ratios are NEGATIVE, `sharpe@252` −1.6085065 / `sharpe@365` −1.9358378 and `sortino@252` −1.6075614 / `sortino@365` −1.9347004, with max drawdown −75.3616% on 2026-06-30 (equal, as it should be on a monotone decline, to the run's −75.4% total return). The cash-only curve read that same fixture, at the same 110 rows, as `sharpe@365` +1.8202136 and `sortino@365` +2435.9905 (`sharpe@252` +1.5124332, `sortino@252` +2024.0882) — a strongly positive risk-adjusted return on a run that lost three quarters of the balance, which is the sharpest single illustration of what DR-61 fixed. The ~+1.83 / ~+2447 pair the DR-56 log carried is the same reading one row shorter (109 rows); both drift daily, which is why neither may be quoted without its row count. `SweepPoint(k, trades, equity_df, spread_band=None, population="all", halves=None, ex_top_event=None, tier_floors=True, size_cap=None, peak_kelly_fraction=None, add_to_held=False, sell_at=None, cap_free_from=None)` stamps the RESOLVED discount (never `None`), the resolved band its entries were detected under, its population, whether they were detected with the tier floors applied (`tier_floors`, `False` only on a tier-off point), the resolved per-trade size cap, the largest uncapped Kelly fraction any candidate reached (cap-independent — see the size-cap gotcha below) and whether the simulation could add to held pairs (`add_to_held`, which `_ex_top_event` re-simulates at — see the backtest add-on paragraph) and the share of potential profit it sold positions at (`sell_at`, `None` for a run that never sells — see the selling gotcha) and the cap above which a walked book stops depending on the cap (`cap_free_from`, see the walked-book gotcha), but deliberately carries no `total_return`/`max_drawdown`/Sharpe of its own — `dashboard.py` imports FROM `backtester.py`, so the reverse would be circular, and the dashboard derives both per point from `equity_df` with the `_max_drawdown`/`_sharpe` helpers it already owns; the only returns it does carry, `halves` and `ex_top_event` (set on a band sweep's "all" and "time_series" points), belong to re-simulations whose curves are not kept. `backtest.py` itself was switched to call the richer entry point instead of `run_backtest`: `run_backtest_sweep(..., interval_discount=None, sweep=True, spread_band=None, band_sweep=False, tier_off_sweep=False, cap_sweep=False, add_on_sweep=False, sell_sweep=False, *, depth_model=None) -> BacktestSweep` runs one `_prepare_candidates` pass and one `_entries_for_band` time-series pass per spread band (one band unless `band_sweep`; on a band sweep only the no-band pre-pass scans every pair, and every other band rescans only the pairs that entered there) — plus, with `tier_off_sweep`, a second pass per binding band, rescanning from a tier-off pre-pass of its own — one `_interval_calibration` measurement per band (and, with `tier_off_sweep`, a second per binding band, labelled with the floor alone; see below — this report is produced ONLY through this entry point, never through the plain `run_backtest()` wrapper), simulates the primary `k` with the caller's override passed through verbatim (sentinel included, so a no-override run prices identically to `run_backtest()`), then — unless `sweep=False` — runs one more `_simulate_at_discount` per point of `sorted(set(config.INTERVAL_DISCOUNT_SWEEP) | {effective_k})`, so the primary is always an EXACT grid member (`--interval-discount 0.62` still gets its own entry in the dashboard filter bar's `k` select rather than the nearest standard point) and is the SAME `SweepPoint` object inside `BacktestSweep.points`, never a duplicate. `BacktestSweep(primary, points, calibration, label_coverage=None, scenarios=[], same_title_point=None, calibrations_by_band={}, same_event_ladders=None, split_date=None, corpus_provenance=None, config_same_event_ladders=None, tier_off_scenarios=[], tier_off_calibrations_by_band={}, cap_sweep=None, tier_off_cap_sweep=None, same_title_size_cap=None, live_tier_floors=None, live_spread_band=None, live_categories=None, live_tags=None, entry_checkpoint=None, live_origin=None, live_interval_discount=None, live_size_cap=None, live_same_title_size_cap=None, add_on_cap_sweep=None, add_on_tier_off_cap_sweep=None, live_add_to_held_pairs=None, live_sell_at=None, live_sell_min_days=None, sell_sweep=None, depth_model=None)` — `points`/`calibration` are always the PRIMARY band's; `scenarios`, `same_title_point`, `calibrations_by_band` and `split_date` are the band sweep's payload; `same_event_ladders` is the resolved ladder setting and `config_same_event_ladders` the configured switch it is judged against; `label_coverage` (below) and `corpus_provenance` (see the backtest cache-semantics gotcha) each record one fact about the corpus; `tier_off_scenarios` and `tier_off_calibrations_by_band` are the band sweep's tier-floors-off family, empty unless `tier_off_sweep`; `cap_sweep` is the lazy size-cap sweep (`None` unless asked for), `tier_off_cap_sweep` its twin over the tier-floors-off family (`None` unless both it and the family were asked for); `same_title_size_cap` is the extra same-title cap every simulation sized under (the backtester's binding of `config.SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP`, validated like the per-trade cap); `add_on_cap_sweep` and `add_on_tier_off_cap_sweep` are the dashboard's lazy add-on family (`None` unless `add_on_sweep` was asked for — see the size-cap gotcha); `sell_sweep` is its lazy Sell family (a `SellSweep`, `None` unless `sell_sweep` was asked for — see the selling gotcha); `depth_model` is the depth table the trades' synthetic books came from (`None`: every trade fills at the top of the book — see the walked-book gotcha); and the eleven `live_*` fields are the saved live defaults' time-series rule, category/tag filter, origin, `k`, caps, whether they add to held pairs and their sell level and minimum of days, from one read before the fetch — reporting only (see the scenario-explorer paragraph); `entry_checkpoint` is the label of the `SCHEDULED_RUN` the run's checkpoints came from (`ScheduledRun.label()`, e.g. `Monday 09:00 America/Los_Angeles` — see the entry-checkpoint gotcha). When `_prepare_candidates` hits its Monday-feasibility short-circuit, `run_backtest_sweep` still returns one empty point, `calibration=None`, `label_coverage=None`, `scenarios=[]`, `calibrations_by_band={}`, an empty tier-off family, `cap_sweep=None`, `tier_off_cap_sweep=None`, both add-on sweeps `None` and no Sell family, with the resolved ladder setting, the configured switch, the same-title cap, the depth model, the eleven `live_*` fields and the entry checkpoint still recorded, so no caller needs a special case for that path. `label_coverage` hangs off the sweep for the SAME stated reason `calibration` does — one measurement over one corpus, `k`-independent, valid at every point — and never off a `SweepPoint`, where thirteen copies of one measurement would invite thirteen disagreements. It is DEFAULTED so no hand-built construction breaks, which is the accepted residual: a caller that omits the keyword renders "not measured" on the page rather than the caveat, so the two production constructions — `run_backtest_sweep`'s infeasible branch and `_sweep_from_candidates` — must always pass it. The empirical measurement, `_interval_calibration(raw_entries, spread_min=None, *, tier_floors=True) -> IntervalCalibration | None` (`spread_min` only LABELS each gap band's tier with the band floor its entries were detected under, and `tier_floors=False` — a tier-off calibration's — labels it with that floor alone, so at a floor of 0 every bucket's tier is 0.0, which a renderer following the documented `tier <= 0` "no floor to print" sentinel would print as "-"/"—" like the pooled row; no tier-off calibration is logged — only the primary band's tier-on one is) (logged by `_log_interval_calibration` at INFO; since DR-72 `None` logs one explanatory line — "no time-series candidate entry with a readable settlement in this window — empirical k_hat is not measurable" — rather than nothing at all; the wording says "with a readable settlement" because the `None` branch also fires when time-series entries existed but every one was dropped for a non-binary outcome, though the sub-counts inside a non-`None` report, e.g. the excluded-premise-violations line, stay silent at zero), computes `k_hat = realised in-between rate / mean(mid spread)`, pooled and per deadline-gap band (`0-7d` / `8-15d` / `16-30d`, edged on `SHORT_DEADLINE_GAP_DAYS`/`MAX_DEADLINE_GAP_DAYS` so no band straddles a price tier) as `IntervalCalibrationBucket(label, tier, n, realised_rate, mean_implied, empirical_k)` rows, where `empirical_k` is `None` — not `0.0` — whenever `mean_implied <= 0`, since the ratio is undefined rather than zero. Each observation's `implied` is the pair's mid spread at its first qualifying Monday's four quotes, `config.time_series_mid_spread(pA, nA, pB, nB)` — the later market's midpoint minus the earlier one's, the very input the forecast multiplies by `k` (DR-78) — so `k̂` and `k` are fractions of one quantity; one pair's mid spread can be zero or negative only when its earlier market's quote is crossed (its YES ask below its own YES bid; on any other entry `_find_entry`'s fee check keeps the later book's width — its YES ask plus its NO ask, less 1 — under the YES-ask gap, which keeps the mid spread above half of it), and so can a bucket's mean, which then reports no `k̂`. **Every `k̂` recorded before DR-78 divided by the mean YES-ask gap `pB − pA` instead, and is not comparable with one measured since** — among them every `k̂` this file quotes from before it, such as the decision record's pooled 0.799 over 545 entries (0.45 / 0.996 / 0.994 by gap band), the entry-checkpoint A/B's 0.891 → 0.958 (0.884 → 0.927 with the tier floors off), DR-73's 0.87 on n=71 and the void 1.114, and every `k̂` in `config.TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS`' comment (DR-73's bands and the 365-day ladder run's 0.889), which says so. On the 2025-10-01 cumulative subset, run through the backtester's own calibration before and after DR-78 over the same entries (the DR-78 gotcha's Measured paragraph), `k̂` reads 0.008 to 0.022 higher on the mid spread; the analysis behind DR-78 put it about 0.04 higher on the 365-day data. The report also carries the population it pooled: `IntervalCalibration.observations`, one frozen `CalibrationObservation(gap_days, implied, in_between, event_ticker, category)` per measured candidate in measurement order (market A's event ticker as the entry carries it, and `infer_category` of it — the fallback `BacktestTrade.category` carries), so `_calibration_bucket(label, 0.0, observations)` on that very tuple reproduces the pooled row exactly (a regrouped, reordered or reassembled copy agrees only to float rounding) and a report can regroup the same population through the same arithmetic instead of re-deriving which entries count — the dashboard's `k̂` breakdown does, by Kalshi category, tag and spread band. `event_ticker`/`category` are REQUIRED (the DR-71 reasoning: a forgotten field would file an observation under a category no market has), while `observations` defaults to `()` so hand-built calibrations still build — a consumer tells "not carried" from "no candidate" by comparing `len(observations)` with `pooled.n`, never by testing `observations == ()`. It holds strings and scalars only, at entry scale, never a market record, so the per-band calibrations a sweep keeps pin no record alive (TS-07) — which is why it does not collide with DR-66b's rule that `OutcomeLabelCoverage` must carry no per-category breakdown: that rule is about a CORPUS-scale census holding record examples. Its population is deliberately `k`-INDEPENDENT: read straight off the prepared entries (`_prepare_entries()`'s, or one band's `_entries_for_band()` output inside a sweep), never off `_simulate_at_discount()`'s Kelly-surviving candidates, because filtering by the Kelly gate first would make the estimate circular — a wrong `k` would confirm itself by excluding exactly the candidates that would have disproven it; this is also why an `IntervalCalibration` is computed ONCE per spread band (the primary band's as `calibration`, every swept band's in `calibrations_by_band`) and hangs off `BacktestSweep` rather than off any single `SweepPoint` — it is valid for every `k` at that band. Two same-shape, differently-named premise-violation counts now exist and must never be conflated: `_simulate_at_discount`'s own local `premise_violations` (its WARNING level — now a `logging.log(...)` that drops to DEBUG only on a `quiet` size-cap re-run — and its statement order unchanged from before the split; DR-72 rewrote the wording's named causes, not its position in the pipeline) counts candidates AFTER the Kelly gate — load-bearing statement order, preserved exactly — so it is itself `k`-DEPENDENT, since a different `k` changes which candidates even reach the check; `IntervalCalibration.excluded_premise_violations`, logged separately by `_log_interval_calibration` at INFO, counts over the WHOLE `k`-independent population instead, so it is generally the LARGER of the two. Both exclude the same cell (earlier YES / later NO, impossible for a cumulative-deadline pair) from their own denominator and neither pays it; the distinct names exist precisely so a reader never mistakes one count for a bug in the other. The AST pin that used to assert `run_backtest` calls `time_series_profit_prob` directly is now a two-link chain, since the priced call moved into `_simulate_at_discount`: `tests/test_strategy.py::test_ast_backtester_prices_through_helper` asserts both `_function_calls(backtester, "_simulate_at_discount", "time_series_profit_prob")` and `_function_calls(backtester, "run_backtest", "_simulate_at_discount")` — same intent as the single-link pin it replaced (the backtester must price time-series through the shared config helper, never reimplement the formula), preserved across the extraction. `dashboard.py` gained a seventh section, `_section_interval_discount(sweep, kd=None, *, bar=True, kd_failed=False)` (the outcome-label coverage line or banner — see the next sentence — then KPI cards for the `k` selected / pooled empirical `k̂` / their delta ("k̂ − k (primary band, pooled)"), the bucket table, ONE equity-curve trace (`div_id="kd-equity"`) and a per-`k` trades/return/final balance/drawdown/Sharpe table (`<tbody id="kd-rows">`), all at the primary spread band). It used to carry a native Plotly `updatemenus` dropdown that switched the curve between one trace per swept `k` and drove that section alone; since the filter bar gained its `k` select (C3) the bar's `k` and size cap drive the section instead — see the `k̂`-cards sentence below. A later commit added `_section_scenario_explorer(sweep)` after it (the eighth section added, described in full at the end of this paragraph; the `k̂` breakdown now sits between the two), with band, `k` and size-cap `<select>`s of its own, and a Tier floors `<select>` when the run carries the band sweep's tier-floors-off family (all of which the filter bar moves too — see below) — so of the nine sections on the page today, the other SEVEN — six trade-derived ones (performance, decomposition, calibration, diagnostics, risk, benchmark) and the `k̂` breakdown — are RENDERED at the primary scenario, meaning the primary `k`, the primary spread band (`--spread-min`/`--spread-max`) AND the run's own per-trade size cap (`config.BUDGET_FRACTION`), with the deadline-gap tier floors on, as the run simulated it (the `k̂` breakdown is `k`- and cap-independent; the `k` is only its reference line); the scenario explorer's own selects move that section alone. **A third set of controls, the sticky page-wide filter bar (2026-09), re-scopes exactly those seven** (plus the header's trade count and the performance section's two `k̂` cards)**, moves the interval-discount section to its `k` and size cap (at the primary spread band, always with the tier floors on), and moves the scenario explorer's band, Tier floors, `k` and size-cap selects to its scenario — nothing else**: its Spread band, `k` and Size cap choices pick a SCENARIO — that band's OWN run at that `k` and per-trade size cap, a standalone simulation, so every figure is genuine: the band sweep's `population="all"` point at the run's own cap, or, for any other cap, the lazy `BacktestSweep.cap_sweep`'s point, simulated as the page is built (see the size-cap gotcha below) — and its Category and Tag choices show a SLICE of that run, filed by `historical.series_labels` (imported as `dashboard._series_labels`: Kalshi's category and the series' FIRST tag, the rule the Returns Decomposition already used, so every breakdown partitions — and the rule `main._filter_by_category` files a live pair by, so `--category C` (or saved live defaults whose `categories` are C) keeps exactly the Category slice C, and `--category C --tag T` the Tag slice "C · T" but for a first tag Kalshi spells in two casings — a `--tag` without `--category` spans every category; see the category/tag filter paragraph under Key Patterns), whose figures drawn from an equity curve — return, drawdown, Sharpe, Sortino, the median monthly return and the benchmark's strategy row — come from `backtester._build_equity_curve` over the slice's trades alone: the slice's contribution, not a standalone simulation, and the bar's summary line says so. Its Tier floors choice (#68, 2026-09-26), right after the Spread band select, switches every band — for the six trade sections, the header's trade count, the `k̂` chart and the `k̂` cards alike — between that run (on: the deadline-gap tier floors applied, as simulated) and, when the run carries the tier-floors-off family, the band's tier-off `population="all"` point at the chosen `k` with its tier-off calibration (off: the band's own floor alone — also a standalone simulation, filed into the grid by `dashboard._with_tier_off` under the run's own size cap, walked by `_walk_grid` after the tier-on cells through the visitors' opt-in `off` method, named by `dashboard._tier_off_where` with `backtester._band_label`'s bare "floor-ceiling" rather than `_row_label`'s `max(tier,…)`, the primary band's summary closing on its own `other_run` note); a band whose floor sits at or above both tiers (`backtester._tier_floors_bind` False) was never simulated again, so its off view IS its tier-on run — its row of the base block's `grid_off` is its `grid` row, the same chunks, relabelled, its summary saying the tiers never bind there. The family's eager points are its run's-own-cap view; at every other cap a binding band's off view is the lazy tier-floors-off `CapSweep`'s point (`BacktestSweep.tier_off_cap_sweep` — see the size-cap gotcha), which `_with_tier_off` reads through `_GridSource.off_cell` when the grid is a size-cap grid, so every tier x band x `k` x cap scenario on the page is a real simulation. A binding band's off view at another cap shows the `missing` template only when the page has no such sweep to read (a run or a hand-built sweep without one, or one the page set aside — its caps, `k`s, binding bands or primary cap not the grid's, or its entry events unreadable — with a WARNING and the header saying it could not be used), or one an off cell of which raised while the walk read it (see the next sentence), as does a binding band's cell the family lacks at some `k` (never from `run_backtest_sweep`, whose family names every binding band at every `k`). A tier-floors-off cap cell that raises while the walk reads it costs the off view's other caps only: one WARNING (with the traceback), every visitor's tier-off state dropped (`reset_off` — `_ChunkVisitor` drops the chunks and row heads the off cells packed, `_ExplorerVisitor` its off grid), and the off cells walked again from the family's eager points at the run's own cap (`_GridSource.off_fallback`), the tier-on cap axis kept and the header's run-settings line saying the tier-floors-off half of the size-cap sweep could not be used (`_run_settings_html`'s `tier_off_cap_sweep_unused`); a tier-ON cell that raises still costs the whole cap axis, as before. It fails CLOSED: with no family (a hand-built sweep, `tier_off_sweep=False`, or the CLI's `--no-band-sweep`), an unrecorded band, or a family missing a band the tiers bind at (`dashboard._tier_off_binds` returns `None`) there is no off view at all, never a relabelled tier-on run — the base block's `grid_off`, `bands_off` and `khat_off` are null — and the select stays disabled with "(not simulated for this run)" beside it (`flt-tier-note`; pinned by `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestTierOffGrid::test_no_off_view_unless_the_family_is_complete` and `::test_no_family_means_no_off_view_even_where_no_tier_binds`). Its options read "on (0.15 / 0.30 by deadline gap)" and "off (each band's own floor alone)" and its title spells both rules out — every number from `config` (`_TIER_OPTION_ON`, `_TIER_SELECT_TITLE`) — and the `k̂` chart grouped by spread band names the setting in its title (`_KHAT_TEXT`'s `khat_scope_tier_off`). The Category and Tag selects list one set of categories and tags for both settings — including any seen only in a tier-off run, offered at 0 with the tiers on — so their options keep their indices across a toggle. Every view is computed in Python by the helpers the sections render with (`_performance_kpis`, `_performance_series`, `_decomposition_aggregates`, `_category_table`, `_reliability`, `_best_and_worst`, `_kelly_points`, `_capital_deployed`, `_strategy_row`) — `dashboard._list_payload` per scenario trade list, inside ONE walk over the whole grid (`dashboard._grid_source` picks it: the size-cap sweep's bands x `k`s x caps when the sweep carries one, else the eager points at the run's own cap, else one "not recorded" scenario; `dashboard._walk_grid` reads it one (band, `k`) cell at a time, the primary cell and cap first, handing each scenario to visitors that keep only what they build, so only one cell's points are ever alive; a size-cap cell that cannot be simulated — or a size-cap sweep whose axes or entries' events cannot be read — costs the cap axis alone: one WARNING, the eager points walked instead, and the header's run-settings line saying the sweep could not be used, so the one-option Size cap select has its reason on the page; every point's curve is cut to the page's last date first, never modified in place) — and every list is its own gzip + base64 chunk (`<script id="dash-chunk-N">`, `_packed_json_script`) beside one base block (`id="dash-data"`, `dashboard._filter_payload`: the axes, a `[band][k][cap]` grid of chunk ids, the labels, templates and styles, the `k̂` breakdown per band, and the shared trade-row heads — and, with a tier-floors-off family, `tier_binds`, `grid_off` in the grid's shape, `bands_off` and `khat_off`). Scenarios whose trade lists are equal at an equal `k` — a tier-off one included — share one chunk (`dashboard._list_key` — `k` is in the key because the Kelly scatter is priced at it; every cap at or above a cell's sharing floor, `backtester._sharing_floor`, trades the same list), curves ship as change points only, and a best/worst row ships as its head (`_trade_row_head`, each distinct head once per page, in the base block; it shows what each leg paid, so over a walked book it can differ by size) and its tail (`_trade_row_tail`: `n`, cost, profit), which the script joins, so `_trade_row` — and the diagnostics section — stay byte-identical. Measured 2026-09-26, after the `k̂` cards and the interval-discount section's data joined the base block (C3): `TestFilterPageSize`'s fixture (36 bands, each a different 40-trade list) was an 819 KB page of 36 chunks (812 KB the same day before them; 745 KB as the one block it was before the chunks, since each list compressed alone forgoes what one shared block compressed across lists), and a synthetic 36-band x 13-`k` x 20-cap grid whose cells share 4 lists packs 52 chunks and a 56 KB base block — 707 KB of JSON once inflated, 254 KB of it the section's `"kd"` and 216 KB the `k̂` breakdown with its `k̂ − k` per `k` — into a 1.23 MB page in 5.0 s — a pre-C4 measurement, before the scenario explorer's blocks joined the walk; the explorer's own figures (a 1.21 MB page in 6.7 s on a band-sweep version of this grid, and `TestFilterPageSize` at 804 KB before the merge of #68) are in the scenario-explorer paragraph below (on the same harness before C3: a 35 KB base block of 320 KB JSON, and a 1.28 MB page, since the section then drew a trace per `k`). Re-measured at the merge of #68 (same day, same environment), with the explorer's own Tier floors view: `TestFilterPageSize`'s page is 815,777 bytes without a family and 1,098,598 with #68's worst case, the 18 bands a tier binds at each trading yet another list (54 chunks, the base block carrying the off grid, the off band names and the off `k̂` breakdown); main measured #68's single-block page on a synthetic 36-band run whose bands each traded a different 200-trade list over ~2,460 days (a 300-entry `k̂` population per band) at 11.7 MB of compact JSON, 2.19 MB packed and a 3.02 MB page, and with a tier-floors-off family whose 18 binding bands each traded yet another such list (54 lists, main's worst case) at 17.6 MB, 3.30 MB packed and a 4.18 MB page; and on the DR-73 calibration corpus's own band sweep (start 2020-01-01, ladders on) at a block of 0.45 → 0.67 MB and a page of 4,071,051 → 5,489,336 bytes, 1.19 MB of it the explorer's unpacked tier-off view — figures for main's single-block, unpacked-explorer design, not re-measured on this one (main's CLAUDE.md and `_packed_json_script` docstring at 2bfe939 keep the full record; this tree's docstring keeps only the 4,071,051 → 5,489,336 page and the 1.19 MB). The page is streamed to disk piece by piece through one open handle (every chunk, then the base block, then the script) and renamed over the old one, never joined into one string. `_FILTER_JS` computes no figure and words nothing about the data itself (its one sentence of its own is the line it shows when the base block cannot be loaded) — the summary line is `dashboard._SUMMARY_TEMPLATES`, filled by `_filter_summary_text` for the rendered view and by the script for every other, and the `k̂` chart's words are `_KHAT_TEXT`, used the same way — and draws each trace by copying the one Python drew and swapping its data (the trade-type lines, which another band may hold where the primary holds none, from the styles Python drew them with). On load it inflates the base block and the primary scenario's chunk with the browser's `DecompressionStream`, sets the nine selects back to the rendered view (a browser can restore a stale choice on reload; they are also rendered `autocomplete="off"`) and only then enables them (the Tier floors select only when the base block carries a tier-off view, `grid_off`, the Add to held pairs select only when it carries an add-on view, `grid_add`, the Sell select only when it names the Sell blocks, `sell_blocks`, and the Min. days to maturity select only while Sell names a level) — Python renders them disabled, so a category or tag change never meets an unloaded chunk — and it redraws nothing until a `<select>` changes: the page as rendered IS the primary scenario's unfiltered view with the tier floors on. Any other scenario's chunk is inflated when first chosen — read from the page, or, for an id at or past the base block's `inline_chunks` (a Sell level's), loaded from its sidecar file — (a `loading` line meanwhile) and kept while among the last 16 drawn (`KEEP`; the primary's always — a chunk that arrives after the reader has chosen another scenario was never drawn, so it is dropped rather than counted, and the chunk on screen is never pushed out); a choice made while a chunk is still loading supersedes it (`SEQ` — a chunk arriving after a later choice is never drawn); a scenario the run never simulated shows the empty view and says so (the `missing` template); and a chunk that cannot be loaded is named on the summary line (`unavailable`), every select — the category and tag too, since one chosen while it loaded was never drawn either, and the Tier floors select, whose band labels go back with it — goes back to what is still shown (`SHOWN`), and choosing it again retries it. The `k̂` chart reads `SHOWN`, not the selects, so a Group-by change while a chunk is loading describes the sections on screen. Each redraw starts from the chart's layout as Python drew it, captured on load, so a reader's zoom, hidden legend line or box selection never carries into another view; `sizeTo` writes a row-dependent height on both the chart's box and the one around it, since plotly.py puts it on one or the other by version. `generate_dashboard` resolves ONE `k` for the page as rendered — the `interval_discount` override, else the sweep's primary `k` — for the Risk section's Kelly scatter, the primary scenario's chunk, the summary line and the `k̂` chart's reference line (with neither, the scatter prices at the config constant, the summary says "k not recorded" and the `k̂` chart draws no line); with a sweep the bar's `k` axis is the sweep's own, its primary entry `sweep.primary.k`, so an override that differs from it (never in production — `backtest.py` passes `result.primary.k`) is unsupported: the scatter, the primary chunk and the `k̂` line as rendered price at the override while the summary names the sweep's `k`, and a WARNING says so (the performance section's `k̂ − k` card is priced at the sweep's `k`, like the interval-discount section's and the bar's); a chosen scenario's chunk is priced at its own `k`, and the `k̂` chart's line moves to it (drawn from the base block's `khat_ref` line, so the script's line and Python's cannot differ). A filter that cannot be built — the base block (`_filter_payload`, the seam the tests break), or any chunk (the chunk visitor catches its own failure) — costs the bar, never the page: it is written without the bar, its chunks and its script, with `dashboard._FILTER_UNAVAILABLE_HTML` in the bar's place and a WARNING in the log. `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestFilterScript` and `::TestFilterKAndCap` run the script itself over the rendered page under `tests/js/filter_harness.js` (node in CI, macOS's JavaScriptCore `jsc` locally, skipped when neither is present; some in its strict mode, where a missing element is null as in a browser), and `::TestScenarioExplorerScript` and `::TestExplorerCapAndMetrics` run the scenario explorer's script before it, in the page's order. The `k̂` breakdown (`dashboard._section_khat`, the section right after this one) is the one of the seven that does not describe trades: it regroups each band's carried `IntervalCalibration.observations` through `backtester._calibration_bucket`, filed by `_series_labels` on market A's event ticker (the leg `BacktestTrade.event_ticker` records), by Kalshi category, by tag or by spread band — a `Group by` `<select>` of its own, outside the chart's body so it stays reachable when a view has nothing to draw, chooses the axis while the bar chooses the band, its tier floors setting (with the tiers off, a binding band's tier-off calibration) and the category or tag, and grouping by one of them shows every value of it with the selection highlighted. Each bar names its entries and its distinct events (a ladder's rungs share one event; one question listed as several events still counts each), a dashed line marks the page's `k` (the run's own as rendered, the bar's `k` once one is chosen), and its cells, bar labels and hover figures are pre-formatted in Python (`_khat_stat`), so neither the script nor Plotly's hover can round a figure differently from the table. A band with no calibration reads "not recorded", never "none measured". Of the interval-discount section the bar reaches the `k` and size cap only, always at the primary spread band and with the tier floors on (its band, Tier floors choice, Add to held pairs choice, category and tag never reach it); of the scenario explorer, its band, Tier floors choice, `k` and size cap (C4, and the merge of #68) — the explorer's own script defines `window.dashScenarioSelect(band, k, cap, tier)`, which `_FILTER_JS` calls after every redraw, only when it is a function (a page without the explorer's grid has none), with the option labels of the scenario ON SCREEN (`SHOWN`, never the live selects), matched by label (`_band_option` / `_k_option` / `_cap_option` name both grids; the tier by "on" / "off", read only when the explorer renders a Tier floors select): an axis moves only when the bar's label on it changed since the bar's last call (the first call is measured against the run's primary scenario, which the bar shows on load), so a category or tag redraw — or a bar change on another axis — never undoes a choice the reader made in the explorer itself; a label the explorer lacks leaves that select as it is, and a call made before the explorer's data is unpacked is applied once it is; an axis the explorer REFUSED — a size cap with the tier floors off that the page holds no tier-off block for (every cap but the run's own, when the page has no tier-floors-off size-cap sweep to read — see the filter-bar paragraph), or a block it could not unpack (a cap's tier-on block, or its tier-off block), both named on its status line with every select put back — is marked pending (`PENDING`) and applied again on the bar's next change to any axis, so a refusal never leaves the explorer silently on another scenario than the bar's, while a bar call that changes no axis (a category or tag change) re-attempts nothing; the explorer's selects stay usable on their own and never move the bar. The summary line says what the bar reaches (`dashboard._bar_reach`). **The bar's "Save as live defaults…" button opens the defaults server's confirmation page for the bar's scenario on screen (`SHOWN`) — never the Scenario Explorer's own selects, and never the selects' choice while a chunk loads.** It sits after the Tag select (`_filter_bar_html`), rendered disabled, and its label, hover text and grey note are Python's (`dashboard._SAVE_LABEL`; `_SAVE_TITLE`, plus `_SAVE_TITLE_UNFILED` on a page filed by ticker prefix; `_SAVE_NOTE`, "(needs ./start_dashboard.sh running)", after the "Trade using defaults…" link that follows the button — `_TRADE_LABEL`/`_TRADE_TITLE`, a plain link to `/trade` in a new tab that the script never touches), so `_FILTER_JS` still words nothing about the data. A click calls `window.open(url, '_blank', 'noopener')` on `http://<config.DEFAULTS_SERVER_HOST>:<DEFAULTS_SERVER_PORT>/confirm?…` (the host and port read from config when the page is built, never spelled in `dashboard.py`), its query built with `encodeURIComponent` alone (no `URL`, which macOS's `jsc` lacks): `tier_floors` (the Tier floors choice on screen), `spread_min`/`spread_max` (the band's bounds, each band entry's `value` in the base block), `k` and `size_cap` (the axes' `value`s, each written by `String()`, whose shortest form reads back as the same float), `add_to_held_pairs` (`on` or `off`, the Add to held pairs choice on screen — sent only from a page whose base block carries the add-on view, so from any other the server keeps the saved value, or the seed's when none is saved), `same_title_size_cap` (the run's `BacktestSweep.same_title_size_cap`; left out when not recorded, so the server keeps the saved same-title cap, or the seed's (`config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_SEED`) when none is saved), `category` — and `tag`, only with its category — for a slice, and `source`; the add-to-held choice is sent only from a page that shows it, and otherwise left out, so the server keeps the saved choice (the seed's, with none saved). The base block's `save` (`dashboard._save_target`) carries the address, that cap, `filed_by_listing` (whether the page files trades by Kalshi's series listing, the rule the live category and tag filter reads) and the source note, `backtest dashboard for <start> to <today>` plus ` (same-event ladders on|off, config.py on|off: its pairs are not the live bot's)` when the run's recorded ladder setting departs from the recorded switch, checked against `config.LIVE_DEFAULTS_SOURCE_PATTERN` (whole, ASCII digits, as the server checks it) and left out should it ever not match. The script (`saveHref`/`refreshSave`) enables the button only while the scenario on screen can become live settings — drawn, simulated, its band recorded, its `k` and size cap recorded and above zero, and a category or tag only on a page filed by the listing — disables it while another scenario's chunk loads and when the base block or the primary chunk cannot be loaded, and re-enables it for the scenario still shown when a chunk fails; anything else `LiveSettings` refuses (a same-title cap off the 5% grid, say) is refused by the server's `_proposal`, with its reason. The page writes nothing: the confirmation page saves only on its own Confirm and save (or Confirm and trade, which saves before it runs; see the `defaults_server.py` Module Map row), and a page whose filter bar could not be built carries no save button — only the trade link, under the notice in the bar's place, whose id (`flt-trade`) sits in the page's opening part, where the defaults server's start-up check reads (`tests/test_dashboard.py::TestTradeUsingDefaultsLink`). Pinned by `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestSaveLiveDefaultsButton`, whose round-trip test hands the clicked address to `defaults_server._App.handle` (200, with and without saved defaults) and to `defaults_server._proposal`, and compares the `LiveSettings` it reads, field by field, with the scenario's own values from the base block. **The `k̂` cards and the interval-discount section follow the bar (C3).** Portfolio Performance carries two more cards, "Empirical k̂" and "k̂ − k (this selection)" (`dashboard._khat_kpis`, appended with a caption through `_section_performance(..., extra_kpis=)` — the call without it is byte-identical, which the golden pins): the `k̂` of the band and category or tag shown, and it minus the `k` shown, through `dashboard._khat_delta`, the one definition of that figure (red when positive — the sizer sized too big — green otherwise, "—" in the default colour when either value is unknown; the difference is rounded to the three decimals shown before its sign and colour are read, so it never prints "-0.000" or a red "+0.000"). As rendered they come from the primary calibration itself (`_calibration_khat_stat(_primary_calibration(sweep))`, against `sweep.primary.k` — the `k` the bar shows for the primary scenario, never the `interval_discount` override — so the card as rendered, the script's redraw of the same view and the interval-discount section's own `k̂ − k` card are one figure even on an unsupported override), never from the filter's data, so they render even when the bar cannot be built; the script (`renderKhatCards`) rewrites them from the `k̂` breakdown's own group for the selection (the tier-off breakdown, `khat_off`, with the tier floors off) — the category, or the tag when one is chosen — each group carrying its `k̂ − k` per `k` (`_khat_band(..., ks=)`), so a card is the `k̂` chart's highlighted bar whenever that bar is the selected group: grouped by tag or by spread band always, grouped by category whenever no tag is chosen (with a tag chosen, grouping by category highlights the tag's category, which the cards do not show). On a tainted corpus the `k̂` card's label is suffixed " (see caveat in Interval Discount)" and recoloured. The interval-discount section's data at every `k` and cap of the primary band comes from the SAME walk (`dashboard._KdVisitor`, a third visitor, so a size-cap sweep's cells are still simulated once), shipped as the base block's `"kd"`; the script (`renderKd`) redraws the curve and its title, rewrites the "k selected" and "k̂ − k (primary band, pooled)" cards and rebuilds the table at that cap, the `k` shown in bold ("(primary)" still marks the run's own). Both renderers read `SHOWN`, so they describe the scenario on screen (a chunk that fails to load leaves them as drawn), and both return at once on a page without their elements or data (tested under the harness's strict mode). A section that cannot follow the bar renders statically from `sweep.points` at the run's own cap, with a grey line saying why — the bar could not be built (`bar=False`) or the section's own data could not be (`kd_failed`, one WARNING, costing nothing else); a hand-built sweep that records no band follows the bar at its one "not recorded" band, which is its primary, and with no sweep the section is its placeholder.
