The summary line's closing sentence never claims a reach the page lacks: `dashboard._bar_reach(kd_follows, explorer_follows, *, explorer_caps=True, explorer_tiers=True)` picks one of ten — `_BAR_REACH` (both the interval-discount section and the explorer follow the bar), `_BAR_REACH_OWN_CAP` (both, but the explorer only by band and `k`: its data was rebuilt from the sweep's own eager points at the run's own cap while the bar offers more caps), `_BAR_REACH_NO_EXPLORER` (the page carries no explorer grid: no band sweep, or its data could not be built at all), `_BAR_REACH_EXPLORER_ONLY` and `_BAR_REACH_EXPLORER_ONLY_OWN_CAP` (the base block carries no `"kd"`) or `_BAR_REACH_STATIC` (neither) — or, when the explorer renders no Tier floors select of its own (every page whose run carries no complete tier-floors-off family), one of the four `_BAR_REACH_NO_EXPLORER_TIERS` sentences, which say the explorer does not follow the Tier floors choice; every one of the ten says the Interval Discount section never follows it (pinned by `TestFilterSummary::test_every_reach_says_the_interval_discount_ignores_the_tier_choice`), and on a page with an Add to held pairs view each ends with one more sentence, `_ADD_ON_REACH` (`_ADD_ON_REACH_NO_EXPLORER` where the page has no explorer grid), saying that choice changes the trade sections only, that the `k̂` figures do not depend on it and that the explorer and the interval-discount section always show it off (the interval-discount section alone where the page has no explorer grid) — chosen in `_filter_payload` (its keyword-only `explorer`, which `generate_dashboard` sets only when the explorer's data was built with the band sweep's populations, `explorer_caps`, set only when the explorer's cap labels are the walked grid's, and `explorer_tiers`, set only when the explorer's data offers a Tier floors select), so Python's render and the script's fill say the same. It takes the sweep WHOLE rather than its parts precisely so its inputs cannot drift, which is why carrying the census through `BacktestSweep.label_coverage` needed no signature change; `dashboard._label_coverage_html(coverage)` renders it in three distinguishable states — `None` ⇒ "not measured for this run" (an older caller, a hand-built sweep, or the feasibility short-circuit), `total == 0` ⇒ "no eligible markets to census" (the fraction is UNDEFINED there, never `0%`), otherwise this run's own counts and percentages. **A healthy run renders the figure too, not just a silence:** absence of a warning must never be the only signal, since that is indistinguishable from the feature not existing — the exact failure DR-66 closed on the log side. Below the floor it renders a red banner ABOVE the KPI cards, and the `Pooled empirical k̂` card is recoloured and its label SUFFIXED (`… (see caveat above)` — suffixed, never replaced, since `tests/test_dashboard.py` substring-matches that label), so the caveat travels with the number even in a screenshot of the cards alone. The verdict is the CARRIED `below_floor` flag, never re-derived: the log's WARNING and the page's banner must fire on the same condition, and `BACKTEST_OUTCOME_LABEL_WARN_FRACTION` is imported into `dashboard.py` only to PRINT where the floor sits. `generate_dashboard` additionally emits a one-line header notice under the "Period:" line on the same verdict, reading the `sweep` it already receives (no new parameter), because a strike-blind corpus changes WHICH PAIRS EXIST and therefore taints all nine sections, not only the one that renders the census. `_kelly_fraction` gained the same optional `k` (fixing a real latent bug: it previously always priced the config constant, so an `--interval-discount` run's Risk-section Kelly-vs-actual scatter would have silently plotted the wrong model against override-sized trades), threaded through `_section_risk(..., k=...)` (since the page-wide filter, the one `k` `generate_dashboard` resolves for the whole page — see the filter-bar sentence above); `generate_dashboard()` gained `sweep`/`interval_discount` as keyword-ONLY params with defaults, so its pre-existing 4-positional-argument call still works verbatim. **The scenario explorer (2026-09 follow-up on the follow-up) is the backtest-only band × `k` × population sweep this section's `spread_band`/`population` fields were built for.** `run_backtest_sweep(..., spread_band=None, band_sweep=False, tier_off_sweep=False)` resolves and validates the PRIMARY band — and refuses `tier_off_sweep` without `band_sweep` — before anything is fetched or logged (`config.time_series_spread_band`), then composes `_prepare_candidates()` (fetch through candlesticks — depends on neither the band nor `k`) with `_sweep_from_candidates()`: one time-series `_entries_for_band()` `_find_entry` pass per band (one band unless `band_sweep`) plus one same-title pass, one `_interval_calibration(spread_min=band[0])` per band (`BacktestSweep.calibrations_by_band`, labelled with that band's own floor), and — per band × swept `k` — a standalone `population="all"` simulation plus, when `band_sweep`, standalone `"time_series"`/`"ladder"`/`"cross"` simulations for every non-empty population, a split-half out-of-sample check on the "all" and "time_series" points (`HalfSplit`, split at `BacktestSweep.split_date`, each half simulated alone from the initial balance) and, on every "all" and "time_series" point that traded at least one event, a re-simulation excluding the single most P&L-concentrated event's entries (`SweepPoint.ex_top_event`, `None` on a point with no traded event) — collected as `BacktestSweep.scenarios`, alongside the band/`k`-independent `same_title_point` (simulated once) and the resolved `same_event_ladders`. **On a band sweep only ONE time-series pass per tier setting scans every pair** (2026-09-23; the tier-floors-off family below runs its own, with the tiers off): the no-band band `(0.0, 1.0)` is scanned first, and every other band rescans only the pairs that produced an entry there, through `_entries_for_band`'s private keyword-only `_pairs` (a new list; `candidates.all_pairs` is never mutated). That changes no band's entries, because every band's accepted Mondays are a subset of the no-band band's — the floor only rises (`max(tier, floor) >= tier`), the leg-price-sum ceiling `1 − threshold` therefore only falls, the spread ceiling only drops, and nothing else `_find_entry` tests reads the band — pinned against a full scan per band by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestBandSweepPhaseOneSubset` — including on its own fixture, a pair `_find_entry` hands back with its legs SWAPPED (so the pre-pass must match entered pairs on an UNORDERED ticker pair) and a 16–30-day pair at a 0.25 spread that only a band floor loosening the 0.30 tier could admit — and checked on the DR-73 calibration corpus (all 36 bands identical to a full scan, 330 of its 10,733 pairs rescanned). A single-band run keeps its one full scan. **The populations, and which one the explorer reads.** "All" is every entry at the band, same-title included (`entries_by_band[band] = ts_entries + st_entries` — the run's actual result); "time_series" is every time-series entry at the band — ladders and cross-event together, same-title excluded, simulated alone — and is the population the dashboard's heatmap, fragility banner and equity curve read, because the band and `k` act on time-series pairs only and a same-title result (band- and `k`-independent) would otherwise dilute the comparison; the DR-73 calibration evidence the grid is read against was time-series only (its "All" row was 330 = 299 ladder + 31 cross-event entries). On a corpus with no same-title pair the two are identical cell for cell (measured on the calibration corpus: all 468 time-series cells equal their "all" cells). "Ladders" and "Cross-event" split the time-series entries further, and "Same-title" is simulated once, independent of band, `k` and the tier floors. **The split date is the median_low of the primary band's TIME-SERIES entry dates** (same-title entries left out, so they cannot move where the time-series halves split); because median_low is one of the dates, H1 (strictly before it) is EMPTY whenever AT LEAST half of those entries share the earliest date (one of two is enough) — and, because that ONE date serves every cell, any other band's cell or an "all" point carrying same-title entries has an empty half whenever all of its own entries fall on one side of it (unwarned, but blanked on the page like the rest) — and an empty half's `0.0` return is not a measurement — so `HalfSplit` carries `h1_entries`/`h2_entries` (appended with a `None` default, meaning "not recorded" on a hand-built instance), `_sweep_from_candidates` logs a WARNING naming the split date when it leaves a half of the primary band's time-series entries empty ("split date X leaves H1 empty; the split-half check is not measurable for this window"), and the dashboard renders such a half's return as "—" and leaves the cell out of its split-half Spearman. **`band_sweep` defaults to `False` in `run_backtest_sweep()` itself, but `backtest.py`'s CLI turns it ON by default** (`band_sweep = not args.no_band_sweep`, so `--no-band-sweep` is the opt-out) — a hand-built caller gets the pre-sweep single-band behavior unless it asks for more, while the shipped CLI always computes the explorer's grid unless told not to. **The tier-floors-off family (2026-09-26) rides the band sweep the same way.** `run_backtest_sweep(..., tier_off_sweep=False)` is opt-in in the function (a `ValueError` before anything is logged or fetched when it is set without `band_sweep`, since its grid, split date and rescans are the band sweep's), and `backtest.py` passes `tier_off_sweep=band_sweep`, so the CLI computes it by default and `--no-band-sweep` skips it with the grid — there is no flag of its own. With it, `_sweep_from_candidates` enters and simulates again, with the deadline-gap tier floors NOT applied (`_find_entry(..., tier_floors=False)`, i.e. `min_price_diff_for_gap(..., tier_floors=False)`), every band where a tier binds — `backtester._tier_floors_bind`, one definition computed through that helper at every gap `_find_entry` admits: a floor below a tier, on the shipped grid floors 0, 0.20 and 0.25, 18 of the 36 bands. "Off" means the band's own floor is the only floor: `pB − pA ≥ floor` and `pA + nB ≤ 1 − floor` (the sum ceiling stays tied to the floor, as it is for bands), with the spread still strictly positive (a Monday with `pB − pA ≤ PRICE_EPSILON` is refused whatever the tiers — inert with them on); the 30-day gap cap, the band ceiling, the live-quote and fee checks, the Kelly gate, ladders, same-title pairs and the primary scenario do not move. At floor 0 both tiers go; at floors 0.20 and 0.25 only 16–30-day pairs change; a band at or above both tiers enters the same pairs on the same Mondays either way, so it is not re-run: its tier-on cells ARE its tier-off cells. Phase 1 gains its own no-band pre-pass at (0, 1) with the tiers off (the subset argument holds unchanged: the threshold is the floor alone, still only rising from 0.0) and 17 rescans on the shipped grid of what entered THERE, all BEFORE the candles are released — never the tier-on pre-pass's list, since a pair the tiers refuse at every band can still enter with them off (pinned on its own fixture, where the 0.30 tier refuses one pair everywhere, by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestBandSweepPhaseOneSubset::test_the_tier_off_rescan_comes_from_its_own_pre_pass`, which the golden fixture cannot do: its entered pairs are the same either way); Phase 2, after the same-title point, gives each binding band a tier-off calibration labelled with the floor alone and, at every `k` of the SAME grid, the same scenario block as the tier-on sweep — `_band_sweep_cell`, extracted from the tier-on loop together with `_entered_pairs` and `_band_populations` as a pure move (same object-mutation, log and `scenarios` order) — with halves split at the ONE `split_date`. The results ride on `BacktestSweep.tier_off_scenarios` (same order and shape as `scenarios`, every point stamped `tier_floors=False`) and `tier_off_calibrations_by_band` (keyed by exactly the binding bands). The family's eager points are simulated at the run's own per-trade size cap and never seed the tier-on `CapSweep` (pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestCapSweepNeverSeedsTierOff::test_a_tier_off_point_is_never_a_cap_sweep_seed`); with `cap_sweep` too they seed a second, tier-floors-off `CapSweep` of their own (`BacktestSweep.tier_off_cap_sweep`), which covers the family at every other cap — see the size-cap gotcha. Every tier-on figure — primary, points, scenarios, every calibration, `same_title_point`, `split_date` — is exactly what the run returns without the family (pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestTierOffSweep`, whose golden fixture's ladder, a 19-day stated gap at a Monday-1 spread of 0.25 and `pA + nB` = 0.75, enters on 2026-01-05 with the tiers off instead of 2026-01-12, and measured on the DR-73 calibration corpus against the pre-change code — see `SPREAD_BAND_SWEEP_FLOORS`' comment for its measured cost). What it admits, measured 2026-09-26 on the DR-73 calibration corpus (start 2020-01-01, ladders on) at the no-band band `(0, 1)`: 608 entries with the tiers off against 330 with them on — 278 pairs that enter only with the tiers off (222 at a spread below their tier, 56 at or above it that only the sum ceiling of 1, rather than `1 −` the tier, admits), and 74 of the pairs both settings enter doing so earlier; at their entry prices, 47 of those 278 clear the Kelly gate at `k` = 0.75 and none reaches the 20% `BUDGET_FRACTION` cap then shipped, against 160 at `k` = 0.40, 129 of them at that cap — the model's own extrapolation below the tier thresholds live trading applied until the 2026-09-27 decision turned `config.TIME_SERIES_TIER_FLOORS` off (`main.py --tier-floors` restores them for one live run). Its announcement lines begin "Tier floors off", never "Spread band " or "Split-half check: split date", which tests count; its completion lines carry " with the tier floors off" after the band, and a premise-violation WARNING from one of its simulations ends " [tier floors off]" (the tier-on text unchanged). It changes nothing the live bot does, like the band itself. `dashboard._section_scenario_explorer(sweep)` is the section this grid feeds (6th of nine on the page, see above), and every population on it is labelled from one `dashboard._POPULATION_LABELS` map: a fragility banner (the cells computed, and, for the time-series population only, the share with a positive return and the split-half Spearman rank correlation of H1-vs-H2 cell returns over the cells whose two halves both had entries — the point of the banner is that the best of that many correlated cells overstates what a reader should expect, "that many" counting the time-series cells the heatmap shows a value in, never the whole grid), a band (row) × `k` (column) heatmap of the time-series population at the size cap shown (div `scn-heatmap`), its title naming that population and the cap, with a scripted `<select id="scn-metric">` over nine metrics (`dashboard._EXPLORER_METRICS`: mean per trade — the default — median per trade, total return, Sharpe ratio (365-day base; blank on a cell with no trade, in the matrix only — `_point_kpis` and the KPI table keep its 0.0), H1 return, H2 return, trade count, empirical `k̂`, which repeats each band's pooled `k̂` from `calibrations_by_band` across every `k` column because it is measured per band and cannot depend on `k` or the cap, and `k̂ − k`, that pooled `k̂` minus the column's `k`, rounded as the cards round it (`dashboard._khat_delta_value`, the one arithmetic `_khat_delta` formats) and coloured as they are — positive, the sizer sized too big, red: `go.Heatmap(colorscale="RdBu_r").colorscale` — which the empirical `k̂` metric uses too, centred on the run's `k`, so a band whose `k̂` sits above `k` is red in both views, while the return and Sharpe metrics use plotly.py's "RdBu", red low and blue high (the heatmap's negative side is blue where a card is green); the same per-band figures also render as a one-row-per-band table under the heatmap), each metric change one `Plotly.update` of z, colour scale, centre, hover, hover data and title together (a native `updatemenus` dropdown bakes one matrix per metric into the figure and could not follow the size cap), three `<select>`s (band, `k`, size cap — `scn-band-select`, `scn-k-select`, `scn-cap-select`; four, with a Tier floors `scn-tier-select` after the band's, when the grid carries a tier-floors-off view — see below — rendered disabled with `autocomplete="off"` until the script has unpacked its data, preselected to the primary) driving a per-population KPI table (mean AND median return per trade beside win rate and total return; Time-series, with an H1/H2-and-top-event sub-row; Ladders; Cross-event; All — time-series + same-title — with its own sub-row; Same-title, independent of band, `k` and the tier floors — so it reads the same under both Tier floors settings — but not of the cap) and the selected band's own calibration table, and the time-series equity curve rendered once and redrawn in place — a `Plotly.update` that autoranges both axes and titles it for the Tier floors setting shown, so a reader's zoom on one cell's curve never carries into another's — by a small inline script (`_SCENARIO_EXPLORER_JS`) — hand-scripted rather than Plotly-native because a native dropdown cannot express independent axes of selection. **Its data comes from the page's ONE walk (C4):** `dashboard._ExplorerVisitor` rides `_walk_grid` beside the filter bar's visitors, so a size-cap sweep's cells are simulated once for the whole page (the walk also reads the same-title population once, at every cap, for the visitor that shows it — the explorer's same-title row; if that simulation raises, only the same-title rows at the other caps are lost — `dashboard._same_title_points` logs one WARNING and hands on the run's own point at its own cap — never the cells already walked or the bar's cap axis); every figure is computed on curves cut to the page's last date (`_cut_to_axis`), as the filter bar's are, so a cell simulated on a later UTC day than the primary shows the same Sharpe and Sortino in both places (the explorer read `sweep.scenarios` uncut before C4); within a cell each population's row is computed once per distinct (trades, curve, halves, top event, population), keyed on those objects' identities and forgotten with the cell, and kept only as strict JSON text. It ships as gzip-packed blocks — a base block (`scn-data`: the axes and their option labels, the populations, the explorer's date axis (`_equity_axis`: every date up to 400 rows, one per week beyond), each band's calibration rows, the metric specs, the cap-independent `k̂` and `k̂ − k` matrices, the primary `[band, k, cap]`) unpacked on load, and one block per size cap (`scn-cap-<i>`: every cell's per-population rows — the headline one with its curve as change points (`dashboard._explorer_curve`), which the script expands — that cap's same-title row, its fragility banner (`dashboard._explorer_banner`, over THAT cap's headline cells), its seven cap-dependent matrices and the nine heatmap titles) unpacked by `unpack(el)` only when that cap is first chosen; a cap block that cannot be unpacked is named on the section's status line and every select goes back to the scenario still shown, and a base or primary block that cannot be leaves the selects disabled with the reason in the KPI table. An explorer visitor that fails costs only the explorer's cap axis: the section is rebuilt from the sweep's own eager points at the run's own cap (`dashboard._explorer_from_sweep`, also the direct call's path) — which a grey line in the section says when the run carried a size-cap sweep (`_EXPLORER_OWN_CAP_HTML`), and the bar's summary sentence when the bar offers more caps (`_BAR_REACH_OWN_CAP`) — and only when even that fails does a notice (`_EXPLORER_UNAVAILABLE_HTML`) take its place — the page is always written. Without the band sweep (`not sweep.scenarios`, or a walked grid whose `checks` is False) the section keeps its "band sweep off" placeholder whatever the walk produced, and the page carries no explorer script. **The explorer's Tier floors view (#68, re-expressed on the packed grid at the merge).** When the walk's grid carries a complete tier-floors-off family (`_GridSource.tier_binds`, the test the filter bar starts from, so the two classify every band alike) and a size cap has off cells, the section renders a Tier floors `<select>` of its own (`scn-tier-select`: the bar's options, title and "on" / "off" values) — so the off view is reachable without the bar, and is shown even when the bar cannot be built — and `_ExplorerVisitor`'s `off` method keeps the binding bands' tier-off cells beside the tier-on ones (the eager fallback, `_explorer_from_sweep`, walks them too). With the tiers off, a band they bind at reads its own tier-off points (only points stamped `tier_floors=False`, and a point so stamped is never a tier-on cell: `dashboard._eager_source` skips it) and its tier-off calibration, and every other band its tier-on cells and calibration, since the tiers never bind there (#68's rule), so no cell ships twice: a packed block per size cap the walk handed off cells at (`scn-off-cap-<i>` — in production every cap, from the tier-floors-off size-cap sweep; the run's own alone when the page has none to read) holds the binding bands' rows only (every other row null, read from the tier-on block), that grid's fragility banner — opening on `_SCENARIO_TIER_OFF_LEAD` ("Tier floors off: each band's own floor replaces max(tier, floor) as the time-series entry threshold …", which names what still applies — the band's ceiling, the `MAX_DEADLINE_GAP_DAYS` cap, the positive-spread rule, the fee check and the Kelly gate — with the tiers and the cap read from config) and counting the combined off grid, its "(N scenario points" included — its matrices and its heatmap and curve titles, each ending on `_SCENARIO_TIER_OFF_SUFFIX` (" — tier floors off"); the base block gains `tier_binds`, `band_labels_off` and `band_options_off` (`backtester._band_label`'s bare floor-ceiling: the floor alone gated those runs), `calibration_by_band_off` (a tier-off calibration at a floor of 0 labels its buckets 0.0, which ships as null — "—" — exactly as the pooled row's tier always has), the off `k̂` and `k̂ − k` matrices, `curve_titles` and `off_caps`. A tier change relabels the band options, toggles the two `k̂` tables Python drew (`scn-khat-on`, and the hidden `scn-khat-off`), redraws the heatmap with the off matrices and band labels, and retitles the curve. A cap without an off block (only when the page could not read a tier-floors-off size-cap sweep) is refused with the tiers off: every select goes back to the scenario still shown, and the status line says so in Python's words (`_SCENARIO_TIER_OFF_MISSING`: "… was not simulated by this run; the explorer still shows …"); a cell or calibration the family lacks reads "—", as a missing tier-on one does. Without a complete family there is no select, no hidden table and no off block, and the section is exactly what a run without the family renders. Like the band, the tier setting chosen here changes nothing the live bot does. Measured 2026-09-26, before the merge of #68: `TestScenarioExplorerPageSize`'s 468-cell fixture rendered a 1,724,773-byte page (3,902,183 before, when the explorer's data was one JSON block with every curve dense), a 36 × 13 × 20 fake size-cap grid whose cells share 4 trade lists packed the explorer's 21 blocks into 249,128 bytes of base64 in a 1.21 MB page built in 6.7 s (`TestExplorerFullGrid`, not re-measured since), and `TestFilterPageSize`'s page was 804,179 bytes (819,347 the same day before C4). Re-measured at the merge, same day and environment, with the explorer's Tier floors view: `TestScenarioExplorerPageSize` renders 1,736,090 bytes without a family and 2,632,657 with one for its 18 binding bands at every `k` (its `scn-off-cap-0` block 56,340 bytes of base64), and `TestFilterPageSize` 815,777 and 1,098,598 (its off block 3,712); both tests are parametrized over the family, each variant budgeted at its measurement + 20%. Main measured #68's own construction of the first — its family trading the tier-on list, with the explorer's tier-off view unpacked — at 2,909,152 bytes without the family and 4,122,568 with it, and on the DR-73 corpus's band sweep found the family adding 1.19 MB to its explorer section (3,510,114 bytes against 2,315,905) — 1,097,569 of that its unpacked data block (3,290,508 against 2,192,939) and 96,640 its hidden tier-off block — and taking its page from 4,290,438 bytes, before its explorer followed the tier choice, to 5,489,336 (4,071,051 without the family); those, like the DR-73 corpus figures above, describe main's design and were not re-measured on this one. **The test seam moved with the split**: seven tests that used to monkeypatch `_prepare_entries` around `run_backtest_sweep` now patch `_prepare_candidates` instead, with no assertion line changed: `TestOutcomeLabelCoverageIsCarried::test_the_sweep_carries_the_census` and `::test_the_infeasible_window_carries_no_census`, `TestRunBacktestSweep::test_preparation_and_calibration_run_once`, and `TestSimulationsAreLabelledWithTheirDiscount`'s `::test_every_swept_point_is_distinguishable`, `::test_the_primary_slot_is_announced`, `::test_the_ladder_flag_reaches_prepare_entries_unresolved` and `::test_the_resolved_ladder_setting_is_logged_with_its_source`. The tests of `run_backtest` itself (`TestOutcomeLabelCoverageIsCarried::test_run_backtest_keeps_its_two_tuple`, `TestSimulationsAreLabelledWithTheirDiscount::test_run_backtest_leaves_the_ladder_flag_to_the_config`) still patch `_prepare_entries` and are untouched, because `run_backtest` still calls it directly. **The 5,000-candle cap no longer truncates a long window (fixed 2026-09-23).** Kalshi rejects a candlestick request spanning more than 5,000 hourly candles (~208 days) with HTTP 400 ("max candlesticks: 5000", observed during the DR-73 calibration fetch), and `historical.fetch_candlesticks` fail-softs every fetch error to `[]`. It used to send each ticker's whole window as ONE request, and `_fetch_candles_parallel` opened every window at the run's global `--start-date`, so on a window longer than ~207 days every ticker closing later than that got no candles and could never enter — visible only in `_fetch_candles_parallel`'s `N of M tickers returned no candles` line — and the explorer, like every other strategy figure, described a truncated population (the default `--start-date 2024-01-01` is far past it). `fetch_candlesticks` now pages any window longer than `config.CANDLESTICK_MAX_CANDLES_PER_REQUEST − 1` candle periods (`historical._candle_request_windows`: requests of at most that many periods, overlapping by one period so the merged result equals one uncapped request whichever way the endpoint treats a window's ends) and merges them ascending by timestamp with the overlap's repeats dropped (`_merge_candle_pages`). Each request is the same retried read-only GET; the window is all-or-nothing — any failed request returns `[]` and caches nothing, never a partial series cached as the whole window — and it is cached as ONE entry tagged with the whole `[open_ts, close_ts]`, so the covers-the-window cache test is unchanged. A window that fits is still one request with the same parameters. `_fetch_candles_parallel` also opens each ticker's request at the later of `--start-date` and that market's own `open_time`, floored to the hour (`backtester._candle_window_open`; a missing or unreadable `open_time` — every cache record written before `_market_to_dict` carried it — keeps `--start-date`), so a market that opened after the window began costs one request for its own life instead of several for months of candles it never had. That is result-neutral: across the 573 cached series of the DR-73 calibration corpus whose request opened before the market did, no candle ends at or before the start of the hour the market opened in; the open is floored because 58 of 1,093 series requested from an off-the-hour `open_time` start later than the opening hour, and a request starting on the boundary gets that hour's candle however the endpoint reads a mid-period `start_ts`. PB7's `dashboard._candle_cap_notice` (a red header line, repeated as the explorer banner's first line) described exactly this defect and was removed with it. **One caveat the fix does not reach:** the DR-73 calibration corpus the explorer is read against (`.git/dr73-calib/candles_ts_all2.json.gz`) was fetched outside the production path, one request per leg from the leg's own `open_time`, so it still lacks candles for the 115 of its 3,704 legs whose own open-to-close-plus-a-day span exceeds the cap (up to 15,527 hours); refetching them through the paged `fetch_candlesticks` would fill it. **Picking a scenario here changes nothing the live bot does by itself:** live trading reads its own toggles — the tier floors and the spread band, `k` and the two per-trade caps, the category/tag filter, adding to held pairs, and the sell level with its minimum of days — from the saved live defaults (`live_defaults.json`; see that paragraph at the top of Key Patterns), and the ladder switch from `config.py`, never from a backtest run (`main.py`'s toggle flags override any of those ten for one live run, and never reach a backtest either). The live enforcement of a band and of the tier floors exists (the directional-filter paragraph under Key Patterns), so applying a chosen scenario live is saving it as the live defaults — or, for one run, `main.py`'s matching flags (`--tier-floors`/`--no-tier-floors`, `--spread-min`/`--spread-max`, `--interval-discount`, `--size-cap`) — and a Category or Tag slice of it is the saved `categories`/`tags`, or `--category`/`--tag` for one run (the filter bar's Tag option "Sports · Basketball" is `--category Sports --tag Basketball`; see the category/tag filter paragraph under Key Patterns). **The explorer is where a scenario for the live toggles is found** — choosing it on the page applies nothing by itself: to apply it, set the filter bar to it (never the Scenario Explorer's own selects, which are not what is saved) and click the bar's "Save as live defaults…" button, which opens the defaults server's confirmation page for it (nothing is saved until that page's Confirm and save, or its Confirm and trade, which saves before it runs), or pass `main.py`'s matching flags for one run. The operator decision of 2026-09-27 applied one cell by editing config.py's constants — spread band 0–0.5, tier floors off, `k` 0.80, cap 20% (shipped as no per-trade cap with a 20% same-title cap, which size alike at `k` 0.80) — before the saved live defaults existed (see its addendum); the live-defaults decision record of 2026-09-27 under Key Patterns carries the dashboard's figures for that cell, V0's live-contest replay (verdict STOP on drawdown) and the checkpoint-anchor finding it was shipped against. The page names the live rule itself, not only how to reach it: a second header line, `dashboard._live_rule_html(sweep, bar=...)` (right under `_run_settings_html`'s own line, which it never modifies — twelve tests pin that line's exact `</p>` suffix), reads `BacktestSweep.live_tier_floors`/`live_spread_band`/`live_categories`/`live_tags` (with `live_origin`, `live_interval_discount`, `live_size_cap` and `live_same_title_size_cap`) — the ONE read of the saved live defaults (`config.live_defaults()`) that `backtester.run_backtest_sweep` takes before its fetch (`_live_settings_for_report`, handed to whichever construction builds the sweep), never `main.py`'s per-run overrides, which a backtest cannot see — and names: the rule, through `config.describe_time_series_rule`; the category/tag filter, only when one is set, in `config.describe_trade_filter`'s words (`backtester._live_filter_text`, pinned equal to it); the same-title cap every same-title row was sized under at every cap the page offers (the lower of the size cap shown and `BacktestSweep.same_title_size_cap` — `config.pair_size_cap`'s rule — with its value at the run's own cap); and where the page shows the rule. That last is one of: the primary scenario; the filter bar's options that select it, in the bar's own option texts (Spread band `max(tier,X)-Y` and Tier floors on, or Tier floors off first and then the bare `X-Y`, since the bar relabels its bands when the tiers go off), plus the one Category or Tag option the live filter covers — or the several it covers, which the bar shows one at a time and never as their union, or that no scenario filed a pair under it; "not shown" when the grid holds the rule but this page cannot show it (no filter bar, or no such option); or "not simulated by this run". Where the run's grid holds it is `backtester._live_rule_view`, the ONE verdict the run's own log line states too — `Live time-series rule (saved live defaults): <rule>[; category/tag filter (...)] — <where>[; <sizing note>][; <add-on note>]` (`_live_rule_line`), logged once the sweep exists, on every path including the Monday-infeasible one, so it is the last line `run_backtest_sweep` logs — read off what the sweep RECORDED: the primary when the live band is the primary's and the rule is tier-on (or tier-off at a band no tier binds at, `_tier_floors_bind` False, where off and on are one rule); another cell when the live band is a key of `calibrations_by_band` and, with the tiers off at a band a tier binds at, the tier-off family names every binding band (`dashboard._tier_off_binds`' rule, since the page withholds the whole off view otherwise); nothing otherwise — so a Monday-infeasible run, which built only its empty primary, never claims a view its page lacks. A run whose same-event ladder setting departs from `config_same_event_ladders` says its pairs are not the live bot's. The page's line is labelled `Live rule (saved live defaults):` and, when the saved `k` or caps are not this run's primary's, ends with `backtester._live_sizing_note` and, only where the line says the page shows the live rule (the primary, or a filter-bar option it names), how the filter bar shows it at that sizing (`dashboard._live_sizing_bar_clause`: its k and Size cap options, or what it cannot show — a k or cap it does not offer, a cell the bar holds no scenario at (a tier-floors-off cap the page could not simulate), or a same-title cap, which is never a bar choice); a line that says the rule is not simulated or not shown ends on the note alone. When the saved defaults add to held pairs (`live_add_to_held_pairs`), the log line ends with `backtester._live_add_on_note`'s clause after the sizing note — "; the live defaults add to held pairs, which this run's primary does not" — since no point a backtest run keeps adds to a pair it still holds; the family the dashboard's filter bar offers for it is the one in the size-cap paragraph below, and the page's own live-rule line, where it shows the rule, says "— choose Add to held pairs on in the filter bar to see it" only when the bar's add-on grid holds a scenario at that band and Tier floors setting (`dashboard._add_on_shown_at`), else "— this page has no Add to held pairs view". When they sell (`live_sell_at`), the log line ends with `backtester._live_sell_note`'s clause after that — "; the live defaults sell at 85% of potential profit (at least 3 days before maturity), which this run's primary does not" — and the page's live-rule line, where it shows the rule, names the Sell level and Min. days to maturity that show the saved sale rule at that band and Tier floors setting ("— choose Sell "…" and Min. days to maturity "…" in the filter bar to see it", `dashboard._sell_choice_in_bar`; with no saved minimum it points at the smallest option, "the nearest to no minimum"), or says that the page cannot show that level and minimum of days. A run with no live defaults saved logs one INFO line and a refused file one WARNING (at the read, before the fetch); either records `None` in all eleven fields, and the live-rule line still appears, as `Live time-series rule: none recorded — no usable live defaults were saved when this run started, and live runs refuse to start without them`, the page rendering the same words (`backtester._LIVE_RULE_NONE`; a page built with no sweep at all reads "Live rule: not recorded") — rather than aborting the run over a reporting line. `backtest.py`'s pre-fetch echo names the same rule (and filter, when set) and the file's origin as `| live rule=...`, from a read of its own that fails soft to `none saved (live runs refuse to start)` or `not recorded — the saved live defaults are refused (...)`.

**The per-trade size cap is a backtest parameter, and the cap sweep is LAZY (2026-09, backtest-only).** `backtester._simulate_at_discount(..., *, tier_floors=True, size_cap=None, quiet=False, end_date=None, add_to_held=False, sell_at=None)` sizes every candidate at `min(config.pair_size_cap(pair type, cap, SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP), f*)` — `cap` is the cap for EVERY pair, and a same-title candidate also stays under `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` (the backtester's by-value binding of the config constant, read at call time and held by `_resolve_same_title_size_cap` to the rule `_resolve_size_cap` holds the per-trade cap to — a NaN, a value outside `(0, 1]`, one off the `config.SIZE_CAP_STEP` grid or a non-number raises before any entry is scored, and `run_backtest_sweep` refuses it before anything is logged or fetched, where `min()` would otherwise read a NaN as no cap; `0.20` since the operator decision of 2026-09-27, `1.0` — adding no cap — before it), exactly as live sizing caps it through the same helper — where `size_cap=None` resolves `backtester.BUDGET_FRACTION` at CALL time (`_resolve_size_cap`, which accepts any `numbers.Real` in `(0, 1]` on the 5% grid — numpy scalars included — returns it as a builtin float, and refuses everything else before any entry is scored: a bool or numpy bool, NaN, out of range with a range message, off the grid with a message naming the value, a string or `Decimal` with a message naming the type; it reads the module binding, so `TestCheckpointOpeningBalanceSizing`'s monkeypatch of `backtester.BUDGET_FRACTION` still binds); every existing call therefore sized, stamped and logged exactly as before when the same-title cap was added (at `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP = 1.0`, its value until the 2026-09-27 decision, it is inert, so a same-title candidate sizes at `min(cap, f*)` as it always did; since that decision `BUDGET_FRACTION` is `1.0` and a default run sizes a same-title candidate at `min(0.20, f*)`). The point is stamped with the resolved `size_cap` and `peak_kelly_fraction` — the largest UNCAPPED `f*` over the candidates that passed the Kelly gate (counted before the outcome and premise checks, 0.0 when none passed), which is cap-independent because Pass 1b scores every entry before any sizing — and `cap_free_from`, the cap above which a walked book also stops depending on the cap (see the walked-book gotcha). `run_backtest_sweep(cap_sweep=True)` — the CLI's default, `--no-cap-sweep` the opt-out, `False` in the function itself — returns `BacktestSweep.cap_sweep`, a `backtester.CapSweep` over `backtester.SIZE_CAP_SWEEP` (`0.05`…`0.95` and `1.0` = no cap, unioned with the run's own cap) that **simulates nothing during the run**: every eager point, completion line and simulation of the run is identical with the flag on or off (pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestCapSweep::test_off_is_the_default_and_adds_no_simulation`), and each other cap is simulated only when a reader calls `cell(band, k)` / `same_title()`, one (band, `k`) cell at a time, with no memo — keeping every band × `k` × cap × population point would hold gigabytes of trades and curves (the plan ESTIMATED 2.5–5 GB; the per-object figures behind it, re-measured 2026-09-26 with tracemalloc on synthetic records: one `BacktestTrade` retains ~2.3 KB, one equity curve ~27 KB at 370 rows and ~114 KB at 2,463 rows), so the reader is meant to summarise each cell and drop it. The dashboard is that reader: `dashboard._walk_grid` reads every cell once as the page is built, for the page-wide filter bar, the interval-discount section and the scenario explorer together (and `same_title()` once, for the explorer's same-title row at every cap) — so a default CLI run's dashboard phase simulates the whole cap grid (reading the golden fixture's full grid took 23.9–25.6 s against its eager run's 2.3–2.4 s, see below) — and `--no-cap-sweep` is the way to skip it. **Tier floors off at every cap: a SECOND `CapSweep` (C5, after the merge of #68).** One `CapSweep` holds ONE Tier floors setting (`CapSweep.tier_floors`). The tier-on one (`BacktestSweep.cap_sweep`) is seeded from tier-on points only: `_sweep_from_candidates` records its seeds in its tier-on loop and from the same-title point, never in the family's loop (pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestCapSweepNeverSeedsTierOff::test_a_tier_off_point_is_never_a_cap_sweep_seed`, which also checks that a cap cell at a band the family re-runs returns the tier-on point at the primary cap). With `cap_sweep` AND `tier_off_sweep` — both on by default in the CLI — and at least one binding band, the family's loop records every point of its cells (`_band_sweep_cell`'s, "all" first) in a separate seed map, and a second, `tier_floors=False` `CapSweep` is built over the binding bands' tier-off entries (`entries_by_band` = the family's own, `bands` = the binding bands, `st_entries=[]` — the same-title entries never read the tiers, so the tier-on sweep's `same_title()` serves both — `checks=True`) and returned as the appended `BacktestSweep.tier_off_cap_sweep` (default `None`; `None` too with either flag off, on the infeasible window, or with no binding band), announced by one INFO line, `Tier floors off: size-cap sweep: N caps x B binding band(s) x K k, simulated on demand, …`, after the tier-on sweep's own. The two seed maps share keys but never an object, so neither sweep can hand one setting's simulation back under the other's (band, `k`, population) key; the seeding rule is the tier-on one (the eager point itself at the primary cap, shared copies above the eager peak only when the primary cap reaches it). It simulates nothing during the run (the run's simulations, their order and keywords, are identical with it or without it). Its `_by_cap` forwards `tier_floors=False` to every cap point, both halves and the ex-top re-simulation, so every point it returns is stamped `tier_floors=False` (a simulated one by `_simulate_at_discount`, a shared copy by the point it copies). Pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestTierOffCapSweep` (every cap of every off cell of a narrowed golden sweep — 2 binding bands x 3 `k` x 4 populations x 20 caps — equals a fresh `_simulate_at_discount(..., tier_floors=False, size_cap=cap)` on trades, equity, halves and ex-top; the seeding identity; the flags; the summary line) and `::TestCapSweepTierSetting` (the forwarding, on hand-built points). On the golden fixture's full grid (measured 2026-09-26, synthetic data), reading every off cell returned 18,720 tier-off cap points — 936 eager identities, 10,844 shared copies and 6,940 simulated — in 12.7 s. The dashboard reads its cells through `_GridSource.off_cell` (see the filter-bar paragraph for what a failure costs), so a binding band's tier-off view is a real simulation at every cap; only a run without it (or a page that could not use it) keeps the family's run's-own-cap view alone, with the filter bar's `missing` template and the explorer's refusal (`_SCENARIO_TIER_OFF_MISSING`) at the other caps. **The reuse rule, and the one correction to its plan** (the golden-fixture figures below were derived at `k` 0.75 and a 20% run cap, which every class-scoped sweep fixture still pins through conftest's `apply_pre_toggle_defaults` — see the CI section): every cap `C` at or above a point's `peak_kelly_fraction` sizes identically (over a walked book, at or above the larger of that and its `cap_free_from`, `backtester._sharing_floor`, wherever this paragraph says "peak"; the walked-book gotcha says why) — `min(pair_size_cap(type, C, SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP), f*)` is `f*` for a time-series candidate and `min(SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP, f*)` for a same-title one, neither depending on `C` (pinned at a patched same-title cap of 0.2 with no per-trade cap by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestCapSweepSameTitleCap`, every cap of every cell of the narrowed golden sweep against a fresh simulation, shared copies included) — and so do that point's split-half and ex-top re-simulations (subsets of its entries, whose peaks are no higher). `CapSweep._by_cap` therefore returns, per population, (1) the eager point ITSELF at the primary cap — this branch comes first, so identity holds even where the peak sits below the cap (the golden fixture's time-series population at `k = 1.0`, whose peak is 0.0: at `k = 1` no time-series Monday on uncrossed quotes passes the Kelly gate, and a crossed one is skipped); (2) a `dataclasses.replace(eager, size_cap=cap)` copy sharing its trades, curve, halves and top-event check for every cap at or above the eager peak — **but only when the primary cap is itself at or above that peak**; (3) otherwise a quiet simulation, whose first run at a cap at or above its own peak is then shared by every larger cap. The plan this was built from seeded (2) whenever `cap >= peak`, which is wrong when the eager point was CAPPED below its own peak: on the golden fixture the "all" population at `k = 0.75` has a same-title `f*` of ~0.77, so the 20%-capped eager point does not size as the 80%–100% caps do, and parity with a fresh simulation fails (pinned by `TestCapSweepSeeding::test_a_capped_eager_point_does_not_seed_the_larger_caps` and by `TestCapSweep::test_every_cap_equals_a_fresh_simulation`, which checks every cap of every cell of a narrowed 12-cell golden sweep against a fresh `_simulate_at_discount(..., size_cap=cap)` on trades, equity, halves and ex-top). Reading every cell of the golden fixture's full 36 × 13 grid (measured 2026-09-26, synthetic data, not a production estimate) returned 34,860 cap points: 1,743 eager identities, 19,769 shared copies and 13,348 simulated cap points — `CapSweep.simulated` counts one per cap point, and those points made 41,581 `_simulate_at_discount` calls in all once their split halves and ex-top re-simulations are counted, against 4,479 calls for the eager run — in 23.9–25.6 s against 2.3–2.4 s (the call ratio, ~9.3x, is what tracks the time ratio, not the 13,348). **Every sweep helper that forwards to `_simulate_at_discount` forwards only what differs from its defaults** (`_sim_options(size_cap, quiet, *, end_date=None, tier_floors=True, add_to_held=False, sell_at=None)`: nothing on a default call, a cap equal to `BUDGET_FRACTION` included, `tier_floors` only when it is `False`, `add_to_held` only when it is `True` and `sell_at` only when it is set — the one convention `_half_split`, `_ex_top_event` and #68's `_band_sweep_cell` share), so the fixed six-parameter test spies (`golden_band_sweep`'s and `cap_sweep_run`'s `simulate_spy`, and the `fake_simulate`s of `TestBandSweepSplitAndPopulationWiring`, `TestTimeSeriesPopulation` and `TestBandSweepPhaseOneSubset`'s tier-on sweep) keep passing — a forwarded default would raise there — while the tier-off stand-ins accept `tier_floors`; `TestSweepHelpers::test_ex_top_event_picks_the_largest_summed_profit`'s verbatim kwargs assertion was re-pinned at the merge without #68's `"tier_floors": True` (a tier-on call forwards nothing) and gained a `tier_floors=False` call that forwards it. `_half_split` takes `*, tier_floors=True, size_cap=None, quiet=False, end_date=None, add_to_held=False, sell_at=None` and `_ex_top_event` `*, tier_floors=True, quiet=False, end_date=None`, re-simulating at the point's own `size_cap`, `add_to_held` and `sell_at`. `CapSweep._by_cap`'s own simulation builds `quiet`, its pinned `end_date`, its `tier_floors` and its `add_to_held` through `_sim_options(None, True, …)` too, but passes `size_cap` EXPLICITLY beside them: `_sim_options` drops a cap equal to `BUDGET_FRACTION`, which would hand the simulation `None` — resolved at call time, so a moved binding would size the cell at a cap its caps tuple does not name — and would change what `TestCapSweepSeeding`'s stand-ins record; a tier-on `CapSweep` therefore calls the simulator with exactly the keywords it always did (pinned by `TestCapSweepTierSetting::test_a_cap_equal_to_the_budget_fraction_is_still_named`). **Every lazy cap simulation is pinned to its eager point's last curve day** (`backtester._curve_end_date`, forwarded as `end_date` to the point, its halves and its ex-top run, and on to `_build_equity_curve(..., end_date=)`): `_build_equity_curve` otherwise ends every curve on today (UTC) as read at simulation time, and a dashboard reads every cell long after the run, so a cell read across UTC midnight gave its simulated caps one row more than the eager point and the copies sharing its curve — neighbouring caps' Sharpe/Sortino over different spans, and the class-scoped parity test failing spuriously (pinned by `TestCapSweepEndDate`, which reads a cell four minutes after a run frozen at 23:58 UTC). The eager points still read the clock as they are simulated, exactly as before, so an eager phase that straddles midnight can still end two cells' (or two populations') curves on different days; every cap of ONE population ends on the same day. A hand-built `CapSweep` whose eager point has no readable `date` column simulates unpinned. The eager seeds are recorded by `_sweep_from_candidates` as each TIER-ON point is simulated — the "all" point BEFORE the `if not band_sweep: continue`, so a `--no-band-sweep` cap sweep still has its seeds and its cells carry "all" alone, without halves, then each population point `_band_sweep_cell` returns — and `_population_subsets` is the ONE split into the time-series / ladder / cross populations, shared by `_band_populations` (the band sweep's eager loops, tier-on and tier-off, which add the halves split at the one `split_date`) and `CapSweep.cell` (an empty population is skipped by both). **Retention (declared, TS-07):** a `CapSweep` keeps `entries_by_band` alive — one small entry dict per band per entry, and every entered pair's two market dicts, bounded by the no-band band's entries since every band's entered PAIRS are a subset of its: 405 (399 time-series + 6 same-title; 60–405 across the 36 bands) on the 2026-09-26 01:22 365-day run's log, so at most ~810 distinct market dicts, a few MB; nothing else from the corpus. The tier-floors-off `CapSweep` keeps the family's entries alive the same way (they were a local released at return before C5): the binding bands' tier-off entry dicts — the same-title ones shared with the tier-on sweep — and the market dicts of pairs that enter only with the tiers off, since every other pair's are already held; measured on the golden fixture (2026-09-26) the tier-on sweep holds 108 entries over 36 bands (73 distinct entry dicts, 10 market dicts) and the tier-off one 63 over 18 (46 distinct, 45 of them new) and ZERO new market dicts; on a real corpus the new market dicts are bounded by that corpus's own tier-off-only pairs at the no-band band (those entering with the tiers off and not with them on), a bound to re-measure per corpus: on the DR-73 calibration corpus that is its 278 tier-off-only pairs, so at most 556 dicts (derived, not measured) beside at most 660 for its 330 tier-on no-band entries, while the ~810 bound quoted for the tier-on sweep comes from a different corpus, the 2026-09-26 01:22 365-day run (405 no-band entries), whose tier-off-only count was not measured (see `CapSweep`'s docstring). `run_backtest_sweep` logs `Per-trade size cap (backtest): <cap>% (config.BUDGET_FRACTION); size-cap sweep on/off` right after the band line (and the entry-checkpoint line right after it — see the entry-checkpoint gotcha) — with `, same-title never above N% (config.SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP)` before the semicolon only when `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` sits below the run's cap: at the values before the 2026-09-27 decision the line was byte-identical to the pre-cap one (`20% (config.BUDGET_FRACTION); …`), and at the shipped ones it reads `Per-trade size cap (backtest): 100% (config.BUDGET_FRACTION), same-title never above 20% (config.SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP); size-cap sweep on` — and records the same-title cap on `BacktestSweep.same_title_size_cap` (both production constructions), and a `Size-cap sweep: N caps (...) x B band(s) x K k, ...` summary once the sweep is built, followed — the last line `run_backtest_sweep` itself logs — by the `Live time-series rule (saved live defaults): ...` line (see the scenario-explorer paragraph); the cap runs' own completion lines — a simulated cap point's AND its split halves' and ex-top re-simulation's, each forwarded `quiet=True` — and their premise WARNINGs are DEBUG (pinned on a checked band-sweep cell by `TestCapSweep::test_a_checked_cell_s_cap_runs_stay_out_of_the_info_log`, and on `_half_split` directly by `TestSizeCap::test_half_split_quiet_sends_both_halves_to_debug`), so reading every cell adds nothing to the INFO log (TS-02). **Live sizing never reads any of this** — `strategy.py` never imports `backtester`, and live sizing reads its caps from the run's `LiveSettings` (the saved live defaults' — while `config.BUDGET_FRACTION` and `config.SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` are the eager backtest's caps — or, for one live run, `main.py`'s `--size-cap` / `--same-title-size-cap`, which no backtest reads). **The cap axis therefore means "the cap for every pair, with same-title pairs also under `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP`"**: at a `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` of `1.0`, its value until the 2026-09-27 decision, it was simply the per-trade cap; at the shipped `0.20` every same-title row above 20% sizes at 20%, and the axis's "no cap" option — a default run's own cap, since `BUDGET_FRACTION` ships `1.0` — leaves same-title capped at 20% (the option labels do not say so — `dashboard._cap_option`'s docstring records it, and the page's `Live rule (saved live defaults)` header line states the same-title cap at every cap it offers). **The grid lives in `backtester.py`, not `config.py`,** by the operator's instruction for this change (only the dashboard and backtest code could move) — the one named exception in the constants rule; moving it is a one-line change. `_cap_percent` renders a cap as the exact decimal shift of the `repr` of its builtin float value (`0.55` → `55`, `0.19999999999999998` → `19.999999999999998`; the `float()` first matters because numpy's `float64` reprs as `np.float64(0.35)`, which `Decimal` cannot parse), which is injective; the plan's float-product fallback (`repr(cap * 100)`) is not — `0.003` and the double below it both print `0.3` there — so it was not used.

**The add-on family: two more lazy `CapSweep`s for the dashboard's "Add to held pairs" view (DR-77, backtest-only).** `run_backtest_sweep(add_on_sweep=True)` — the CLI's default, `--no-add-on-sweep` the opt-out, `False` in the function itself — returns `BacktestSweep.add_on_cap_sweep`, a `CapSweep` over the same bands, `k`s and caps as the size-cap sweep (the run's own cap alone when the size-cap sweep is off) whose every simulation adds to held pairs (`add_to_held=True`), and, when the tier-floors-off family ran, `add_on_tier_off_cap_sweep` over its binding bands (`tier_floors=False`). Four things set them apart from the size-cap sweeps. (1) **They have no eager point to start from**, because no eager run of the backtest adds to held pairs: they are built with `eager={}` and no `same_title_eager`, so each cell is simulated when a report reads it — every cap in turn until one reaches its peak Kelly fraction (its `max(peak, cap_free_from)` over a walked book), which every larger cap then shares. (2) **Each cell ends its curves on the day its eager twin's curve ended.** `_sweep_from_candidates` records the last curve day of every eager "all" point as it simulates it — the tier-on loop's in one map and the tier-off loop's in another, since the two share keys — and hands each sweep its map as `end_dates`, keyed by the exact band and `k` objects the cells are read with (pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestAddOnSweep::test_each_cell_has_the_day_its_eager_twin_ended_on`, across UTC midnight by `::test_a_cell_read_after_utc_midnight_ends_every_cap_on_the_eager_day` and, under a clock that moves one day on every read so that every eager point ends on a day of its own, by `::test_every_cap_of_every_cell_ends_on_its_own_twin_s_day`). (3) **Only the "all" population, and no checks** (`checks=False`, no split date, no same-title population of their own, since "all" already holds the same-title entries): the filter bar reads the "all" point alone, so a cell is one simulation per cap. (4) **The run itself is unchanged.** Nothing is simulated while the sweeps are built, so the run's simulations (their order and keywords), completion lines and every figure it returns are those of a run without the flag (pinned by `::test_on_adds_no_simulation_and_no_run_figure` and `::test_on_adds_no_info_line_but_its_own`). The run logs one setting line right after the "Entry checkpoint (backtest)" line (the size-cap line still comes immediately before the checkpoint line) and, with the sweep on, one summary line per sweep. Each sweep keeps alive the entries of the sweep it mirrors (`entries_by_band`, `tier_off_entries` — the same objects the size-cap sweeps hold, so with the size-cap sweep on the add-on sweeps add no retention, and with `--no-cap-sweep` they are what keeps them, bounded as `CapSweep`'s retention note says). **Cost.** Every add-on cell is simulated when the page is built, cap by cap (no eager point stands in for any). Measured on the test suite's golden fixture over its full 36 x 13 x 20 grid (synthetic data), before trades were sized on the portfolio value (the simulation counts do not depend on that; the times and page sizes may): reading every tier-on add-on cell took about 8.1 s (468 (band, `k`) cells, 7,488 simulations) and every tier-off one about 4.7 s (234 cells, 3,744), against 28.4 s for the size-cap sweep (468 cells, 13,333 simulations); a real `run_backtest_sweep(add_on_sweep=True)` page over that fixture at 6 bands x 3 `k` x 20 caps (the tier-floors-off family on) grew from 665,339 to 950,892 bytes (+43%), with `generate_dashboard` taking 8.95 s against 5.75 s (+56%). **A real 365-day page has not been measured with the family:** expect it to grow by a similar share (the 365-day page on disk when this was reviewed was 111,986,368 bytes, 4,094 chunks, so about 50 MB more, an extrapolation and not a measurement) and its dashboard step to take longer. `--no-add-on-sweep` skips the family, and the sweeps are `None` for the Monday-infeasible window.

**The bar's Add to held pairs select (DR-77, reporting only).** The filter bar's seventh select, after Size cap and before Sell (`_filter_bar_html`, id `flt-add`, `SHOWN[6]`; the Sell choice is `SHOWN[7]`), offers "off" — the page as rendered — and "on (up to the size cap)": the scenario on screen re-simulated with adding to held pairs, so a pair the run still holds can be bought again on a later Monday, sized so old and new together, with the fees paid, stay within the size cap's share of the portfolio value, as the select's tooltip (`_ADD_ON_SELECT_TITLE`) says (the add-on family and DR-77's backtest paragraph above). **How the family joins the page.** `dashboard._with_add_on(source, sweep)` attaches the family to the walked grid (`_GridSource.add_cell`, `add_off_cell`) only if its bands, `k`s and caps are exactly the grid's; `_walk_once` reads its cells after the tier-floors-off ones (`_walk_add_cells`) and hands them to the visitors' opt-in `add` method (`_ChunkVisitor`); `_ExplorerVisitor` and `_KdVisitor` have none, so the Scenario Explorer and the Interval Discount section never follow the choice, and the summary line says so. The base block carries `grid_add`, with the tier floors off `grid_add_off` (a band no tier binds at reads its add-on tier-on cell, as `grid_off` does) and `add_state`. Every category and tag the family's entries can trade is listed up front, as for the size-cap sweep; with `--no-cap-sweep` that list is every entry's event (the eager grid lists only traded ones), so a voided pair's category can appear as a new Category or Tag option (at 0) and re-index the categories, tags and `k̂` groups, with every figure unchanged. **It fails closed, and costs that choice alone.** A run with no family (`--no-add-on-sweep`), a family whose bands, `k`s or caps are not the grid's, one the page cannot read, a size-cap sweep the page set aside (so the grid is the eager one, whose caps are the run's own alone) and an add-on cell that cannot be simulated each leave the select disabled beside a short note — "(not simulated in this backtest)" or "(not available on this page; see the log)" — and each has a WARNING of its own, a lost size-cap axis included (the size-cap sweep's WARNING, then `_warn_add_on_lost_with_the_cap_sweep`'s, so the note's "see the log" is true); a run without the family has none, since nothing failed. A raising add-on cell calls `reset_add` on the visitors, which drops only the chunks, row heads and grids the add-on cells made (every other choice's chunks are untouched, and the page is the page without a family), and an all-empty add-on grid is never shown as a view. A tier-floors-off twin that does not match or cannot be read costs the tier-off half of the choice alone: with the tier floors off, adding is shown at a band no tier binds at (its tier-on add-on run stands in) and reads as not simulated at a band the tiers bind at. After a tier-floors-off size-cap fallback the two settings can show a different mix of caps (tiers off with adding off, the run's own cap alone at a binding band; tiers off with adding on, every cap); each figure is real. **The script.** "On" at the primary cell is not the page as rendered (`isPrimary` also needs adding off), so it reads as another run; the bar's call to the explorer's `window.dashScenarioSelect(band, k, cap, tier)` keeps its four arguments and never carries the choice. **The save button** sends `add_to_held_pairs` for the scenario on screen only from a page whose base block has the add-on view, and stays disabled for a scenario the page never simulated (adding on with the tier floors off at a band the twin lacks, say); the address read back through `defaults_server._proposal` gives the scenario's own `LiveSettings`. Saving from the bar's default state sends `add_to_held_pairs=off`, which would turn off live adding the saved defaults have on, so on a page with the view whose run recorded saved defaults that add to held pairs (`_save_target`'s `live_adds_to_held_pairs`), a grey note right after the button says so (`_ADD_ON_SAVE_NOTE`). **What a reader sees for an add-on.** An add-on is a `BacktestTrade` of its own (`add_on=True`) that books its own count, cost, fees and payout, so the trade counts, the performance figures, the decomposition and the diagnostics count it as one more trade, with no marker in the tables or caption under the chart (either would move a golden digest); in the Risk section's Kelly-vs-actual scatter its x is the pair's uncapped Kelly fraction at the prices it paid and its y what it spent, fees included, over the portfolio value before it (`balance_at_entry`), so it sits below the 1:1 line and can read as badly undersized (y/x as low as 0.002 in runs made before trades were sized on the portfolio value; to re-measure). The select's tooltip (`_ADD_ON_SELECT_TITLE`) says so, and while adding is on the summary line says that each purchase that adds to a held pair counts as a trade of its own and, in the Kelly chart, shows only what it added (`_ADD_ON_SUMMARY_NOTE`, from `D.text.add_on_note`). Pinned by `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestAddOnView`, `::TestAddOnScript`, `::TestAddOnEndToEnd` and the add-on variants of `::TestFilterPageSize`, whose budgets are their measurements + 20%.

**Selling a position early — the backtest's take-profit rule (2026-10; live selling applies the same rule — see the live-selling paragraph under Key Patterns, which also records where the two differ).** `backtester._simulate_at_discount(..., *, sell_at=None)` sells a whole position before it pays out once it has realized `sell_at` of its potential profit and stayed there for `config.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS` days in a row (see **Days in a row** below); `None`, the default, never sells, and the walk is then unchanged (the commit that added it changed no existing test, the golden fixtures included). Its arithmetic is shared with live selling: `_sells_at` calls `config.take_profit_reached`, `_days_left` calls `config.days_to_maturity`, `_ladder_average` calls `scanner.walk_bids`, `_usable_ask` calls `historical.usable_candle_ask`, `_leg_quotes` records its sale bids with `historical.candle_sale_bids` and `_HOLD_DAYS_MAX` is `config.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS_MAX`, each a one-line wrapper that leaves the backtest's results unchanged (`tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_the_backtest_reads_the_sell_rule_where_live_code_can`). **The definitions** are the operator's — realized profit = current market value − total cost, potential profit = potential total return − total cost — read as follows. A POSITION is an open trade with every open trade joined to it by a shared market (`_positions`, union-find on tickers): without `add_to_held` every open trade is a position of its own; with it, a pair with everything added to it, a lone-leg add-on included. Its total cost is its trades' contract cost plus entry fees; its potential total return its contract pairs × `CONTRACT_PAYOUT_DOLLARS` (what a win pays); its current market value WHAT SELLING IT WOULD RETURN (`_position_sale_value`, the one reader of a bid and of a sale ladder): each market's contracts, totalled by ticker and side held across the position's trades (a lone-leg add-on can hold one market as A in one trade and as B in another), walk that market's modeled bid ladder (`LegQuotes.sale_ladder`), anchored at the fresh bid of the side held (`LegQuotes.bid_at_checkpoint`: one minus the other side's ask on the latest candle that ended at or before the checkpoint AND within one candle period of it, that ask usable by `_usable_ask`'s rule — never carried forward, NaN otherwise), and each trade's contracts sell at the average bid reached, less the taker fee on that sale (`config.fee_leg_exact(n, average bid)`, per trade per leg); a position whose ladder at any check holds fewer contracts on a market than the position does is not sold that Monday; with no depth model, no volume data for the market at that check, or a bid below 1c or above 99c (which the model has no ladders for, `depth_model.can_start_at`), the sale is the single fresh bid in any size; a leg whose market has paid out by the checkpoint (`LegQuotes.paid_at_checkpoint`, from the exact settlement time) counts at its payout, with no fee. A position sells when `_sells_at` holds — potential > 0 and realized ≥ `sell_at` × potential − `PRICE_EPSILON` — at the checkpoint and at each daily check before it (next paragraph). A position with a trade that has no quotes, an unpaid leg with no fresh bid, or a ladder too thin for it, is not sold at that checkpoint. The checkpoint's own check and the proceeds are the same number, while the equity curve still values an open trade at the ASK (see the market-valuation paragraph below), so a sale shows on its day as a step down of the spread and the sale's fees. **Days in a row (`config.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS`, 3).** A position is sold at a checkpoint only when it has reached its level there AND at a daily check on each of the `TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS` − 1 days before it, so a price that jumps for a moment does not trigger a sale. The checks run 24 hours apart, back from the checkpoint's own instant (`_checkpoint_datetime` less whole days — never the wall clock, so a clock change between two checks moves none of them), and each earlier check values the position as a sale then would (`_position_sale_value(position, day, days_back)`): each leg at the bid from the LAST QUOTE of the 24 hours before that check — the latest candle that ended in (check − 24 h, check], never carried forward (a candle exactly 24 hours old belongs to the check before) — or at its payout once its market had paid out by that check; the checkpoint's own check keeps the fresh-bid rule above, since the sale happens at it. With a depth model, each check walks the ladder built from its own 24-hour volume (`sale_ladder(day, bid, days_back)`). A check that cannot be valued (an unpaid leg with no quote that day, or a ladder too thin for the position there) holds the sale back like a check below the level. `LegQuotes` keeps the checks as a YES and a NO bid, a paid-out marker and a 24-hour volume a day from `first_day` (`yes_bid_daily`, `no_bid_daily`, `paid_daily`, `volume_daily`, in its fingerprint; the check on a checkpoint's own date is that checkpoint), read through `bid_at_checkpoint`/`paid_at_checkpoint`/`sale_ladder`'s `days_back` (1 to 6; 0, the default, is the checkpoint). `_hold_readings` is the one reader of the checks (the one caller of `_position_sale_value`) and `_reached_every_day` the one test (`_sells_at` at every check): the walk's sales, `_position_sells` and `_sale_reach` all decide through them (pinned by AST in `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_open_trades_are_valued_through_one_definition`). With `level=`, `_hold_readings` stops at the first check below that level, so the walk reads no check after a failing one. Every check of a sale falls after the previous checkpoint (`backtester._HOLD_DAYS_MAX` = 7), so every trade the position holds at the sale was already held at each check (trades are bought only at checkpoints), and the rule values those trades at every check (with `add_to_held`, a trade that paid out between two checks is valued at none of them, though it was held at the earlier one). `_resolve_hold_days` reads `backtester.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS` when called (patch the backtester's binding in a test, never `config`'s) and refuses anything but a whole number from 1 to 7 — in `_simulate_at_discount` only when it sells, in `_sale_reach`, and in `run_backtest_sweep` before anything is fetched when the sell family is on, whose setting line names the rule (`Selling early (backtest): on — a position is sold once it has stayed at or above a level for 3 days in a row (config.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS); …`). The dashboard hands it to each Sell worker (`_SellTask.hold_days`) and names it in the Sell select's hover text (`_sell_select_title`). 1 is the checkpoint alone, the rule before the days were added. **Every Sell figure recorded before the days were added sold on the checkpoint's bids alone, so none is comparable.** **A minimum of days before maturity (`sell_min_days`).** `_simulate_at_discount(..., *, sell_min_days=N)` (a whole number of at least 1, set only with `sell_at`; `None`, the default, sets no minimum and changes nothing) sells a position that has reached its level only while at least N days remain before it matures; nearer than that it is not even valued (`_far_enough` is asked before `_hold_readings`), and since its days left only shrink at later checkpoints it is held until it pays out (unless, with `add_to_held`, an add-on brings in a market that closes later). **Maturity is the day the position's LAST market stops trading** (operator decision): `_days_left(position, day)` is the latest `close_date_a`/`close_date_b` among its trades less the checkpoint's date, in whole calendar days — the close date as the market's `close_time` writes it (UTC on every Kalshi timestamp) against the checkpoint's UTC date — and `None` when a trade has no close date (only a hand-built one), which a minimum never sells. Both the walk's sale step and its quick test at a checkpoint with no candidate (`_position_sells(..., min_days)`) ask `_far_enough` first, so the walk applies one rule. A minimum of 1 day is almost, but not exactly, the no-minimum rule: the two differ only where the position's last market closes on or before the checkpoint's own UTC date (0 or fewer days left). **Sales still come before every purchase at a checkpoint** (operator requirement): the walk runs `release(d)` (the day's pay-outs), then `sell(d)`, then the valuation (`checkpoint_value`), then the candidates, new pairs and add-ons alike, so a sale's proceeds are cash for that checkpoint's trades, each is sized on the value after the sales, and a sold pair's markets are neither bought nor added to there (`sold_here`); the days rule sits inside the sale step and the quick test, so it cannot move that order. Every selling run records one frozen `SaleCheck(days_left, profits)` per position it sells, in order, on `SweepPoint.sales` (`()` when it sold nothing, `None` when it never sells; `repr=False`, `compare=False`; a copy `CapSweep` shares at another cap keeps it), and stamps `SweepPoint.sell_min_days`; the completion line's label adds `, at least <N> day(s) before maturity` after the sell level. `_sim_options` forwards `sell_min_days` only when set, `_half_split` and `_ex_top_event` (from `point.sell_min_days`) re-simulate at it, and `CapSweep(sell_min_days=…)` (refused without `sell_at`, checked by `_resolve_sell_min_days` in `__post_init__`) forwards it to every simulation and check; the rule reads only close dates and the checkpoint date, which no cap moves, so cap sharing stays exact. **Residual (look-ahead):** the cache's `close_time` is a settled market's REALIZED close, so for an event decided early the backtest sees maturity as near when a live trader, reading the scheduled close, would not. Pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestSellMinDays` (a position 3 days from maturity sold at N ≤ 3 and held at 4, both through the sale step and the quick test; the later market sets maturity; a lone-leg add-on matures with its latest market; 1 day against no minimum on the closing date; a missing close date; bad values refused before any entry is scored; the label; one `SaleCheck` per position sold), `::TestSalesComeBeforePurchases` (with no minimum and with one that holds nothing back: a sale funds a new pair and an add-on at its own checkpoint, each equal to live `compute_trade` on the value and cash after the sale and unaffordable without it; the sold pair is neither bought nor added to there; a sale at a checkpoint with no candidate funds the next one; a sale the days rule holds back frees no cash), `::TestCapSweepSells` (forwarding, refusal, and every cap of a `CapSweep` with a minimum equal to a fresh simulation) and by AST in `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_open_trades_are_valued_through_one_definition` (`_simulate_at_discount` and `_position_sells` call `_far_enough`, which calls `_days_left`). **When:** at every entry checkpoint (`SCHEDULED_RUN`'s weekday) from the first candidate's to the last pay-out of any candidate (`_sale_stream` over `_sale_checkpoints`) — checkpoints where nothing is bought, and those after the last purchase, included — after that day's pay-outs and before its valuation and candidates, positions in the order of their first trade. A position that pays out between two checkpoints is held to settlement. **A sale** replaces each trade of the position with its sold copy (`_sold_copy`): `exit_date` the sale day, `actual_payoff` the sale value net of the sale's fees, `profit`, `profit_ratio`, `monthly_profit_ratio`, `holding_days` and `slippage` recomputed (so `profit = actual_payoff − total_cost − fees` still holds and the equity curve needs nothing new), `sold=True`, `sale_price_a`/`_b` (the average price each leg sold at; `None` for a leg that had paid out) and `sale_fees`; `outcome_*` and `settled_date_*` keep the markets' real results, so nothing that measures the model moves (the `k̂` calibration reads entries, not trades). The cash takes the value; the tickers, ladder labels and pending exit are released; a sold pair may be bought again only at a LATER checkpoint — at the sale's checkpoint neither it nor any pair touching its markets is bought or added to — so a time-series pair through its later Kelly-passing Mondays (DR-76), while a same-title pair, only ever tried on its first passing Monday, is never bought again (the existing residual). With `add_to_held` as well, a position not sold may be added to as before. **Logs:** the completion line's label ends `, selling at <N>% of potential profit` (after `, adding to held pairs`), and two silent-at-zero lines, `Positions sold before they paid out (<label>): N` and `Pairs bought again after a sale (<label>): N`. **The size-cap sweep stays exact:** `peak_kelly_fraction` is unchanged (every Monday a pair may trade on was already scored) and the cap still reaches the walk only through the sizes, so every cap at or above a point's sharing floor (the larger of its peak and its `cap_free_from`) sizes — and sells — identically. **The exact shortcut:** `_sale_reach(point, levels)` replays the sell test on a run that never sold — at every checkpoint, the trades open there (entered before it, paying out after it) grouped into positions and valued as a sale values them — and returns, for each of the levels it is given at which some position would sell, the most days before maturity such a position had left (`_days_left`; a position with no close date is skipped). It reads each position at the checkpoint and every daily check before it (`_hold_readings`), so the lowest check decides. `_reached_every_day` holds at every level below one at which it holds, so the levels reached are a prefix of the ascending levels, and a run selling at a level not reached, or with a minimum above that level's value, walks as the no-selling run until its first sale, of which there is none, so it IS the no-selling run (only its `sell_at` and `sell_min_days` stamps differ).
