The guarantee covers the levels given, not every other share — a share between two levels given can still sell — so a caller must pass the levels it simulates (`SellSweep.sold_grid` passes its own `levels`). **The family:** `run_backtest_sweep(sell_sweep=True)` — the CLI's default, `--no-sell-sweep` the opt-out, `False` in the function — returns `BacktestSweep.sell_sweep`, a frozen `SellSweep` over `config.TAKE_PROFIT_LEVELS` (`SellSweep.levels`: 21 levels, 80% to 100% at 1%, checked and sorted by `_resolve_sell_levels`, which `run_backtest_sweep` also runs before anything is logged or fetched when the family is on, so no levels at all, a repeated level or one that is not a real number in (0, 1] is a `ValueError` up front — a repeated level would leave one of its places in the dashboard's Sell rows unfilled), `config.TAKE_PROFIT_MIN_DAYS` (`SellSweep.min_days`: the minimum-days options, 1 to 7, 14 and 21, checked and sorted by `_resolve_min_days_options`, which `run_backtest_sweep` also runs before anything is logged or fetched when the family is on, so no options at all, a repeated option or one that is not a whole number of at least 1 is a `ValueError` up front) and the add-on family's grid (its bands, `k`s and caps, tier floors on and off), holding the very entry maps the size-cap and add-on families hold (no retention of its own) and the add-on family's end-date maps. `sweep(level, *, min_days, tier_floors, add_to_held, caps)` is a `CapSweep(sell_at=level, sell_min_days=min_days)` (a `min_days` not among the options, or given with no level, is refused) — which, like `add_to_held`, refuses eager points; `sell_at` combines with `add_to_held`; `_sim_options` forwards it only when set, and `_half_split` and `_ex_top_event` re-simulate at the point's own; `cell(level, band, k, *, min_days, …)` reads one cell of it; `sold_grid(band, k, *, tier_floors, add_to_held, caps, stats)` — what the dashboard's Sell build reads — yields every (level, minimum) of one cell, levels ascending and each level's minimums ascending, each cap one of `None` (the no-selling run), a `SameSale(level, min_days)` (exactly the run it yielded earlier as a point at that setting and the same cap) or a simulated point, and every cell is exact for one of two reasons. **Pruning:** `_sale_reach(point, levels)` (the shortcut above) gives each level the most days left of any position that reaches it at every check (a position with no close date is skipped, since a minimum never sells it); a selling run walks as the no-selling run until its first sale, so (L, N) with L not a key or N above `reach[L]` is the no-selling run, and every other run sells at least once (`sold_grid` raises `RuntimeError` on a simulated run with no sale). **Covering:** `_sale_cover(sales, levels)` reduces a simulated run's `SweepPoint.sales` to the fewest days left among them and the index of the highest level every sale reached at every check, and a run at (L0, N0) is the same run as any (L, N) with L0 ≤ L ≤ that level and N0 ≤ N ≤ those days: the stricter setting sells nothing the run did not, and makes every sale it made, so the two walks decide alike at every checkpoint — whatever a position's days left do over time (an add-on to a lone leg can join a market that closes later). The cover needs both days bounds, since a run from an earlier level can have a larger minimum than the cell's, and each comparison is tested: the two upper bounds by `_sale_cover` mutants (a cover that drops its days bound or its level bound) failing the parity check over the fixture's four options, and the lower days bound and the prune's boundary (`min_days > top`, never `>=`) by a parity check over every minimum from 1 to 61 days — every boundary the fixture's sales and reaches set — which fails on `sold_grid`'s source compiled with either comparison changed (the four options never sit on a boundary, so they cannot tell those two apart). Between yields it keeps only each simulated run's (level index, minimum, cover), never a point; its `stats` gain `same` and `pruned` cell counts beside `CapSweep`'s `simulated` and `reused`. On the test suite's selling golden fixture (8 settings × 4 levels × 4 minimums × 20 caps = 2,560 cells) it yields 826 points, 666 `SameSale` cells and 1,068 pruned (1,032 at a level no position reaches, 36 at a minimum past the reach), simulating 802 cap points in all, the no-selling runs included, and sharing 184 more across caps; `for_band(band, *, tier_floors)` is the one-band copy a worker process is handed; and `entry_events()` covers both Tier floors settings. Nothing is simulated during the run: one setting line (`Selling early (backtest): on — …`) and one summary line; `None` on the Monday-infeasible window. Pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestLegQuotesBids` (the daily bids too), `::TestSellAtShareOfPotentialProfit`, `::TestSellingWithAddOns`, `::TestSaleNeedsDaysInARow` (a level reached at the checkpoint alone, a day below it or a day with no quote holds the sale back; 1, 2 and 7 days; a leg paid out between checks; a bad day count refused before any work), `::TestSaleReach` (on three fixtures, one with add-ons and one whose checkpoint is above the days before it, with and without a depth model, each level's reach is, by brute force, the largest minimum at which a run still sells, and the next minimum — at a level not reached, the first — is the no-selling run itself; the lowest check decides the highest level reached), `::TestSaleCover` and `::TestSellSweep` (every level and cap equals a fresh simulation; every (level, minimum, cap) of `sold_grid` equals a fresh simulation — a `SameSale` the earlier point it names, `None` the no-selling run — with only the cells yielded as points simulated, every kind of cell present, and the parity check failing on a cover that drops either bound; the run adds no simulation; a band's copy holds that band's entries alone and, pickled, reads the grid as the family does; every minimum from 1 to 61 days equal to a fresh simulation, failing on `sold_grid` with no lower days bound or with the reach itself pruned; a bad `TAKE_PROFIT_LEVELS` or `TAKE_PROFIT_MIN_DAYS` refused before any fetch), and by AST in `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_open_trades_are_valued_through_one_definition` (a market's bid, paid-out marker and sale ladder are read only in `_position_sale_value`, and the ladder is built only by `LegQuotes.sale_ladder`; `_hold_readings` is the only caller of `_position_sale_value`, and only it and `_reached_every_day` call `_sells_at`; the walk, `_position_sells` and `_sale_reach` each call `_hold_readings` and `_reached_every_day`, and `_sale_cover` calls `_reached_every_day` — the pin checks which functions call which, while the behaviour tests pin how many days each site checks). **Residuals, recorded:** a sale walks a modeled bid ladder, with the limits the walked-book gotcha records, and with no depth model, no volume data or a bid below 1c or above 99c it fills at the candle's bid in any size; the daily checks sample one moment a day, so a dip between two checks goes unseen, and they read the same hourly candles, which have gaps, so a quiet day holds a sale back too (on 4,000 randomly chosen cached candle series, measured 2026-10-03 at 16:00 UTC each day: a fresh candle stood at 68% of the daily moments, and of the Mondays with one, both of the two days before had a quote in their 24 hours 54% of the time); a position with add-ons pays the sale's fee per trade per leg, up to a cent more per extra trade per market than one combined order would; the corpus holds settled markets only, so the window's last weeks hold only pairs that have paid out, and a pair sold before its markets settled is missing until they do; and the live path (`seller.plan_sales`, `trader.sell_positions`) is a separate implementation of the same rule, with the differences the live-selling paragraph records.

**The bar's Sell select: worker processes and sidecar chunk files (2026-10; it reports a backtest, and the Save button sends its choice as the live sell level).** The filter bar's eighth select, after Add to held pairs (`_filter_bar_html`, id `flt-sell`, `SHOWN[7]`), offers "no selling" — the page as rendered — and every level of `config.TAKE_PROFIT_LEVELS` ("sell at 80% of potential profit" … "100%"), over every scenario the bar shows: every band, Tier floors choice, `k`, size cap and Add to held pairs choice, with category and tag slices as before. The ninth, "Min. days to maturity" (id `flt-days`, `SHOWN[8]`, before Category), offers every option of `config.TAKE_PROFIT_MIN_DAYS` ("1 day" … "21 days"; its value the option's index into the base block's `sell_days`, the first selected; words `_sell_days_option`, `_sell_days_phrase` and the hover text `_SELL_DAYS_TITLE`, all Python's): a position at its level is sold only while at least that many days remain before its last market stops trading. It has no "no selling" option, since Sell already has one: the script keeps it disabled while Sell says "no selling" and on a page without the Sell view (`refreshDays`, run on load, in `draw()`, on a Sell change and when a choice is put back), and its change listener draws only while a level is chosen. **How it is built.** `dashboard._with_sell(source, sweep)` attaches `BacktestSweep.sell_sweep` to the walked grid only when its bands and `k`s are the grid's and its caps include the grid's (so the walk's own-cap fallback grid still fits), adding its `entry_events()` to the bar's categories and tags up front; otherwise one WARNING and the select stays disabled. The page's one walk does not read the family: after it, `_build_sell_grid` makes one `_SellTask` per (Tier floors setting, band) — the family narrowed to that band (`SellSweep.for_band`), the page's `k`s, caps and axis, the cells the page shows there, the labels and series categories, the rates, the page's own chunks' list keys, and the parent's `backtester.SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP`, `SCHEDULED_RUN` and `TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS`, which a worker installs before simulating (a spawned process imports `backtester` afresh, from `config`) — and runs them through `_run_sell_tasks`: in the main process with `sell_workers=1` (what every test but one uses), else on a `ProcessPoolExecutor` with the SPAWN start method (safe on macOS, where fork is not). So a script that calls `generate_dashboard(..., sell_workers=N)` with N > 1 must keep its entry point under `if __name__ == "__main__"`, as `backtest.py` does: a spawned worker re-imports the caller's main module. A worker (`_run_sell_task`) reads every (level, minimum) of each cell through `SellSweep.sold_grid` — the no-selling run first, then per cap a simulated point, a `SameSale` naming an earlier setting of the same cap that is exactly the same run, or `None` where the setting sells nothing — fills one row per cell, one entry per (level, minimum) (a `SameSale` copies the entry its earlier setting left in the same row, with no simulation and no file), computes each simulated run's view with the helpers the page renders with (`_list_payload`, on curves cut to the page's axis, with a row-head table of its own), writes each list whose `_list_key` is new and not already in the page as a sidecar file, and holds no point across yields. Its `_SellResult` is compact, since the parent holds every task's result until the last task ends: `keys` (every distinct list key its rows name) and `rows` — `(adds, k index, cap index)` → an `array("i")` with one entry per (level, minimum), level by level, each the key's place in `keys` or -1 for the run without selling — beside the keys written and `sold_grid`'s four counts (`simulated`, `reused`, `same`, `pruned`). The parent numbers the files in task order (`chunk-<id>.js`, the ids continuing from the page's own chunks), so a build's files never depend on which worker finished first (`tests/test_dashboard.py::TestSellGrid::test_worker_processes_build_what_the_parent_builds` compares a 2-worker spawned build with the in-process one, blocks and files), and builds a `_SellGrid(blocks, index, levels, days, sidecars)`: one packed block per finished task, in task order (`<script id="dash-sell-<i>">`, written into the page right after its own chunks and before the base block, and dropped with the bar on every path that drops it), holding `[adds 0/1]` → null for a base view the page lacks, else `[k][cap]` → null for a cell not covered, else one entry per (level, minimum), level by level and each level's minimums in order: the chunk id, or -1 for the scenario's own chunk (the run without selling); and the index `[tier 0/1][band]` → block number or null, which the base block carries as `sell_blocks` beside `sell_levels`, `sell_days` and `sell_state` (with the tier-floors-off view, a band no tier binds at names its tier-on block, as `grid_off` shows its tier-on run). The chunk ids stay out of the base block because the page inflates that block on load, and 189 settings per scenario would put about 7 M ids there (~50 MB of JSON) for the 365-day page. A task that fails costs its own band's cells (one WARNING naming the band; its index entry null, its files deleted, its cells shown as not simulated); a build that cannot run at all costs the select (one WARNING, `sell_state` "unavailable"). Progress is logged per band, and the build closes on `Dashboard: Sell select built in N s: N cap points simulated, N shared across caps, N cells repeating an earlier run, N cells without a sale; N chunk file(s) (X MB) in <folder>`. **Sidecar files.** Each holds `window.__dashChunk(document.currentScript,"<gzip+base64>");`, in `backtest_dashboard_files/<build id>/` beside the page (`config.DASHBOARD_FILES_DIRNAME`); the base block carries `inline_chunks` (how many chunks the page itself holds — every id at or past it is a sidecar) and `sidecar_dir`. A page opened from disk may load a script beside it but cannot `fetch()` one, so `_FILTER_JS`'s `load` appends a `<script src>` element and inflates the text the file hands over with the same code as an inline block (`inflateText`); a file that cannot be loaded, or hands nothing over, is named on the summary line in Python's words (`_SIDECAR_MISSING` — which says to keep the folder beside the page — and `_SIDECAR_EMPTY`), and every select goes back to what is still shown (`putBack`, the Sell and Min. days choices included). **The Sell blocks in the script.** `chunkAt(b, k, c, t, a, s, d)` has three results: a chunk id; null for a scenario the run never simulated; or `undefined` while a level's band block is not yet inflated. `choose()` then shows the loading line, keeps Save disabled, inflates the block once (`loadSell`, kept in `SELL` for the page's life; a failure is dropped so a later choice can retry) and chooses again if no later choice came first; a block that cannot be inflated puts every select back (`putBack`). -1 resolves to the base grid's id (`gridAt(t, a)`, which holds no Sell branch). `summary()`, `saveHref()` (which returns null while the scenario's chunk is not loaded or was never simulated, and otherwise sends the Sell level and minimum shown) and `load()`'s keep test read `chunkAt` knowing the three results. `scenarioAt(b, k, c, t, a, s, d)` adds the level's phrase and then the minimum's (`D.sell_days[d].phrase`). **The folder's lifecycle**, under an flock on `backtest_dashboard_files/.lock`: a build makes its folder with a `.writing` marker; `_publish_page` then, under the lock, renames the new page over the old one, deletes every other build folder — except one still marked as being written and younger than `config.DASHBOARD_BUILD_STALE_SECONDS` (two days; an older mark is a crashed build) — and removes its own marker, so the page in place always has its folder. The rename is the publication: nothing after it raises (a folder that cannot be listed, read or deleted is a WARNING, and one whose mark cannot be read is kept), so the page in place never loses its own folder to a failed cleanup; and a lock that cannot be taken still publishes the page, deleting no folder, with a WARNING, as a page was always published before these folders existed. A page that fails to be written deletes its own new folder and leaves the previous page and folder, and a build that wrote no sidecar file (no sell view, or no sale anywhere) deletes its folder. Keep the page and the folder together when copying or moving them. **Choosing a level** (or a minimum) re-scopes the trade sections and the header's trade count, never the `k̂` figures, the Scenario Explorer or the Interval Discount section (`_SELL_REACH`, ending the summary line's reach sentence); the bar's call to `window.dashScenarioSelect` keeps its four arguments; "Save as live defaults…" sends the choice (`saveHref`, on a page whose base block carries the Sell view, `D.sell_blocks`: `sell_at` is the level shown and `sell_min_days` the minimum shown, and both are `off` under "no selling"; a scenario still loading or never simulated keeps the button disabled), and when the saved defaults sell, a grey note beside the button (`_SELL_SAVE_NOTE`) says that saving with "no selling" turns live selling off; the summary line adds `_SELL_SUMMARY_NOTE`; the select is reset to "no selling", and Min. days to its first option, on load; and a sold trade's row in the best/worst tables names, per leg, its sale ("sold on <day> at $<average price> (settled <X> on <day>)") or a payout that came before it (`_trade_row_head`). The golden-pinned sections are unchanged. **Cost — not yet measured on a real window:** 21 levels × 9 minimums = 189 settings per scenario, before the pruning, the repeated runs and cap sharing, divided among the workers; the build logs the real figures. On the test suite's selling fixture (1 band × 1 `k` × 20 caps × 4 levels × 9 minimums, both Tier floors settings, adding on and off, in one process) it simulated 342 cap points, shared 80, found 1,296 repeated and 1,242 unsold settings and wrote 183 files (0.5 MB) in about 4 s. `--no-sell-sweep` skips it. Pinned by `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestSellGrid` (every setting's id, -1 for the own chunk and a repeated run's the id of the run it names; full rows; a task's result one `array("i")` row per covered cell naming only its own keys; the tier-off alias; a failed band's null index and deleted files; no `grid_sell`), `::TestSellPage` (the Min. days select right after Sell, its nine options and its title; the blocks decode), `::TestSoldTradeRow`, `::TestSellScript` (the harness inflates the Sell blocks and loads sidecar files as a browser would, deferred, damaged, failed and empty loads included: Min. days shut under no selling, a level inflating its band's block then loading its file, another minimum another chunk, -1 the page's own chunk, a damaged block or a missing file putting both choices back, a later choice superseding a block still inflating, the tier-on block read with the tiers off where they do not bind), `::TestSellEndToEnd` (every level, minimum and cap of a real sweep's chunk KPIs equal a fresh `_simulate_at_discount(sell_at=…, sell_min_days=…)`, with simulated, repeated and unsold settings all present) and `::TestSidecarFolders`.

**The equity curve is a PORTFOLIO VALUE, not a cash balance — open positions are carried at COST (DR-61, fixed 2026-09-16).** `backtester._build_equity_curve` used to accumulate cash alone (`-= total_cost + fees` on the entry date, `+= actual_payoff` on the exit date), so an open position was carried at **ZERO** for its whole holding period and the curve dived on entry and recovered at settlement *whether the trade won or lost*. It now tracks cash PLUS the cost basis of everything still open, which leaves a trade exactly two moves: **`-fees` on the entry date** (cash falls by `total_cost + fees`, the contracts enter at `total_cost`) and **`+(actual_payoff − total_cost)` on the exit date** (the position is written off at cost, the gross receipt credited). The two sum to the trade's own `profit`, so **`total_return`, the final balance and the trade list are identical to within float noise — only the PATH between entry and settlement moves.** ("Identical to the last cent", not literally byte-identical: the position accumulator adds `+cost` on entry days and `−cost` on exit days, so when entries and exits interleave the pair need not cancel to exactly `0.0` in IEEE754. Fuzzed worst case ~4e-12 on a $10,000 balance — under a ULP of the rendered `%.2f`, which is why the parity test uses `pytest.approx(abs=1e-9)` rather than `==`.) That endpoint invariance has one unstated precondition: every trade must settle inside `[start_date, today]`. It does in production — `exit_date` comes from the `settlement_ts` of two already-settled markets — but a trade exiting past the curve's last row would stay carried at cost forever, ending at `initial − fees` where the cash-only curve ended at `initial − cost − fees`. Fees are deliberately NOT capitalised into the carrying value: they buy nothing that can be sold on, so they land on the day they are charged. **Why it mattered:** that series is the sole input to the "Max Drawdown" KPI, the "Drawdown (%)" chart, `_sharpe`/`_sortino` (via the derived `daily_return` column), the per-`k` sweep table's drawdown and Sharpe columns, and the benchmark row that sits in the SAME COLUMN as `^GSPC`'s genuine mark-to-market drawdown — so every one of them was reporting capital DEPLOYMENT as loss. The real 2026-05-01 run is the proof: its **`k = 1.00` point had 3 trades, all 3 profitable and a +4.8% return, and rendered "Max Drawdown −60.0%"**, while its `k = 0.40` point rendered **−100.0% (total ruin) against a final balance of $4,655.87**; the decoded `k = 1.00` trace was flat at $10,000 through 2026-05-24, ONE declining step to $4,001.03 on 2026-05-25 (a pure deployment day), then monotonically non-decreasing. The primary `k = 0.750` card meanwhile showed Sharpe 0.73 and Sortino 2.09 beside a Total Return of −27.0% — the same inversion from the other end. **The accepted cost** is that carrying at cost is not a true daily mark-to-market: an unrealized swing inside the holding period is invisible, so the reported drawdown is REALIZED drawdown and a lower bound on the intraperiod one. Mark-to-market was considered and rejected as materially larger, but **not because the data is missing**: `backtester._fetch_candles_parallel` already fetches each leg's WHOLE hourly series (from the later of `start_date` midnight UTC and the market's own `open_time`, floored to the hour, through one day past its close) and disk-caches it per ticker (a window longer than Kalshi's 5,000-candle-per-request cap is paged — see the scenario-explorer paragraph above). What mark-to-market actually costs is PLUMBING plus a policy — `_find_entry` returns entry-checkpoint prices only, so `candles_by_ticker` lives on the `_Candidates` object only until the last entry pass (it dies with `_prepare_entries` on `run_backtest`'s path, and `_sweep_from_candidates` deletes it before its first simulation), and a per-day series would have to be threaded through `raw_entries` and `_simulate_at_discount` into `_build_equity_curve`, with a rule for the days a leg has no candle. Price it as a data-threading change, never as a new multi-hour fetch. Cost-basis carry is the minimal change that makes each metric mean what its label says; if it is ever revisited, the direction is a strict refinement of this, not a return to cash-only. **DR-03 is untouched and is the constraint any future change here must keep**: the curve still opens one day BEFORE `start_date` at the untouched initial balance, and that row is still what keeps the `cummax` peak at the initial balance rather than at the already-charged day-0 value (see the DR-03 sentences above for the measured before/after). **Every backtest drawdown, Sharpe and Sortino figure ever recorded before this change measured peak deployment and does not reproduce** — including the DR-03/DR-56 fixture log in the paragraph above, which is now marked historical. `_simulate_at_discount`'s own Pass-2 accounting (`cash`, `open_trades`, `checkpoint_value`) is SIZING and is kept apart from the curve: the equity curve is reporting-only, and the two must not be unified. Since 2026-09-29 Pass 2 values its open trades the way the curve does — at cost until 2026-09-30, at market since — when it sizes each Monday on the portfolio value (see the portfolio-value paragraph under Key Patterns); it keeps its own running figures rather than reading the curve. With `add_to_held`, an add-on is sized on that same portfolio value, while its pair's stake is the pair's open trades at that same valuation plus the fees paid for them, which the value has already lost; `tests/test_backtester.py::TestAddToHeldPairs::test_the_account_value_is_the_equity_curve_s` pins the portfolio value an add-on is sized on to the curve's value at the close before its Monday (its trades have no quotes, so both are at cost). **This paragraph is the history: since 2026-09-30 an open trade with quotes is valued at market (next paragraph), the strict refinement it anticipated.**

**Open trades are valued at market, on the curve and in sizing (user decision, 2026-09-30).** The backtest values an open trade the way live values a held pair: each leg at the ask of the side it holds (a YES leg at the YES ask, a NO leg at the NO ask), the latest usable one on an hourly candle at or before the moment — the end of each UTC day for the curve, the entry checkpoint for sizing; at its payout (1 or 0 a contract) once its market has paid out; and at the price it paid (`_paid_prices`) only before its side has had a usable ask. A usable ask is a number strictly between 0 and 1 (`backtester._usable_ask`, the rule live's `scanner._held_leg_worth` applies to a held pair's ask), and for a NO ask also below 0.99 (`backtester._CANDLE_NO_ASK_CEILING`): the fetch stores a candle's NO ask as 1 − its YES bid clamped into 0.01–0.99, so 0.99 is also what an empty YES-bid book reads as. A side keeps its last usable ask through anything else — an empty YES ask book (1.00), an unreadable candle, a NO ask at the clamp, the time between its last candle and its payout — so a nearly won leg is not marked back down to the price it paid, and a sub-cent ask on a fine grid counts as the price it is. The prices come from the candles the backtest already fetches, so nothing new is fetched: `backtester._attach_leg_quotes`, the one writer, samples them into one `LegQuotes` per market (its day-end and checkpoint asks and its payouts, with the bids and paid-out markers the sell rule reads and the 24-hour volumes a modeled book reads; no candle list is kept, about 47 bytes per market per day of its life) and stores the pair's two on each entry record as `rec["leg_quotes"]`, in `_prepare_entries` after its entry pass and in `_sweep_from_candidates` just before the candles are released; `_simulate_at_discount`'s Pass 1b, the one reader, hands each trade its two markets' quotes as `BacktestTrade.marks` (not compared and not shown, but read by the dashboard's `_list_key` through each `LegQuotes.fingerprint`, so two trade lists equal but for their quotes, which draw different curves, never share a chunk). `_open_value(trade, day)` is the one valuation at a checkpoint (Pass 2's portfolio value, and an add-on's stake), and `_open_value_path(trade)` the one day-end path, from which `_carry_steps` (what the curve carries in the trade) and `_value_steps` (what it adds to the portfolio value each day) are built; `_build_equity_curve`, `dashboard._return_by_trade_type` and `dashboard._carried_on_days` (what the risk-free hurdle charges) all read them, pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_open_trades_are_valued_through_one_definition` (one writer and one reader of `leg_quotes`, and every reader of the quotes through those helpers). A trade with no quotes (`marks` is `None`: every hand-built trade, and a pair whose markets have no candles) is valued exactly as before, at cost: its steps are the two at-cost steps, bit for bit, and the equivalence check run for this change (the code with `_attach_leg_quotes` switched off against the code before it, on the golden fixture with every family and cap cell, the ladder and cap-bound fixtures, four seeded corpora and a corpus whose prices move, comparing trades, curves, checks, logs and the dashboard page's bytes) found no difference. What does not change: a trade's steps still sum to its profit, so every curve still ends at the start plus every trade's profit, and DR-03's leading row stays. What changes: a swing while a trade is open now shows in the drawdown, Sharpe and Sortino, the trades themselves move through sizing (a portfolio value that moves with the market), and the curves change on most days a trade is open, so the page carries more points. Every curve stays daily, by the user's choice (2026-09-30; the explorer still turns weekly only above 400 rows, `dashboard._EQUITY_DAILY_MAX_ROWS`, as at cost), so on the test fixtures the filter page grows 823,634 → 1,397,952 bytes (+70%) and a full 20-cap explorer grid's blocks 249,188 → 7,608,372 bytes (`tests/test_dashboard.py::TestFilterPageSize`, `::TestExplorerFullGrid`); the quoted page-size budgets are their measurements + 20%, with no ceiling. A real 365-day page has not been measured at market. Pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestLegQuotes`, `::TestSizesAtMarket`, `::TestSameTitleFlipAtMarket`, `::TestAddOnStakeAtMarket`, `::TestMarketCapSweepParity` (every cap of the size-cap, tier-off, add-on and add-on tier-off sweeps equals a fresh simulation on a corpus whose prices move), `::TestUnquotedExactness`, and `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestOpenTradesAtMarket`. Known limits, recorded: a quote has no age limit, so a leg that stops trading keeps its last usable ask until it pays out (a flat line, then a jump; a DEBUG count, silent at zero, names the legs valued on a quote more than 7 days old), and that covers a nearly won leg whose book has emptied, which stays at its last usable ask (a YES leg below 1.00, a NO leg below 0.99) rather than its payout until it pays out; the ask overstates what a leg would sell for, by the spread; a real NO ask of 0.99 cannot be told from the clamp, so it is not used either; candles end one day after a market's close, so a leg that pays out later is valued on its last usable ask until then; and a swing inside one day is not seen. **Every backtest result recorded before 2026-09-30 valued open trades at cost**, in sizing and on the curve, so its sizes, drawdowns, Sharpe and Sortino are not comparable.

**The backtest walks a modeled order book (2026-10, backtest-only).** Kalshi keeps no historical order books, only hourly candles, so the backtest used to fill every trade at one candle price, in any size. It now sizes each trade over a modeled book, with the live bot's own code. **The depth table.** `python3 -m kalshi_betting.depth_model snapshot` saves the live books of up to `config.DEPTH_SNAPSHOT_MARKETS` open non-combo markets, each with its 24-hour volume read from its recent candles (read-only GETs, under `backtest_cache/depth_snapshots/`). `depth_model.fit` builds a table from every saved snapshot: for each 24-hour volume bucket (`config.DEPTH_MODEL_VOLUME_EDGES`) and best-bid price band (`DEPTH_MODEL_PRICE_EDGES`), the median contracts resting within each distance of the best bid (`DEPTH_MODEL_DISTANCES`, 0 to 20c). One table serves buys and sales, since buying YES walks the NO bids and selling YES walks the YES bids. A cell with fewer than `DEPTH_MODEL_MIN_LADDERS` ladders reads its volume row, then the whole table. `backtest.py` fits it before the fetch. **The book at a checkpoint.** `LegQuotes.book_at` builds each market's book from the table, the market's 24-hour volume at that checkpoint (`depth_model.volume_24h` on the cached candles, the function the snapshot reads volume with) and the candle's own quotes: the YES bids start at 1 minus the NO ask and the NO bids at 1 minus the YES ask, so the top of the book is the quote `_find_entry` priced, to within float noise. **Sizing.** `_size_trade` passes the two books to `scanner._enrich_pair` and then `strategy.compute_trade`, the live code, under one `config.LiveSettings` per simulation, with the portfolio value and the cash in whole cents (`_cents`). So the live caps, the edge-after-fee cut, the price-sum ceiling, the crossed-book guards, the FoK reachability check and Kelly at each size (with the chance of profit read from the mid spread at the book's top, as live sizing reads it) all apply, and a trade pays the average price over the levels it walks. `BacktestTrade.fill_price_a`/`_b` record that average, and `_paid_prices` is the one reader of what a trade paid: the open-trade valuations, the equity curve, the dashboard's trade rows and its Kelly scatter read it there. The entry scan (`_find_entry`), Pass 1b's Kelly gate (at the top of the book: the prices of one contract pair, the smallest size the live sizer can return), the `k̂` calibration and the dashboard's entry-price buckets stay on the candle quotes. A candidate whose walked book `_enrich_pair` refuses, or for which no size fits, is skipped and counted by reason (`Trades refused when their book was walked (<reason>)`), except one the cash left cannot buy one contract pair of: `_enrich_pair` calls it unaffordable and the cash, not the Kelly share of the portfolio value, sets its budget (`scanner._cash_binds`, the test behind enrichment's "the $C of cash binds" note). The book did not cause that skip, and a run with no depth model skips the same candidate uncounted, so it has its own line, `Trades skipped with no cash left for one contract pair`. Both lines are silent at zero and DEBUG when `quiet`. **Sales.** A sale walks the modeled bid ladder from the bid of the side held (`LegQuotes.sale_ladder`), at the checkpoint and at each daily check before it (`config.TAKE_PROFIT_HOLD_DAYS`), each ladder built from that check's own 24-hour volume (`LegQuotes.volume_checkpoints` at the checkpoint, `volume_daily` at an earlier check); a position larger than its ladder at any of them is not sold that Monday. With no depth model, no volume data at that check, or a bid below 1c or above 99c (`depth_model.can_start_at`), it sells at that one bid in any size (see the selling paragraph). **No depth data.** With no depth model, or no volume data for either market that Monday, `compute_trade` sizes at the candle prices (the top of the book, in any size). The trade has `book_walked=False` and is counted: in the log (`Trades sized at the top of the book (no depth data)`, silent at zero) and in the dashboard's Fills line, which is red when the run had no depth model at all. **The size-cap sweep.** Over a walked book the cap also bounds how deep the live enrichment averages (`config.max_kelly_fraction`), so a point records `cap_free_from`: the larger of its peak Kelly fraction and, for each pair type with a candidate whose Monday has a book, the cap above which that bound stops moving (`round(1 − k, 12)` for time-series; the lower of the same-title cap and `SAME_TITLE_CO_RESOLVE_PROB` for same-title). It is decided from the candidates, never from the trades reached, because which trades are reached depends on the cap. `CapSweep` shares one simulation only at caps at or above `max(peak_kelly_fraction, cap_free_from)` (`_sharing_floor`, in both its sharing branches), so a cap below `1 − k` is simulated even where it is above the peak. The add-on and Sell families build `CapSweep`s and inherit the rule (pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestCapSweepSharesFromCapFreeFrom`, below). So a higher cap can give a smaller walked trade, or none: the cap sets how deep enrichment averages the book, and that bounds the live sizer's search. **What the live sizer demands.** The backtest's `k` must be above 0 and at most 1, and its caps on the 5% grid (`config.SIZE_CAP_STEP`), exactly as `LiveSettings` requires; `backtest.py --interval-discount 0` exits 2. **Recorded limits.** The table does not vary with the time of day or the time to close. A snapshot is a thin sample: more of them, taken near Monday 09:00 PT, fit it better. A candle dropped for a missing price loses its hour's volume. When `--start-date` is a Monday, that first Monday sees only the hours since its midnight UTC (about 16 hours of volume), because candle requests open there. The book is typical depth for a market like it, not that day's real book. **Every backtest result recorded before this change filled every trade at one candle price, in any size, so none is comparable:** trade sizes, which trades enter, returns, drawdowns and every figure that follows from them move. Pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestWalkedTradeParity` (a walked trade is the live spec on the same books, value and cash: time-series, same-title and add-on; a walked trade the cash left cannot buy is counted on its own line), `::TestLegQuotesCarryTheBook` (the volume at each checkpoint and each day's check, the model and the book survive a pickle round trip, as the dashboard's Sell workers need), `::TestPositionSaleValueWalksTheLadder` and `::TestWalkedSales` (a sale at the ladder's average; a ladder too thin for the position never sells; a quiet day before a sale, whose ladder is too thin, holds it back), `::TestCapSweepSharesFromCapFreeFrom` (what pins sharing over a walked book: in every kind of `CapSweep` — the size-cap family, tier floors off, adding to held pairs, selling, and with the split-half and excluding-top-event checks — every cap equals a fresh simulation, on fixtures where a cap between the peak and `cap_free_from` buys something different; each case fails if `_sharing_floor` returns the peak alone), `::TestWalkedCapSweepParity` (every cap of the size-cap, tier-off, add-on and sell families equals a fresh simulation on the walked golden fixture; it still passes if sharing ignores `cap_free_from`), `::TestCapFreeFrom`, `::TestNoDepthModelChangesNothing` (with no model the golden band sweep's eager trades, curves and checks are exactly the ones the code before the walk gave, and so is each point's peak Kelly fraction except where a crossed quote had set it, which the Kelly gate now skips — DR-78; elsewhere, since the sizer reads the value and the cash in whole cents, a no-model trade whose cost meets its budget or the cash exactly to the cent can differ by one contract pair, and an add-on's recorded `kelly_fraction` can differ in its last digits) and `::TestDepthModelIsRecorded`; `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_the_price_paid_and_the_book_each_have_one_reader`, `::test_ast_each_simulation_hands_the_live_code_its_own_settings` and `::test_ast_no_live_module_imports_the_depth_model`; `tests/test_backtest.py::TestDepthModel` (the model reaches the sweep, loaded before the fetch, and the echo's `fills=` clause); and `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestFillsHeader` (the Fills line's three states) and `::TestFillsOnRowsAndScatter` (trade rows and the Kelly scatter at the fills, the entry quotes kept where they belong, chunk keys that separate lists differing only in fills).

**Sharpe and Sortino subtract the 8-week T-bill yield in force each day (2026-09, backtest dashboard only).** `generate_dashboard(risk_free=)` takes a `treasury.RiskFreeRates` (see the `treasury.py` Module Map row), and every ratio on the page subtracts, row by row, the yield of the latest 8-week bill auction on or before that row's date (`RiskFreeRates.annual_on`) — per period, `rf / 365` on a strategy curve and `rf / 252` on the `^GSPC` row's own trading days, so the `sqrt(365/252)` identity above now holds only at `rf = 0` — charged on a strategy curve's capital in open trades only (rule 3). Four rules. (1) **`rf` is POSITIONAL.** `annual_on` returns an `np.ndarray` on purpose and `_sharpe`/`_sortino` subtract `np.asarray(rf)`: a date-indexed Series subtracted from a RangeIndex returns Series aligns on the index and turns every ratio into NaN, and `_view_payload` cuts a curve to the page's axis without resetting its index — each caller takes the hurdle from the SAME frame whose `daily_return` it passes, and a wrong-length array raises. (2) **A curve that never moves reads 0.0** (`dashboard._varies`, checked first in both): with a nonzero rate every flat day is the same negative excess return, which read `_sharpe` −9.6e16 (a float std of ~1e-20, not 0) and `_sortino` exactly `−sqrt(365)` on any zero-trade scenario — both at a constant rate; at a rate that changes inside the window each is a large finite negative instead, and the guard returns 0.0 either way. "Never moves" means its largest and smallest returns differ by at most `config.FLAT_RETURN_TOLERANCE` (1e-12) — a tolerance, not an exact comparison or rounding, so float noise alone never counts as movement. (On a strategy curve the deployed-capital hurdle of rule 3 already charges a curve with no trade nothing; the guard still matters for a direct call and the fully invested `^GSPC` row.) A wrong-length `rf` array raises `ValueError` only on a series that varies (a flat one returns 0.0 first), and a length-1 array broadcasts like a scalar. At `rf = 0` the arithmetic is bit-identical to before on every series that varies — and an equity curve's first return is always 0.0, so a curve either varies or is flat. (3) **The yield is charged on deployed capital only; idle cash is taken to earn it** (operator decision, 2026-09-27). The backtester books idle cash at 0%, so the first version, which charged the whole balance, charged every idle day the full rate: under the tests' two-auction `_rates()` (3% then 5%) the `_ex_sweep` fixture's card read Sharpe 1.10 → −3.33 and Sortino 3.70 → −8.57, and one $3.50 trade on a $10,000 curve (`make_trade()`) Sharpe 1.03 → −234.59, both over 267 rows. A strategy curve's row `t` is now charged `annual_t × f_t` (`dashboard._rf_hurdle(risk_free, equity_df, trades)`), where `f_t = open[t−1] / portfolio_value[t−1]` (`f_0 = 0`) and `open` is what the curve carries in every trade with entry ≤ row date < exit (`dashboard._carried_on_days`: its value at market for a trade with quotes, from the backtester's one day-end path, and its cost WITHOUT fees for one without, matched on exact dates through `_deployed_on_days(..., include_fees=False)`; with nothing quoted it is exactly that cost) — so a trade is charged exactly `exit − entry` days and a same-day trade nothing; the same two fixtures now read 1.10 / 3.70 (Sharpe 1.0961 → 1.0954, Sortino 3.7000 → 3.6968) and 1.03 / 9.13 (Sharpe 1.0297 → 1.0277, Sortino 9.1475 → 9.1269), 267 rows. A curve with no trade held overnight equals `rf = 0` exactly. **Why what the curve carries, without fees:** with idle cash earning the yield `y`, a row's excess return is `r + y·cash/V − y = r − y·open/V`, where `V = cash + open` is the portfolio value — which carries an open position at market (at its cost when it has no quotes), its fees already spent — so `open` must be the very value `V` holds. Then `f ≤ 1` wherever cash ≥ 0, which the backtester's sizing guarantees on every curve it simulates (`_simulate_at_discount` never spends more than its running cash); a category or tag slice's curve (the starting balance plus that slice's own trades — a contribution, not a simulation) can exceed 1 only where the slice's trades were sized on another slice's winnings, its own cash negative, which the same identity then charges the yield as borrowing. (An earlier cut of this rule divided the fee-inclusive capital deployed by `V`, which overstated `f` by the open trades' fees and let it top 1 on a fully deployed row.) A row whose previous value is not positive (a ruined curve) is charged nothing, without dividing. Every strategy site hands the hurdle the curve's OWN trades — `_performance_kpis`' `trades`, `_kd_cells`' and `_point_kpis`' `point.trades`, `_strategy_row(trades=)` (`_view_payload` passes the view's `sel`, so a category or tag slice is charged on its own trades; `generate_dashboard` passes the run's through `_section_benchmark(trades=)`) — and rates without trades raise `TypeError`, never a silent full-balance or zero hurdle. The `^GSPC` row is fully invested and keeps the whole yield (`_rf_hurdle_invested`). **One definition of capital deployed, read two ways:** `_deployed_on_days(trades, days, *, include_fees)` (per-day sums with `np.add.at` in trade order, exact-match lookup, a sequential `cumsum`, a `> 0.0` floor), whose required keyword makes each caller say which it reads — the Risk section's "Capital Deployed Over Time" chart stays FEE-INCLUSIVE (`_capital_deployed`, `include_fees=True`; TS-12 made that deliberate, and its golden digest did not move), the hurdle reads the cost alone (`include_fees=False`) for every trade with no quotes, through `_carried_on_days`, and a quoted trade's value at market instead. With fees it is bit-identical to the per-row loop it replaced for `datetime.date` trade dates (`BacktestTrade`'s type) — pinned by `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestCapitalDeployedParity` (the loop kept verbatim as its oracle, over off-axis entries and exits, same-day, overlapping, Timestamp and tz-aware date columns, NaN and `−0.0` costs and 400 random fixtures) and by the Risk section's golden digest; a trade dated with a datetime or Timestamp now matches its calendar day, where the loop's dict lookup matched nothing; a `None` date matches nothing, as before (`_trade_day`), so a hand-built trade never raises in the Risk section. A trade whose ENTRY falls before the curve's first row is never added but its exit is still subtracted, so the running sum goes negative and masks later trades (floored at 0 on output) — unreachable in production, since a curve opens on `start_date − 1` and is only ever cut at its end, and the loop's behaviour too. A curve's dates are converted to day numbers once and read for both the yield (`RiskFreeRates.annual_on_days`) and the open capital. (4) **`None` subtracts 0%** and reads no date or trade — every direct section call and `tests/dashboard_golden.py` pass none, so no golden digest moved — and a grey header line under the run-settings and live-rule lines says so (`_risk_free_html`: grey when downloaded, with the window's average yield and the latest auction; amber when the download failed and an earlier download stands in; red when neither exists and every ratio subtracts 0% — worded "no usable earlier download is saved", like the loader's WARNING, since a copy saved for another term or field, a corrupt one, one stamped naive or out of range, or an unreadable directory is no copy either; with rates it says the yield is charged on the capital in open trades at each previous close (so a same-day trade is charged nothing), idle cash is taken to earn the same yield, and the S&P 500 row, when shown, is fully invested and is charged the whole yield). A cached download time is normalized to UTC as it is loaded (`treasury._read_cache`), so a hand-edited stamp with no UTC instant (`9999-12-31T23:30:00-01:00`, `0001-01-01T00:30:00+01:00`) is no usable copy rather than an `OverflowError` out of the header after a finished backtest, and `_risk_free_html` itself reads such a stamp on a hand-built `RiskFreeRates` as "at a time not recorded". The rate is one per page, so no chunk or explorer cache key carries it, and neither page script computes a ratio. Every function and visitor between `generate_dashboard` and a ratio takes `risk_free` keyword-only and passes it on, stored in the visitors' `__init__` (never `reset()`, which the walk's fallback calls again); `tests/test_dashboard.py::TestRiskFreeIsThreaded` pins by AST — on VALUES, not just keywords — that every `_sharpe`/`_sortino` `rf` is a hurdle helper's result (`_rf_hurdle`/`_rf_hurdle_invested`, called directly or through a name bound only from one in the same function) whose first argument is the page's `risk_free`; that every `risk_free=` keyword passes a name or attribute spelled `risk_free` (so `risk_free=None` fails); that every in-module call to a keyword-only `risk_free` taker passes it; and that a call passing rates to a taker that also takes a keyword-only `trades` (`_strategy_row`, `_section_benchmark`) passes trades, never `None` — each rule fail-closed (it asserts the takers, the ratio sites and both hurdle helpers were found) and self-tested on mutant snippets, since a dropped or `None` pass-through would compute that path at 0% with no error. The pin checks how each pass-through is spelled, not what the name is bound to: a local rebinding, a walrus or a helper taking `risk_free` positionally passes it — the by-value page tests are what catch those. Two page tests cover the paths a `risk_free=None` mutant slipped past before the pin checked values: the scenario explorer rebuilt from the sweep's own points (`_new_explorer_visitor` returning `None`) and the static Interval Discount section (`_filter_payload` raising), each through `generate_dashboard`. **Page-build cost**, measured 2026-09-27 (min of 3 builds, `generate_dashboard` with vs without 416 weekly auctions): TestScenarioExplorerPageSize's 468-cell page (a 2,459-day curve, 88 trades) 2.50 → 3.34 s, TestExplorerFullGrid's 36 × 13 × 20 size-cap grid 7.47 → 7.91 s; the full-balance hurdle this replaced cost about the same (2.54 → 3.30 s and 7.10 → 8.15 s in the same session). The largest single piece (about 40% of `_rf_hurdle`'s ~0.45 ms on a 2,460-row curve with 88 trades, that page's count; a third of ~0.52 ms with 300) is converting each curve's dates to day numbers (`treasury.day_numbers`, ~0.17 ms per 2,460 `datetime.date` rows through `toordinal`, against ~0.27 ms through `pd.to_datetime`); a curve with no trade skips it. **`treasury.load_risk_free_rates` never raises** — a cache read that fails in a way `_load_json_cache` does not catch (an unsearchable directory's `PermissionError`, a deeply nested file's `RecursionError`), a cached yield no float can hold (`OverflowError`, skipped as an unreadable record) and any failed save each degrade with a WARNING, and a JSON `true`/`false` yield is skipped as unreadable rather than read as 1%/0% (`bool` is an `int` subclass) — and its worst case is time: a host that swallows packets costs each of `api_call_with_retry`'s 6 attempts at least the 30 s `TREASURY_API_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (30 s per resolved address — the timeout bounds each socket operation, not the whole request) plus 62 s of backoff: about 4 minutes for a single-address host, before the fallback.

**Live fetching bypasses the SDK's response models (2026-07 API drift).** The Kalshi API (a) rejects events page sizes above 200 with HTTP 400 (`MARKET_PAGE_SIZE` is now 200 and must stay ≤ 200), and (b) no longer populates the legacy integer fields the pinned SDK's response models type as required: markets lost `yes_ask`/`no_bid`/… (prices now only in `*_dollars` strings) and positions lost `position`/`market_exposure`/… (count now in the `position_fp` string) — so modeled calls that deserialize nested markets, any non-empty positions page, or any order (`Order` lost `yes_price`/`fill_count`/…) raise pydantic `ValidationError`. Therefore `scanner.fetch_open_events_with_markets()`, `scanner.get_held_positions()` (which `get_held_tickers()` reads) and `trader._position_count()` call the SDK's `*_without_preload_content` raw-response variants and parse the JSON themselves (markets into `scanner.ApiMarket` dataclasses). Order submission was the highest-stakes case: the modeled `create_order` (for the retired `/portfolio/orders` endpoint) would raise AFTER the order was placed, which would have pushed every real fill into the ambiguous-exception path; the V2 create-order route has no SDK method, so trader's `_build_no_order_v2`/`_build_yes_order_v2`/`_build_rollback_order_v2` build the JSON body by hand and `_submit_order_v2` signs and sends it through `_http.signed_request_json`. Two rules: (1) the raw variants do NOT raise on 4xx/5xx, so every raw call must go through `_http.fetch_json_page()`, which re-raises non-2xx as `ApiException` to keep prior error semantics (`api_call_with_retry`'s 429/5xx backoff for market data; order submission stays retry-free per the rule above); (2) don't "simplify" back to the modeled calls (`client.get_events(...)`, `client.get_positions(...)`) — they crash on live responses. **Event listings drifted too (2026-08).** They were previously safe through the modeled calls (no nested markets), but the live API now sends `category: null` on some events while the pinned SDK types `EventData.category` as a required `str` — so `get_events` and `get_multivariate_events` raise pydantic `ValidationError` mid-listing. Verified 2026-08-03: this killed a backtest *after* a successful 28-minute fetch. `historical._load_or_build_event_titles` therefore uses `get_events_without_preload_content` / `get_multivariate_events_without_preload_content` through `fetch_json_page` for BOTH bulk listings; the modeled `get_event` (singular) was already unusable — it embeds nested `Market` models — so the per-ticker fallback continues to use a raw GET. There is now no modeled SDK call left in the codebase at all — `verify_auth` (`auth.py`) was the last one, migrated to `get_balance_without_preload_content` for the same reason (BS-10); don't reintroduce a modeled call anywhere. Related drift: the MVE events listing is effectively unbounded (hundreds of thousands of auto-generated collection events) and on prod currently returns zero nested markets regardless of `with_nested_markets` — the MVE pull in `fetch_open_events_with_markets()` bails out after `MVE_MAX_EMPTY_PAGES` (25) consecutive pages that contribute no ACTIVE nested market, and the backtester's title lookup caps its MVE scan at `MVE_TITLE_LOOKUP_MAX_PAGES` (500); without those caps the scan hangs for hours (this is likely why the 2026-07-06 prod run logged nothing after auth). Don't remove the bail-outs. **Every cursor loop in `scanner.py` is bounded twice over (TS-05)** — the two in `fetch_open_events_with_markets()` and the one in `get_held_positions()`: each keeps a `seen_cursors` set, so a keyset that CYCLES (`A, B, A, B, …`) and therefore never repeats *consecutively* is caught as well as the plain stuck cursor the old `new_cursor == cursor` guard proved; and each stops unconditionally at `SCANNER_MAX_PAGES` (config.py, 5000 — ~1M markets at `MARKET_PAGE_SIZE`, against a ~135k-market prod ingest), which bounds the walk against any cursor pathology whether or not anyone enumerated it. These were the last unbounded scans in the ingest path, and `get_held_positions` runs before any pairing, so an unbounded walk there means the run never scans at all. The MVE loop's `MVE_MAX_EMPTY_PAGES` productivity bail-out is independent of both and keeps its own counter and reset semantics — don't fold them together. The warning text still contains `cursor did not advance` (now "(repeated)"), which tests pin. **The counter must track productive (active-market) pages, not raw nested-market presence (fixed 2026-08, found live in sandbox).** The bail-out originally reset on the raw `len(markets)` per page regardless of status; on sandbox, the MVE listing serves thousands of pages whose nested markets exist but are all non-active (closed/settled/determined) — those pages kept resetting the counter forever, so the bail-out never fired and a sandbox validation run paged 4,600+ times flat at the same appended-market count. On prod this bug was invisible because prod happens to return zero nested markets per page either way. The fix counts only markets actually appended (status == "active") toward the reset decision. Same drift hits the orderbook: `scanner._fetch_orderbook()` also uses a raw GET, and the book arrives in one of two **matched** key generations tracked by `scanner._ORDERBOOK_SIDE_KEYS`, where each container key owns an ordered tuple of candidate `(yes_key, no_key, unit)` side-key sets and is NEVER read with another container's sets:
- `orderbook_fp` ⇒ `yes_dollars`/`no_dollars` only, **dollar strings**. This is what production actually receives today; its handling must stay behaviour-identical (a prod dry-run should log zero mismatch warnings).
- `orderbook` ⇒ `yes_dollars`/`no_dollars` (**dollars**) if present, else `true`/`false` (**integer cents**). Both shapes come straight from the pinned SDK's `models/orderbook.py`: the `Orderbook` model *requires* `yes_dollars`/`no_dollars` and aliases its legacy int arrays as `"true"`/`"false"` — it never had `yes`/`no`, which the table wrongly assumed until this was corrected (BS-03).

`_fetch_orderbook` picks the container by key *presence* (not truthiness), then takes the first candidate set of that container whose **both** keys exist — a set is only ever taken whole, so a unit can never be inferred from the wrong keys. Cents levels are validated (whole cent in `[1, 99]`) and converted `/100.0` to dollars **before** `_bids_to_ask_levels`, which stays dollars-only by contract; an invalid level is dropped with a WARNING naming the ticker and the raw value, because cents fed straight through that parser yield `1 - 45 = -44` and get silently discarded, turning a full book into a *silent* empty book (BS-12). Degenerate containers: present but `None` or `{}` ⇒ an **empty book** `{"yes": [], "no": []}` (a market with no resting bids, not drift — no warning, BS-28); a side key present but empty/`null` ⇒ that side has no resting bids, also fine; a **non-empty** container matching none of its own candidate sets (or a non-dict value) is logged as a **potential key mismatch** and returns `None` (fail-closed) rather than cross-reading another generation's keys. Don't collapse this back to a flat `data.get("orderbook_fp") or data.get("orderbook")` with an independent `yes_dollars or true` side lookup: that decoupling is exactly the cross-generation read this guards against. `TestFetchOrderbookKeyMapping` in `tests/test_scanner.py` pins every generation, unit, and degenerate container above against the SDK-documented shapes. **Context (from `main`, 2026-03-12):** Kalshi removed every legacy integer price field from its REST/WS payloads that day, and production serves `orderbook_fp` only — so the `orderbook` container and its cents arrays are not expected on the wire today. They are kept as SDK-shape-derived DEFENSE, not as a live code path (the pinned SDK still models exactly those shapes, and the alternative to parsing them correctly is not "no code" but a silently empty book, BS-12). Nothing about that weakens the fail-closed rule: an unknown container, or a known container matching none of its own candidate sets, still returns `None` with a mismatch warning.

**Exchange sharding — ingest tags every shard; only trading-inactive shards are dropped.** Kalshi has partitioned the exchange into parallel shards identified by an integer `exchange_index` on market payloads, in the account balance breakdown, and in `GET /exchange/status`'s `exchange_index_statuses` array. Each shard has its own markets, order books, and slice of account funds. **Market data is cross-shard** — the events/markets/orderbook endpoints return every shard's markets, tagged — so ingest LABELS and keeps them: `scanner._shard_index()` reads the field fail-safe (missing/null/unparseable => `config.DEFAULT_EXCHANGE_INDEX`, since absence is the pre-sharding/sandbox shape and must never empty the market list) and `scanner._market_from_dict()` stores it on `ApiMarket.exchange_index`. The sole ingest-time shard exclusion is `inactive_shards`, passed into `scanner.fetch_open_events_with_markets(client, inactive_shards=...)` by both `main._run_dev` and `main._run_prod` from `scanner.fetch_shard_statuses()` — and forwarded by `find_time_series_pairs`' own no-`markets` fallback fetch, which used to pass none (TS-27; that fallback is DEAD CODE today, unreachable in production and in the suite, so this closes a trap for a future caller rather than a live hole): nothing on a shard the exchange itself reports `trading_active=false` for can be traded, nor should it linger as a stale candidate. **`trading_active` is TRI-STATE on the parsed statuses — `True`, `False`, or `None` when the payload carried no such key — and only a `false` drops a shard at ingest (TS-04).** An absent or renamed field is "unknown", never "halted": coercing it to `False` (the old `bool(entry.get(...))`) marked every shard inactive, dropped every market, and still exited 0 claiming full coverage, so a single renamed field emptied the whole ingest — the same drift class that already hit markets, positions, orders, events and balance, and the same fail-safe policy `_shard_index` applies to a missing `exchange_index`. `fetch_shard_statuses` logs one summary WARNING naming how many shards carried no flag (silent at zero), and every consumer — `inactive_shard_indexes`, `check_shard_coverage`, `main._log_shard_coverage` — tests `is False` / `is not False`, never truthiness; one leftover truthiness test silently reintroduces the bug. `trader._transfers_active` reads a different key (`intra_exchange_transfers_active`) and keeps its `bool()` coercion, because a missing transfers flag correctly means "don't move money". **Absence is not the only drift shape: a RE-TYPED flag must keep its meaning too (TS-04b).** A JSON boolean that arrives as its string form is truthy in Python — `bool("false") is True` — so the old bare `bool()` coercion read a HALTED shard as open and kept scanning and trading it. Every flag in the parse therefore goes through `scanner._status_flag(raw)`, which resolves, in order: a real bool; the numeric spellings `0`/`1`; then, for strings, the closed token sets `config.EXCHANGE_FLAG_NULL_TOKENS` / `EXCHANGE_FLAG_FALSE_TOKENS` / `EXCHANGE_FLAG_TRUE_TOKENS` (stripped, lower-cased), where a stringified null (`"null"`, `"none"`, `"nil"`, `"undefined"`) reads as unknown rather than as open. An UNRECOGNISED value resolves by truthiness, in the one direction that cannot break a reading that is correct today: a falsy one (`0.0`, `""`, `[]`, `{}`) keeps the `False` that `bool()` already gave it — un-dropping a halted shard is the one change this must never make — while a truthy one (`2`, `"maybe"`) becomes `None`, which every `trading_active` consumer already treats exactly as it treated `True` (the shard stays ingested, audited and scannable). Do NOT "clean this up" into "any non-bool is unknown": that is a no-op for the drifted `"false"` (`None` and `True` are indistinguishable at every consumer, all of which test `is False`) AND it un-drops every falsy non-bool. The three flags then differ in what UNKNOWN means, which is why the final step is deliberately not uniform: `trading_active` stores the tri-state verdict as-is (unknown keeps the shard in the ingest — TS-04), while `exchange_active` and `intra_exchange_transfers_active` store `_status_flag(...) is True`, so anything but a recognised true — absent, null, false or unreadable — is stored as `False` and fails closed. That asymmetry is why the money flag matters at all: `trader._transfers_active` reads `intra_exchange_transfers_active`, and a drifted `"false"` read as `True` fires a real, non-idempotent, never-retried collateral POST at a shard whose transfers the exchange has disabled; `scanner.fetch_shard_statuses` is the single parse boundary, so the value reaching `trader` is always a real bool and the `bool()` there is a no-op on it. A second summary WARNING (separate from the absent-flag counter, silent at zero) names each drifted shard, flag and raw value, with the value's `repr` bounded by `config.EXCHANGE_FLAG_DRIFT_REPR_MAX_CHARS` so one drifted array cannot put a multi-KB line in the log on every run (the TS-02 failure class). The absent-flag counter still counts only genuinely ABSENT keys — a key that is present but unreadable is named by the drift line instead. A non-zero skip count logs `Skipped %d markets on trading-inactive exchange shards %s` (silent at zero), and the per-shard ingest census `Ingested markets by shard: {...}` is logged on EVERY fetch — it is the only signal of which shards a run actually saw, and is what makes a category migration visible. There is no `_on_routable_shard` any more; do not reintroduce a drop-at-ingest shard filter. **Migration facts:** shard 0 and shard 1 were both observed live on 2026-08-14; all combo/MVE markets moved to shard 1 on 2026-08-17; crypto moved to shard 2 and tennis/baseball to shard 3 on 2026-08-24. A shard-0-only scanner is therefore blind to most of the exchange — that is what this flip fixes.

**Coverage is audited, never assumed.** `scanner.check_shard_coverage(advertised, market_shards, balance_shards)` is a PURE comparison (no I/O, no logging) returning `(critical, warnings)`; `main._log_shard_coverage()` is the only thing that decides how loudly to report them and is called from both run modes immediately after the fetch, BEFORE held-ticker/horizon filtering (a blind spot must be measured against what ingest actually saw). The severity split is deliberate: an advertised `trading_active` shard that produced ZERO ingested markets is **CRITICAL** when it also holds account funds — money on a shard whose book we never scanned is a real blind spot — and only a **warning** when it holds none, because an advertised-but-empty shard is expected during the rollout and must not critical-alert every week. Markets ingested from a shard `/exchange/status` does not advertise are always critical (the two data sources disagree); funds on an unadvertised shard are a warning. A shard whose `trading_active` is explicitly `false` is never flagged — its ingest drop already warned — but a shard whose flag is absent (`None`, TS-04) IS still audited, because it stays in the ingest. `advertised is None` (sandbox / pre-sharding / any failure inside `fetch_shard_statuses`, which is fail-soft by design) returns `([], [])` and logs "coverage not assessable". **The check NEVER aborts a run** — it reports and trading continues on whatever was covered; the one run-ending verdict, the all-shards-inactive `EXIT_NO_TRADEABLE_SHARDS` short-circuit (TS-01), is decided in `main._run_dev`/`_run_prod`, never inside this pure helper. `balance_shards` must be pre-filtered to shards with a NONZERO balance by the caller (`{s for s, c in shard_balances.items() if c > 0}`); the function only tests set membership.

**Sizing is cross-shard; collateral is made fungible before execution.** `auth.read_account_balance()` returns each shard's cash as `dict[int, int]` (exchange_index -> cents, `AccountBalance.shard_cash_cents`) and `main._run_prod` spends `sum(...)` of it — the total across shards, not any one shard; Kelly fractions are taken of that sum plus the open positions' value (see the portfolio-value paragraph under Key Patterns) — because `trader.ensure_shard_collateral(client, portfolio, shard_balances, shard_statuses, dry_run=...)` runs after `pre_execution_check` and moves cash onto the shards the selected legs actually settle against (each leg charges its own market's shard). It degrades, never aborts: no deficit => untouched portfolio and zero API calls; `dry_run` => plan logged, never POSTed; transfers inactive on either endpoint shard, a failed POST (single-shot — the transfer endpoint is NOT idempotent, so never retry it), or funds that don't settle within `TRANSFER_SETTLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` => only the trades needing that shard are dropped, and `_run_prod` early-returns when nothing is left.

**Order routing is per leg.** Each V2 order body carries ITS OWN market's `exchange_index` (each `_Leg.market.exchange_index`: the NO leg's market for the NO leg and its rollback — `market_a` for same-title, `market_b` for time-series — and the YES leg's market for the YES leg), so a pair whose legs sit on different shards is now tradeable. It is always explicit and **never the `-1` auto-route sentinel**: an explicit shard makes the exchange reject loudly if our notion of a market's shard is wrong (rather than silently settling against an unmodelled shard), and it bills only that shard's rate-limit bucket where auto-route bills every nonzero shard's. `main.print_pairs_table`'s "Shards" column (`a/b`) shows which shard each leg's order routes to. The backtest path stores but does not filter: `historical._market_to_dict` passes `exchange_index` through raw into cache records (pre-existing caches read back `None`) so backtest data can distinguish shards, but no historical/backtester code drops markets by shard — all historical markets are shard 0, and filtering would silently change backtest results with no live-safety gain.

**The balance read is raw, shard-aware, and returns PER SHARD — callers sum for gate/sizing (2026-08-14).** `auth.verify_auth()` no longer calls the modeled `client.get_balance()` — that deserializes through the pinned SDK's strict pydantic model (`balance`/`portfolio_value`/`updated_ts` all required ints), the exact drift-fragile pattern that already broke events, orders, and positions. It now calls `get_balance_without_preload_content` through `_http.fetch_json_page()` (wrapped in `api_call_with_retry`, since it's a read-only GET), so non-2xx still raises `ApiException` and bad credentials still fail loudly. Don't "simplify" it back to the modeled call. `verify_auth()` returns `dict[int, int]` — spendable cents keyed by `exchange_index` — not a scalar. `auth._balance_cents_by_shard()` builds that dict from the raw body: (1) every parseable `balance_breakdown` entry, keyed by ITS OWN `exchange_index` (no shard-0 preference — a later collateral-transfer planner and a shard-coverage check both need every shard's balance, not just the routable one); (2) only when the breakdown is missing or entirely unparseable, top-level `balance_dollars` (the cross-shard aggregate) attributed to `config.DEFAULT_EXCHANGE_INDEX`; (3) same fallback tier, legacy top-level integer `balance`, also attributed to `config.DEFAULT_EXCHANGE_INDEX` — this is the sandbox shape. A breakdown that exists but yields no parseable entries at all logs a WARNING and falls through to the aggregate rather than reading `$0` (a false `MIN_BALANCE_CENTS` abort); a malformed or non-dict breakdown entry is skipped rather than aborting the scan of the good ones. If nothing parses, it raises `ValueError` — a deliberate loud-failure carve-out to the return-`None`-on-validation-failure convention, because sizing real trades against an unknown balance is worse than aborting the run. **There is deliberately no scalar-returning wrapper.** A single number would hide funds parked on other shards, and every caller's actual need is either the full per-shard map or its sum, never one shard in isolation — the `MIN_BALANCE_CENTS` gate and Kelly sizing read that sum plus the open positions' value, the portfolio selection spends the sum, and the shard-coverage check and the collateral transfer read the per-shard map; a dual API would invite a future caller to size against the wrong (single-shard) number. The read itself is `auth.read_account_balance()`, which returns an `AccountBalance` — this per-shard cash dict plus Kalshi's value of the open positions (`positions_value_cents`, see the money-units paragraph) — from the same one GET and the same `Auth OK` line; `verify_auth()` is that read returning only the cash dict, so `read_account_balance()` is the one definition of the balance read and `verify_auth()` is not a second one. `main._run_prod()` reads `account = read_account_balance(client)`, keeps `shard_balances = account.shard_cash_cents` for the coverage check and the transfer planner, spends `cash_cents = sum(shard_balances.values())` — the total across shards, because collateral is made fungible across shards by the pre-execution transfers — and sizes on that sum plus `account.positions_value_cents` (`main._bankroll_cents`; see the portfolio-value paragraph under Key Patterns); the post-trade balance line for the Excel log sums the cash the same way. **Same-key-different-units trap:** inside a `balance_breakdown` entry, `"balance"` is a fixed-point DOLLAR STRING (live: `"1.1407"`); at the TOP level, `"balance"` is legacy INTEGER CENTS (live: `114`). Never route the top-level field through `auth._dollar_str_to_cents()` and never return an entry's field as-is. Within an entry the field is chosen by **presence, not truthiness** (`"balance_dollars" in entry`, never `a or b`): a numeric `0` is a real answer, and an `or` would discard it and read the stale legacy field, reporting a genuinely-empty shard as funded — the one OVER-statement this function can produce, against the flooring and drop-with-a-WARNING rules that exist to guarantee the opposite (TS-16). A repeated `exchange_index` keeps the last entry and WARNs; silently last-winning hid a changed payload shape. Dollar strings are converted with `Decimal` and **floored, not rounded** (`ROUND_FLOOR`), so the bot is never told it holds a sub-cent it cannot spend; float parsing would reintroduce the truncation noise the `*_dollars` fields exist to avoid.

**Collateral transfers are centicent-denominated, asynchronous, and never retried.** A run spends the SUM of every shard's cash (Kelly fractions are taken of that sum plus the open positions' value) but an order settles against its own market's shard only, so `trader.ensure_shard_collateral()` moves cash onto the shards a selected portfolio actually draws from before execution: it totals each shard's requirement from the legs' `cost_with_fees_a`/`cost_with_fees_b` (`_required_cents_by_shard`, through `config.leg_cash_cents` — `math.ceil(round(cost * 100, 6))`, the one rounding `strategy.select_portfolio` spends the cash with, so a portfolio it admitted is never a cent short on any shard — ceiling because under-funding a shard by a fraction of a cent gets the order rejected for insufficient collateral, while over-funding by a cent costs nothing; the `round()` first stops binary float noise claiming an extra cent, same idiom as `fee_leg_exact`), plans a pure deterministic greedy fill out of surplus shards (`_plan_transfers` — deficits in ascending shard order, sources ranked by largest REMAINING surplus, so the number of non-idempotent POSTs is minimized), POSTs each planned transfer to `config.TRANSFER_PATH`, and confirms it actually settled. Three rules are load-bearing. (1) **Centicents are the codebase's THIRD money unit** — the endpoint's `amount` field is 1/100 of a cent (dollars for contract prices, integer cents for balances, centicents here and nowhere else). The conversion lives ONLY in `trader._cents_to_centicents()`; never inline the factor, because a missing ×100 moves 1% of the intended collateral (surfacing much later as an unexplained insufficient-funds rejection) and a doubled one moves 100× out of a shard. (2) **A 2xx means accepted, not settled** — the endpoint is asynchronous, so an order submitted immediately after could still be rejected for insufficient collateral. `_await_transfer_settlement()` re-reads the per-shard balance through `auth.read_shard_balances` — the SINGLE-SHOT (retry-free) variant of `verify_auth`'s parse, because `verify_auth`'s `api_call_with_retry` backoff could hold one read for ~60s and silently stretch the "bounded" wait — every `TRANSFER_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (2) against a `time.monotonic` deadline of `TRANSFER_SETTLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (30); a balance read that raises is treated as "nothing observed", never as success, and the await targets ONLY the shards accepted transfers were headed for (an unfundable deficit shard must not burn the timeout or miscast settled transfers as in-flight). `trader.py` imports the reader at module scope — layering-legal (`config`/`_http` → `auth`/`scanner` → `strategy` → `reporter` → `trader`) — and tests substitute it by monkeypatching `trader.read_shard_balances`. (3) **A retried transfer moves the money twice** — the endpoint is not idempotent, so `_execute_transfer` submits through `_http.signed_request_json` (single-shot by construction, never `api_call_with_retry`, which `_execute_transfer` deliberately does not use — `trader.py` DOES import `api_call_with_retry` at module scope, for the retried `_position_count` reads; the no-retry rule is asserted per-function on the submission/transfer sources and at `_submit_order_v2`'s two call sites, never as a module-wide import ban) and one failed POST is logged and abandoned, never re-sent. An **accepted-but-unparseable** response is NOT a failed POST: `_check_and_parse` validates the status before parsing, so a `JSONDecodeError` out of that call proves a 2xx and therefore that the money moved. `_execute_transfer` catches it, logs `logging.critical` naming MONEY IS IN FLIGHT, and returns `None` — the value that already means "accepted, id unknown" — so the shard is still awaited and still eligible for the in-flight critical. Letting it propagate instead reported a FAILED POST, skipped the settlement poll entirely and suppressed that critical (TS-17). Nor is an **accepted-but-non-object** response a failed POST: `_check_and_parse` does not narrow the parsed body to a `dict` (both `_http.fetch_json_page` and `_http.signed_request_json` are annotated `-> Any` and say so in their Returns), so a 2xx body of `"accepted"`, `[]`, `123`, `true` or a literal `null` reaches `_execute_transfer` as a `str`/`list`/`int`/`bool`/`None` and `data.get("transfer_id")` raised `AttributeError` into the very same generic handler — the same misreport, one exception type over (DR-05). An `isinstance(data, dict)` guard now logs its own MONEY IS IN FLIGHT critical (naming the type, so payload-shape drift is distinguishable from a parse failure) and returns `None` on the same in-flight contract; an id-less but genuine JSON OBJECT is untouched and still returns `None` with no critical. The other readers of a 2xx body are deliberately left loud, for two different reasons. The read-only ones have moved no money by the time they parse: the `.get(...)` readers (`auth._balance_cents_by_shard`, `scanner.fetch_open_events_with_markets` and the other scanner cursor loops) raise the same `AttributeError`, and `historical`'s subscripting `/historical/cutoff` read (`cutoff["market_settled_ts"]`) raises `TypeError`. The ORDER-submission reader **in `trader.py`** is the exception to that rationale and still must NOT get this guard: a 2xx there can mean a FILL, so money HAS moved — `_submit_order_v2` reads through `_v2_fill_status`'s `data.get("order")` and raises `AttributeError`, and stays loud precisely because raising is what routes `_execute_one` into its ambiguous-submission path, where the outcome is resolved against the account's position DELTA — a stronger remedy than an in-flight critical. **`v2_probe.py` is the named carve-out to that carve-out and DOES get the guard (DR-58).** Its submissions are order submissions, but nothing resembling `_execute_one` sits above them: the probe IS the top of its own call stack, so raising resolves nothing and merely kills the process mid-step — after a real ask has been submitted, before the position read, before any "FLATTEN IT MANUALLY" line, and (in `_step_no_mapping`, the only step that has one) before the reduce-only close, leaving a real position open with nothing to tell the operator it exists. `v2_probe._non_object_body_fail` therefore guards both submission-response READERS — `_step_no_mapping`'s NO buy and `_step_unfillable_ask`'s ask — printing a FAIL that names the body type, reading the position and telling the operator what to flatten. **READERS, not submissions: the probe makes THREE submissions and only two of them read
