One silent-at-zero line per simulation, at the simulation's own level (DEBUG when `quiet`, so reading every lazy cap cell adds nothing to the INFO log): `Time-series pairs skipped on a Monday because we still held a trade on the same ladder (<the completion line's label>): N` — N counts pair-Mondays, so a pair refused on three Mondays counts 3; the completion line is built from the same label and is byte-identical. (5) **`SweepPoint.peak_kelly_fraction` is now the largest uncapped `f*` over every Monday a pair may be traded on — every Monday a time-series pair passes the Kelly gate, a same-title pair's first, and, with `add_to_held`, a same-title pair's later same-legs Mondays (without it, a same-title pair's later Mondays are never scored)** — still taken before the outcome and premise checks, still cap-independent — **and that is REQUIRED for `CapSweep`'s reuse rule to stay exact.** A time-series pair can now be traded on any of its passing Mondays, and WHICH one depends on the walk (the cash left, the ladders in use), which depends on the cap: at `k` 0.40 with no cap, a pair that does not fit beside another on 2026-01-05 enters on 01-12, while at caps of 20% and 45% both fit on 01-05 (`TestOpenLadderExposure`'s cash-retry fixture, checked 2026-09-27 on a $10,000 balance, when Pass 2 still skipped a trade its cash could not afford whole; since 2026-09-29 it shrinks the trade to the cash left, so a pair waits for cash only when its budget buys no contract pair that still pays in a win after the exact fees — which still depends on the cap — and that fixture now runs on $1.20). At a cap at or above that peak (over a walked book, `max(peak, cap_free_from)`) no size depends on the cap (a same-title size stays under `SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP` at each), so the whole walk is identical at every such cap; a peak read only off each pair's first passing (or traded) Monday would let a cap between that Monday's `f*` and a later one's share the simulation of a cap that sized a retried pair differently (a mutant doing exactly that fails `TestOpenLadderExposure`'s cap-parity test on its parity check, and one reading only the first and last passing Mondays fails `::test_the_peak_covers_a_middle_monday`). The halves (`_split_halves` cuts an H1 pair's Mondays at the split and never re-lists it — the full run opens a same-title pair only on its first passing Monday, so a re-listed copy could enter H2 on a Monday the full run never tries and lift H2's peak above the full run's — so each half's (pair, Monday) candidates are a subset of the full run's) and the ex-top run simulate subsets whose peaks are no higher. This amends, without editing them, the interval-discount paragraph's description of the `k`-dependent tail (its one-pair-per-group dedup is now same-title only, and Pass 2 applies the ladder rule) and the size-cap paragraph's `peak_kelly_fraction` ("the candidates that passed the Kelly gate" are now one per passing Monday of a time-series pair). (6) **What does not move:** `_find_entry`, the entries, the `k̂` calibration, the split date, the populations, the cache tags (it acts at simulation, so no `SETTLED_PREFILTER_CACHE_TAG` bump), `dashboard.py` and its golden digests, and same-title pairing (first passing Monday, the whole-window group contest, no retry). **Every backtest time-series trade count, return and drawdown recorded before this rule is not comparable.** On the golden fixture (`TestPrepareEntriesGolden`, the full 36 × 13 band sweep with its tier-floors-off family and both size-cap sweeps, at `apply_pre_toggle_defaults`; measured 2026-09-27 against the tree before this change, under the skip-when-short sizing Pass 2 had until the portfolio-value change of 2026-09-29 — the cap-point counts and timings below were not re-measured after it): every eager point's trades, halves and ex-top check are unchanged (no golden test moved); 280 eager tier-floors-off points' `peak_kelly_fraction` rose (the tier-off ladder's later Mondays quote a wider spread than its first); and 3,150 of the 53,580 lazy cap points gained trades (1,224 tier-on and 1,746 tier-floors-off "all" points and 180 tier-floors-off "time_series" points; their halves and ex-top checks moved with them, and no point lost a trade), all at caps of 35% or more — every added trade is EA/EB, retried on 01-12 after being skipped on 01-05, or the ladder, retried on 01-12, 01-19 or 02-02 after being skipped on its earlier passing Mondays (it first qualifies on 01-05 with the tier floors off and on 01-12 with them on); none of those points refused a pair on a busy ladder, so every skip was for want of cash, behind the stakes taken earlier (the same-title SB/SA's, and EA/EB's where it traded first). In 480 tier-on points the ladder moves from 01-12 to 01-19, because EA/EB, retried on 01-12 and ordered ahead of it, takes that Monday's cash first. Reading every cap cell of that grid (the dashboard's walk) took 41.80–41.91 s against 38.42–38.70 s before (two runs each; tier-on cells 27.03–27.09 s against 25.20–25.39 s at the same 13,348 simulated cap points, tier-off cells 14.77–14.82 s against 13.22–13.30 s, 7,134 simulated against 6,940, since the larger tier-off peaks share fewer caps), and the eager run 3.87–3.88 s against 3.55–3.65 s — the extra per-Monday candidates and the ladder bookkeeping. **Residuals.** (a) A same-title pair Pass 2 cannot take is still not retried, and its group contest is still the whole-window look-ahead. (b) The two paths still pick the rung differently: each live run keeps one pair per group, by widest `pB − pA` before Kelly; the backtest takes, per Monday, the highest-ratio Kelly-passing candidate whose ladder is free — so they can replay different rungs. (c) That ratio still reads the realized close (the DR-75 look-ahead note): for a time-series pair now only to order one Monday's candidates, for a same-title pair still to pick its group's winner (residual (a)). (d) A market with no event ticker whose question normalizes away carries no label and is never blocked (a time-series market in the pipeline always has a non-empty group key). (e) A pair dropped because of how it paid out — no yes/no result (voided), the impossible earlier-YES/later-NO cell, or no readable settlement date — never becomes a candidate, so it never holds a ladder, and its ladder-mates can trade where a live run, which cannot know the result in advance, might have been holding that ladder (a voided rung ranking first on its ladder on a Monday leaves its ladder-mate to trade there). Its cash already went to others before this rule; post-DR-67 premise violators are near zero, so voided pairs are the realistic case. Recorded, not fixed: entering such pairs in Pass 2 as claim-only candidates that hold their labels until their markets pay out would move results, a separate change. (f) Ladders are released at the start of the payout DAY, like tickers and cash: the Monday checkpoint can enter a ladder whose market pays out later that same day, while a live run at that hour would still hold it. (g) The labels are the live rule's, but the inputs are not: `event_title` is blank on most cached records, so a cached market's question label is title + outcome only, and two markets of different events worded identically are one ladder in the backtest where live would keep them apart by event title — the pre-existing grouping-key difference, now reaching ladder blocking (a same-title trade's label can block time-series pairs). (h) `_split_halves` loses a second class of pair: an H1 time-series pair the full run can only take after the split because its ladder was busy (or cash short) on every earlier passing Monday trades in neither half (re-listing it in H2 would count it twice); this can bias the split-half check near the split date on ladder-heavy runs. Pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestOpenLadderExposure` (a second rung waits for the first to pay out, and a quiet run logs the count at DEBUG; a shared event blocks a pair asking another question, through either market; one question across events is one ladder; a ladder is free on the day it pays out; each market frees its own labels; a pair trades at most once, its later Mondays never counted; of two pairs on one ladder on one Monday the better ratio trades; a time-series pair whose cash left buys no contract pair is tried again and a same-title one is not, while one the cash left can buy is shrunk to it rather than retried; an open same-title trade blocks a time-series pair and not the reverse; both of a same-title trade's markets hold their event and their question, market B's included, each until it pays out; a retried pair is ranked and held from the Monday it trades, which decides a Monday-2 contest with a ladder-mate; a pair held back by a ticker is not counted as a busy ladder; the premise count is one per pair; the peak covers every passing Monday, a never-traded pair's included, and a middle Monday's, with a half's peak at or below it; every cap of a multi-rung cell — where a pair refused on Monday 1 trades on Monday 2 capped at 0.45 and uncapped at 0.50, priced, sized and held on Monday 2 — equals a fresh `_simulate_at_discount(size_cap=cap)` on trades, equity, halves and ex-top; neither half's peak exceeds the full run's) and `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_the_backtest_reads_ladders_through_the_one_definition` (`_simulate_at_discount` calls `_ladder_keys_dict`, which calls `ladder_keys` and `_ts_group_key`).

**Adding to a held pair in the backtest (DR-77) — `_simulate_at_discount(add_to_held=True)`, off unless a caller asks; the dashboard's add-on family is the caller.** With `add_to_held` the cash walk may add to a pair it still holds, as the live sizer does for a held pair. **What adds:** a candidate whose pair has an open trade, with neither market paid out that Monday, trades again as a new `BacktestTrade` with `add_on=True` (the same two markets bought the same way round, its own count, cost, fees and payout, the same exit date; the open trade is never changed). A time-series pair's later passing Mondays are candidates already (DR-76); a same-title pair also keeps its later passing Mondays whose legs are its first one's, the same way round, as add-on-only candidates, which never contest the one-pair-per-group choice (the winner's are kept, a loser's dropped) and never open a pair. **Sizing:** Pass 2 first asks `config.held_pair_fraction(kelly_f_capped, the pair's stake, the checkpoint's portfolio value)` and skips a pair already at its share; `_size_trade` then hands `compute_trade` a `HeldPair` carrying the stake, which sizes the add-on as live does — `held_pair_fraction` on its own capped fraction, then the one `config.kelly_budget(portfolio value, fraction, cash)` every trade uses: Kelly sizes the whole position as a share of the portfolio value (the checkpoint's cash plus every open trade at market, fees left out, as the equity curve carries them), the pair's stake being its open trades at market plus the fees paid for them (`held["trades"]` keeps each of the pair's trades with what it spent, fees included, and the stake is the sum of what each spent plus its change in value since: `paid + (_open_value(t, d) − t.total_cost)`, which without quotes is what it spent), and an add-on spends at most the cash left and never more than a new pair would; a pair already at its share is skipped. **Refusals:** another open trade holding one of its ladder labels, or two markets sharing no label (live, `scanner.held_pairs` adds only to two held markets that share a label and no other held market shares); a new time-series pair on a held ladder is still refused as DR-76 has it. Three outcomes each have a silent-at-zero line, DEBUG when `quiet`: `Trades that added to a held pair (…)`, `Adds skipped because the held pair is already at its target size (…)` and `Adds skipped because another open trade is on the same ladder, or the two markets share no ladder (…)`; an add-on skipped for any other reason — one of its markets already paid out, a budget (the cash left binding it) that buys not one contract pair, fees eating the profit — is not counted (except that, over a walked book, a refusal by the walk, or no size fitting, is counted on the `Trades refused when their book was walked` line, and one the cash left cannot buy one contract pair of on the `Trades skipped with no cash left for one contract pair` line, as for any trade). The completion line's label ends `, adding to held pairs`, and the point is stamped `SweepPoint.add_to_held`. **Why the size-cap sweep stays exact:** the cap reaches an add-on only through `min(pair cap, f*)`, as it reaches any trade, and `peak_kelly_fraction` covers every Monday an add-on may be made on (a same-title pair's later same-legs Mondays included), so at any cap at or above the peak (over a walked book, `max(peak, cap_free_from)`) the whole walk is the same. `CapSweep.add_to_held` forwards the setting to every simulation and check, and `CapSweep.end_dates` gives a cell with no eager point its end day, so an add-on family built with `eager={}` simulates each cap until one reaches its peak and shares that one above. Such a sweep refuses what would make it silently wrong: built with eager points (a non-empty `eager`, or a `same_title_eager`) it raises `ValueError`, since those points never added and would be handed back as its own; and a cell `end_dates` has no day for raises `ValueError` naming the (band, `k`, population) — the keys must be the exact band and `k` objects the cells are read with — rather than ending that cell's curves on whatever day it is read. **Off unless asked for:** the default is `False` (`_sim_options` forwards it only when `True`), and no eager run sets it, so every backtest result and log line is what it was; only the add-on family (see the size-cap gotcha) simulates with it on. **Why the stake counts the fees (operator decision, 2026-09-30):** fees leave the portfolio value the day they are paid (they buy nothing that can be sold on), so a stake that left them out would find a held pair short of its share by about (1 − its fraction) × those fees on every later Monday it passes, and top it up by about a contract pair each time, each top-up paying a rounded-up fee of its own. Counted in the stake, they tip the check the other way: at the quotes it was bought at, a pair bought at its full Kelly share adds nothing, and an add-on comes only from a larger Kelly share (better quotes, or a larger portfolio value after another trade paid out) or from a first trade the cash left had shrunk — and can still be small. Live sizing counts the fees the same way (`HeldPair.stake_dollars`). **Measured on the golden fixture** (2026-09-30, synthetic data, `apply_pre_toggle_defaults`, band 0–1, $10,000, 2026-01-01 to 2026-04-01): 31 add-on trades over its 3 `k` × 20 caps (11 at `k` 0.5, 20 at 0.75, 0 at 1.0), with open trades valued at market (71 at cost). At `k` 0.5 and a 5% cap the ladder RUNG-EARLY/RUNG-LATE opens 792 contract pairs on 01-12, then adds 49 on 02-02, when the portfolio value at market is $10,572.99; SB/SA and EA/EB make no add-on, and 13 times a held pair is skipped on a later Monday as already at its target size. Pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestAddToHeldPairs`, `::TestAddToHeldCapParity` (every cap of `SIZE_CAP_SWEEP` of an add-on `CapSweep` against a fresh `_simulate_at_discount(..., add_to_held=True, size_cap=cap)`, on the golden fixture, `TestOpenLadderExposure`'s multi-rung fixture and a fixture whose time-series add-on the cap binds at 5% and 10% only and whose same-title add-on it still binds at caps from 25% to 50%, above that pair's first Monday's fraction and below its later one's; a rule scaling the portfolio value by the cap fails it, and so does a peak read off a same-title pair's first Monday alone; and an add-on sweep's two refusals), `::TestAddToHeldPairs::test_the_account_value_is_the_equity_curve_s` (the portfolio value an add-on is sized on is the equity curve's at the close before its Monday), `::TestAddToHeldPairs::test_a_held_pair_at_unchanged_quotes_adds_nothing` (three Mondays at unchanged quotes: the first buys the pair's whole Kelly share, the next two add nothing, where a stake without the fees would leave room for 111 more contract pairs), `::test_an_add_on_sizes_as_live_compute_trade_does` and `::test_the_cash_left_limits_an_add_on_as_it_limits_live` (the same size as live `compute_trade` on the same value, cash and stake, the second with another open trade leaving too little cash), `::TestAddToHeldCapParity::test_every_cap_of_a_cash_bound_add_on_equals_a_fresh_simulation`, `::TestSizeCap::test_sim_options_forwards_add_to_held_only_when_true`, and by AST in `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_add_ons_size_through_held_pair_fraction` (`_simulate_at_discount` calls `held_pair_fraction`) and `::test_ast_the_backtest_sizes_add_ons_on_the_checkpoint_value` (one `held_pair_fraction(fraction, stake, checkpoint_value)` before the one `_size_trade(c, d, checkpoint_value, cash, settings, held_pair, markets)` call, which is handed the held pair built from the stake, every trade records `kelly_fraction=spec.kelly_fraction` and spends `spec.total_cost_with_fees`, the stake is summed as `paid + (_open_value(held_trade, d) − held_trade.total_cost)` over `held['trades']`, each trade appended with what it spent (`(trade, invested)`, contracts plus fees), and `checkpoint_value` is `cash + sum(_open_value(t, d) for t in open_trades)`; `::test_ast_the_backtest_check_catches_its_mutants` shows the check catches changed copies).

**Adding to a held pair (DR-77) — switched on by the eighth live toggle, `add_to_held_pairs`.** A production run with `LiveSettings.add_to_held_pairs` on (the saved live defaults', or `main.py --add-to-held-pairs` for one run; config.py's `ADD_TO_HELD_PAIRS` and the seed ship it on, but a saved file that leaves the key out reads it as off — see the saved-live-defaults paragraph) may add to a pair the account already holds: exactly the same two markets, buying the same side on each, judged by every rule any other trade is judged by, and sized so that Kelly covers the whole position. With it off, a held market is never traded again, as DR-76 describes. `scanner.get_held_positions` is the one reader of the positions listing: each held market's signed count, `market_exposure_dollars` and `fees_paid_dollars` (`HeldPosition`; a count or cost it cannot read is `None`, and a ticker listed on two rows stays held with its count and costs `None`, so it is never paired; a flat row whose ticker is a JSON list or object is skipped, as before), with the held set exactly the one `get_held_tickers` returned before — `get_held_tickers` is now `set(get_held_positions(...))`, with no caller on the live path, pinned against a reference copy of that per-row rule by `tests/test_scanner.py::TestGetHeldPositions::test_the_held_set_matches_the_reference_rule` — and its keyword-only `complete_out` set `False` on entry and then to whether the walk read the listing to its end (a cursor guard or `SCANNER_MAX_PAGES` stopping it early reads `False`). `resolve_held_ladders(..., labels_out=)` hands back each identified held market's ladder labels, and `scanner.held_pairs(positions, labels_by_ticker, markets_by_ticker)` joins held markets that share a label: a group of exactly two with opposite, equal counts (compared exactly) and readable costs, each exposure at least `MIN_ACTIVE_PRICE_DOLLARS` × its count, is a `HeldPair` (its `sides`, `count`, `cost_dollars` — both exposures plus fees paid, named in the logs — `value_dollars` — the pair's worth at today's prices, fees left out, also named in the logs: each market's contracts times the ask of the side held there, from the run's full market list (`markets_by_ticker`, built before held markets are dropped), or the market's exposure when the list lacks it or its ask is not a number strictly between 0 and 1 — and `fees_dollars` — both legs' fees paid — with the property `stake_dollars`, that worth plus those fees, what sizing and the at-cap check read); every other shape (one leg, three held markets on one ladder, unequal counts, one side twice, an unreadable count or cost) is never added to, and it returns no pair at all when any held market is missing from the labels or has none (a market whose ladder is unknown could share one with a pair). It logs either `Held pairs to add to: none (a held market's ladder is unknown)` or one line per pair, such as `Held pair to add to: YES A / NO B, 30 contracts each, cost $18.90 (fees $0.90), worth $22.50 at today's prices` (the cost includes the fees), and then `Held pairs to add to: N (other held markets, never added to: M)`. Both finders take a keyword-only `add_on_pairs` (`{frozenset of two tickers: HeldPair}`): the time-series finder lets the exact pair past its held-ladder refusal, refuses on that same line any other candidate touching one of the pair's markets, and checks the pair's sides with `HeldPair.matches` after a ladder may have swapped its legs; the same-title finder refuses every other candidate touching one of the pair's markets before any other check (so an unheld pair of the group can still win the group's slot), and refuses the pair itself when its prices have crossed so it would buy the other way round. With no held pair to add to, neither finder looks one up. Each refusal has its own silent-at-zero line, the time-series finder logs `Time-series pairs that add to a held pair: N` whenever it is handed any, and a kept pair carries the `HeldPair` on `CandidatePair.held` (copied through enrichment and `compute_trade` by `dc_replace`), read only through `scanner.pair_held`, by type. `strategy._evaluate_size` sizes an add-on on its whole position through `config.held_pair_fraction`, the one definition, before the `config.kelly_budget` every trade uses: it stakes what the held pair's stake — its worth today plus the fees paid for it — is missing of `min(f*, cap)` of the portfolio value, and like every trade never more than the cash, so its budget is `min(fraction × portfolio value − the pair's stake, cash)`, never more than a new pair's, and `config.max_kelly_fraction` still bounds every size; a held pair that has gained value gets a smaller add-on, one that has lost value a larger one; `compute_trade` logs one INFO line when it sizes an add-on to nothing, once per pair — `Not adding to held pair '…': it already holds its Kelly share (30 contracts each, worth $30.00 at today's prices, fees paid $0.90, portfolio value $211.00)` when `held_pair_fraction` refused it at the smallest size, `Not adding to held pair '…': no size fits this run` for any other refusal — and its `Trade computed` line ends ` | adds to N held` for an add-on (every other line is unchanged). `select_portfolio` blocks an add-on only by the ladders of this run's earlier picks (`picked_ladders`), because `held_pairs` adds only to a pair no other held market shares a ladder with — and only a spec whose `HeldPair` `matches` its own two markets and sides counts as one; every ordinary time-series spec on a held ladder is refused as before, and a spec shrunk to the cash left keeps its `HeldPair` and stays an add-on. The next paragraph says how `main._run_prod` switches this on and the one after it why it is safe; the order-time check and the operator texts follow them, and the backtest's mirror is the "Adding to a held pair in the backtest" paragraph above.

**How `main._run_prod` switches adding to held pairs on (DR-77).** It reads `get_held_positions(client, complete_out=held_listing)` (its keys are the held tickers `get_held_tickers` would return; only a run past the $50 minimum reads it), checks Kalshi's value of the open positions against it (`_checked_positions_value`, see risk (4) of the portfolio-value paragraph), reads `resolve_held_ladders(..., labels_out=held_labels)` and builds `markets_by_ticker` (every market of the run by ticker, before held markets are dropped). With the setting on, and only when `held_ladders` is not `None` (every held market identified), `held_listing["complete"] is True` (the listing read to its end) and, when anything is held, Kalshi's value of the open positions was read and kept, it calls `held_pairs(held_positions, held_labels, markets_by_ticker)`; otherwise it logs one WARNING, `Not adding to held pairs this run: a market the account holds could not be looked up`, `… the list of the account's positions was cut short, so a held market may be missing from it`, `… Kalshi's value of the open positions could not be read, so the portfolio value counts only the cash` or `… Kalshi's value of the open positions was refused, so the portfolio value counts only the cash`, and adds to nothing: an unknown or unlisted held market could share a pair's ladder, and being alone on its ladder is what makes an add-on safe, while a portfolio value that counts only the cash leaves out the very positions an add-on tops up. Holding nothing, the last two never fire. It then leaves out every pair with no room even at the run's per-trade cap (`held_pair_fraction(settings.size_cap, pair.stake_dollars, portfolio_value_cents / 100) <= 0`: the pair's worth at today's prices plus the fees paid for it already fill its cap's share of the portfolio value), logging `Held pairs already at their size cap, not added to this run: N`, so such a pair's markets stay blocked like any other held market's and cannot take their same-title group's one slot only to size to nothing; `add_on_tickers` is built from that same, final `add_on_pairs` — the dict both finders then get as `add_on_pairs=add_on_pairs` — and `blocked_tickers` is the held tickers less those markets. `blocked_tickers` is the only thing that keeps every other held market out of both finders (the same-title finder has no ladder refusal of its own), so a set built from `held_pairs`' result, or before the at-cap drop, would let a full pair's markets into an ordinary pair. With the setting off, `add_on_pairs` is `{}` and `held_pairs` is never called, so every pair-finding, sizing and order line is what it was; only the settings text (the `Live settings:` line, the trade-log run note, the run result, the defaults server's tables, `live_settings_argv`) gains `add to held pairs off` / `--no-add-to-held-pairs`. A dev run reads no positions (see the dev-mode gotcha), so the setting changes nothing there, on or off. The defaults server's confirmation page keeps the saved value (the seed's, with none saved) when a link leaves the field out; the dashboard's save button sends the choice only from a page that shows it. A link carrying the seed's note that leaves the field out takes the seed's value instead, so an out-of-date seed link still proposes exactly the seed, with the change shown.

**Why adding to a held pair is safe (DR-77).** Both finders' exemption and `select_portfolio`'s rest on one guarantee: a `HeldPair` is built only by `held_pairs`, which returns one only when its markets (two for an exact pair, one for a lone leg) are alone on their ladder and every held market's ladder is known; `pair_held` reads it by type, and `HeldPair.matches` checks both tickers and the side on each after a ladder may have swapped its legs (a same-title pair whose prices have crossed, so it would buy the other way round, is refused). Just before the NO leg is sent the trader checks again that the legs and the positions still match the held pair exactly (check 2, the next paragraph). **Kelly on the whole position.** `config.held_pair_fraction` makes an add-on stake what the held pair's stake — its worth today plus the fees paid for it — is missing of `min(f*, cap)` of the portfolio value (the cash plus Kalshi's value of the open positions), and like every trade it spends at most the cash, so it never stakes more than a new pair would, `config.max_kelly_fraction` still bounds every size and `live_rule_warnings` needs no new sentence. **The value it sizes on is checked.** Kalshi's value of the open positions counts only when the contracts held can back it (`main._checked_positions_value`), and when it is unread or refused the run adds to no held pair, so an add-on is never sized on a value the run could not read or one that overstates what the account holds. A value of 0 is always kept: it counts the positions as nothing, which can only make an add-on smaller. **Rollbacks touch only the new trade** (by count and by confirmed fill), and every alert names the older holding (next paragraph). **Residuals.** A same-title held pair with room at the per-trade cap but not at the lower same-title cap, or whose stake (its worth at today's prices plus its fees) already holds its Kelly share, can still take its group's one slot and size to nothing (`compute_trade` logs it), so a run never trades two pairs of one same-title group, as before. `market_exposure_dollars` is documented only as "Cost of the aggregate market position in dollars"; that it excludes fees, is positive for a NO position and falls with a partial close is inferred, and the code fails closed on a missing, unreadable, negative or implausibly small value (below `MIN_ACTIVE_PRICE_DOLLARS` × the count). The portfolio value counts the positions at Kalshi's own valuation while a held pair counts at the ask of the side held, so the two need not agree; a held market whose ask is missing, 0 or 1 counts at its exposure, not at a settled price, and its fees still count in the stake (`tests/test_scanner.py::TestHeldPairs::test_a_leg_with_no_usable_ask_still_counts_its_fees_in_the_stake`). The stake counts the fees paid for the pair while the portfolio value leaves them out (they are spent), so the check leans toward adding less: at the prices it was bought at, a pair bought at its full Kelly share adds nothing (a stake without the fees would top it up by about a contract pair on every later run, each paying a rounded-up fee of its own; see the backtest add-on paragraph); an add-on comes from a larger Kelly share, a pair that lost value, or a first trade the cash had shrunk. Pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestComputeTradeAddsToHeldPair::test_a_pair_that_bought_its_share_adds_nothing_at_unchanged_prices` (it adds nothing, where a stake without the fees buys one more pair), `::test_at_unchanged_prices_no_new_pair_is_topped_up_next_run` (777 starting values, none tops up) and `::test_the_fees_paid_shrink_the_add_on`, and `tests/test_config.py::TestHeldPairFraction::test_a_stake_with_its_fees_leans_safe_at_unchanged_prices`. **The first run to check:** before the setting goes on for every run, run `python3 -m kalshi_betting.main --mode prod --dry-run --add-to-held-pairs` and read `kalshi_arb.log`: the `Sizing on portfolio value $X = cash $Y + open positions $Z` line, whose `$Z` must be a dollar figure (not `not read`) with no `Kalshi's value of the open positions (…) is not used` WARNING after it, one `Held pair to add to: …, cost $C (fees $F), worth $W at today's prices` line per held pair whose legs both still show, `Time-series pairs that add to a held pair: N`, and `(adds to N held)` in the pairs table; check each held pair's cost and fees against its fills in the Kalshi UI (count × price + fees) and its worth against the asks there (count × the ask of the side held), and if the value is missing or refused, or a figure is wrong or missing, keep the setting off. The first-run order in the saved-live-defaults paragraph under Key Patterns puts this check between saving the seed values with adding off and turning adding on (with nothing saved, this dry run exits 2). Pinned by `tests/test_config.py::TestHeldPairFraction`, `::TestContractPayout` and `::TestLiveSettings::test_every_optional_toggle_is_a_field_with_a_default_at_the_end`, `tests/test_scanner.py::TestGetHeldPositions`, `::TestHeldPairs`, `::TestHeldPairMatches`, `::TestPairHeld`, `::TestFinderAddsToHeldPairs`, `::TestSameTitleAddsToHeldPairs` and `::TestResolveHeldLadders::test_labels_out_names_every_identified_market`, `tests/test_strategy.py::TestComputeTradeAddsToHeldPair` and `::TestSelectPortfolioAddOns`, the flags by `tests/test_main.py::TestLiveSettingsFlags`, the confirmation page by `tests/test_defaults_server.py::TestConfirmPage::test_a_missing_add_to_held_pairs_keeps_the_saved_value`, `::test_a_named_add_to_held_pairs_is_proposed`, `::TestSeed::test_a_seven_toggle_file_is_proposed_the_seed_with_adding_on` and `::test_a_seed_link_that_leaves_out_adding_to_held_pairs_still_proposes_the_seed`, end to end by `tests/test_main.py::TestRunProdAddsToHeldPairs` (production dry runs over a live-shape client holding an exact time-series pair: on, it is added to on its whole position and every log line above appears; off, or through `--no-add-to-held-pairs`, it is refused as before and `held_pairs` is never called; unequal counts, one side held twice, a cost not reported, a third held market on the ladder, a failed held-market lookup and a listing that stopped early each mean no add-on, the last two with their WARNING; an unread or refused positions value means no add-on too, with its WARNING, and holding nothing no such WARNING; a same-title held pair at its cap is left out so its group's unheld pair trades, the cap read on the portfolio value and the pair's stake, its worth at today's prices plus its fees (`::test_the_cap_check_reads_the_portfolio_value_and_the_worth_at_todays_prices`, and `::test_the_cap_check_counts_the_fees_paid_for_the_pair`, where the worth alone leaves room at the cap and the stake with its fees does not, so the pair is left out), with both legs of every trade checked and a book on every market of the group, so an ordinary pair on a held market would trade if the run let one through; and a dev run with the setting on reads no positions) and `::TestRunProdAddsToHeldPairsLive` (live runs, every order body recorded, over a three-rung ladder with YES held on its Dec 13 rung and NO on its Dec 20 rung: `::test_on_trades_only_yes_13_and_no_20` — adding on sends one order per leg on those two rungs alone, each for the new contracts only, never touching Dec 27, which the run trades with nothing held, while adding off or the holding the other way round sends nothing on the ladder; `::test_the_first_no_fill_of_the_process_is_an_add_on` — an add-on as the process's first NO fill confirms the V2 NO-leg mapping from the change across its own fill; `::test_a_killed_yes_leg_unwinds_only_the_new_contracts` — the unwind is a reduce-only bid for the new contracts only, leaving the 30 held), and by AST in `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_add_ons_size_through_held_pair_fraction` (`_evaluate_size` calls `held_pair_fraction` and `pair_held`, `select_portfolio` calls `pair_held` and `matches`, and both finders call `matches`), `::test_ast_the_live_run_refuses_pairs_on_held_ladders` (`_run_prod` calls `get_held_positions` and `held_pairs`, never `get_held_tickers`, and hands `add_on_pairs=add_on_pairs` to both finders, which read it), `::test_ast_the_run_lets_back_in_only_the_pairs_it_hands_the_finders` (`add_on_tickers` is read from `add_on_pairs` alone, after its last change; `blocked_tickers` is `held_tickers - add_on_tickers`, handed to both finders and filtering the market list; four changed copies of `_run_prod` are each caught), `::test_ast_add_ons_size_on_the_portfolio_value_before_the_budget` (`_evaluate_size` calls `held_pair_fraction(kelly_fraction_capped, held.stake_dollars, portfolio_value_cents / 100.0)` before its one `kelly_budget` and `max_affordable_pairs`, and every `return None` of `compute_trade` comes right after a `_log_no_add_on` call) and `::test_ast_the_live_run_values_held_pairs_at_market` (`_run_prod` makes one `held_pairs(held_positions, held_labels, markets_by_ticker)` call, builds `markets_by_ticker` from the full market list before held markets are dropped, calls `_checked_positions_value` once, between `get_held_positions` and `held_pairs`, and reads the at-cap check on `pair.stake_dollars` and the portfolio value), each with a test showing its check catches changed copies (`::test_ast_the_add_on_checks_catch_their_mutants`, `::test_ast_the_live_run_check_catches_its_mutants`).

**An add-on is sent only onto exactly the pair it adds to, and its rollback touches only the new contracts (DR-77, order time).** Kalshi nets every trade on a market into one position, with no lots, so "only the new trade" is kept by count and by confirmation, never by a position read: the unwind is always `no_leg.count` (this pair's), reduce-only and immediate-or-cancel, and it is sent only after THIS order's NO fill is confirmed (a 2xx full fill, or a position delta of exactly −`no_leg.count`), so a held −30 that this pair took to −40 goes back to −30 and never below. With an older position on the NO leg's market, `reduce_only` no longer makes a mistaken unwind harmless — it would close the older contracts — so those confirmation gates are load-bearing: do not loosen them, and never size an unwind from a position read. **Check 2:** in `trader._execute_legs`, right after both baselines are read and before the NO leg's POST (so no pacer place is taken and nothing is sent), `trader._add_on_mismatch(spec, no_leg, yes_leg, before_no, before_yes)` refuses an add-on (`scanner.pair_held(spec.pair)` not `None`) whose NO leg is not on the ticker held NO, whose YES leg is not on the ticker held YES, either of whose positions could not be read or did not read as a finite number, or whose positions no longer read the held count (−count on the NO market, +count on the YES market): one WARNING (`Not sending '…' (A=… B=…): it adds to a pair the account holds, but <reason>. Nothing was sent.`, the reason e.g. `the account does not hold NO on T` or `T now holds 20 NO contracts; the held pair had 30 NO contracts`) and `status="failed"`, error `not sent: this does not match the held pair it adds to (<reason>); nothing submitted`. The held count it compares with is the one read from the positions listing at the start of the run, so a trade by anyone else on either market since then (by hand, or another client) makes the add-on "failed" with nothing sent, never a resized one. Nothing in the same run can change it: `select_portfolio` takes a spec only if neither of its tickers was used by an earlier pick, so no other pair of the run trades either market before the add-on's NO leg goes out. A spec `select_portfolio` shrank to the cash left is checked the same way: the check compares the held counts, never the new count, and the unwind is the shrunk count. It returns `None` for every ordinary pair, and it runs after the disproven-latch stop, which stays first. Every live add-on reaches it: a pair carries a `HeldPair` only when `main._run_prod` handed its finder that held pair (see "How `main._run_prod` switches adding to held pairs on" above). **Operator text:** every alert that has the position read before the pair names what the market held there when that was a number other than zero, in plain words (`trader._pre_pair_note`: ` Before this pair the account already held 30 NO contracts (position -30) on T. Those are not this pair's: close only this pair's contracts and leave those alone.`, placed before ` Manual review required` in both "could NOT be attributed" CRITICALs and `_rollback_no_leg`'s three ORPHANED alerts, which take the baseline as the keyword-only `before`), and every "flatten this position by hand" remedy says instead to put the position back to what it held: both disproof CRITICALs and the already-disproven branch through `trader._restore_remedy` (`put the position on T back to 30 NO contracts (-30) by hand in the Kalshi UI (that is what the account held there before this pair; do not close it to 0)`), and the defaults server's attention banner (`_ATTENTION_TAIL`) by saying to close out a market the account did not hold before the run and put one it did hold back to what it held; `execute_trades`' worker-crash CRITICAL adds ` (this pair adds to a held pair of N contracts on each market: check both positions and undo only what this pair added)` for an add-on, built from `pair_held`, never from the exception. These texts apply to any pair whose market read a position other than zero before it, an ordinary pair included (a position opened by hand after the run read its positions, say); check 2 and the markers below apply only to an add-on. Every text is byte-identical when the position before the pair was 0 or unknown (a reading that is not a finite number counts as unknown), and every `TradeResult.error` is unchanged. **Markers** (reporting only, exactly "adds to N held", N written exactly by `config.count_text`): the trade log's Notes bracket ends ` adds to N held` (no column is added), `main.print_pairs_table`'s Recommended Trade cell ` (adds to N held)`, `main._print_portfolio`'s line ` — adds to N held`, the dry-run line ` | adds to N held`, the trade log's rescue dump ` (adds to N held)` right after `y=`, the run result's `TradeRecord.adds_to_held` (the held count, `None` for any other trade) and the defaults server run page's Note cell `(adds to N held)`. Pinned by `tests/test_trader.py::TestAddOnRollbacksTouchOnlyTheNewTrade` and `::TestAddOnIsSentOnlyOntoItsPair`, end to end through a live `main._run_prod` by `tests/test_main.py::TestRunProdAddsToHeldPairsLive::test_the_first_no_fill_of_the_process_is_an_add_on` and `::test_a_killed_yes_leg_unwinds_only_the_new_contracts`, the re-pinned `::TestV2NoMappingBackstop::test_external_short_position_cannot_mask_a_disproof` (an account already −100 on the NO market now gets the restore remedy: flattening would close 95 unrelated contracts), `::TestHeldPositionWords`, `::TestAnOrdinaryPairOnAHeldMarket`, `tests/test_reporter.py::TestResultToRow::test_an_add_on_row_names_the_held_count` and `::TestTradeRecord::test_an_add_on_records_the_held_count`, `tests/test_config.py::TestCountText`, `tests/test_main.py::TestAddOnMarkers`, `::TestResultFile::test_the_rescue_dump_names_an_add_ons_held_count` and `tests/test_defaults_server.py::TestRunPage::test_a_trade_that_added_to_a_held_pair_says_so_in_its_note`. Residual: a reduce-only unwind against a LARGER existing position has never been exercised live (the probe only closes from flat), so watch the first add-on rollback.

**Adding to a lone held leg (DR-77, PR #88 review, 2026-09-30).** When one market of a held pair pays out (its position leaves the positions listing) while the other is still open, the open one is still a held market — it stays in the held set and keeps blocking its ladder (DR-76) — and, with `add_to_held_pairs` on, it is a **lone leg** a run may add to (operator decision). `scanner.held_pairs` returns it as a `HeldPair` with ONE side (`HeldPair.lone`, keyed by a one-ticker frozenset in `add_on_pairs`): a held market alone on its ladder (no other held market shares a label, every held market's ladder known), a readable count, exposure and fees, and an exposure of at least the finest price times the count. It is valued like a pair's leg (`_held_leg_worth`: the ask of the side held, or its exposure with no usable ask), and its stake is that worth plus that market's own fees — the partner's payout is already cash, so it counts nothing. A candidate adds to it when it buys the held market on its held side beside a market the account does not hold (`HeldPair.matches`; the finders' lookup is `scanner._add_on_for`, which refuses a candidate touching two held markets that are not one exact pair), and, on the time-series finder and in `select_portfolio`, only when that new market meets no held ladder but the lone leg's own (`HeldPair.labels`): otherwise a new market on another held market's event would stack a trade beside it. Sizing, the at-cap drop and the R5/R6 fail-closed rules are the pair's (`held_pair_fraction` on `stake_dollars`). At order time `trader._add_on_mismatch` requires the held market to still read its held count and the new market to read nothing (`"<ticker> now holds …; it was not held"`). Log lines: `Held market to add to (its partner has paid out): NO <ticker>, N contracts, cost $C (fees $F), worth $W at today's prices`, and, silent at zero, `Held markets to add to whose partner has paid out: N`; the `Held pairs to add to: N (other held markets, never added to: M)` line counts exact pairs only and leaves lone legs out of M. **Shapes it does not cover:** a partner that has CLOSED but not yet paid out is still in the positions listing, so the two still form an exact pair the finders cannot trade (the closed market is not in the run's list) and the open market stays blocked until the payout; a lone held market that was never one leg of a pair (an orphan left by a `rollback_failed`, or a position bought by hand) cannot be told apart from one whose partner paid out, and is added to the same way; and a lone leg that has been added to is no longer lone (its new partner is held), so it is added to again only as an exact pair — never, since the counts differ — until that partner pays out too. **Backtest mirror:** `_simulate_at_discount(add_to_held=True)` keeps each open leg (`open_legs`: its trade, side, ladder labels and the days its market and its partner pay out), and a candidate whose one held market has only legs whose partners have paid out, all on the side the candidate buys there, and whose other market no open trade holds, adds to it (`_lone_leg_records`), refused on the add-on ladder line when another open trade holds a ladder label of either market; its stake is that leg alone in each of its open trades, at market plus its exact fee (`_open_leg_stake`, through `_leg_mark`, now the one reader of a checkpoint quote, which `_open_value` also reads through). It is sized through `_size_trade` like any trade, so the cap reaches it as it reaches any trade and `CapSweep`'s reuse rule (the sharing floor) is unchanged. Pinned by `tests/test_scanner.py::TestHeldPairs::test_a_lone_leg_whose_partner_paid_out_is_added_to`, `::TestFinderAddsToALoneLeg`, `::TestAddOnFor`, `::TestHeldPairMatches::test_a_lone_leg_matches_its_held_side_beside_any_other_market`, `::TestSameTitleAddsToHeldPairs::test_a_lone_leg_pairs_with_a_market_not_held`, `tests/test_strategy.py::TestSelectPortfolioAddOns::test_a_lone_legs_new_market_on_another_held_ladder_is_refused`, `tests/test_trader.py::TestAddOnIsSentOnlyOntoItsPair::test_a_lone_leg_add_on_is_refused_unless_it_still_matches`, `tests/test_main.py::TestRunProdAddsToHeldPairsLive::test_a_lone_leg_adds_only_beside_its_held_side` and `tests/test_backtester.py::TestAddToALoneLeg`.

**Cross-strike time-series pairs — the group key must carry the outcome label (DR-01, fixed 2026-09).** A Kalshi daily price family lists dozens of strikes under ONE market title (event `KXBTCD-26SEP1517`: 80 markets, all titled "Bitcoin price on Sep 15, 2026?", the strike only in the subtitle — "$67,500 or above" … "$87,250 or above"). While the time-series key was `normalize_title(pair_key(m))`, every strike of every deadline in such a family landed in ONE group, and the one-best-pair rule (tradeable first, then largest `pB − pA`) then selected the highest earlier strike against the lowest later one: two different questions sized and traded as one cumulative-deadline pair, with no in-between cell at all. Measured on the 2026-09-15 prod dry run's market snapshot, roughly 974 of 979 tier-qualifying combinations in one BTC group were cross-strike; a second read of that snapshot counted 1,021 of 1,027, the two differing in how the group was delimited rather than in the finding — both are over 99%, and it is the proportion, not the count, that the fix rests on. And **four of the six trades that dry run selected were cross-strike** (a fifth was the same-series pair DR-02 names); the six together were ~98% of the cash balance. No money was lost only because the scheduler daemon has never been started. **The fix is one helper on both paths.** `scanner.time_series_group_key(combined_title, subtitle)` returns `"<normalize_title(pair_key)> | <normalized subtitle>"` (the title alone when the subtitle is empty, `""` when the title itself normalizes away), and BOTH `scanner.find_time_series_pairs` and `backtester._group_by_normalized_title` call it — the latter, since SS-1, through `backtester._ts_group_key`, the backtester's one definition of the key, which `_index_eligible_keys` also reads to decide which records are materialized for grouping at all — pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_finders_group_through_the_shared_key_helper` (a two-link chain on the backtester side), because the backtester previously keyed the same wrong way and therefore reproduced the defect rather than detecting it. Three details are load-bearing. (1) **The subtitle is normalized by `scanner._normalize_subtitle`, NOT `normalize_title`.** That pass strips any bare `20xx` token as a year, and a subtitle is exactly where a bare 4-digit strike ("2050 or above") lives — running it over an outcome label would merge every strike in 2000–2099 into one key, reintroducing DR-01 through the subtitle. `_normalize_subtitle` strips only the unambiguous DATE shapes — `_EXPLICIT_DATE_PATTERNS`, every entry taken BY INDEX from `_DATE_PATTERNS` so a copy can never drift: the month-name / abbreviated-month / numeric / ISO shapes that carry a year, the deadline-anchored month + day shape from detail (2), and the pre-existing bare abbreviated month + day — plus trailing punctuation, so "$80,000 by June 30" and "$80,000 by July 31" are one strike at two deadlines and "Donald Trump" and "Donald Trump." are one option label. Whitespace and punctuation are trimmed in ONE `rstrip`, because a stripped date leaves a space in FRONT of any trailing punctuation ("$80,000 by June 30." → "$80,000 by .") and trimming them in sequence left that space in the key, silently dropping a genuine same-strike pair. (2) **`_DATE_PATTERNS` gained ONE shape — deadline preposition + full month name + day ("by June 30") — placed AHEAD of the `by/before/until/through/after <Month>` clause** — that clause's optional day group only matches when a year follows, so from behind it consumed "by June" and stranded the day, leaving "30" against "31" to split two titles that differ only in their deadline. **It is anchored to the preposition deliberately.** A bare `<Month> <day>` also erases the date from SNAPSHOT titles ("... on June 30"), which would put two snapshot markets of one family into a single time-series group — the shape DR-67 now screens at pair formation on the legs' wording, with the backtester's counter as defence in depth. Anchored, the pattern fires only where that clause already fired and stranded a day, so no title that used to stay apart is merged; the ABBREVIATED spelling ("by Oct 1") is deliberately left behind the clause and still splits, unchanged. `tests/test_scanner.py::TestNormalizeTitle` is the gate CLAUDE.md names for any `_DATE_PATTERNS` change, and it now pins both directions ("by June 30"/"by July 31" merge; "on June 30"/"on July 1" do not). (3) **Non-string subtitles read as absent, never raise** (a `MagicMock` auto-attribute, `None` from an old cache record) — the same fail-safe-by-type rule `leg_sides` and `strategy._depth_levels` follow. **Cache caveat:** backtest day slices written before the 2026-08 subtitle fix carry `subtitle=None`, so a backtest over them still groups time-series candidates by title alone and stays strike-blind; refreshing them is the deletion procedure in the subtitle-drift gotcha below. **And the guard is only as good as the field.** `subtitle` is normalized in at ingest from `subtitle`/`yes_sub_title` (see that same gotcha, which records that the field comes and goes): if BOTH ever go missing again, every market keys with an empty outcome label and the time-series key silently reverts to the pre-fix, strike-blind `normalize_title(pair_key(m))` — with real money on it and no warning of any kind; and when only ONE leg of a genuine pair carries the label, the two legs key apart and the pair is silently dropped (a missed trade, also unlogged). **The live and backtest paths have DIFFERENT symptoms, and the long-standing counter covers only one of them.** `scanner.find_time_series_pairs` — the LIVE finder, and the counter's only home — logs `Distinct normalized title+outcome keys with >= 1 market`; a count collapsing toward the market count, or a time-series pair count dropping to zero, is the symptom to check first on the live path. That is the ONE-leg-labelled case, where the two legs key APART and keys SPLIT. It says nothing about a backtest, for two independent reasons: the backtester never calls that function (it calls `_group_by_normalized_title` directly), and when BOTH legs are unlabelled the keys MERGE instead, so the count moves the other way entirely. `backtester._log_outcome_label_coverage` is the backtest-path signal (DR-66; since SS-1 `_prepare_candidates` runs its two halves, `_OutcomeLabelTally` and `_report_outcome_label_coverage`, directly — see below): an INFO census of `subtitle` and `event_title` coverage over the eligible markets on every run, escalating to a WARNING naming the consequence and the cache-refresh remedy when subtitle coverage falls below `config.BACKTEST_OUTCOME_LABEL_WARN_FRACTION`. It exists because a backtest over a pre-2026-08-14 cache reported pair counts, trades, a return figure and an empirical `k̂` recommendation for `TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT` that all described the strike-blind strategy, while being indistinguishable in its own output from a run on a good cache — measured on the assembled 2026-05-01 cache: 2,344,886 records, 98.14% with a blank subtitle and 87.89% with a blank event title, against ZERO blank subtitles on either post-fix assembled cache. **The DASHBOARD says so too, and that half is DR-66b.** The log alone was not enough: `backtest.py`'s log ends by pointing the operator at the HTML (and then at its "Save as live defaults…" button), and a dashboard generated from a run whose census had logged 0.00% coverage contained none of the strings "subtitle", "coverage", "strike-blind" or "cache" anywhere in its 140 KB, rendering a bare `Pooled empirical k̂` card — a recommendation for the real-money `TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT` — with no caveat of any kind. (A reviewer was fooled by exactly this and reported a pooled `k̂` of 1.114 upstream as a finding about the strategy. "The number is arithmetically correct for the corpus it was handed" is the same defence that would have excused the log silence, and it is not enough.) `_log_outcome_label_coverage` therefore RETURNS the frozen `OutcomeLabelCoverage(total, with_subtitle, with_event_title, subtitle_fraction, event_title_fraction, below_floor, cumulative_markets, snapshot_markets, unknown_deadline_markets)` it just logged — same single pass, same position in `_prepare_candidates` (the first half of `_prepare_entries`), same log wording byte-for-byte (since SS-1 `_prepare_candidates` uses the census's two halves directly: it feeds an `_OutcomeLabelTally` inside its first walk over the corpus and logs through `_report_outcome_label_coverage` at that same position; `_log_outcome_label_coverage` is their one-shot composition and still accepts any iterable, walked exactly once) — which rides out on `_Candidates.label_coverage` onto `BacktestSweep.label_coverage` and into `_section_interval_discount` (`_prepare_entries` also returns it as element 1 of its pair, which `run_backtest` discards). Four things are load-bearing. (1) **A RETURN VALUE, not an optional sink.** An `emit`-style callback (the idiom `_fetch_archive_day` already uses) would have cost zero test edits, but a caller can FORGET it and silently lose the caveat — reproducing DR-66's own failure mode inside the mechanism built to close it. The tuple cannot be omitted; the defaulted `BacktestSweep` field is the one remaining place an omission is possible, which is why both production constructions pass it together — true only since DR-71 (fixed 2026-09) made the three phrasing fields (`cumulative_markets`/`snapshot_markets`/`unknown_deadline_markets`) REQUIRED, with no default: before it, a construction that forgot them silently defaulted to `0, 0, 0`, a second, unmentioned place an omission was possible — and a bad one, since it made the page's strongest sentence ("could not have produced a time-series trade at all") render as if measured. A forgotten phrasing keyword is now a `TypeError` at construction, the same "cannot be forgotten" reasoning this return value already used. The nine constructions that pass all three explicitly (counted 2026-09-23 by grepping `OutcomeLabelCoverage(` over `kalshi_betting/` and `tests/`): two in `backtester.py` (the empty-corpus branch passes `0, 0, 0` because that IS a measurement, and the main branch reads them off the census); one in `tests/test_backtester.py` (`TestOutcomeLabelCoverageIsCarried::test_the_sweep_carries_the_census`); five in `tests/test_dashboard.py` (the module-level `_coverage` and `_scn_coverage` helpers, `TestOutcomeLabelCoverageIsRendered::test_the_verdict_is_carried_not_recomputed` and `::test_an_empty_corpus_is_not_reported_as_zero_percent`, and `TestDeadlinePhrasingIsRendered._coverage`, whose `**base` dict carries all three); and one in `tests/dashboard_golden.py` (`_coverage`). A tenth, `tests/test_backtester.py::TestOutcomeLabelCoverageIsCarried::test_the_phrasing_fields_are_required`, deliberately omits one of them per case to prove the `TypeError`. (2) **ONE measurement, so the page and the log can never disagree** — including the threshold, which is evaluated once and carried as `below_floor`. Measuring over `_interval_calibration`'s entries instead was rejected for the sharper reason that it would measure the SURVIVORS of the very grouping under suspicion, and would vanish entirely on a same-title-only run — exactly the run where the lost same-title discriminator (TS-11) is the whole exposure. (3) **The rendered figure describes the ELIGIBLE-MARKET CORPUS, not the `k̂` population**, which is a far smaller, differently-selected subset (entered, binarily settled, non-premise-violating time-series candidates). Phrase it as "N of M eligible markets", never as "of the pairs behind `k̂`", and never put it inside the calibration table, whose rows are per-bucket counts of the `k̂` population. The banner's consequence sentence is the one claim that legitimately spans both. (4) **`subtitle_fraction`/`event_title_fraction` are `None`, not `0.0`, on an empty corpus** (undefined rather than zero), and the carrier holds scalars only (counts, fractions and one verdict) and no reference to any market dict — enriching it with sample tickers or a per-category breakdown would pin every record alive past `_prepare_candidates`' release of the corpus and the groupable subset and undo TS-07's residency work. Every number rendered is this run's own count or the configured floor; the historical figures above stay in `config.py` and this file, never in an emitted string (TS-07). **Concentration moved too:** the one-best-pair-per-group rule used to bound a whole daily family to ONE pair, and now bounds one STRIKE to one pair, so an N-strike family can contribute up to N pairs — all on the same underlying over the same window. `strategy.select_portfolio` used to dedup tickers only, with no per-event or per-family cap; since DR-76 it takes at most one time-series trade per ladder (per event, or per question), and a daily family lists every strike of one deadline in ONE event, so any two of its pairs that share a deadline's event never both trade in one run — and a later run refuses a new pair on a ladder the account still holds (see the DR-76 gotcha). Two strikes' pairs over four different events share no label and can still both trade. **Sibling (DR-17): the outcome label is now visible.** `scanner.display_title` appends " — <subtitle>" unless the subtitle IS the label already (`market_title` falls back to it when the title is empty), which is what the prod Excel "Market A"/"Market B" cells render; and `main.print_pairs_table` gained its own "Outcome A"/"Outcome B" columns, because `main._truncate` cuts the two title cells at 40 characters and a real `display_title` is well past that — so appending alone would have left the four cross-strike rows of that dry run looking identical, which is exactly why nobody caught them in review. `main._print_portfolio` — the log of the trades actually SELECTED — names both tickers for the same reason: it prints `canonical_title[:55]`, and a time-series canonical title now ends with the outcome label, which that cut drops. **A strike spelled inside the TITLE is a different case and is NOT addressed here** (DR-26, deliberately out of this plan): a `$80k`-shaped strike survives `normalize_title`, so two such markets already key apart, but a bare 4-digit one is eaten by the standalone-year pattern and nothing added here guards that.

**Identical wording across two events of ONE series is two fixtures, not one question (DR-02, DR-54, fixed 2026-09).** A Kalshi event ticker is a series prefix followed by an instance stamp (`KXNPBRFI-26SEP160500FUKORI`, `KXTTELITEMATCH-26SEP101835KKAMOL`, `KXMVECROSSCATEGORY-SHARD1-S6471E4699E9`), so two events of ONE series are two instances of one recurring fixture — two ball games, two 15-minute price windows, two combos of different games. The date lives only in the event ticker, the event sub-title, the close time and the rules text — none of which any grouping key reads — so the `event_ticker` inequality guard both finders already had *passes* on exactly this shape. Identical wording across them is the same question asked about two DIFFERENT events, and neither strategy's premise survives it: the 95% co-resolution prior is simply false (the 2026-09-15 dry run's NPB pair — `KXNPBRFI-26SEP160500FUKORI-Y` and `…150500FUKORI-Y`, identical title, subtitle and event title, closes 24 h apart — was quoted 0.97 and 0.01, and `strategy._kelly_p` priced it at the flat `SAME_TITLE_CO_RESOLVE_PROB` 0.95, sizing 16 contracts a side for $12.39 against a reported "$3.61 profit floor" whose true expected value, on the two markets' own prices with independent games, is negative), and there is no cumulative-deadline premise either, because the deadline lives entirely outside the wording. 71 of that run's 75 same-title rows were this shape. **The rule is fixture identity, not close time** — for the ONE-SERIES rule; since DR-74 a close gate sits ON TOP of it for CROSS-series same-title pairs (see the next gotcha), as a second condition, never as a replacement. The sweep's adversary critic measured 117,801 identical-wording NON-combo pairs off the settled cache: they co-resolved 96.3–98.2% of the time in EVERY close-gap band, so no threshold isolates the bad ones (that figure pools both sides of this rule — the same-series pairs it now rejects and the different-series ones it keeps — and it is an UNCONDITIONAL rate, whereas the finder only ever prices a pair the market has already diverged 5% on; treat it as the reason a close-gap gate was rejected, never as a co-resolution estimate for what survives) — while identical-wording COMBO pairs closing within one hour of each other still settled differently 13.8% of the time (an admitted example closed 96 seconds apart and settled yes vs no). A one-hour gate was therefore rejected: it would drop pairs that co-resolve almost always and still admit two matches of one table-tennis player (`KXTTELITEMATCH-26SEP101835KKAMOL` vs `…101810MOLJMI`, about half an hour apart, settled yes and no) and two consecutive 15-minute gas windows. (That rejection was of a one-hour gate as a REPLACEMENT for the series rule, measured on a different corpus. DR-74's one-hour gate is an ADDITIONAL condition on pairs the series rule already admits, and on its 365-day corpus identical-wording non-combo pairs settle the same way 68.2% of the time same-series and 65.6% cross-series, while cross-series pairs settle the same way 99.2% of the time at a 0 s close gap against 60.7% beyond 1 min — so on the cross-series side proximity DOES separate the two, which is all DR-74 relies on.) **Both finders carry it, and that is the whole point.** `scanner.find_same_title_pairs` skips a candidate when `_same_series(m_outer, m_inner)` (its group key already guarantees identical wording), counting the skips and reporting them once as `Same-title candidates skipped as two instances of one event series (identical wording, different fixture): N`; `scanner.find_time_series_pairs` skips when `_identical_wording(mA, mB) and _same_series(mA, mB)`, and since M10 counts those skips too (`Time-series candidates skipped as two instances of one event series (...): N`), as `backtester._extract_pairs` does on both of its branches — see the DR-67 observability paragraph for the exact lines. Every one of these lines is silent at zero, and counting moves no control flow: M10's pair lists were compared byte for byte with its parent's on seven synthetic corpora (one combo-heavy, six seeded fuzz), ladders on and off, through both `_extract_pairs` branches and both live finders — 28 cases, every one identical. Gating only the same-title finder would have RELABELLED the trade rather than removed it: the same two NPB tickers clear the time-series filter too (`pB − pA` = 0.96, gap 1 day), and `main._dedup_pairs` only ever dropped that copy because a same-title copy existed — 39 of that run's 75 same-title rows would have come back as time-series pairs. `backtester._extract_pairs` mirrors both branches through `_same_series_dicts` / `_identical_wording_dicts`, which derive the prefix from the same `scanner.event_series`, so live and backtest can never disagree about what a series is; all of it is pinned by AST in `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_finders_apply_the_one_series_rule` and `::test_ast_the_series_prefix_has_one_definition`. **`event_series` fails CLOSED.** An empty, hyphen-leading or non-string event ticker yields `""`, and `_same_series` reads an unknown prefix on either side as the SAME series — a pair whose fixture identity cannot be read must not be priced on the co-resolution prior. **What still pairs under THIS rule:** two DIFFERENT series asking one question (the shape the same-title strategy was built for) — and, since DR-74, only when their two markets also close within 1 hour of each other; and any pair of ONE series whose deadline is IN the wording — `KXSOLD-26SEP14` / `KXSOLD-26SEP18` titled "Solana price on Sep 14/18, 2026?", or the FIDE Oct/Nov markets from the same run, make `_identical_wording` false. Note both of those named examples are nonetheless refused as TIME-SERIES pairs by DR-67, on the separate ground that "price on \<date\>" and "top-10 in \<month\>" are snapshot wordings; `tests/test_scanner.py::TestOneEventSeriesIsTwoFixtures` pins both verdicts on the same fixture, so a reader can see which rule decides what. **This rule decides fixture identity only, in both directions.** It does not certify that a dated one-series pair is genuinely CUMULATIVE — "price on <date>" is exactly the SNAPSHOT shape the time-series paragraph above names (SOL ≥ $180 on Sep 14 does not imply SOL ≥ $180 on Sep 18; top-10 in October does not imply top-10 in November). **DR-67 now answers that separately**: the cumulative-deadline rule refuses both of those examples on their wording, so the `KXSOLD` Sep 14/18 pair and the FIDE Oct/Nov pair named above as "what still pairs" are eligible under THIS rule and refused by that one. And different series is a NECESSARY condition for a same-title pair, never a sufficient one: the same prop listed for two different games in two different leagues shares its wording too (`KXBRASILEIROB1HTOTAL-26SEP10VINGGO-1` and `KXUCL1HTOTAL-26SEP10MUNSBH-1`, both "Over 0.5 1H goals scored", settled no and yes), and only the `event_title` component of the same-title key separates them — which live ingest populates but pre-`event_title` cache records leave `""`, so the backtest path grouped them where the live path usually did not. Since DR-74 they are refused on BOTH paths unless the two games close within 1 hour of each other: the close gate reads no `event_title`, so it does not depend on the cache carrying one. **The accepted cost:** a genuine cumulative-deadline pair of ONE series whose deadline appears *only* in the event ticker — never in the title, subtitle or event title — is dropped along with the recurring fixtures, because at that point nothing in the data the finders read separates the two. That is deliberate, not an oversight: the same-title finder had exactly this ambiguity before and resolved it the other way, with real money on it. **What stops entirely:** every same-title pair between two MVE combo EVENTS. Kalshi lists combos under several series prefixes, all beginning `KXMVE` — a census of `backtest_cache/event_titles.json` (3,996,906 keys, 2,444 distinct prefixes, measured 2026-09-16) found exactly four: `KXMVECROSSCATEGORY` (2,960,840), `KXMVESPORTSMULTIGAMEEXTENDED` (906,157), `KXMVECROSSCATEGORY0` (94,230) and `KXMVENBASINGLEGAME` (61), with no `KXMV*` prefix that is not also `KXMVE*` — so `scanner.event_series` collapses the whole family onto one series (`config.MVE_SERIES_FAMILY_PREFIX = "KXMVE"`, DR-55) and the one-series rule treats any two combo tickets as two instances of one recurring fixture. Reading the literal prefix DID refuse two combos listed under ONE of those prefixes; what it could not see was a pair spanning TWO of them, which read as two different series and was priced on the 0.95 co-resolution prior. That exposure is measured, not assumed: in `backtest_cache/live_days/2026-09-14.json.gz` (9,009,087 records, 8,939,229 of them `KXMVE*`), 10,643 distinct `(event_title, title, subtitle)` wordings appear under TWO literal `KXMVE` prefixes — every one of them `KXMVECROSSCATEGORY` x `KXMVECROSSCATEGORY0` — forming 26,072 cross-prefix market pairs of which 17,829 co-resolved and **8,243 settled DIFFERENTLY, a 68.4% co-resolution rate against the 0.95 prior**. That 68.4% carries the SAME caveat as the 96.3–98.2% above and for the same reason: it is an UNCONDITIONAL rate over every record in each cross-prefix wording class, with no price, liquidity or activity filter, whereas the finder only ever prices a pair the market has already diverged 5% on — treat it as the measured reason the family rule exists, never as a co-resolution estimate for the pairs that would actually have been traded. A FAMILY PREFIX rather than an enumerated allowlist, because enumerating the combo series would BE an allowlist and the codebase's own prior belief ("every combo sits under `KXMVECROSSCATEGORY`") was already such an allowlist with one of the four entries — the artefact that was measurably wrong here — and it rots every time Kalshi adds a combo series (`KXMVENBASINGLEGAME`, 61 keys, appears in no day slice at all yet). Over-collapsing is the safe direction since `_same_series` only ever REFUSES pairs, so its worst case is a missed trade between two genuinely independent `KXMVE*` series, or a still-eligible runner-up promoted where the refused pair was a group's best candidate, never a trade priced on a false premise. The family literal is deliberately narrow — prefix containment among ordinary series is common (`KXART` vs `KXARTISTSTREAMS` are unrelated), so it must never be widened to `KXMV` or to a generic containment rule. That is a pairing rule, not an ingest exclusion, and it is the intended reading of DR-54; if combo duplicates are ever wanted the discriminator is `mve_selected_legs`, which ingest does not carry today. **Backtest impact:** the `Potential pairs` count drops (fewer, correct pairs), `_can_ever_enter` is untouched so there is no `SETTLED_PREFILTER_CACHE_TAG` bump, and any later profiling baseline must be taken after this change. **Skipping is not only subtractive.** Both finders keep ONE best pair per group, so removing a group's top candidate PROMOTES the runner-up: a group whose widest gap was a one-series look-alike can now yield a time-series pair on tickers the previous code never proposed at all (pinned by `tests/test_scanner.py::TestOneEventSeriesIsTwoFixtures::test_skipping_the_widest_candidate_promotes_the_next_one`). That is intended — the promoted pair is the one the group actually supports — but it means this rule can ADD a trade as well as remove one, so a diff of two runs' pairs tables will not be purely a deletion. **Test fixtures moved with the rule:** every synthetic same-title pair a FINDER actually forms was re-pinned onto distinct series — the one-prefix `EVT-A`/`EVT-B` and `EV-A`/`EV-B` fixtures in `tests/test_scanner.py` and `tests/test_backtester.py` became `EVA-1`/`EVB-1` or `EA-1`/`EB-1`, and the eight event tickers in `tests/test_main.py`'s `_build_events` became eight distinct series. Fixtures that only exercise GROUPING, or that build a `CandidatePair` directly without going through a finder (`_st_candidate`, the enrichment and reachability pairs), keep their one-prefix tickers because no finder ever reads them. The synthetic TIME-SERIES fixtures also kept their one-series tickers and had their titles dated instead (`_ts_pair_markets`, both `TestTimeSeriesBestPairPerGroup` groups and `TestTimeSeriesGrouping::test_pricier_earlier_contract_is_not_a_candidate` now spell each market's own close date, the shape a real two-deadline family has) — that last one matters because without the dated titles the one-series rule, not the directional price filter, was what made its assertion pass. No assertion changed in either group.

**Identical wording on two DIFFERENT series is one question only when both markets close at the same moment (DR-74, fixed 2026-09).** The one-series rule above is NECESSARY for a same-title pair, never sufficient, and this is the shape it misses. The 365-day backtest (`--start-date 2025-09-24`, same-event ladders off — their value until 2026-09-26) made 21 trades, all same-title, for +4.7%, max drawdown −48%, and −37.5% without its best event. Rebuilt with the backtester's own functions, 17 of them paired a **men's** and a **women's** college basketball game between the same two schools — `KXNCAAMBGAME-26JAN13WIUEIU-WIU` vs `KXNCAAWBGAME-26JAN13WIUEIU-WIU`, identical title ("Western Illinois at Eastern Illinois Winner?"), identical subtitle and event title, on two different series, so the one-series rule let them through. One more paired the Champions League and La Liga "Atletico vs Barcelona" matches, 10 days apart; only the 3 Brent/copper weekly-vs-monthly trades asked one question. The 95% co-resolution prior (`SAME_TITLE_CO_RESOLVE_PROB`) is false for such pairs, and **the exposure is live**: the live finder uses the same key and the same series rule, and the 2026-09-23 prod dry run (`kalshi_arb.log:13954-13961`) formed 4 same-title pairs whose two legs close on different days — among them "India A Women vs Australia A Women", 2026-10-06 vs 2026-09-27, and "Texas vs Tennessee", 2026-09-27 vs 2026-09-28. None traded only because the price filter's fee check found them untradeable (each A leg was quoted 95% on both outcomes — an empty book — so their fixture identity is inferred from the dates, not the quotes). Each of the four closes at least 16 h apart.

**The rule.** A same-title candidate is refused when its two markets close more than `config.SAME_TITLE_MAX_CLOSE_GAP_SECONDS` (`60 * 60` — 1 hour) apart, or when their closes cannot be compared: it fails CLOSED like `_same_series`, on anything that is not a datetime and on one naive close beside one aware close. `scanner.closes_apart(close_a, close_b)` is the one definition, with a strict `>` (a gap of exactly the bound is admitted); `scanner._closes_apart(mA, mB)` is the live reader (reading `.close_time` by TYPE, so a `MagicMock`'s auto-attribute fails closed), called in `find_same_title_pairs` immediately AFTER the `_same_series` check, so the two older counts do not move and each candidate lands on exactly one line. `backtester._extract_pairs`' same-title branch mirrors it after `_same_series_dicts`: `backtester._comparable_closes_dicts` parses both cached close strings (`None` when either is absent or unparseable, or one naive and one aware) and the branch calls `closes_apart` on the result. Pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_paths_apply_the_same_title_close_gap` (which also asserts the ABSENCE of the gate from `find_time_series_pairs`) and `::test_ast_the_close_gap_has_one_definition`, and by value in `tests/test_scanner.py::TestSameTitleCloseGap` / `::TestClosesApart` and `tests/test_backtester.py::TestSameTitleCloseGapBacktest`. The gate reads `scanner`'s binding of the constant, so a test narrows it by patching `scanner.SAME_TITLE_MAX_CLOSE_GAP_SECONDS`, never `config.*` — the `TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS` precedent — and that one patch narrows the backtest's gate too, since there is no second copy. It also moves the bound both refusal lines PRINT: each formats it through `scanner.close_gap_bound_text()`, which reads that same binding at call time (whole minutes as minutes, anything else in seconds, so a patched bound is never truncated into a false one), and `backtester` imports no copy of the constant at all — pinned by `tests/test_backtester.py::TestSameTitleCloseGapBacktest::test_the_gate_is_the_scanner_definition`, which asserts the backtest line under a patched bound, by `tests/test_scanner.py::TestClosesApart::test_the_bound_text_never_misstates_the_bound` / `::test_the_bound_text_reads_the_gates_binding_at_call_time`, and by the AST pin `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_the_close_gap_has_one_definition`, which asserts both line sites call the helper.

**Why close time, and why 1 hour.** Checked against live and archived Kalshi payloads on 2026-09-25. The event's `product_metadata.competition` label separates both bad shapes ("College Basketball (M)" vs "(W)"; "Champions League" vs "La Liga") but is absent for commodities, and over the 1,906 backtest candidates both sides carried it on 1,377, neither on 459 and one side on 70. The event `sub_title` is identical for a same-day M/W doubleheader. A series' `settlement_sources` cannot tell UCL from La Liga and costs an extra `/series/{t}` call. Series titles always differ and tags never do. A market's `rules_primary` is free text the backtest cache lacks, and so are `occurrence_datetime`/`expected_expiration_time`. A series-ticker allowlist rots (DR-55). `close_time` is carried by both paths already. A read-only replay of the real `_extract_pairs` → `_find_entry` → `_simulate_at_discount` over the 365-day corpus's 1,906 same-title candidates (competitions from the public `/events/{t}` for 1,101 events; the regression critic independently reproduced the first and "close" rows):

| Rule | Candidate pairs | Settled the same way | Entries | Trades | Return | Max DD |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Today (no extra gate) | 1,906 | 65.6% | 25 | 21 | +4.7% | −48.0% |
| Same competition (refuse when both named and different) | 635 | 79.1% | 6 | 3 | +4.8% | −1.9% |
| Same competition, strict (one-sided name refused) | 565 | 80.2% | 6 | 3 | +4.8% | −1.9% |
| Same event `sub_title` only | 358 | 91.6% | 22 | 18 | +30.2% | −41.5% |
| **Close within 1 min / 15 min / 1 h** | 245 | 99.2% | 6 | **3** | +4.8% | −1.9% |
| Close within 3 h | 280 | 95.0% | 18 | 14 | +0.9% | −41.8% |
| Close within 24 h | 313 | 92.3% | 24 | 20 | +2.3% | −48.0% |
