**Tick structure drives the V2 order price cap (2026-08).** Live market payloads now carry `price_level_structure` (a string naming the tick regime — observed: `"linear_cent"` = 1c ticks, `"tapered_deci_cent"`, `"deci_cent"` = $0.001 ticks, and `"center_deci_edge_centi_cent"` = the regime all MVE/combo markets migrated to on 2026-08-17, with $0.0001 ticks below $0.01 and above $0.99 and $0.001 ticks in between) and `price_ranges` (an array of dollar-string bands `{"start", "end", "step"}` — a tapered market has multiple bands, e.g. finer ticks near 0/1 and coarser in the middle). `scanner.ApiMarket` now carries both (`price_level_structure: str = ""`, `price_ranges: list[PriceRange] | None = None`, parsed by `scanner._parse_price_ranges`, fail-soft to `None` on anything malformed), and `historical._market_to_dict` passes both through **raw** (not parsed) into the backtest cache, since that dict is JSON-serialized directly. **`scanner.tick_size_for_price(market, price_dollars)` now reads both** to derive the tick grid a V2 order price cap must land on: it returns the **FINEST** `step` among the `price_ranges` bands containing the price, as a `Decimal`, short-circuiting to `Decimal(config.DEFAULT_TICK_SIZE_DOLLARS)` (`"0.01"`) when `price_level_structure` is `""`/`"linear_cent"` or the bands are absent, and falling back to that same default with a `logging.warning` when no band contains the price or every containing band's step is nonpositive. **Finest, not first (TS-10).** A price sitting exactly on a band boundary belongs to two bands, and first-match is only "finer" at a band's LOWER edge — at an UPPER edge the earlier band is 10x COARSER, so `BUY_SLIPPAGE_TICKS` was multiplied by a 10x tick exactly there and the buy cap, which is a bid, was LOOSENED (`tick_size_for_price(m, 0.99)` returned 0.001 where the finest containing band is 0.0001). Scanning every band also means one malformed band no longer discards the valid ones after it, which the old `break` did. The fallback is always safe because the grids are nested ($0.01 ⊂ $0.001 ⊂ $0.0001) — a coarse-grid price is valid on any finer grid. The reverse is NOT true: a fine-grid cap that steps up into a coarser band (or is complemented across the book) can land off that band's grid, which is why `_v2_limit_price` re-quantizes the final price onto the grid of the band containing it. Old backtest cache records (written before this change) lack both keys entirely and read back as `None`; every reader goes through `.get()`, so this is not a crash risk. **On 2026-08-17, all MVE/combo markets migrated from $0.001 to the center_deci_edge_centi_cent regime described above.** That is what made the legacy order path's price protection untenable: its `buy_max_cost` cap was denominated in whole cents: 1c of tolerance — one tick on a whole-cent grid, but 10 ticks on a $0.001 grid and 100 on the $0.0001 edge bands. **Resolved by the V2 migration:** order submission goes to the V2 endpoint `/portfolio/events/orders` (`config.V2_ORDER_PATH`, `ORDER_API_VERSION="v2"`) — dollar-string fill-or-kill LIMIT prices, fixed-point counts, bid/ask sides on the single YES book, and an explicit `exchange_index` — where the tick-aware cap `_v2_limit_price()` (scanned price ceiled to `tick_size_for_price()` plus `BUY_SLIPPAGE_TICKS` ticks) is finally expressible. The legacy `/portfolio/orders` path (integer-cent `buy_max_cost`) is not an alternative: Kalshi deprecated those endpoints in June 2026 and no longer accepts orders on them, and its code was deleted on 2026-09-28 (see the V2-semantics bullets below); `main.py` and `v2_probe` refuse to start on any `ORDER_API_VERSION` but `"v2"`, and the side/price mapping lives in `trader._V2_LEG_SIDE`. Fractional-contract sizing remains a deferred follow-up (`_format_count` always emits `.00`).

**V2 order semantics — built from the docs, confirmed live on 2026-09-28 (see the order-path bullet below).** Every piece of the mapping is deliberately arranged so a correction is a one-line change:
- **Side mapping** (`trader._V2_LEG_SIDE`, the single point of correction): buy YES = `bid` at the capped YES price; buy NO = `ask` at `1 − cap` (selling YES you don't hold IS a long NO); the rollback closes a held NO by buying the YES short back, i.e. a **reduce-only `bid`** capped at the loss floor mirrored onto the YES book and never above the highest tradeable level of the market's own grid (`trader._v2_rollback_price()`; that top level is the finest-grid target `V2_ROLLBACK_BID_PRICE_DOLLARS` = "0.9999" floored onto the market's grid — 0.99 on linear-cent, 0.999 on deci-cent, 0.9999 on a centi-cent edge band — since a flat 0.99 would fail to cross asks resting in (0.99, 1) on sub-cent regimes, and $1.00 is not a tradeable level; see the rollback paragraph above). A held YES is sold by the mirror image, a **reduce-only `ask`** on the YES book (`close_yes`, built by `trader._build_sale_order_v2` for live selling), and a held NO by the same `close_no` bid as the unwind. `close_no` was confirmed live on 2026-09-28; a reduce-only ask that sells a held YES has never been sent live by anything else, which is why `v2_probe --step yes-close` exists: you run it, and it has to PASS before live selling is turned on. If the exchange reads a leg the other way round, flip the value in that dict — no builder logic moves.
- **Runtime backstop on the NO-leg mapping** (`trader._confirm_v2_no_mapping(client, spec, no_leg, before_no)`): the side mapping above cannot be proven offline, so the **first V2 NO-leg fill of each process** is checked against the account's own signed positions ledger before the YES leg is submitted. It is judged by the **position DELTA across the fill, never by the absolute sign** — the module's standing rule (see the delta gotcha above). `_execute_one` hands in the NO-leg baseline it already took before submitting, and the backstop compares `_fill_delta(before_no, after)` against `-no_leg.count`. Absolute sign was wrong in both directions: an account already long +100 YES on the NO leg's market that buys 10 NO nets +90, a *positive* reading that would have declared the mapping disproven and stopped the pair at `manual_review` with a real unhedged NO leg, while an account already short −100 masks a genuinely wrong mapping and latches a false confirmation for the rest of the process. A delta of **−`no_leg.count`** confirms the hypothesis (an `ask` really did open a NO position) and latches the module-level `_V2_NO_MAPPING_CONFIRMED`, so the check costs one extra positions read per **process**, not per trade — the mapping is a property of the exchange, and one confirmation proves it for every later trade in the run; the latch is deliberately not persisted, so a fresh process re-verifies. For a time-series pair the NO leg is `market_b`, so the read is of `market_b`'s ticker; the latch is **shared across pair types** because it verifies the exchange's side mapping, not a market — a same-title confirmation covers later time-series trades and vice versa. **Any other delta** means the mapping is DISPROVEN live; a delta of **zero** is re-read after each pause in `config.V2_MAPPING_ZERO_RECHECK_DELAYS_SECONDS` (1, 2 and 4 s, so up to 7 s; the first re-read that moved is judged) before that verdict, because an unmoved ledger immediately after a confirmed fill is usually read-after-write lag, not disproof — a lag of about 1 s was seen live on 2026-09-28, so the single 1 s re-read this check used before could read a healthy fill as disproof, and a wrong mapping moves the position the wrong way rather than not at all (a zero that survives every re-read — fill reported, ledger flat — is still contradictory and goes to manual review; the price is that a filled NO leg checked while the mapping is unverified — usually only the process's first — can wait up to 7 s unhedged, and only when the ledger lags). `v2_probe` still judges a zero on one re-read after `_V2_MAPPING_RECHECK_DELAY_SECONDS` (1 s), as do `_execute_one`'s DR-63/DR-64 ambiguous-leg re-reads, so a ledger lagging past 1 s can FAIL a probe step that the trader's check would accept. On disproof: the pair stops at `status="manual_review"` with **the YES leg unsubmitted** (it would hedge a position we don't hold) and **the NO leg deliberately left in place** — the unwind is a `bid` resting on the same disproven hypothesis, so an automated unwind could double the error rather than reverse it (`reduce_only` would make a wrong unwind fail safe into `rollback_failed`, but a human must decide); a `logging.critical` names the remedy: stop trading until the disproof is understood (stop the scheduler daemon, do not run `main.py --mode prod`, and, if the defaults server is running, stop it with Ctrl-C in the terminal running `./start_dashboard.sh` or `python3 -m kalshi_betting.defaults_server` and do not press Confirm and trade) and flatten the position by hand in the Kalshi UI — there is no other order path to fall back on — and says the rest of the run is stopped. When the NO leg's market was not flat before the pair (an add-on to a held pair, or any earlier holding), flattening would close those older contracts too, so the remedy reads instead, e.g., "put the position on T back to 30 NO contracts (-30) by hand in the Kalshi UI (that is what the account held there before this pair; do not close it to 0)" (`trader._restore_remedy`, which says the holding in the words the Kalshi UI uses through `trader._held_words` and writes the number exactly through `config.count_text`; the ambiguous NO leg's run-stopping CRITICAL and the already-disproven branch say the same). A disproof needs a baseline that was read, since an unknown delta is not a disproof; a baseline that was read but is not a finite number (the listing sent something like "nan") gives the flatten text, as a flat one does (`trader._flat_before`). **A disproof stops the rest of the process, and until the mapping is settled pairs run one at a time.** The disproof branch sets a second process-lifetime latch, `_V2_NO_MAPPING_DISPROVEN` (every disproof sets it, a zero delta that survives every re-read included, since the ledger then contradicts the fill report), and `_execute_one` checks it first of all — before any position read, order build or pacer place — returning `status="failed"` with the error `NO leg not sent: V2 NO-leg mapping disproven earlier in this run; nothing submitted`: nothing was sent, so there is nothing to unwind, the run still exits `EXIT_TRADES_NEED_ATTENTION` through the disproving pair's `manual_review`, and `main`'s undetermined-fill count names only that pair. An **ambiguous NO leg** can disprove the mapping too: when the NO POST raises (or answers a 2xx body `_v2_fill_status` cannot classify) the check never runs, so `_execute_legs` treats a KNOWN position move of anything but 0 or −`no_leg.count` as a disproof while the mapping is unconfirmed (a wrong mapping moves it by +count) — it sets the latch and says so in its CRITICAL, and the result's error ends `; V2 NO-leg mapping treated as disproven, rest of run stopped`. Without that, a mapping flip that also changed the response shape would send every pair down the ambiguous path, one wrong-side position each, with the latch never set. An unknown move (the read failed), or any unexplained move once the mapping is confirmed (an unrelated trade), stops only that pair, as before. A latch alone does not bound the damage, because `execute_trades` runs pairs concurrently and every pair already running sends its NO leg before the first check can finish: on the first live run (2026-09-28 02:26 PDT) all 7 NO legs were POSTed before the first mapping confirmation was logged, and without the latch an offline reproduction of 12 pairs whose NO fills all moved the position the wrong way gave 12 NO-leg POSTs, 12 `manual_review` results and 12 CRITICALs. So while neither latch is set (`trader._v2_mapping_unverified()`), `execute_trades` holds each pair back until the one before it has settled the mapping or finished, checking every `config.V2_MAPPING_VERDICT_POLL_SECONDS` (0.05 s); the next pair starts the moment the mapping is settled, even while the pair that settled it is still sending its YES leg or an unwind, and once it is confirmed the rest start together, as before. **The phase is bounded in time.** A pair can finish without a verdict — its NO leg killed, its NO POST raising without a disproving move, its worker raising, or the check unable to read the account — and the next pair then runs alone in turn; when position reads keep failing every pair finishes that way behind two retried baseline reads (~62 s each), which one at a time stretched 7 pairs from about 2 minutes to about 15 and would pass the scheduler's `SCHEDULER_JOB_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` at about 30 pairs. So once the phase has lasted `config.V2_MAPPING_CHECK_SERIAL_BUDGET_SECONDS` (300, operator decision 2026-09-28), `execute_trades` stops waiting for the pair still running (the wait is cut at the deadline), logs one WARNING (`V2 NO-leg mapping still unverified after 300s of running pairs one at a time — …`) and starts the rest together, each still checking its own NO fill. The bound is time, not a count of pairs without a verdict: a first cut capped the phase at three such pairs, and a killed NO leg — one round trip — used up a slot, so three kills in a row started the rest together and a wrong mapping then opened a wrong-side position on every pair in flight (measured by the review: 12 pairs, first three killed, 8 wrong-side positions). The clock starts with the run, so slow position reads spend it before any NO leg is checked: a first budget of 60 s ran out during the first pair's two baseline reads when each read succeeded only after ~35 s, and a wrong mapping then opened 7 wrong-side positions instead of 1 (review measurement) — hence 300 s, which covers a first pair behind such reads (~105 s to a verdict) plus two or three killed legs at that speed. When every position read fails, about three pairs run alone before the rest start (7 pairs ~10–13 min, 30 pairs ~15 min, inside the scheduler's one-hour timeout). The budget also bounds how long a stuck order POST holds the OTHER pairs back; `execute_trades` still returns only when that POST does, since order POSTs carry no request timeout (a pre-existing residual). A disproof inside the budget costs one wrong-side position, plus any earlier pair that went ahead because its check could not read the account (see below); after the budget it stops only the pairs that start after it, so up to `TRADER_MAX_WORKERS` pairs in flight can still send their NO legs. The cost is paid once per process: usually just the first pair's NO POST and check (a few round trips, up to 7 s more when the ledger lags), and never more than the budget. A dry run never reaches `_execute_one`. **Pairs that went ahead unchecked are named.** A pair whose check could not read the account sends its YES leg and reports `executed`, so if a later pair disproves the mapping that position rests on the same wrong mapping. The check's unknown outcome records the NO leg's ticker in `_V2_UNCHECKED_NO_LEGS` (and, when the position read before that pair was a finite number other than zero, that reading in `_V2_UNCHECKED_BASELINES`, rendered after the ticker as, e.g., `(held 30 NO contracts before that pair)`), and every disproof CRITICAL (the check's and the ambiguous leg's) ends by naming them: `Earlier pairs of this run went ahead after their NO-leg fill could not be checked, so they rest on the same mapping: check the positions on … too.` `_confirm_v2_no_mapping` checks the disproven latch before the confirmed one and reads nothing when it is set: a pair whose NO leg filled after another pair's disproof stops at `manual_review` before its YES leg, with no unwind. While pairs run one at a time that cannot happen, since no pair is between its start and its check when the latch is set; it can after the budget, or for a caller that runs `_execute_one` concurrently some other way. **Only a new process clears either latch, so each scheduled Monday run starts clear.** After a genuine disproof, every weekly run therefore opens one more wrong-side position (its first NO fill), and exits 20, until someone stops the scheduler or corrects `trader._V2_LEG_SIDE` — the latch bounds a run, not the weeks. Each run the defaults server's Confirm and trade starts is a new process too, so each press after a disproof would open another; that is why both disproof CRITICALs (the check's and the ambiguous NO leg's) and `v2_probe._STOP_TRADING` name every way a real-money run starts: stop the scheduler daemon, do not run `main.py --mode prod`, and stop that server and do not press the button. `tests/conftest.py` clears the disproven latch, `_V2_UNCHECKED_NO_LEGS` and `_V2_UNCHECKED_BASELINES` before every test. An **unknown delta** (either snapshot missing) is not disproof: it warns, proceeds to the YES leg, and does NOT latch, so the check re-arms on the next NO fill — one flaky positions read must never stall trading. **This read is ONE OF THE TWO SINGLE-SHOT position reads in that window** (`trader._position_count_once`, sharing `_read_position`'s parse with the retried `_position_count`; the other is `_execute_one`'s NO-leg ledger-lag re-read, DR-64, which also pays a 1s pause): it is a blocking call inside the window where the NO leg is filled and unhedged, `api_call_with_retry` can hold a call for ~62s of sleeps, and because a failing endpoint never latches, **every** V2 trade in a 429 storm would pay that stall with a naked NO leg — the same bounded-wait reasoning `_await_transfer_settlement` uses for its unretried balance reads. Don't retry-wrap it, don't make this check unconditional per-trade (it is a per-process property), don't turn the disproven branch into an automatic rollback, and don't drop the disproven latch or `execute_trades`' one-at-a-time phase — without the phase the latch stops only pairs that start after the disproof, which on a portfolio no larger than `TRADER_MAX_WORKERS` (8) is none of them — and don't remove the phase's time budget, or turn it back into a count of pairs, which killed NO legs use up: the budget is what keeps a positions outage, or a stuck order, from holding the whole portfolio one pair at a time.
- **Fill classification:** a full FoK fill reports `fill_count == count`. **A kill arrives as an error, not a 2xx:** the first live probe (2026-09-28) got HTTP 409 `{"error":{"code":"fill_or_kill_insufficient_resting_volume",…}}` for a fill-or-kill ask that could not fill, with the position unchanged. `_submit_order_v2` maps exactly that — status `config.V2_FOK_KILL_HTTP_STATUS` AND code `config.V2_FOK_KILL_ERROR_CODE`, on a `fill_or_kill` body only, recognised by `trader._is_fok_kill` (the probe reads the same helper) — to `"canceled"`, so a killed NO leg ends `failed` and a killed YES leg rolls back at once, with no position read and no pause; any other error response still raises into the ambiguous path, and an error on the IOC unwind stays `rollback_failed`. A 2xx with `fill_count == 0` is still read as a kill. Two residuals: "409 means nothing filled" rests on that one observation, an order with no crossing depth at all — no 409 has been seen for a fill-or-kill that crosses some depth but not enough; and a killed YES leg is now unwound within about one round trip of the NO fill (the ambiguous path it took before waited at least a second), while no live run has shown a `reduce_only` order accepted that soon after the position opens — the probe's close came after DR-21's re-read. If the exchange checks `reduce_only` against a lagging position, that unwind fills nothing and ends `rollback_failed` (a CRITICAL, exit 20). `_v2_fill_status()` maps only the two 2xx shapes to the `"executed"`/`"canceled"` statuses `_execute_one` and `_rollback_no_leg` branch on; a partial fill (a FoK invariant violation on a buy leg), a missing `fill_count`, or anything else logs `logging.critical` and **raises**. That is the design, not an oversight: raising routes into `_execute_one`'s existing ambiguous-exception path, which consults `_position_count` (the account's ground truth), so a mispredicted response shape degrades safely into `failed`/`rolled_back`/`manual_review` instead of being misread as a clean fill or a clean kill. Never "simplify" it into returning a guessed status. **On the unwind a partial fill is a real outcome**, not an invariant violation: the reduce-only unwind is `immediate_or_cancel` (see the rollback paragraph), so it can close part of the position. The same `ValueError` is raised for it, but it never reaches `_execute_one`'s ambiguous path — `_rollback_no_leg`'s `except` catches it and returns `rollback_failed`, sending no further order. It also reads the fill count itself, so its CRITICAL and the error text can name exactly how many NO contracts are still open (N − fill_count): `_submit_order_v2` re-raises the classifier's `ValueError` as `trader._UnclassifiableV2Response` — still a `ValueError`, with the same message, so a buy leg's caller sees what it always did — carrying the parsed body, and `_rollback_no_leg` reads it through `_partial_unwind_counts`, which unwraps it exactly as `_v2_fill_status` does and reads it with the same `_parse_fixed_point`, trusting only a finite count strictly between 0 and N whose remainder it can compute exactly (a local decimal context traps `Inexact`, so a count too long for decimal's default 28 digits is unknown rather than printed wrong); anything else, and every error response or transport error, keeps "up to N", and reading the count sits in its own `try` so it can never cost the orphan alert. The count covers this pair's NO contracts as the response reports them; the account may hold others on the market, so the CRITICAL still says to check the account. `_v2_fill_status` stays one reader for all three orders on purpose: a "partial" status returned there would read a partial NO leg as `failed` and a partial YES leg as a confirmed non-fill, which unwinds the NO leg while a naked YES position is open.
- **Response shape:** the order object may arrive wrapped under an `"order"` key or flat (the probe's NO-buy fill on 2026-09-28 came back flat), and counts as either `fill_count` or the fixed-point string `fill_count_fp` — all four combinations are accepted (`_parse_fixed_point` prefers the `_fp` variant).
- **Sandbox V2 support is unknown.** Dev mode never submits orders, so it cannot verify this; the smallest possible prod run is the real check — and `python3 -m kalshi_betting.v2_probe` is the ~1-cent way to verify the NO-leg mapping, FoK kill semantics, and the transfer centicent unit before real size flows. If anything misbehaves, stop trading and undo by hand in the Kalshi UI what the CRITICAL names — flatten a market the account did not hold before the pair, and put one it did hold back to what it held (see the order-path bullet below): V2 is the only order path there is. **The probe verifies a SIDE mapping, not a price, so two of its own prices are deliberately NOT the builders' (DR-04).** `v2_probe._no_close_body` overrides the rollback builder's price with `trader._format_price(trader._v2_top_of_grid_price(market))` — the same level `_step_unfillable_ask` submits at. Exactly TWO keys are overridden, the price and the count (the V2 fractional minimum, `PROBE_COUNT_STR`); every other key — side, `reduce_only`, time-in-force (`immediate_or_cancel`), `self_trade_prevention_type`, ticker and shard — stays the builder's. The builder's price is a loss floor derived from the leg's scanned entry, and the probe's placeholder entry of 0.5 puts that floor at **$0.62 on every market and every tick regime**, so the close was structurally killed on any market whose YES ask is above 62c: the one gate deciding whether the bot stays on V2 FAILed for a reason unrelated to the mapping, told the operator to downgrade to legacy, and left a real 0.01 NO position open. `reduce_only=True` is what bounds the top-of-grid bid — it can only close the 0.01 contracts just opened. Same commit, same reasoning one step earlier: a position of exactly 0 after a reported full fill is **re-read once** after `trader._V2_MAPPING_RECHECK_DELAY_SECONDS`, BEFORE any sign branch, so a re-read that comes back positive (disproven) or `None` (lookup failed) still takes its own branch (DR-21); a still-zero ledger keeps its FAIL, now worded "still 0 after a re-read". That re-read uses the RETRIED `trader._position_count` — acceptable at 0.01 contracts in a human-supervised tool, and never to be copied into `trader._confirm_v2_no_mapping`, whose read must stay single-shot. **A 2xx order body that is not a JSON object is handled the same way, one step earlier again (DR-58):** `_fill_counts` and `_report_fee` both `.get()` the body, so `"accepted"`, `[]`, `123`, `true` or `null` raised an uncaught `AttributeError` straight after a real ask, skipping the position read, every flatten warning, and the reduce-only close. `v2_probe._non_object_body_fail` now guards both submission-response READERS — the probe submits three orders but only the NO buy and the unfillable ask read their body, since the NO close's response is only `_emit`'d and its verdict comes from `trader._position_count` — restoring the position read (with its own re-read, on a flat OR unreadable first result) and the warning but NOT the close — see the DR-05 paragraph for why the probe is carved out of the rule that keeps the `trader.py` order reader loud, and why the close stays unsubmitted. **A body that IS a JSON object but whose `fill_count`/`remaining_count` cannot be read is the same epistemic state one step later, and in `_step_no_mapping`'s NO buy it now takes the same remedy (DR-60).** That branch used to decide from a SINGLE un-refreshed position read — DR-21's re-read is gated on `filled`, which is `None` and therefore falsy exactly there — and printed a warning only when that read was truthy, so a lagging ledger and a FAILED lookup both came out as silence while a real 0.01 NO position could be open. The re-read-and-report tail is extracted as `v2_probe._recheck_and_report_position(client, ticker, observed)`, ONE definition — since DR-20 below, called from three branches, and the helper's own docstring is the authority on which: it re-reads once after `trader._V2_MAPPING_RECHECK_DELAY_SECONDS` when the caller's first read is `None` or `0` (a read that already moved is evidence and is not re-polled), then prints lookup-failed / position-open / genuinely-flat as three distinct outcomes. The verdict and the exit code are unchanged (`_FAIL`, 1), and the `filled is True` / `filled is False` branches — including DR-21's own re-read and the kill NEUTRAL — are untouched. **Scope, recorded rather than implied: DR-58's guard covers BOTH submission-response readers, DR-60's covers only the NO buy.** `_step_unfillable_ask` has a structurally identical unreadable-fill-counts branch that returns `_FAIL` above its only post-submission position read, so a 2xx object body with no readable fill counts there still prints one FAIL line and never looks at the account. That is a real residual, not a stated trade-off — the step's own DR-58 comment already argues that "an unreadable 2xx body is not proof that it was killed", the same reasoning that would apply here — and it was left alone only because that step was explicitly out of DR-60's scope. Three further branches sit in the same state and also do not call the helper: `_step_unfillable_ask`'s `not killed` branch — the direct sibling of DR-20's new one below, which names the counts and FAILs but reports the account from a single un-refreshed read and never says FLATTEN — and both steps' post-submission EXCEPTION handlers for any error other than the kill response (below), where `_step_no_mapping`'s bare `if after_err:` collapses a failed lookup into "flat". Do not read the helper's "single definition" wording as coverage of any of these four. **A READABLE response can be impossible too, and `_step_no_mapping` used to call it a clean kill (DR-20).** `filled = remaining == 0 and fill == PROBE_COUNT` collapsed every non-full outcome into one boolean, so a PARTIAL fill (0.005 filled, 0.005 remaining against a count of 0.01) took the `not filled` branch — where DR-21's re-read does not run, because that re-read is gated on `filled` — and, with a ledger that had not caught up, printed "the fill-or-kill was killed unfilled and the account is still flat", exited NEUTRAL (code 2) and gave no flatten warning while a real 0.005 position was open on the production account; `_report_fee`'s absent-`average_fee_paid` line added a second, independent "(nothing filled)" claim just above it. The step now carries a separate `conforming` flag — `filled or (fill == 0 and remaining == PROBE_COUNT)` — and anything else (partial, over-fill, stale remainder) is the fill-or-kill INVARIANT VIOLATION this probe exists to catch: it FAILs, names the actual `fill_count`/`remaining_count`, prints the stop-trading-and-flatten remedy (`v2_probe._REMEDY`), and reports the account through the same shared `_recheck_and_report_position` tail. This is the ONE `filled is False` change DR-60 left untouched; the TRUE-kill NEUTRAL (verdict pinned by `tests/test_v2_probe.py::TestNoMappingVerdict::test_no_fill_is_neutral_and_submits_no_unwind`, wording by `::TestNonConformingFillOrKill::test_a_true_kill_with_a_flat_account_is_still_neutral`), the `filled is True` branches and DR-21's own re-read are all unchanged. `_report_fee` no longer asserts "nothing filled" — it reads no fill counts and so cannot say. The sibling `_step_unfillable_ask` already classified this way (`killed = fill == 0 and remaining == PROBE_COUNT`, FAILing a partial and printing the counts), so the two steps now apply the SAME kill test — their remedies still differ, since that step's ask is meant to be unfillable, so it folds a complete fill into the same FAIL and reports the account from one un-refreshed read. **The probe deliberately holds a STRICTER kill contract than `trader._v2_fill_status`, and the FAIL text says so rather than claim the live path would have caught the same body.** That reader consults ONLY `fill_count` (it never reads `remaining_count` anywhere in `trader.py`), so it genuinely raises on a partial or an over-fill — `fill_count` neither the requested count nor zero — but a STALE REMAINDER (`fill_count == count` with a non-zero `remaining_count`) books as `"executed"` there and a kill whose `remaining_count` is not the full count books as `"canceled"`. The probe reads both counts because an unexpected `remaining_count` is itself a finding on the one gate that decides whether the bot stays on V2, and because matching `_step_unfillable_ask`'s long-standing test is what makes the two steps agree. If a kill ever arrives as a 2xx (the first live probe's came as an HTTP 409 — below), one reporting `fill_count=0` with a zeroed rather than full `remaining_count` is a FAIL naming both counts, where it used to be a NEUTRAL with a retry hint — and it would have FAILed the kill-semantics step regardless, since `_step_unfillable_ask` has required the full remainder since it was written. **The kill response and the lagging ledger (first live probe, 2026-09-28).** The exchange killed the unfillable ask with the HTTP 409 kill response above, and the probe, which read every exception as a FAIL, printed "submission raised". The same run showed the positions ledger lagging: after the filled NO-buy ask the position read 0.0, then −0.01 on DR-21's re-read a second later; after the filled reduce-only close the single read showed −0.01 and the step FAILed ("did NOT return the position to flat") while the account was already flat, as a later read and the fills history showed. Both order steps now recognise the kill through `trader._is_fok_kill`: the unfillable ask PASSes and the NO buy is NEUTRAL on a flat account, each judged after re-reading once (`v2_probe._position_after_kill`) when the first read is not exactly 0 — a failed lookup, or a position the kill cannot explain (the account started flat, so a lagging ledger could only show 0); any other error keeps its FAIL, and the 2xx fill-0 shape is still accepted. A first read of exactly 0 is judged at once, so an order that did fill behind a kill response could still pass while the ledger lags — a recorded residual. The close's verdict is judged on one re-read after `trader._V2_MAPPING_RECHECK_DELAY_SECONDS` whenever the read straight after it is not exactly 0; how long the ledger lags after a close was not measured, so one re-read can still FAIL a close that worked. The stand-in exchange in `tests/test_v2_probe.py` (`FakeExchange`) answers a fill-or-kill that does not cross with that 409. **And `--dest-shard` may not name the source shard (DR-22).** It is declared `type=int` with no `choices`, and `_step_transfer`'s two guards — is the shard advertised, are its transfers active — are both satisfied by shard 0 BY CONSTRUCTION, since it IS `_TRANSFER_SOURCE_SHARD`; so `--dest-shard 0` POSTed a real, non-idempotent, never-retried transfer with `source_exchange_shard == destination_exchange_shard`. **Derived from source, not observed** (the probe was never run this way): the POST is what the bug is, and IF the exchange accepts it, a net-zero move cannot raise that shard's balance by the probe cent `_await_transfer_settlement`'s target demands, so the poll burns the full `TRANSFER_SETTLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` and reports a FALSE `MONEY MAY BE IN FLIGHT`; if the exchange 400s it instead, the step FAILs on the POST with a different message. The refusal is a NEUTRAL at the TOP of `_step_transfer`, before any transfer I/O (`main()` still builds the client and reads the balance before dispatching to any step) — not in argparse — because `_step_transfer` is what spends the money and is reachable from `main()`'s step dispatch and from any future caller, not only from the CLI. Negative and unknown destinations need no separate check: they are not advertised, so the existing advertised-shard guard already short-circuits them to NEUTRAL.
- **Required fields, and the optional ones left out.** The endpoint requires `ticker`, `side`, `count`, `price`, `time_in_force` and `self_trade_prevention_type`, and every V2 body sends all six. `self_trade_prevention_type` comes from `config.V2_SELF_TRADE_PREVENTION_TYPE`, `"taker_at_cross"`: an incoming order that would trade against another order on this account is cancelled at that point, where `"maker"` would cancel the account's resting order instead. The bot never leaves an order resting, so the only order it could meet is one placed outside the bot (by hand, or by another client on this account), and `taker_at_cross` leaves that order alone. What the endpoint reports for the bot's cancelled order has not been observed; the trader handles each shape through its existing paths — nothing filled is an ordinary non-fill, a part fill or an error response on a buy leg goes to the position-delta check (a part fill the account shows ends as `manual_review`), and either one on the unwind is `rollback_failed`. `v2_probe` checks only that the probe ticker's POSITION is flat, so cancel any resting orders of your own on that ticker before running it. `subaccount`, `cancel_order_on_pause`, `expiration_time` and `order_group_id` are still not sent and rely on server defaults. The first live production submission, on 2026-09-28, was rejected with HTTP 400 for the missing `self_trade_prevention_type` on every NO leg it sent, before any side mapping was exercised and with nothing filled; the docs' rule that `reduce_only` requires `immediate_or_cancel` was found in the same review of the docs. Pinned by `tests/test_trader.py::TestV2OrderBuilders::test_every_v2_body_carries_the_documented_required_fields`, `::test_reduce_only_only_with_immediate_or_cancel` and `::test_self_trade_prevention_type_comes_from_config`, and `tests/test_v2_probe.py`'s stand-in submission seams (`FakeExchange.submit` and the `submits` fixture) answer a body that breaks either rule with the endpoint's HTTP 400.
- Other fixed choices: the two buy legs are `fill_or_kill` (the whole count at or under the price cap, or nothing) and the unwind and a sale are `immediate_or_cancel` (the only time in force the endpoint accepts with `reduce_only` — see the rollback paragraph); prices are 4-decimal dollar strings (`_format_price`, exactly representing every grid point of every regime); counts are fixed-point strings (`_format_count`); each order body's `exchange_index` is that leg's OWN market's `exchange_index` (the NO leg and its rollback from the NO leg's market — `market_a` for same-title, `market_b` for time-series — and the YES leg from the YES leg's market; a pair's legs can sit on different shards) and never `-1` auto-route, so a wrong shard model is rejected loudly by the exchange instead of silently routed, and an explicit-shard write bills only that shard's rate-limit bucket where auto-route bills every nonzero shard's. `post_only` is always `False` (we are deliberate takers) and `reduce_only` is `True` only on the rollback and on a sale.
- **V2 is the only order path; any other `ORDER_API_VERSION` is refused at startup (2026-09-28).** Kalshi's changelog (docs.kalshi.com/changelog), June 18, 2026, "Legacy order mutation endpoints deprecated": the legacy `/portfolio/orders` mutation endpoints (POST `/trade-api/v2/portfolio/orders`, DELETE `.../orders/{id}`, POST `.../orders/{id}/amend` and `.../orders/{id}/decrease`, POST and DELETE `.../orders/batched`) were to be deprecated between June 18 and June 25, after which a call returns `Please switch to the V2 endpoints` with a link to the V2 order API reference. So there is no second order path to fall back on. (Its June 4, 2026 entry, "Legacy order mutation rate-limit costs increase to 10x V2", priced those endpoints at 10x their V2 equivalents; `GET /account/endpoint_costs` on 2026-09-28 listed no override for POST `/trade-api/v2/portfolio/orders`, so the listing and the changelog disagree, and the "5x" this file and `config.py` once quoted matches neither.) The V2 NO-leg side mapping was confirmed live on 2026-09-28 (a `v2_probe` PASS and the first live trades, PR #78). `config.ORDER_API_VERSION` stays `"v2"` as a checked setting: `config.order_api_version_error()` returns `None` only for exactly the str `"v2"` (read at call time), and `main.main()` and `v2_probe.main()` call it right after parsing their arguments — before `_resolve_live_settings`, logging, the probe's banner, a client or any request — and hand its one-line message to `parser.error`, so any other value (`"legacy"`, `"V2"`, `""`, `None`, …) exits `2` in either mode with the reason on stderr. The message names the value and points only back at `"v2"`. **The remedy for a misbehaving V2 path is to stop trading and flatten by hand in the Kalshi UI** (stop the scheduler daemon if it is running, do not run `main.py --mode prod`, and, if the defaults server is running, stop it with Ctrl-C in the terminal running `./start_dashboard.sh` or `python3 -m kalshi_betting.defaults_server` and do not press Confirm and trade) — except on a market the account held before the pair (an add-on to a held pair, or any earlier holding), which goes back to what it held rather than to 0: `trader._confirm_v2_no_mapping`'s CRITICAL says so, naming that holding when there is one (`trader._restore_remedy`; and the ambiguous NO leg's run-stopping CRITICAL in `trader._execute_legs` names the same remedy and the same ways to stop); the `v2_probe` FAIL lines of `--step no-mapping` and `--step yes-close` that doubt the path after a submission on an account the probe had verified flat — a disproven NO-leg mapping or a buy that opened the wrong side, a non-conforming fill-or-kill response, a close or sale that did not return the position to flat (`v2_probe._REMEDY`, the stop-trading sentence `_STOP_TRADING` followed by the flatten sentence `_FLATTEN`) — say to flatten, since the probe starts only from a flat ticker, so any position there is its own. The probe's closing line after its RESULT banner depends on the outcome and the step, and never by itself says to flatten, because a FAIL before anything was submitted (a market that could not be read, a starting position that was not flat — possibly the bot's own live position) opened nothing: after an order step's FAIL it prints `_STOP_TRADING` and says to act only on the position warnings printed above; after a `--step transfer` FAIL, `_STOP_TRADING` and to check each shard's balance in the Kalshi UI before the bot moves collateral again; after a NEUTRAL, that the result is inconclusive and gives no reason to halt the bot or close a position. Pinned by `tests/test_config.py::TestOrderApiVersionError` (including a scan of every `.py` file under `kalshi_betting/`, comments and docstrings included, that fails wherever the name `ORDER_API_VERSION` is followed within 60 characters by a quoted `legacy`, after joining string literals split across a line break and reading an escaped quote as a quote — checked against the ten shapes the package has used, `_REMEDY_SHAPES`; wording that sets the switch without naming it within that window is outside the scan), `tests/test_main.py::TestOrderApiVersionGate`, `tests/test_v2_probe.py::TestMainDispatch` (`test_a_non_v2_order_path_is_refused_before_anything_runs`, and one test per closing line: an order step's FAIL, a transfer FAIL, a NEUTRAL on every step, and a real FAIL before any submission that must not say to flatten; and `test_stop_trading_names_every_way_a_real_money_run_starts`, which pins how `_STOP_TRADING`, `_REMEDY` and both FAIL closing lines are built) and the remedy assertions in `tests/test_v2_probe.py` and `tests/test_trader.py` (`assert_names_the_remedy` and `assert_names_stop_trading`; `assert_disproof_names_the_remedy` and `assert_ambiguous_stop_names_the_remedy`).
- **The legacy order code was deleted on 2026-09-28, by operator decision.** Removed: `trader`'s legacy builders and submitter (`_build_no_order`, `_build_yes_order`, `_submit_order`, `_buy_max_cost_cents`), the version dispatchers (`_build_no_order_any`, `_build_yes_order_any`, `_build_rollback_order_any`, `_submit_any`), the legacy shard guard (`_legacy_routable`, `drop_legacy_unroutable` and `main._run_prod`'s call to it), the `ORDER_API_VERSION` gates in `strategy` and `scanner.validate_pair_price`, `config.BUY_MAX_COST_SLIPPAGE_CENTS`, and the tests of the legacy wire format; the legacy tests of `_execute_one`'s decisions (statuses, which orders are sent, delta attribution) were ported to the V2 path, or dropped where an existing V2 test already asserts the same scenario. `config.ORDER_API_VERSION` stays as a checked switch whose only accepted value is `"v2"`. `tests/test_trader.py::TestV2IsTheOnlyOrderPath` pins three things by syntax tree: no module imports `CreateOrderRequest` or spells any name containing `create_order` (underscores and case ignored); `config.py` is the only module in `kalshi_betting/` referencing `ORDER_API_VERSION`; and no string constant other than a docstring starts with the retired endpoint's path (`/portfolio/orders` or `/trade-api/v2/portfolio/orders`).

---

## Documentation Standards

**Every file must have a header comment block** at the top (after the shebang/encoding if any) following the pattern already established in every module:
```python
"""
File: filename.py
Author: <name>
Last edited by: <name>

Purpose:
    One paragraph describing what this module does and why it exists.

Dependencies:
    What it imports from the project and what imports from it.

Notes:
    Any non-obvious constraints, SDK quirks, or gotchas specific to this file.
"""
```

**Every function must have a Google-style docstring** matching the existing style:
```python
def my_function(arg: type) -> ReturnType:
    """
    One-sentence summary of what the function does.

    Longer explanation if the logic is non-trivial. Include the invariants
    this function assumes and what it guarantees.

    Args:
        arg (type): Description. Include valid range if numeric.

    Returns:
        type: Description of what's returned and when.

    Raises:
        ExceptionType: When and why this is raised.
    """
```
Functions that return `None` only on failure should document that: `Returns None if <condition>.`

**Every cross-module function call must have an inline comment** explaining the *why*, not just the *what*. The existing codebase already follows this for critical calls (see `main.py` and `strategy.py`):
```python
# Validate depth & replace best-ask with depth-weighted fill price
pairs = scanner.enrich_with_orderbook_prices(client, pairs)
```
Single-line calls to stdlib or well-known helpers (e.g. `logging.info(...)`, `sorted(...)`) don't need comments unless the arguments are surprising.

**Update the README and all relevant comments whenever a change affects behavior.** Specifically:
- If you add, remove, or rename a module: update the Module Dependency Graph in `README.md` and the Module Map in `CLAUDE.md`.
- If you change a constant in `config.py`: update the comment explaining the constant's purpose and rationale.
- If you add a new CLI argument to `main.py` or `backtest.py`: update the Run Commands section in `README.md` and in `CLAUDE.md`.
- If you change the data flow of the live or backtest pipeline: update the corresponding ASCII diagram in `README.md`.
- If you change function signatures or behavior: update the docstring in the same commit, not as a follow-up.

---

## What NOT To Do

- **Do not bypass the rollback logic in `trader._execute_one()`**. If the YES leg is *confirmed* unfilled after the NO leg fills — a clean FoK rejection, or an error whose position delta is 0 on the first read AND on the lag re-read (DR-63) — an unwind must be attempted immediately: an orphaned NO-leg position (`market_a` for same-title, `market_b` for time-series) is an unhedged one-sided position. But an unwind requires *confirmation*, not a guess. When a leg errors and the delta is unattributable (the lookup failed, or the position moved by an amount the order can't explain), the correct behavior is `status="manual_review"` with no order submitted — on the YES leg because an unwind could reverse a real fill, and on the NO leg because a reduce-only unwind of a position this order may not own could close an unrelated holding. Do not "restore" the NO leg's old unconditional unwind-on-unknown. The unwind's count is always `no_leg.count`, never a position read, and on a market the account held before the pair (an add-on) that confirmation is what keeps it off the older contracts; nor drop `trader._add_on_mismatch`, which sends an add-on only while both markets still hold exactly the pair it adds to.
- **Do not add retry logic to order submission** — this covers `trader._submit_order_v2` and `trader._submit_sale_v2` (both via `_http.signed_request_json`) and `trader._execute_transfer` (the non-idempotent collateral POST) equally; a resent sale could sell twice. One retry after a fill-or-kill rejection could submit the NO leg twice at different prices, creating an unhedged position, and a retried transfer moves the money twice. **The read-only position lookups in `trader._position_count` ARE retried and must stay that way** — a GET cannot duplicate a trade, but an unretried transient 429 there turns a resolvable ambiguity into a rollback or `manual_review`. Assert the rule per-function on the submission/transfer sources and at `_submit_order_v2`'s two call sites (`_execute_one`, `_rollback_no_leg`), never as a module-wide "trader.py must not import `api_call_with_retry`" ban. The write pacer (`trader._ORDER_WRITE_PACER`) is not a retry and must stay exactly one place before the one POST — `acquire`, `acquire_hedge`, or a held write's `send` through `_PairWrites` — never a loop around it, and never a reason to re-send after a 429. Nor may a held write stop counting against the bucket before it is sent or released (the refill cap of `burst` minus the held writes): that cap is the only thing keeping a YES leg sent late inside the write limit.
- **Do not unwind the NO leg with an unpriced order.** The unwind is a floored (bid-capped) immediate-or-cancel limit bid (IOC is mandatory with `reduce_only`) that expresses the `ROLLBACK_MAX_LOSS_CENTS_PER_CONTRACT` bound. A market-type (or top-of-grid-flat) unwind has no loss floor at all. (This rule is about the TRADE unwind in `trader.py` and is absolute there. `v2_probe._no_close_body`'s closing bid is deliberately top-of-grid-flat and is not a trade unwind — it is a 0.01-contract `reduce_only` probe order whose whole point is to fill; see the V2-semantics bullet above before "restoring" the builder's price to it.)
- **Do not hardcode `spec.pair.market_a` as the NO leg, or `market_a`'s side as NO, anywhere.** `config.SAME_TITLE_LEG_SIDES`/`TIME_SERIES_LEG_SIDES` via `scanner.leg_sides()`, `scanner.leg_prices()` and `trader._ordered_legs()` are the only source of truth for which side a leg buys, which price it pays, and which leg is submitted first and unwound; for a time-series pair the NO leg is `market_b`.
- **Do not describe, price or pay a fourth time-series settlement cell.** A=YES/B=NO is impossible for a cumulative-deadline pair; the backtester excludes and counts it as a premise violation. No table, docstring, log string or README/CLAUDE.md sentence may assign that cell a payout of any kind; the only double-payout cell anywhere is the SAME-TITLE table's NY cell in `_settlement_receipt`.
- **Do not apply the one-series rule to only one finder, and do not replace it with a close-time gate.** `scanner._same_series` must gate BOTH `find_same_title_pairs` and (conjoined with `_identical_wording`) `find_time_series_pairs`, and `backtester._extract_pairs` must mirror it on both of its branches — the same two tickers qualify for both pair types, so a one-sided gate relabels the trade instead of stopping it (pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_finders_apply_the_one_series_rule`). A close-gap threshold is not a SUBSTITUTE for it: on the corpus that rejection was measured on, identical-wording non-combo pairs (same-series and cross-series pooled) co-resolved 96.3–98.2% of the time in every close-gap band, while combo pairs closing 96 seconds apart still disagreed 13.8% of the time — so for the ONE-SERIES case, fixture identity separates them and proximity does not. That verdict is about the same-series case only: on DR-74's 365-day corpus, CROSS-series identical-wording pairs settled the same way 99.2% of the time at a 0 s close gap against 60.7% beyond 1 minute, so there proximity DOES separate them (see the DR-02 gotcha's annotation and the close-gate gotcha). DR-74's close gate is not a replacement either — it is an ADDITIONAL condition on the cross-series same-title pairs the series rule already admits (see the next bullet), and the one-series rule stays in front of it on both paths.
- **Do not apply the same-title close gate on only one PATH, and do not add it to `find_time_series_pairs` (DR-74).** `scanner.closes_apart` must gate BOTH `scanner.find_same_title_pairs` (through `_closes_apart`) and `backtester._extract_pairs`' same-title branch (through `_comparable_closes_dicts`) — one definition, pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_paths_apply_the_same_title_close_gap` and `::test_ast_the_close_gap_has_one_definition` — or the backtest replays the men's/women's trades the live path refuses, exactly as it reproduced DR-01 before the group key was shared. It belongs on the same-title finder ONLY: identical wording can never form a time-series pair (DR-67), so a same-title-only gate cannot relabel the trade, and a close gate on the time-series finder would refuse every genuine cross-event deadline pair, whose closes differ by design (the same AST pin asserts its absence there). Keep it failing CLOSED on a close it cannot compare, keep it AFTER the series test so the older counts do not move, and keep the backtest's unreadable-close count on its own line so the close-gap line stays the live one's verbatim twin.
- **Do not apply the cumulative-deadline rule to only one path, and do not let a snapshot pair through.** `scanner.cumulative_deadline_pair` (over `scanner.deadline_phrasing`) must gate BOTH `scanner.find_time_series_pairs` and `backtester._extract_pairs`' time-series branch — one shared helper, only the field extraction differing (pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_finders_apply_the_cumulative_deadline_rule` and `::test_ast_the_deadline_classifier_has_one_definition`). Gating only the live path would leave the backtester replaying the defect instead of detecting it, exactly as it reproduced DR-01 before the grouping key was shared. The rule belongs on the time-series branch ONLY — a same-title pair has no deadline concept — and it must keep failing CLOSED: wording that names no deadline is refused, because the YES-on-earlier/NO-on-later trade has no premise without the nesting a cumulative deadline provides. Do not move it into `_can_ever_enter` for speed: that is a MARKET-eligibility predicate named by `config.SETTLED_PREFILTER_CACHE_TAG`, and putting it there silently corrupts every assembled cache. Do not merge the phrasing tables into `_DATE_PATTERNS` — one erases dates, the other reads the preposition around them.
- **Do not order or tier a same-event ladder on `close_time`, and do not apply the ladder rule to only one path (DR-73).** A ladder's legs are ordered by `scanner.stated_deadline` and gapped by `scanner.same_event_ladder`, and that stated gap is what chooses the tier, is checked against `MAX_DEADLINE_GAP_DAYS`, travels on `CandidatePair.stated_gap_days` and is read downstream only through `scanner.pair_gap_days` — because a settled or single-instant event closes every rung together (681 of 1,821 archive same-event pairs have a close gap of ZERO days and 13 are ordered the wrong way round by realized close), so `close_time` silently re-tiers such a pair from the 0.70 ceiling to 0.85 or inverts its legs. The rule must gate BOTH `scanner.find_time_series_pairs` and `backtester._extract_pairs`/`_find_entry`, on the SAME `TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS` resolved at call time — each module's own by-value binding of the config constant, so a test or harness patches `scanner.` / `backtester.` / `backtest.` and never `config.`, which none of them reads — never on `event_ticker` equality alone, which would hand ladder semantics to any caller regardless of the switch — and it is pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_finders_apply_the_same_event_ladder_rule` and `::test_ast_pair_ceiling_reads_the_pair_gap`. A one-sided rule would let a ladder-enabled live run be measured by a backtest that refuses every same-event pair, the same relabelling trap the one-series rule records. It must keep failing CLOSED at every step: an empty shared event ticker, identical wording, a non-cumulative verdict, a deadline stating no placeable calendar day, fields naming irreconcilable days, both rungs naming ONE day, or a stated gap over the cap all refuse the pair. Branch on `is None` / `is SAME_DAY`, never truthiness — `SAME_DAY` is a non-empty string.
- **Do not let the one-open-trade-per-ladder rule fail open (DR-76).** A held market `scanner.resolve_held_ladders` cannot identify must keep meaning NO time-series pair that run — never "treat it as on no ladder", and never a question key built without its event title, which matches none of its siblings. Both `find_time_series_pairs` and `select_portfolio` must be handed the run's held ladders (both default to an empty set, so a dropped keyword refuses nothing silently — pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_the_live_run_refuses_pairs_on_held_ladders`), the resolver must read the market list BEFORE held markets are removed from it, and the labels must come from `scanner.ladder_keys`, the one definition. Keep the finder's refusal FIRST and before the one-best-pair contest, so a runner-up off every held ladder can still trade. Nor apply the rule on only one path: the backtest mirror labels markets through the same definition (`backtester._ladder_keys_dict` → `scanner.ladder_keys`, pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_the_backtest_reads_ladders_through_the_one_definition`), and `SweepPoint.peak_kelly_fraction` must keep covering every Monday a pair may be traded on (every passing Monday of a time-series pair, a same-title pair's first and, with `add_to_held`, a same-title pair's later same-legs Mondays) — a peak read off the traded, first or last Monday only makes `CapSweep`'s shared copies silently wrong.
- **Do not let adding to held pairs fail open (DR-77).** A `HeldPair` comes only from `scanner.held_pairs`, which returns one only when its markets (two for an exact pair, one for a lone leg) are alone on their ladder and every held market's ladder is known — that isolation is the whole safety of the finders' and `select_portfolio`'s exemptions — so never build one anywhere else, and never hand the finders `add_on_pairs` from a positions listing that stopped early, from a run that could not identify every held market, or, when anything is held, from a run whose portfolio value counts only the cash because Kalshi's value of the open positions was unread or refused (`main._run_prod` hands `{}` and WARNs). Keep `pair_held` reading by type, the sides checked with `HeldPair.matches` after a ladder's legs are ordered, and the trader's check just before the NO leg (`trader._add_on_mismatch`). Keep the fees paid in a held pair's stake (`HeldPair.stake_dollars` live, what each of `held["trades"]` spent in the backtest): the portfolio value leaves them out, and a stake without them tops a held pair up by about a contract pair on every run it passes, each top-up paying a fee of its own. Keep the setting read only through `LiveSettings.add_to_held_pairs` (the AST pin forbids `ADD_TO_HELD_PAIRS` on the live path), and keep a saved file that leaves the toggle out reading it as off (`config._OPTIONAL_TOGGLES`): nobody confirmed a value for that file.
- **Do not size on a positions value the contracts held cannot back.** `main._checked_positions_value` is the one check: Kalshi's value of the open positions, when above 0, counts only when it is at most `config.CONTRACT_PAYOUT_DOLLARS` × the contracts held, over a positions listing read to its end whose every count can be read; a value of 0 is always kept, since it adds nothing. A refused value sizes the run on the cash alone, applies the $50 minimum to the cash again and adds to no held pair. Keep its WARNING in that check, which `_run_prod` calls once it has read the positions, never in `_bankroll_cents` (a test pins that function's WARNINGs exactly), and keep the low-cash WARNING after the check, so it describes the value the run sizes on.
- **Do not let live selling fail open, read a sale's reply as a buy leg's, or move its rule out of the shared functions.** A held market is sold only from a plan whose ladder is known, whose partner (if any) is the one paid-out market that fits, and whose count the account still holds (`trader._holding_problem`, read just before the first order of the position): an unknown ladder, an incomplete positions listing, a missing book or candle and an ambiguous partner each mean no sale and a log line. A sale's count is the plan's, never a position read; its reply is read by `_sale_fill_count`, never `_v2_fill_status` (a part fill is normal, and `_v2_fill_status` raises on one); it goes through `_submit_sale_v2` once, after one pacer place, and is never retried; the reads after a POST that raised stay outside the `except`. The YES ask rounds up and the NO's YES bid rounds down (`_sale_limit`): swapping either sells below the walk. The rule's arithmetic stays in `config.take_profit_reached`/`reached_every_check`/`days_to_maturity`, `scanner.walk_bids` and `historical.usable_candle_ask`/`candle_sale_bids`/`bid_before`; `seller.py` imports `config`, `scanner` and `historical` only and keeps no copy of the fee, the float tolerance or the candle bid rule (`tests/test_seller.py::TestIsolation`); `historical.recent_candles` never reads or writes the candle cache, so a live sale's candle reads touch nothing under `backtest_cache/`. Sales stay before every purchase, and the markets sold stay blocked from both finders and from add-ons (`test_ast_the_live_run_sells_before_it_buys`).
- **Do not import `trader.py` from `scanner.py` or `strategy.py`** — circular dependency.
- **Do not write output files outside `PROJECT_ROOT`**. All logs, Excel files, and HTML dashboards write relative to `PROJECT_ROOT`. The one exception is the live-run lock, `config.LIVE_RUN_LOCK_FILE` (`~/.kalshi_betting/live_run.lock`, whose folder and holder record `run_lock.acquire()` writes): it is machine-wide state rather than an output file, and it sits in the home folder so every checkout and worktree trading the account shares one lock. Never move it under `PROJECT_ROOT`, or each checkout gets a lock of its own and two real-money runs can trade at once. The run result `main.py --result-file PATH` writes is not written by the bot on its own: it goes to the path its caller names (and to a hidden staging file beside it), and any file already at that path is removed when the run starts.
- **Do not modify `_DATE_PATTERNS` without running `test_normalize_title`** — a regression silently causes missed pairs or false positives with no error.
- **Do not import `defaults_server` from any pipeline module, and do not give it a route that serves the dashboard.** It is a human-run tool whose pages write the live defaults every live run trades and start live runs with real orders (pinned by `tests/test_defaults_server.py::TestIsolation`), and any script on a page served from its own address could press its buttons — the dashboard carries scripts. Keep every check in front of a save or a run (Host, Origin, the token over the page's purpose, the fingerprint, the nonce and the raw fields, the used-nonce check right after it, the stale-page re-render, the run's blockers and the attention acknowledgement before any save, the CSP-hashed arming script), and never send `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, under which a browser posts `Origin: null` and every honest save is refused.
- **Do not let the defaults server import `main`, `trader` or any other pipeline module, start a process anywhere but `_App._start_run`, or stop a run.** Its one process start is `self._start_process(...)` in `_App._start_run`, in a new session (`start_new_session=True`), so Ctrl-C on the server never reaches a run placing orders; `start_process` defaults to `None`, which refuses every run, and only `main()` passes `subprocess.Popen`, so no `_App` a test (or `_Handler._app()`'s fallback) builds can start `main.py`. A dry run never saves. Confirm and trade saves only after every blocker and the attention acknowledgement have passed, and starts nothing when the save fails. Never kill a run the server started, from the server or its pages: killing a run mid-trade can leave half of a pair open (pinned by `tests/test_defaults_server.py::TestIsolation`, `::TestActions` and `::TestBlockers`). A start that finds its port taken must bind nothing and start nothing: it may only open a page, and only when the listener's `/checkout` names this checkout's resolved root and its current code fingerprint; anything else exits 2 (pinned by `::TestMain` and `::TestBusyPortOverASocket`, whose guard fails any bind that succeeds).
- **Do not write `live_defaults.json` except through `config.save_live_defaults`, and do not add a config.py fallback to `main._resolve_live_settings`.** The writer parses the text exactly as a live run will before replacing the file atomically, then reads it back; `defaults_server._App._post_confirm` is its one caller (the AST pin's `_DEFAULTS_WRITERS`). With no saved file a live run must exit 2 — falling back to config.py's toggle constants would trade values nobody confirmed.
- **Do not let a run that sends no orders take the live-run lock, and do not release it before the run ends.** Only `--mode prod` without `--dry-run` takes it in `main()`, before `build_client`, and the descriptor is closed in the `finally` after the run mode returns or raises; a dry run or a dev run that took it would block the Monday run, and one released early would let a second real-money run trade the same balance. Keep `run_lock.held()` a momentary shared lock and `acquire()`'s retry longer than one such check, or a check could stop a starting run. Keep an error making or opening the lock file an error (exit 1), never `EXIT_RUN_IN_PROGRESS`, which the scheduler counts as a done slot.
- **Do not start the dashboard's Sell workers from a module without a `__main__` guard, and do not move them to the fork start method.** `generate_dashboard(..., sell_workers=N)` with N > 1 spawns its worker processes, and a spawned worker re-imports the caller's main module, so a script that builds a page at import time builds it again in every worker (`backtest.py` runs `main()` only under its guard); fork is unsafe on macOS. Nor publish a page apart from its sidecar folder: `_publish_page` renames the page and deletes the older build folders together, under the lock, so the page in place always has the chunks it names.
- **Do not import `v2_probe` from any pipeline module** — it is a human-run tool that submits real-money orders; a single import would put probe submissions one code path away from the weekly scheduler (a test asserts this).
- **Do not add tests that call the real Kalshi API**. Use `unittest.mock.MagicMock` for `KalshiClient`. Tests should be runnable offline.
- **Do not change the fee formula without updating both** `fee_per_pair_approx()` and `fee_leg_exact()` in `config.py`. The backtester also uses both.
- **Do not take the fee out of Kelly's denominator, and do not merge Kelly's `b` with the reported `profit_ratio` (DR-62).** `b` divides by `price_a + price_b + fee_per_pair_approx(...)` — the dollars a losing pair actually forfeits — in all three sizers (`strategy._evaluate_size`'s `kelly_b`, `backtester._simulate_at_discount`'s `kelly_b_entry`, `dashboard._kelly_fraction`'s `b`); dropping the fee there overstates modelled EV by exactly `q·fee`, so `f* > 0` stops meaning positive expected value even under the continuous fee approximation the gate prices with. (With the fee in place it means positive EV under that approximation and nothing more — at single-digit `n` the ceiling-rounded exact fee can still leave an accepted spec marginally EV-negative on its own fields; never restate the guarantee without that qualifier.) The fee-less ratio beside it is the REPORTED return on contract cost and feeds `select_portfolio`'s ranking key — two names, two quantities, on purpose.
- **Do not read a live toggle anywhere but through `config.LiveSettings`, and do not give an internal helper a default `settings`.** The live toggles reach the live path as ONE frozen `LiveSettings` per run — the tier floors and the spread band at the finder, enrichment and `validate_pair_price`, the categories and tags at `main._filter_by_category`, adding to held pairs at `main._run_prod` (the held-pairs lookup and the at-cap drop), the sell level and its minimum of days at `main._run_prod`'s sale block (`seller.plan_sales`, which takes `settings` with no default), `k` and the two per-trade caps at the sizer (`strategy.compute_trade`, capping through `config.pair_size_cap`) and at enrichment's affordability bound (`config.max_kelly_fraction`), one object, so the bound always bounds the size; only a whitelisted entry point handed none may resolve `config.live_settings()`, once (the no-settings fallback — tests and direct calls only), no function calls it directly (`_DIRECT_RESOLVERS` is empty), `main._resolve_live_settings` reads the saved live defaults through `config.live_defaults()` — that reader's one live caller (`_DEFAULTS_READERS`), once per run, laying `main.py`'s toggle flags over them — and a helper that reads a toggle (`scanner._pair_max_sum`, `scanner._enrich_pair`, `strategy._kelly_p`, `_evaluate_size`, `_solve_marginal_size`, `main._compute_trade_specs`, `main._filter_by_category`) REQUIRES it — a default there, or a second resolution, lets a run's override apply at one site while another reads `config.py` or the saved file. Never leave `k` or a Kelly fraction to a config default on the live path either: `time_series_profit_prob(..., k=None)` reads `TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT` and `max_affordable_pairs(..., fraction=None)` reads `BUDGET_FRACTION`, so every live call passes the run's value (`strategy._kelly_p_at`'s `k` is required). Never read `TIME_SERIES_TIER_FLOORS`/`TIME_SERIES_SPREAD_BAND`/`TIME_SERIES_INTERVAL_PROB_DISCOUNT`/`BUDGET_FRACTION`/`SAME_TITLE_SIZE_CAP`/`TRADE_CATEGORIES`/`TRADE_TAGS`/`ADD_TO_HELD_PAIRS`/`SELL_AT`/`SELL_MIN_DAYS`, call `min_price_diff_for_gap` or a band helper, or re-derive the spread rule or a pair's cap in a live module: `config.time_series_spread_refusal` and `config.pair_size_cap` are their one definitions (pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_live_path_reads_toggles_only_through_live_settings` and `::test_ast_every_sizer_caps_through_pair_size_cap`, and the spread rule against `_find_entry` by value in `tests/test_backtester.py::TestLiveBacktestSpreadParity`). **A per-run override is a `main.py` flag, never a patched constant or a second resolution:** `--tier-floors`/`--no-tier-floors`, `--spread-min`/`--spread-max`, `--interval-discount`, `--size-cap`/`--same-title-size-cap` (percent, on the `SIZE_CAP_STEP` grid), `--add-to-held-pairs`/`--no-add-to-held-pairs`, `--sell-at`/`--no-sell`, `--sell-min-days`/`--no-sell-min-days` and `--category`/`--any-category`, `--tag`/`--any-tag` (each pair mutually exclusive) are laid over the saved live defaults (`config.live_defaults()`) by `main._resolve_live_settings` and validated by `LiveSettings` itself BEFORE logging is configured (TS-20: no saved defaults, a refused file or a bad flag exits 2 with nothing logged or requested); `main()` hands the result and the saved defaults as the reference to both run modes, and `main._log_live_settings` — which takes both and never resolves either — logs a `Live defaults: <origin>` line, one `Live settings:` line marking each departing field `(default: X)`, a WARNING when a production run that submits orders departs, and every `config.live_rule_warnings` sentence; `_run_prod` hands the trade log's separator row the same marked line ending `| defaults: <origin>`, so the workbook itself tells a flag's rows from the saved defaults' and one set of saved defaults from the next (the values alone cannot). The run's settings travel under ONE name: every live call hands a settings-taking function the bare name `settings`, so the `reference` beside it can never be handed in its place (the AST pin above). The scheduler passes no toggle flag (`tests/test_scheduler.py` pins its argv), so a scheduled run trades exactly the saved live defaults and logs no mark. `tests/test_main.py::TestLiveSettingsReachEverySite` is the runtime half of this rule: a real production dry run and a real dev run, each handed settings that differ in every toggle from the values its `pinned_config_toggles` fixture pins `config.py`'s toggles to, every module's `live_settings` (and `live_defaults` / `read_saved_live_defaults`) raising, spies on the consuming bindings, run once against `config.py`'s toggles as the reference and once with its settings saved as the live defaults and resolved as `main()` resolves them (and, in prod, `pre_execution_check`'s swallowed-exception WARNING asserted absent) — when `LiveSettings` gains a toggle (a compared field, so it joins `config.LIVE_TOGGLE_FIELDS`), pin its constant in `tests/conftest.py`'s `apply_pre_toggle_defaults` (which that fixture delegates to, so the pre-flip values have one definition) and give the class's `_SETTINGS` a value that differs from it (the test asserts every toggle differs — `origin` is not a toggle; a `config.py` edit cannot break it, since the fixture, not `config.py`, is what it differs from).
- **Do not price the time-series forecast at the fills, at the prices a trade paid, or at the YES-ask gap (DR-78).** The forecast reads the mid spread: the live sizer through `scanner.pair_mid_spread` (the `CandidatePair.mid_spread` enrichment writes from the books' tops), the backtest's gate through `config.time_series_mid_spread` of each Monday's quotes, and the dashboard's scatter through it of each trade's entry quotes; the fills set only the cost, the fees and Kelly's `b`. Nor measure `k̂` or the dashboard's spread calibration on another basis: `k̂` divides by the mean mid spread at each pair's first qualifying Monday (`backtester._interval_calibration`) and the spread calibration scores each trade's entry mid spread (`dashboard._spread_observations`), so both describe the quantity `k` multiplies. Keep `compute_trade` refusing a time-series pair without a usable mid spread rather than letting the forecast's zero clamp size it as riskless (pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_the_forecast_reads_the_midpoint_spread` and `::TestForecastReadsTheMidSpread`).
- **Do not restore enrichment's scan-time `pB` fallback for time-series pairs, delete either of its crossed-book guards (the later book's or the earlier book's, DR-78) or the backtest gate's skip of a crossed Monday, or drop the fee truncation (`scanner._levels_with_edge_after_fee`) from enrichment or from `validate_pair_price`.** The fail-closed reference and the crossed-book guards keep every time-series Kelly fraction under `1 − k`, which is what makes `config.max_kelly_fraction` — the affordability bound — a true bound once the per-trade cap is lifted: the forecast reads both books' midpoints, and a crossed book on either market can put the mid spread at or below what a winning pair nets after fees (`1 − pA − nB − fee` at the fills), where Kelly reaches `1 − k` (under the ask-gap forecast, a YES fill of 0.30 against a stale `pB` of 0.50 and a 0.90 YES bid sized at f* 0.74 at `k` 0.75, 0.72 at `k` 0.80); the fee truncation stops a floor of 0 (tier floors off) from averaging no-edge levels into a pair and dropping it (#51 again) in enrichment, and in `validate_pair_price` it is the one pre-submission re-check of the edge once the price-sum ceiling is 1.0. Keep the `round(1 − k, 12)` in `max_kelly_fraction`: `1 − 0.8` is `0.19999999999999996`.
